# SPEC FINAL — Kairos: a detector for process-level readiness violations (a subclass of order violations) in cFS

> **Revision 1.2 (2026-10-15).** This revision applies the PI's resolved decisions of 2026-10-15, recorded in `Architecture/spec/PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` (the "resolved-decisions record"; per-decision entries with value, date and source are in `PI_DECISIONS.md`). They are **decisions [D]** on the research plan, its scope and its wording, not facts about a tool, a paper or a source, and none is a result. The revision builds, installs and runs nothing and sets no research condition: a value that no reference fixes stays `unspecified_by_reference` (`CONDITIONS_POLICY.md`). The main changes: the validation ladder is approved as the first target and the thin MVC is no longer a first completion condition (P-LADDER); the experimental comparison is among existing process-level race work, by six analysis questions and six result states, and thread data-race tools and GPredict-, happens-before- and lockset-style re-implementations are related work only (§8.3); a rule alone never makes an occurrence accepted (QT-4, Q15 strict alternatives); the MLIR stage is removed (D6); an observation-effect check replaces the claim that only timing changes (D1); the case bases and controls are decided (P-R2CTRL, D22, P-C1HOLD, D13, P-C2, P-RGIT, P-F1GATE). The list is §0.10 (the terminology rename and the CORD cleanup are §0.11), and ADR-046 to ADR-052 (§11) record the decisions. **Applied by the later step Spec-C of the same revision (§0.11):** the rename of the judged object to "readiness specification" (decision T1 = (a); the old-to-new mapping is Appendix C) and the removal of every citation of the retired recorder CORD (decision D12). The Korean mirrors and the diagrams still use the old words until their own step.

- Date: 2026-10-12 (revision 1.0); revision 1.1: 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-15 (§0); revision 1.2: 2026-10-15 (§0.10, §0.11), with consistency edits on 2026-10-16 that apply the same decisions (D11's kernel recorded per run; recall placed in the extended evaluation throughout; ADR-013, ADR-020, ADR-034 and ADR-035 annotated; a re-check of the Spec-C sweeps, §0.11 item 4). Role: lead architect of panel AR2. Status: **final architecture specification**. For revision 1.0, nothing was built, installed, run against cFS, modified or committed for this document, and the files written were this one, `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md` and `PI_DECISIONS.md` in the same directory. Revision 1.1 built, installed and ran nothing either; its text is in the repository's history from commit `a92e7e9` (2026-10-13) on. Revision 1.2 built, installed and ran nothing either.
- This document revises `SPEC_v1.md` after three adversarial reviews: [X1] formal soundness (`review_X1-soundness.md`), [X2] cFS fidelity (`review_X2-cfs-fidelity.md`), [X3] feasibility and evaluation (`review_X3-feasibility-eval.md`). Every critical and major issue is fixed in place; the minor ones are fixed as well. The disposition of each issue, and where its fix lives, is in `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md`. Decisions that only the PI can take are in `PI_DECISIONS.md`.
- Name: **Kairos** (tool prefix `kz`). The name is a working label; the PI decided on 2026-10-15 to keep it (P-NAME [D]). No name-collision search was made yet; one is made just before the name is made public. No expansion of the name and no academic meaning for it is stated or invented here.
- Inputs and how they are cited:
  - AR1 reports [R01]–[R07] in `Architecture/background/` (`01_cfs_osal_psp.md` … `07_env_feasibility.md`, listed in its `README.md`), cited with their own section and `path:line` citations.
  - Proposals [A] (`proposal_A-static-model.md`, CAIRN), [B] (`proposal_B-dynamic-predictive.md`, Sextant), [C] (`proposal_C-hybrid-layered.md`, Kairos). Judgments [J1] (formal), [J2] (cFS), [J3] (methods). Reviews [X1], [X2], [X3].
  - Research records: `CONDITIONS_POLICY.md` [POL], `cases/ledger.md` [LED], `results/R2_gdb_v701.md` [R2g], `env/BASELINE_MEASURE.md` [BM]; the user's v0.1 design [v0.1]. **Decision D12 (2026-10-15):** the results of the retired recorder (CORD) are not evidence in current documents and are not cited. The R2 execution evidence is the raw gdb records `env/logs/r2_g1..3_*` with `results/R2_gdb_v701.md` [R2g]. The reference entry `[R2c]` and every citation of it were removed from this text in revision 1.2 (§0.11).
  - The pinned tree `/home/user/work/procrace/cfs_ref/cFS` (cFS `088b2fa8`; cFE `c5fb2b4d`, OSAL `d2d877a6`, PSP `c4b3b0b6`). Code paths are relative to it. Lines re-read for this document are marked "(re-read)".
- Labels: **[F]** fact with citation (code, record or report). **[M]** measured, in [R07], a record, a review ("[X3, here]") or for this document ("measured here"). **[I]** inference or design reasoning; not executed. **[D]** design decision (an ADR in §11 records it). **[U]** unverified. Literature is cited only as [R04]/[R05]/[R06] verified it, with their status tags (V-doc, V-abs, V-me, P, UNVERIFIED). Where a concept is used without a verified citation, the text says "citation: open literature check".
- Policy [POL] (line numbers of `CONDITIONS_POLICY.md`): every bound, budget and parameter is one of
  - `from-reference` (POL L15): a code constant or pinned document, cited;
  - `deviation` (POL L16, L18–21): differs from or is absent from every reference, recorded with reason, effect and necessity. **Every value that a person chooses because resources are finite** (numbers of runs, work-unit caps, sample sizes) is a deviation, never `unspecified_by_reference`;
  - `unspecified_by_reference` (POL L17): no reference fixes it and **no value is chosen**; if the analysis needs a premise, it is recorded as an assumption.
  - Tool parameters follow POL L22: event-based control, or constants that a reference contains. Tool constants appear in code only as `KZ_REF(value, "path:line")` (§9.2). Values used in Kairos's own unit tests are not research conditions (POL L23).

## 0. Summary

**Revision 1.1 (2026-10-12 to 2026-10-15):** answers the external review `history/review_EXT_20261012.md`. The amendment is summarized in §0.7 and recorded in ADR-039 to ADR-044 (§11); the disposition of every review item is in §0.8.

**Revision 1.2 (2026-10-15):** applies the PI's resolved decisions of 2026-10-15 (`PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md`). The changes are listed in §0.10 and §0.11 and recorded in ADR-046 to ADR-052 (§11); the evaluation plan (§8) and the first target (§9.3) change most. Statements of §0.1 to §0.8 below describe the earlier revisions and are updated only where a decision of 2026-10-15 changes what they say, and in their wording by the terminology rename T1 and the removal of the retired recorder's citations (§0.11).

### 0.1 What Kairos decides

For every **readiness instance** in a cFS build (an instance of a **readiness specification**: a use of a framework resource that requires that resource to be in some lifecycle state), Kairos produces a verdict record. Its axes are orthogonal (§1.10):

- **per semantics σ and OSAL model level M** (a cell for each (σ, M) that was analysed), and per bound b (b = ∞ for certificates, runs and transfers from them; each completed h for the explorer): the **safety record** (the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i`, with their routes and the decision route of `¬OK_i`, and the bad and good witnesses with their occurrence ordinals), the **class** derived from it, its refinements, the **reach** of the use in that σ (`proved(run | confirmed model path)`, `assumed`, `model(b)`, `unreached(b)`, `contested`), the **progress result** (`stall-witness`, `no-stall-within(b)` or `unknown(reason)`, by default `unknown(not_checked)`) (§1.8, §1.10), and, for every certificate-based statement, the **proof status** of the lemmas it uses (`proved`, `depends-on-stated-assumption` or `unproved`; §6.2);
- `provider ∈ {present, none, env-absent, unknown}`;
- evidence on two axes: **realization** (`certificate`, `model(b)`, `predicted`, `predicted-modelled`, `predicted-extrapolated`, `realized`) and **naturalness** of realized runs (`natural`, `held-F0`, `gated-F1`, `timeout-exercised`; flag `derived`), with `n of N` and the pool key;
- shape; at the caller level the outcome label and harm; at the framework level `fw_doc` (per instance) and `fw_conf` (per cell); the observed consequence; chain links, scope (`in-scope` or `teardown`), B hash, Φ hash, tool hash (§1.6 "Three judgements", §1.10);
- the **source tier** of the readiness specification behind the instance (`contract-backed`, `requirement-derived`, `admitted`, `issue-derived`, `code-derived`, `researcher`, `unreferenced`), derived at report time from its provenance records (§1.6 "Naming and source tiers"). An in-scope occurrence rejected by the caller-level Allowed is a **readiness violation**, a subclass of order violation.

`class ∈ {inactive, protected (refinement: allowed-not-ready), fixed-order, no-final-status, schedule-dependent (refinement: timeout-dependent), ordinal-split, unknown(reason)}` is a **safety** classification derived from statements over finite executions: `¬Bad_i` (no in-scope occurrence of the use that is reached is VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED), `¬OK_i` (none is READY or TOLERATED) and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i` (none is decided not ready). An occurrence whose call returns before one of its conjunct LPs, with no conjunct found false, is `NOT_EVALUATED` and counted by none of them. `protected` establishes `¬Bad_i`; `fixed-order` establishes `¬OK_i` together with a decision route (a bad witness, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the occurrence's decision); `no-final-status` both. `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)`. Whether the use is reached at all is recorded on the reach axis. `schedule-dependent` needs a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal; witnesses at different ordinals only give `ordinal-split`. Permanent waiting, deadlock and other progress failures belong to no class: they are the per-cell **progress** result, which has the per-instance refinement `timeout-masked-liveness` and the root flag `tml-root`. A good-side flag `timeout-rescued` marks instances whose good occurrences all need a time-based transition. `σ ∈ {σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched], σ_E}`; `M ∈ {M_api, M_impl}`.

**No class means "safe", and no progress value means "live" or "deadlock-free".** The absence of an alarm is the absence of evidence and maps to `unknown(reason)`. No σ_E class is ever derived from the absence of bad runs. A universal class is never reported without its reach and progress values (§1.10).

### 0.2 The formal core on one page

1. **One typed event model (KEM) and one framework profile Φ_cFS.** Φ is written once in a small declarative DSL with a **typed core expression language over resource state** (§2.3). It declares resource kinds with lifecycle automata, operations with effects, a **linearization point (LP) per conjunct of every readiness precondition** (with **capture points** for values that a caller loads earlier), HB rules with stratum and robustness tag, and readiness specifications with a readiness precondition (the required state), a framework-predicate oracle, allowed not-ready outcomes with a reference horizon, the application or mission requirement `appreq`, harm, provenance (each record with its side: supplier, caller or rule) and an implicit **scope** ("before the shutdown request").
2. **Φ is interpreted three ways over the same event type**, through one evaluator in `libkz-core`:
   - **abstractly**, over configuration-closed per-actor skeletons in two views: a **certificate view** in which every unbounded loop is star-closed, and an **exploration view** cut at a horizon h. Census, MustBefore, PWB and certificates use the certificate view only;
   - **concretely**, over traces of the unmodified gcc binary: HB with vector clocks (handler-scoped program order on loopers), readiness checking at LPs, lock-reversing prediction;
   - **operationally**, in a bounded per-candidate explorer (gated, late).
3. **Static ordering is a must-analysis.** MustBefore is a greatest fixpoint with meets over predecessors (T-PO) and over all spawn sites (T-SPAWN), with ∅ at every root. Observation edges (T-OBS) need a complete, transition-aware writer set, an **await predicate extracted from the code** and a falsifier. Writers that cannot lie between the falsifier and the observation are removed by checkable rules: own-actor no-path, PWB before the falsifier or after the observation (by the frontier or the once rule), and key separation. Branches that can only end in a terminal event are cut (T-TERM). Locks alone give no order.
   - Fixed-order and protected are certified over **precedes-whenever-both-occur** (PWB). PWB needs **once(τ)**: the actor runs at most once per execution, proved by an at-most-once analysis.
   - `kz proofcheck` re-validates the inference steps of every certificate. It is a separate module that shares no analysis code with the producer, except the shared `libkz-core`, and it works **relative to a stated trusted base**: the extracted facts, the skeleton, the closure lists, Φ and `libkz-core`. Steps that call `libkz-core`'s evaluator are marked "via T5". Every certificate and verdict carries a four-part **trust ledger**: re-validated steps, extracted facts, trusted entries and refutable items (§4.5.1); the lemma entries among the trusted entries carry their proof status (§6.2). Certificate-mutation tests check the checker; **program mutants** test the analysis itself (§8.5).
4. **Writer sets** come from a field-based, memset- and out-pointer-aware writer extraction at the Clang AST level under an explicit typed-access assumption A-TYPE (stage S3a), with Φ-declared effects supplying keys and guards. Each writer entry carries a transition status and a **key status** (`MUST_KEY`, `MAY_KEY`, `NO_KEY`). Agreement between the two oracles is obligation O7. Andersen-style points-to over IR is a later option (S3b), adopted only if it removes measured blockers.
5. **Semantics.**
   - The four semantics: σ_U (untimed; retries exhaust only after their reference bound); σ_U∞ (σ_U without **time-based transitions**: no non-periodic timed wait times out; the last attempt of a delay-paced retry has no failing outcome, so it waits until it can succeed when it is one transition at level M, and otherwise leaves its actor stranded; the ES kill timer never kills an app that has not exited; periodic waits, timer ticks and the cleanup of exited apps stay; give-ups that Φ has not yet declared time-based, such as HS's application monitor and TIME's flywheel entry, also stay, and results that depend on them are labelled `tb-undeclared`); σ_R1[P_sched] (fixed priority on one CPU, parameterized by a cited scheduling profile; a separate transition system whose projection is contained in σ_U); σ_E (the real binary).
   - **Safety is defined on finite executions and progress on reachable states** of the product with an instance monitor (§1.8).
   - **Containment is prefix containment, stated per model level**:
     - the finite executions of σ_U∞ are exactly the finite executions of σ_U without time-based transitions;
     - after projection, the finite executions of σ_R1 are finite executions of σ_U at the same M;
     - every finite real run is a finite execution of (σ_U, M_impl).
   - **Maximal executions are not contained.** A timed wait whose predicate never holds ends a σ_U∞ execution that σ_U extends with its timeout.
   - M_api verdicts are claims relative to the OSAL API contract (portability claims). They transfer to σ_E only when the cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation, or when the M_impl verdict agrees.
   - **What transfers.** Each universal safety statement transfers down at the same M and b (a bounded statement to σ_R1 only from a model whose cone contains the σ_U cone), and to σ_E only with b = ∞. Good and bad witnesses transfer up. Reach, `allowed-not-ready`, `schedule-dependent`, `ordinal-split` and progress are computed per σ, except that deadlock cycles and leaked locks are stall states in every σ. Nothing becomes a σ_E frequency.
6. **Strata.** S (structural, must-order), O (observed synchronization, reorderable in prediction), C_σ (schedule constraints of σ_R1, never HB). Between-handler program order on a looper is **not** an HB edge; it survives only as a FIFO-derived constraint in prediction (CAFA, DCatch).
7. **Layers check each other.** Obligations O1–O10 plus the certificate checker. A contradiction suspends the rule or model involved, sets `unknown(model_fault)` with a model-fault record, ends the feedback loop for that Φ version, and is never resolved silently. A contradiction of an M_api-only certificate by the real binary is portability-hazard evidence, not a model fault.
8. **Evaluation measures misses.** In the extended evaluation (revision 1.2, D8/P-RECALL: separate from the five-case validation, results limited to the variant set), a pre-registered recall protocol on history-derived program mutants and pre-fix/post-fix pairs, frozen before F2, reports detected / unknown / missed per layer, with class-correct reported apart where a class label exists, per mutant and per originating fix, with intervals whose unit and independence assumption are stated (§8.7); blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record (§7.5); a certificate contradicted by a mutant's documented behaviour is reported as a soundness failure.

| J3-M7 | Loop reproducibility | Fixed-point test excludes counts and pool keys; work units, never wall clock (revised by X1-m8, X3-M10) | §4.14 |
| J2-17 | Identity and persistence | Generations (B_restart) as well as run sequences; canonical content-only Init hash (revised by X2-12, X3-M3) | §1.7, §3.12 |
| J3-M12, J1-14 | NOT_RUN never a false negative | Unchanged | §8.3, §6.4 |

### 0.6 What the reviews changed (summary; the full table is `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md`)

57 issues: X1 2 critical / 12 major / 9 minor; X2 1 / 9 / 4; X3 1 / 11 / 8. All are resolved in this document; none is rejected. The ten changes with the largest effect:

1. **Two skeleton views** (X1-C1). Universal verdicts and `inactive` come only from the certificate view, in which message loops are star-closed. Results from the h-cut view carry h in b. `kz proofcheck` rejects any certificate step in a truncated region.
2. **Containment per M** (X1-C2). M_api results are API-contract (portability) claims; σ_E transfer goes through M_impl. Since EXT-20261012 §4.2 the containment is stated over finite executions only (prefix containment); maximal containment is not claimed (§1.8).
3. **The R2 certificate is repaired and fully written out** (X2-01, X1-M2, X1-M3, X1-M8, X1-M1): own-actor no-path exclusion, once-PWB, MUST_KEY and allocation records, a code-extracted await predicate, a capture point for the stamped AppId, and every `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entry listed (App. A.1); the full four-part trust ledger follows EXT-20261012 §4.3 (§4.5.1, App. A.1 step 9a).
4. **σ_U∞ and the refinements are well defined** (X1-M5; revised by EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2). σ_U∞ removes time-based transitions. Stall is a progress result defined by dead actors, not a class outcome. timeout-dependent and timeout-rescued are derived from the per-σ safety results, and timeout-masked-liveness from the per-σ progress results.
5. **Shutdown is modelled as on pc-linux** (X1-M6, X2-04): the requester never returns and aborts after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; `OS_DeleteAllObjects` runs only on the PSP main thread after `CFE_ES_Main` returns and `ShutdownReq` is observed; readiness specifications are scoped to "before the shutdown request".
6. **cFS mechanisms corrected** (X2-02 … X2-10): K-SELF.es at seven call sites; OSAL timed waits; SB-actor K-MODE; two-phase transmit and MsgLim; σ_R1 scheduling profiles; basis closure; header-carried MsgIds; loader scope.
7. **Evaluation measures what Kairos misses** (X3-C1): recall on program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, with soundness failures counted (since revision 1.2 part of the extended evaluation, separate from the five-case validation, with results for the variant set only; D8/P-RECALL, §8.1).
8. **Feasibility repairs** (X3-M1, X3-M4, X3-M5): attach cookies instead of IP keys; S3a AST-level writer extraction; a thin MVC with estimates and a cut list (its role as the first target is revised by EXT-20261012 §7: the validation ladder, ADR-044).
9. **POL compliance** (X3-M9, X3-M10): N and caps are deviations; budgets are deterministic work units; replay is byte-exact.
10. **Honest prior-work positioning and stronger baselines** (X1-M12, X3-M6): InitRacer, ExceptioNULL, RVPredict/GPredict credited; comparators given the same property information (a GPredict-style lock-reversing predictor with the readiness precondition and the caller-level Allowed entries encoded as far as its formalism permits, and a plain state monitor), a DefUse-style learner, and helgrind/DRD as an external reference point; a stripped variant is never a baseline and, if kept, is a bundle ablation (P-ABL-ALLOWED) (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.1). **Withdrawn in revision 1.2:** those comparators and the external reference point are related work only, and the stripped bundle is not kept (ADR-046); the credits to prior work stay.

### 0.7 Amendment EXT-20261012 (external review of commit b4ac55f: items §4.1–§4.3, §5.1–§5.4, §6.4, §7 and §8)

1. **Safety and progress are separate** (§4.1). Classes are derived from safety statements over finite executions, `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` and, from a PROTECTED certificate, `¬NR_i`, which certificates prove (L-PR, L-FO); both of the first two together are `no-final-status`, and `fixed-order` also needs a decision route. `schedule-dependent` needs a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal; otherwise `ordinal-split`. An occurrence begins at its call; a call that returns before one of its conjunct LPs, with no conjunct found false, gives `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts; `returned_and_tested` accepts only at a normal continuation that its entry cites (strict alternative Q15, revision 1.2); and no status or horizon verdict is decided after or by a process-terminal step. Permanent waits and deadlocks form a per-cell progress result, evaluated on the product with an instance monitor, with kinds (`deadlock-cycle`, `leaked-lock`), qualifiers (`idle`, `terminal-pending`, `choice-dependent`), wait-for roots and progress chains. Only `exh` decides it, within b; a realized hang with a wait-for fact can witness a stall; otherwise the result is `unknown(not_checked)`. (ADR-039)
2. **Finite executions, prefix containment and per-σ existence** (§4.2). Pref and Max are defined separately, and L-CONT is prefix containment. Maximal containment is not claimed. Reach, witnesses and progress are computed per σ and per bound. σ_U∞ removes every time-based transition (non-periodic timeouts, delay-paced last-attempt failures at their deciding transition at level M, and the give-ups that Φ declares, among them the forced kill of an app that has not exited), and stall is defined by deadness, with excluded states as sinks, instead of global quiescence. σ_R1 is a separate system with a projection. Without the explorer no refinement is established. A disagreement between an `exh` witness and a certificate goes to the new obligation O10. (ADR-040)
3. **The checker's trust scope is explicit** (§4.3). `kz proofcheck` re-validates inference steps relative to a named trusted base, marks the steps that call the shared `libkz-core`, and every certificate and verdict carries a four-part trust ledger. (ADR-041)
4. **Three judgements of a not-ready use** (§6.4). A not-ready use is judged at the framework level (what the supplier documents and does: `fw_doc` per instance, `fw_conf` per cell), at the caller level (what the using code must achieve: the new field `appreq` of the readiness specification, and the Allowed entries that have a `caller` record; since revision 1.2 a `rule` record alone never makes an occurrence accepted, QT-4 strict alternative), and as the observed consequence, which is recorded for every not-ready occurrence. Every provenance record carries `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}`, and the kind `requirement` is added. A supplier's documented result never makes an occurrence TOLERATED, and a conflict between `appreq` and a `caller` entry leaves the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named; the PI decided on 2026-10-15 (QT-5) that the conflict is stated and the judgement withheld, without an invented precedence. The SB no-subscriber loss is therefore reported as a framework-level `documented-not-error` result (cSB4305.5) beside a caller-level LOSS-UNDER-ORDER unless a consumer or mission reference decides (§1.11, §3.9, App. A.1 step 13a), not as a `documented_drop`. No status value, class, horizon kind or monitor kind is added. The PI decided on 2026-10-15: QT-2 and QT-3 approved, the strict alternatives for QT-4 and Q15, QT-5 held, QT-9 separated (ADR-048; §1.6). Decision T1 = (a) renames the judged object to "readiness specification", its precondition `Req` to "readiness precondition" and its violation to "readiness violation"; the rename was applied in the dedicated step Spec-C (§0.11, Appendix C, ADR-052). (ADR-043, ADR-048)
5. **Scope of the checking beyond R2.** The definitions of items 1–4 were walked through C1, C2, C3, C5, R2 and the examples of App. A as expectations [I] while this amendment was drafted; that walk-through is not kept as a separate record, and its results are the expectations that App. A and §9.3 state; nothing was run for this amendment. Only R2 has path-execution evidence on v7.0.1 ([R2g]). C1's basis is decided (D22, 2026-10-15 [D]: the public BP basis, candidate pair CF `281a941` → `833fdbb`), but none of the case bases (C1's, C2's role-derived pair under P-C2, the 6.4.x bases of C3 and C5 under D13) is built or version-checked, and the results that would distinguish the other cases (`timeout-dependent`, timeout-masked-liveness, stall witnesses, `allowed-not-ready`, `ordinal-split`) need the explorer (S7). The litmus programs LP-CASE-C1/C2/C3/C5 and the real code shapes of LP-ALLOW (App. A.8) reproduce each case's mechanism on the v7.0.1 OSAL; they are planned, not run. No validation on the other cases is claimed until they and the bases exist. Item 7 makes each case's path and control normative and requests the basis decisions now; until the bases of C1 (D22), C2 (P-C2), C3 and C5 (D13) are built and give tool output, tool-level evidence on a case's own phenomenon exists for R2 only, and a result confined to R2 is reported as a single-case result (item 6).
6. **Evaluation: comparison kinds, three outcomes, an exposure record and units** (§5.1–§5.4). Original tools, re-implementations of prior techniques given the same property information or their own, and one-element ablations of Kairos are kept apart; neither an ablation result nor a re-implementation's silence is evidence about what a prior technique can express or what an original tool would report, and GPredict is described only as [R04] records it (P-PAPERS). Every tool is reported per labelled unit by detection, classification and evidence; detection counts an alarm and a `not-ready-unjudged` report alike for every tool, and only detection is compared by a paired test. Classification is compared with a pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1. The references call C1, C2, C3 and C5 races and make no class statement for R2; how "race" maps to the per-(σ, M) classes was PI decision P-CLASSGT, decided on 2026-10-15 as no automatic conversion of the word into a class; agreement or disagreement with those statements is reported as an outcome, never scored. A fix text is a ground-truth source, not a source of blindness: blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record. Every count names its unit (U1–U6), every test and interval states its unit and independence assumption (P-STATS), and the five design cases, several of which share a resource family, a fix release or a shape (§8.2), are not counted as independent samples. (ADR-042) **Revision 1.2:** the re-implemented comparators of this item are withdrawn; the experimental comparison is among existing process-level race work (§8.3; ADR-046, ADR-047), and the statistical handling follows P-STATS (§8.7).
7. **First target and per-case paths** (§7, §8). The first target is rung V1 of a validation ladder: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates taken from the stage gates. The full thin-MVC gates stay, as rung V1b. R2's corrected-order control (V2, P-R2CTRL) and C1's rung (V3, D22) follow in parallel, and no V1 result is worded as a certificate before the lemma obligations of that wording are written (V0-P). Every design case has a normative path in §9.3: its cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned revisions and an expectation sheet frozen before any tool output); the first tool step that needs no new decision, which for C1, C2, C3 and C5 is a generic-layer result on a v7.0.1 counterpart and is never reported as case evidence; the first tool step on its own phenomenon, with the decision it needs (D22 for C1, decided 2026-10-15 as the public BP basis, §0.9; P-C2 for C2; D13 with Q16 for C3 and C5); and its control: the pre/post-fix pairs of C1, C3 and C5, C2's 2 × 2, and, since R2 has no merged fix, R2's history and variant substitutes (§8.2). These decisions were taken on 2026-10-15 (§0.10; ADR-050), and the choices and details they need are recorded before F1 (P-F1GATE). The review's sum of the S0–S3a estimates is correct arithmetic on [I] inputs and is not used as a plan value; no estimate exists for any rung. (ADR-044)

### 0.9 Corrections of 2026-10-15 (open items 7, 8 and 12 of `REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md` §5; PI decisions D20 and D22; R2 wording)

These edits answer the blocking formal items that the final check left open, the additional completion conditions of PI decision D20 (option A adopted) and the C1 basis decision D22, and the R2 wording rules of the roadmap of 2026-10-15 (its §3). They change definitions and wording only. Nothing was built, installed or run, no Kairos result exists, and the PI decisions are recorded as decisions [D], not as verified facts. No existing section, item or ADR number changed (§0.9 and ADR-045 are new). The old and the new statement of each correction, with a minimal example, are in `plan/formal_corrections.md`; the proof status of the first R2 result is in `plan/proof_status_R2.md`.

1. **σ_E statements say what L-FO and L-PR prove** (open item 7). Transfer rule 2 (§1.8) and the other σ_E statements (§1.10, G2, L-TRANS (2), App. A.1 step 9, App. A.7) quantify over the decided in-scope occurrences of the finite real runs. `¬NR_i` is "none is decided not ready", i.e. every decided one is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED`, and a `NOT_EVALUATED` early return is allowed by all three safety statements. Litmus LP-EARLY has a PROTECTED variant (c) (App. A.8).
2. **L-CONT (c) and the other lemmas of the reach and transfer steps are in the trust ledger** (open item 8). §4.5.1 part (iii) lists L-CONT (c) and L-STATUS for every cell whose reach or witness rests on a run, the certificate's own cell included; part (iv) lists their run-side refutations (O1, O2, O4, O5); App. A.1 step 9a lists both for R2's ledgers.
3. **Proof status** (D20). The values `proved`, `depends-on-stated-assumption` and `unproved` are defined once, in §6.2, and assigned to every lemma and guarantee in the table at the end of §6.2; the records of §2.3 carry them, and every certificate-based statement is printed with them. A lemma that is only listed as an obligation is never printed as proved (§6.2 PS1–PS4; §6.4 items 44–46; ADR-045).
4. **R2 wording.** An event request that EVS refuses is "rejected", never "lost": the harm token `event_lost_silently` is renamed `event_request_rejected`. A claim about system calls rests on code analysis or on a system-call observation, not on a debugger log (§6.4 item 47; §1.6 `harm`; App. A.1 steps 8 and 13a; §8.3).
5. **C1's basis (D22).** Decided as the public BP basis with the candidate pair CF `281a941` → `833fdbb` [D]. The semaphore names, the startup order and the configured priorities are public values of nasa/bp `b051eb0`, not values of the original reporter's environment; that environment and the full version combination stay unknown (§9.3, §10.1, ADR-044, App. A.4). The earlier statement that the name, order and priority have no reference is withdrawn.
6. **§0.8**'s first paragraph points to `ISSUE_RESOLUTION.md` and `REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md` (open item 12).

The Korean mirrors (`Architecture/01_overview.md` to `10_decisions_adr.md`) are not part of this edit; they follow in their own steps. The terminology rename T1 and the removal of the CORD citations were applied afterwards (§0.11). The places that described D20 as proposed or pending (ADR-039, §10.1) and the decisions of 2026-10-15 other than D20 and D22 (P-R2CTRL, D13, P-C2 and the rest) were updated by revision 1.2 (§0.10).

### 0.10 Decisions of 2026-10-15 applied (revision 1.2)

Source of every item: `Architecture/spec/PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` (the resolved-decisions record). The per-decision entries, with the resolved value, the date and the source, are in `PI_DECISIONS.md`; ADR-046 to ADR-051 (§11) record the decisions with the record as their source. The items are **decisions [D]** on the research plan, its scope and its wording. They are not facts about tools, papers or sources, and none is an execution record: nothing was built, installed or run. Where a decision leaves a value open, the value stays `unspecified_by_reference` and is decided at the stage the decision names (D14, D15), never here. The tables of §0.1 to §0.8 describe the earlier revisions and were changed only where a decision of 2026-10-15 changes what they say.

| Decision | Resolved value [D] | Where it acts in this text |

### 0.11 Terminology rename T1 and removal of CORD citations (step Spec-C of revision 1.2)

Sources: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (T1), §4.1 (D12), §5 and §6.2 (the rules for the citation cleanup); `background/08_terminology.md` (the T1 research). What stays: 24 uses before the publication in each of the three runs (ES 20, EVS 4), 3 violating static sites, 6 task × function contexts, 12 call strings, 223 / 229 / 229 SB-caller uses and 199 / 205 / 205 after the publication and SB's registration, 251 / 257 / 257 hits of `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` (all callers), OPERATIONAL at 1613–1636 ms without gdb, and the 39 SB sites (a source recount in the gdb record). ## 1. Problem definition

### 1.1 Scope: "process-level"

Following v0.1 §2.1 and [R06 F1], *process-level* means ordering between the logical work of different cFS applications, service tasks and execution contexts, independent of OS process boundaries. In the native POSIX port they are all threads of one process ([F] [R05 §1 F1]; `osal/src/os/posix/src/os-impl-tasks.c:568`). The objects of interest are **framework resources**: named OSAL objects, ES app/task/lib records, service identities, SB routes and pipes, pipe capacity, TBL registry entries, phases and app states, CDS blocks, callbacks, loader symbols and files. Raw memory words are not objects of interest.

A memory-level data race found in passing (e.g. the unlocked EVS filter counters, [R02 §3.3]) goes into a separate class **M**. It is never counted as an order violation ([R02 §8.4 item 7]).

### 1.2 Actors, instances, once(τ) and the four identities

**Actors** `T = T_osal ∪ T_raw ∪ T_env` [F] ([R01 §2.6]; [R02 §1.1]):

- `T_osal`: tasks created by `OS_TaskCreate`: cFE core main tasks, app main tasks and child tasks (`ES_BG_TASK`, TIME's tone and local children, FM's child, …).
- `T_raw`: threads without an OSAL task record: the PSP main thread (the "ES startup thread" running `CFE_ES_Main`, later `OS_Application_Run`), the console writer, the OSAL timebase thread `cFS-Master`, signal-handler contexts, and the `linux_sysmon` worker (absent here, [R01 §2.6]).
- `T_env`: environment roots (ground commands, SBN peers, the stop command). They have no incoming HB edges ([R04 §4 row 17]).

A **static actor** τ is a closure entry: a spawn site plus an entry function (§3.1). An **actor instance** is `(τ, generation, n)`, where n counts the executions of τ's spawn sites by its spawner and is 1 for once actors (next paragraph). Skeleton nodes belong to static actors.

**Actor-instance names across executions** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Occurrence ordinals compare occurrences of different executions (§1.6, §1.10), so actor instances need names that do not depend on the execution's identifiers. In the model an actor instance is named `(τ, generation, n)`, where n counts the executions of τ's spawn sites by its own canonically named spawner; n = 1 for once actors and raw threads. A callback is not an actor instance: its occurrences belong to the actor that runs it (the timebase thread for OSAL timer callbacks, TIME's task for synch callbacks, `ES_BG_TASK` for its jobs). In σ_E, the run-level identity `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)` below plays this role. It is mapped to `(τ, generation, n)` through the closure: the creator's canonical name and the call site of its creation event (`creator_event`), matched to τ's spawn sites (for a script app, its startup-script line, §1.7), give τ and its spawner; n is the creation ordinal among that spawner's executions of τ's spawn sites; the generation counts the app's restarts in the run. O5 checks the closure that the map uses. A pair of a run witness and a model witness at the same ordinal is formed only through this map; an identity it does not resolve forms no mixed pair.

**once(τ)** [D] (fixes [X1-M2]). τ is *once* iff in every execution at most one instance of τ exists. It is proved, never asserted:
- the PSP main thread is once by definition (it is the process entry, A3);
- an actor with spawn sites `s_1 … s_k` is once iff (i) every `s_i` lies in a once actor, (ii) no `s_i` lies in a star-closed loop region of the certificate view (§4.3), and (iii) the sites are pairwise exclusive: they lie in the same once actor and no skeleton path connects any two of them in either direction (a run of an actor is one path, so two exclusive sites cannot both execute);
- environment roots and asynchronous contexts (signal handlers, the exception path) are never once.

The facts used are recorded in every certificate that relies on them, and `kz proofcheck` re-validates them with its own once routine on the skeleton, which it trusts (§4.5, T2). For actors that are not once, frontier and once-PWB arguments are forbidden. In the restart family `B_restart` (one documented request, §3.8), the skeleton is duplicated per generation `g ∈ {0, 1}`, each copy with its own spawn site, so that each copy is once.

A node `n` is **once** iff its actor is once and `n` is not inside a star-closed loop region: `n` then occurs at most once per execution.

**Identity record** per instance: `⟨kind, osal_id, serial, tid, name, creator_event, generation⟩`. OSAL IDs are `(type << 16) | serial` and change across runs; cross-run correlation uses `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)` ([F] [R01 §1.2, §6.2 item 3]).

**Four identity fields per event**, never merged ([R06 F1]; v0.1 §2.1):

| Field | Definition | Why it is separate (cFS fact) |
|---|---|---|
| `exec(e)` | the actor instance that executes `e` | SB code runs in the caller's task ([R02 §2.4, §2.8]) |
| `owner(e)` | the ES app whose task set contains `exec(e)` at `e` (generation-aware); `⊥` for raw threads | an app has a main task and child tasks ([R02 §1.12]) |
| `svc(e)` | the stack of active frames: **API frames** (SB, EVS, TBL, …) and **on-behalf frames** `cb(registrant)` | TIME synch callbacks run in TIME's task for the registering app ([R02 §5.3]); FS callbacks run in `ES_BG_TASK` for SB/TBL ([R02 §6.1]); OSAL timer callbacks run in the timebase thread for the timer's creator ([R01 §3]) |
| `stamp(e)` | an identity value the code passes or stores explicitly (the `AppID` argument of `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID`; the pipe owner stored by `CFE_SB_CreatePipe`) | R2: the ES task executes SB code that stamps SB's unpublished AppId ([R2g §2]) |

### 1.3 Resources, kinds, lifecycle automata, generations and keys

A resource is `r = ⟨kind, scope, key, gen⟩`.
- `scope ∈ {process, persistent}`. Persistent resources survive the process: CDS, boot record, reset area, files on `/dev/shm/osal:RAM` (mounted as `/ram`), `EEPROM.DAT` ([R01 §5.3]).
- `key` is a name, numeric ID, `(MsgId, pipe)` pair, slot index, path, symbol with scope, or an **allocation record** `ρ(n)` (the record allocated by reservation node `n`, §4.2). In static mode a key may be **symbolic** (e.g. "the string in CF table field `chan[i].sem_name`") or `⊤`.
- `gen` is the OSAL serial, the cFE resource-ID serial ([R02 §6.2]) or a restart generation.

Each kind `k` has a lifecycle automaton `A_k = (Q_k, q0_k, Σ_k, δ_k)` (typestate; Strom & Yemini, IEEE TSE 1986 [V-me, R06 §9]). Anchors are in [R01 §1.5–1.7], [R02 §1–7] and §3. Examples:

| Kind | States | Publication anchor (LP) |
|---|---|---|
| `osal.obj<T>` | FREE → ALLOC → RES_C (name invisible) → RES_CN (name visible, RESERVED, type lock **not** held) → LIVE → RES_D → FREE | final ID write under the type lock, `osapi-idmap.c:1092`, unlock `:1105` ([R01 §1.5]) |
| `es.app` / `es.task` / `es.lib` | FREE → RESERVED → LIVE → RESERVED (cleanup) → FREE | script apps: reserve `cfe_es_apps.c:821`, publish `:869`, failure free `:893`; core apps: reserve `cfe_es_start.c:803`, publish `:818`, failure clear `:823` (re-read); task record `cfe_es_apps.c:694–703` |
| `es.self(exec)` | ABSENT → LIVE (both the executing task's task record and its owner's App record resolvable) | the publications above; read by `CFE_ES_GetAppRecordByContext` / `GetTaskRecordByContext` (§3.3) |
| `cell(loc)` | INVALID → VALID (generic: a shared location whose value is a resource identity) | the stores to `loc` that make its value valid; e.g. `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` written through `CFE_ES_GetAppID(&CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`). Display names such as `svc.identity(SB)` are labels of a cell, not Φ entries |
| `evs.reg(slot)` | UNREG → REG; `CFE_EVS_Register` first memsets the slot (`cfe_evs.c:56`, re-read): a disabler on re-registration | store `cfe_evs.c:106`, no lock ([R02 §3.1]) |
| `sb.pipe` | FREE → RES (OSAL queue name visible) → LIVE → RES_D → FREE | `cfe_sb_api.c:153,163,190–194` |
| `sb.route(m,p)` | NONE ↔ ROUTED | `CFE_SB_SubscribeFull` under the SB lock ([R02 §2.3]) |
| `sb.queue(p)` | depth `0..cap_B(p)` | `cap_B(p) = min(requested, msg_max)` **only when euid ≠ 0** (`osal/src/bsp/generic-linux/src/bsp_start.c:56–74`; truncation `os-impl-queues.c:98–105`, re-read); requested depth when euid = 0 [I: up to the kernel's hard limit]. [M] `msg_max` 10, 22 of 26 pipes truncated ([BM §3.1]) |
| `sb.msglim(m,p)` | `BuffCount 0..MsgId2PipeLim(m,p)` | limit per subscription; default `CFE_PLATFORM_SB_DEFAULT_MSG_LIMIT` = 4 (`cfe/cmake/sample_defs/example_platform_cfg.h:1098`); checked at the route snapshot (`cfe_sb_priv.c:1058–1064`, re-read) |
| `sb.mode.subreport` | OFF ↔ ON | store in `CFE_SB_SetSubscriptionReporting` (`cfe_sb_task.c:1226`), reached from SB's command handler `cfe_sb_task.c:296` (re-read) |
| `es.phase` | EARLY_INIT < CORE_STARTUP < CORE_READY < APPS_INIT < OPERATIONAL (monotone) | `cfe_es_start.c:87,183,194,216,234` |
| `es.appstate(rec)` | UNDEFINED < EARLY_INIT < LATE_INIT < RUNNING < WAITING < STOPPED | raise `cfe_es_api.c:573` (level by caller type and requested state, `:536–563`), `:471`, `:399` (external apps only); scan `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` (external apps, from RUNNING) (re-read) |
| `es.shutdown` | RUN → SHUTDOWN_REQ → TEARDOWN (process scope; §3.5) | `ShutdownReq = true` (`psp/fsw/pc-linux/src/cfe_psp_support.c:87`); `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (`cfe_psp_start.c:507`) (re-read) |
| `tbl.table(name)` | FREE → REGISTERED → LOADED → ACTIVE, with pending substates and an access lock flag | [R02 §4.2–4.5] |
| `es.cds(name)` (persistent) | ABSENT → REGISTERED(new \| restored) | `es/cfe_es_cds.c:311–477`; restore also within one run after a restart or reload (`:342–348, :461–472`) |
| `time.synchcb(slot)` | EMPTY → SET → EMPTY; keyed by **app index, without generation**; unlocked check-then-set | `cfe_time_api.c:628–640` (re-read); cleared by index `cfe_time_utils.c:1026–1034` |
| `loader.sym(name, scope)` | ABSENT → DEFINED; `scope ∈ {GLOBAL, MODULE(m)}` | provider `OS_ModuleLoad` with constant-evaluated flags: apps LOCAL, libraries GLOBAL (`cfe_es_apps.c:470–491`) |

### 1.4 Operations, effects, linearization points, capture points and model levels

An **operation** `op ∈ Φ.ops` has a binding (symbol or AST pattern, §3.17), parameters with roles (name, id, out-id, AppId, stamp, buffer, msg), an outcome map from result codes to `{ready, not_ready(q), error}` (each not-ready code with its **documented result**: the side effects that a supplier-side reference documents for it, such as a counter, an event or a drop, and the `prov` of that documentation, `side = supplier`, §1.6; e.g. `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg` without a route: `NoSubscribersCounter++`, a `SEND_NO_SUBS` event and `CFE_SUCCESS`, `cfe_sb_priv.c:1082–1087`, cSB4305.5), and a list of **effects**:

- `lifecycle(r, a ∈ Σ_k) when guard`;
- `use(r)`;
- `sync(obj, role)`, `role ∈ {acq, rel, give, take, flush, put, get, create, start, exit, join, observe, timeout}`;
- `env(input)`;
- `mark(handler_begin | handler_end)`.

**Every conjunct of a readiness precondition has its own LP** [D] (fixes [X1-M1]). For a lifecycle effect, the LP is the instruction-level event inside the API at which the effect takes place. For a use, each conjunct `P_j` of the readiness precondition `Req` declares the point at which the program reads the state that `P_j` is about:

- **registry conjuncts** (the state lives in a framework table): the LP is the read inside the API, e.g. EVS's slot check;
- **value conjuncts** (the conjunct is about a value the caller passes, role `stamp`, `key` or `id`): the LP is the **capture point**, the load from which the argument's value derives. `kz-static extract` (AST def-use, S3a) emits `derives(arg, loc, site)` when the argument is a copy chain (loads, stores, parameter passing, casts without arithmetic) from a single shared location `loc` read at `site`. The conjunct is then evaluated at `site` over the support location `loc`, i.e. over `cell(loc)`. This is the **translation rule T-DERIVE**: it is generic and carries no knowledge of SB. If no unique `derives` fact exists, the capture point is the call itself, and every certificate that would need it is withheld (`unknown(writer_incomplete)`).

Examples [F]:

| API | Effect / conjunct | LP |
|---|---|---|
| `OS_*Create` | `lifecycle(osal.obj, name_visible)` | `OS_OBJECT_INIT` writes `name_entry` (`os-shared-idmap.h:106–111`), type lock released |
| | `lifecycle(osal.obj, publish)` | final ID write under the type lock, `osapi-idmap.c:1092` |
| `OS_*GetIdByName` | `use(osal.obj(name))` | the name search under the type lock |
| `CFE_ES_GetAppID` | `use(es.self(exec))`; out-pointer store | `CFE_ES_GetAppRecordByContext()` under the ES lock (`cfe_es_api.c:748–763`, re-read) |
| `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg` | `use(sb.route(hdr.MsgId, ·))` | **route_lookup** under the SB lock (`cfe_sb_priv.c:1007–1104`); then one **put** LP per destination outside the lock (`:1177–1216`) (§3.9) |
| `CFE_EVS_Register` | `lifecycle(evs.reg(slot(owner)), clear)` then `REG` | memset `cfe_evs.c:56`; store `:106` |
| `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` | `valid(arg.AppID)` (value conjunct) | capture point from `derives` (e.g. the load of `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` at argument evaluation in SB code, `cfe_sb_api.c:226–228`) |
| | `evs.reg(arg.AppID) ∈ {REG}` (registry conjunct) | the slot check: the read of `AppDataPtr->AppID` in the match test `EVS_AppDataIsMatch` at `cfe_evs.c:190` (`cfe_evs_utils.h:164–167`, re-read); the call `EVS_GetAppDataByID` at `:185` only computes the slot's address |
| `CFE_ES_WaitForSystemState` | raise (`lifecycle(es.appstate(owner))`) then `observe(SystemState)` | the store under the ES lock at `cfe_es_api.c:573`, only if `GetAppRecordByContext()` ≠ NULL; then each unlocked read of `SystemState` (`:585`) |

**Readiness is evaluated per conjunct at its LP.** The program's own outcome code at the use is a consistency check on the computed state (O4, §2.5). Value conjuncts map to their own outcome codes (for K-ID: `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` ⟺ the value conjunct failed, `cfe_evs.c:185–189`; `NOT_REGISTERED` ⟺ the registry conjunct failed, `cfe_evs.c:190–194`).

**Model levels** [D] (A's M_api/M_impl, [A §1.5]; containment per level fixes [X1-C2]):

- **M_api**: each OSAL API is one atomic transition at its main LP; create is atomic w.r.t. lookup; lookups return found or not found. `Pref(B,σ,M_api)` is the set of finite executions of a **hypothetical OSAL backend that conforms atomically to the OSAL API contract**. M_api verdicts are therefore **claims relative to the API contract** (portability claims), not claims about the POSIX binary.
- **M_impl**: for the OSAL idmap, task, binsem and queue paths, the skeleton builder projects the OSAL shared and POSIX source onto a lower vocabulary Φ_L-1 (pthread mutex and condvar incl. `pthread_cond_timedwait`, `sem_*`, `mq_*`, `clock_nanosleep`, `pthread_create/cancel/join`). The API is split at its LPs, the RESERVED windows appear, the bounded RESERVED wait is modelled with **both** wake causes (§3.2), and the lock-leak path exists ([R01 §1.5–1.8, §2.2]).
- OSAL-internal locks constrain interleavings only under M_impl. They never create shape-HB edges at either level ([R01 §3, §6.2 item 2]).
- **M-relevance.** An operation is *M_impl-relevant* if M_impl models it differently from M_api: idmap create, lookup, delete and token conversion; task create, prepare and delete; binsem; queue create, put and get. A cone (§4.10) or certificate is M_impl-relevant if it contains such an operation.
- **When the levels disagree**, the report says so:
  - protected under M_impl, violable under M_api → **portability hazard**;
  - violable only under M_impl → **implementation-defect candidate** of OSAL POSIX at the pin;
  - an M_api-only certificate contradicted by an observation of the real binary (O4(b)) → **portability-hazard evidence** (the binary is outside the API-contract model there), never a model fault.

### 1.5 Events, templates, traces and concretization

An event is `e = ⟨id, actor, seq, op, phase, args, outcome, keys, site, ι(e), lp_intervals, t⟩`:
- `seq` is the per-actor index (the program-order token);
- `phase ∈ {call, return, point}`;
- `outcome` is the result the program itself received ([R06 F11]);
- `ι(e)` is the identity quadruple;
- `lp_intervals` brackets the LP of each conjunct between two observed points (§4.8);
- `t` is a timestamp kept **as evidence only**: physical time gives no HB (Lamport, CACM 1978 [V-abs]).

A **trace** π is a sequence of events in which each actor's events appear in program order.

A **static template** has the same type with symbolic fields and a path condition. Templates are **context-qualified**: `(static actor, inlining call string, site)` [D]. The SB event site inside `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` reached from `CFE_ES_TaskInit` is therefore a different template from the same site reached from ES's command loop. Recursion makes the affected summary `unknown`. Inlining full call strings is worst-case exponential in call depth; the fallback is stated in §4.3 (fixes [X1-m7]).

A template `t` *concretizes* to an event `e` (`t ⊒ e`) iff the actor and call string of `e` match `t`, `site(e) = site(t)`, and the fields of `e` satisfy `t`'s constraints. This single type lets the static, trace and model layers share one checker.

### 1.6 Readiness specifications

A **readiness specification** (decision T1 = (a); naming and source tiers below, Appendix C) is `κ = ⟨id, U, sel, Req, Oracle, Allowed, appreq, harm, prov, authored, guard, scope⟩`:

- `U`: a use pattern (operation plus site predicate, in the pattern language of §2.3).
- `sel`: a resource selector from event fields to a key (e.g. `osal.countsem(arg.name)`, `evs.reg(arg.AppID)`).
- `Req`, the **readiness precondition**: a conjunction `P_1 ∧ … ∧ P_n` of required lifecycle or value predicates, each written in the core expression language (§2.3) and each with its own LP (§1.4).
- `Oracle`: the **framework validity predicate** for each conjunct. Examples: `CFE_ES_AppRecordIsMatch(CFE_ES_LocateAppRecordByID(a), a)`; `EVS_GetAppDataByID(a) != NULL && EVS_AppDataIsMatch(...)` ([A §7.2]). It is never executed by Kairos. Statically its read set defines the **support locations** of the conjunct (§4.2). Dynamically the program evaluates it, and the outcome code is the observed image of the conjunct (O4) ([J1 X9]).
- `Allowed`: the not-ready outcomes that the readiness specification accepts, from a **finite vocabulary**, each with a **continuation horizon taken from a reference** [D] ([J1 X2]); which entries decide statuses (the caller-level Allowed) is stated under "Caller-level Allowed" below (EXT-20261012 §6.4):

| Allowed entry | Meaning | Horizon (reference) | Static check | Trace check | Explorer check |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `none` | no not-ready outcome is acceptable | the use itself | — | — | — |
| `documented_drop(src)` | the using code drops its own request by design, documented by a **caller-side** text in the module that owns the use: EVS's own-ID guard, whose comment belongs to EVS, the using code (`cfe_evs_utils.c:613–623`; `caller`); **SB's per-task event recursion guard** (`cfe_sb_priv.c:305–328, 773–790`; "This prevents recursive events from occurring", `cfe_sb_priv.h:550–558`; `caller`), whose user is SB's event code. A drop that no caller-side text documents is no `documented_drop` entry, and no Kairos rule accepts it: SB's drop of an event whose caller has no ES task record (`cfe_sb_priv.c:315–318`) has no entry (§3.9). A supplier's documented drop of a caller's request (SB's no-subscriber drop, cSB4305.5) is framework-level information in the op's outcome map (§1.4), not a `documented_drop` entry of the caller's readiness specification | the use itself | outcome class | outcome code | outcome |
| `returned_and_tested(codes)` | the use returns a documented code; the caller branches on it and reaches its **normal continuation**: the node named in the entry with its citation (strict alternative Q15, decided 2026-10-15: an entry that cites none is `unspecified_by_reference`; the nearest post-dominator of the test node is then only extracted as a fact, see "Horizon monitors"). An **error exit** (`CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE`, `CFE_PSP_Restart`, a terminal op) or a return of a non-success status before it is not one, and a REJECT at the normal continuation wins, unless a reference documents that the API propagates the status to its own caller (e.g. `cfe_evs.h:98`); then test nodes inside the API are ignored and the caller's handling is judged by the same rule (§1.6 horizon monitors) | until the normal continuation (ACCEPT), or until a REJECT event: an error exit, an error return without documented propagation, the next operation of the same actor on the same resource, or actor exit | path check within the horizon: every `Sk^cert` path from the not-ready branch reaches the normal continuation without a REJECT event (ACCEPT), or none does (REJECT) | continuation events up to the normal continuation or a REJECT event; trace ends early → `unknown(horizon)` | suffix |
| `retry_until_ready(codes, bound)` | the caller retries on the not-ready codes | the loop's bound, constant-evaluated in the build (e.g. CF's effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`) | loop unrolled exactly (§4.3) | later iteration observed ready within the bound | suffix within the bound |
| `fallback(op′)` | the caller uses a documented alternative | the next operation of the actor | path check | next event | suffix |
| `defer_by_design(src)` | the reference documents the deferral (TO_LAB subscribes after `CFE_ES_WaitForStartupSync`, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`) | the documented deferral point, executed by the deferring actor, which may not exist yet when the use is decided | path check from the deferring actor's control node, or, if it does not exist yet, from its nearest existing spawn-chain ancestor through the spawn edges; no REJECT while a path to a process-terminal step exists, and none by or after such a step | event | suffix |
| `exit(code, src)` | a documented exit | actor exit | path check | event | suffix |
| `unspecified_by_reference` | no reference says whether the not-ready outcome is acceptable | — | — | — | — |

  `unspecified_by_reference` sets harm `unspecified_by_reference`. For route and capacity specifications with no must-deliver `appreq` it also sets the outcome label **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER**. Neither erases the observed effect, which the verdict records as its `consequence` (§1.10).

  **Horizon monitors** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2). Each Allowed entry other than `none` and `unspecified_by_reference` is read as a monitor μ over a finite execution ρ. The entry names its **monitored actor**, its ACCEPT event and its REJECT event:
  - `documented_drop(src)`: the using actor; ACCEPT at decision if the outcome is the documented drop; REJECT at decision otherwise.
  - `returned_and_tested(codes)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when, after a not-ready outcome in `codes`, it passes a **test node** (a branch on the returned status) and then reaches the entry's **normal continuation**; REJECT if the outcome is not in `codes`, or if, before ACCEPT, the actor reaches an **error exit**, returns a non-success status from the function that contains the test, performs its next operation on the same resource, or exits; a REJECT event at the normal continuation wins. The normal continuation is **the node named in the entry with its citation**, taken from a reference (`prov` of kind `api_doc`, `requirement`, `code_admission` or `issue`, side `caller`). **An entry that names no cited continuation is `unspecified_by_reference`** (strict alternative Q15, decided 2026-10-15): it has no monitor, its not-ready occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED, and neither an ACCEPT nor a REJECT is derived. What the code shows is still extracted and reported as facts, apart from any judgement. For a test node n in function f, the **candidate continuation** `cont_candidate(n)` is the nearest post-dominator c of n in f's certificate-view control-flow graph made acyclic per loop iteration (loops unrolled exactly are taken unrolled; in every other loop of f that contains n, the back edge is redirected to the loop's exit) such that c is neither f's return nor f's exit, and neither the returned status nor any variable written on the not-ready branch between n and c is live at c (f's return value counts as a use), or `none` if there is no such c. Beside it the occurrence records the code facts *error exit reached*, *non-success status returned*, *next operation on the same resource* and *actor exit* on the paths from n. These facts belong to the occurrence's `consequence` (§1.10). Reading `cont_candidate(n)` as an allowed recovery is a judgement, and it needs a reference; without one it is not made.  An error exit is a call of `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_PSP_Restart`, or a terminal op (§4.3). Where a reference (`prov` of kind `api_doc` or `code_admission`) documents that an API returns the not-ready status to its own caller, the entry may say so (**documented propagation**; e.g. `cfe_evs.h:98` for `CFE_EVS_Register`); test nodes and joins inside the API are then ignored, its return of the status is not a REJECT, and the obligation starts at the API's return in its caller, whose handling of the returned status is judged by the same rules. A propagation read only from code gives no such entry.
  - `retry_until_ready(codes, bound)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when a later attempt of the same loop instance is READY (a `NOT_EVALUATED` attempt is not); REJECT when the loop takes its exhaustion path, or an attempt's outcome is outside `codes`.
  - `fallback(op′)`: the using actor; ACCEPT if its next operation is `op′`; REJECT if it is another one, or the actor exits first other than by a process-terminal step.
  - `defer_by_design(src)`: the actor that executes the documented deferral point (for TO_LAB, its main task, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`), which may not exist yet when the occurrence is decided; ACCEPT when it executes the deferral point; REJECT if it terminates first, other than by a process-terminal step.
  - `exit(code, src)`: the using actor; ACCEPT when it reaches the documented exit with `code`; REJECT if it exits otherwise, other than by a process-terminal step.

  μ also gives REJECT as soon as no `Sk^cert` path from the monitored actor's control node reaches the ACCEPT event and none reaches a process-terminal step (the path check); for an actor that does not exist yet, the path starts at its nearest existing spawn-chain ancestor and runs through the spawn edges. μ makes no ACCEPT and no REJECT on or after a process-terminal step (§4.3), so an occurrence that is PENDING when the process ends stays PENDING; real runs have no events after such a step. **Teardown decides no default-scope occurrence** [D] (the scope is "before the shutdown request"): for an occurrence of the default scope, μ makes no ACCEPT and no REJECT at or after the TEARDOWN LP (`OS_DeleteAllObjects`, §3.5), whose deletions end the monitored actor without any event of its own, so such an occurrence that is PENDING when the teardown starts stays PENDING (a run then gives `unknown(horizon)`), as the states after the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP are excluded from its progress (§1.8). Events of the monitored actor between the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP and the TEARDOWN LP still decide it. ACCEPT and REJECT are final in every extension of ρ; otherwise μ is OPEN. The static, trace and explorer checks of the table compute μ on `Sk^cert` paths, on traces and on explorer prefixes respectively. **OPEN never counts as ACCEPT.** A horizon that never ends is a progress matter (§1.8). A trace that ends while μ is OPEN gives `unknown(horizon)`.

  **Three judgements** [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4; QT-3 approved 2026-10-15). A not-ready use is judged three times, and the record keeps the three apart. A framework that documents a returned result as allowed does not thereby satisfy the caller's requirement (QT-3), and the SB rule for a send with no subscriber (cSB4305.5, a result the sender cannot see) is a different statement about a different API from R2's `ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, the EVS API's return for an invalid AppId (`cfe_evs.h:169–172`); neither is evidence about the other (QT-2):
  - the **framework-level judgement** (API behaviour): what the supplier returns and does when the resource is not ready. It is stated by the op's outcome map with its documented results (§1.4), checked by O4(a), `kz conform` (§4.11), the §3.9 counter oracles and the trace's event attempts, and recorded as `fw_doc` and `fw_conf` (§1.10). It never makes an occurrence TOLERATED;
  - the **caller-level judgement** (application or mission requirement): what the using code must achieve and which not-ready outcomes it accepts. It is stated by `appreq` and the Allowed entries read below, and it alone decides the statuses below, the classes of §1.10, the outcome label and `harm`;
  - the **observed consequence**: what happened (failure, loss, exit), recorded as `consequence` with its observed counts whatever the other two judgements say (§1.10).

  **Side of a provenance record.** Each `prov` record carries `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}`, judged by the author for the pair (record, κ) and recorded. `supplier`: the record says what the supplier returns or does when the resource is not ready, or whether the framework counts that result as an error (`\retval`/`@retval` lines; cEVS3103.1 and cSB4305.3–.5 for uses of applications that call EVS or SB; a documented propagation such as `cfe_evs.h:98`; a barrier's documented timeout; a `code_pattern` record over the supplier's own code, such as K-SELF.es's source in §2.3). `caller`: a text that says what the using code — the caller, or for route and capacity specifications the consumer, or the application or mission on whose behalf it runs — must achieve, or how it handles the not-ready outcome and that this handling is acceptable (`cfe_evs.h:169–172`; CF's admission `cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`; TO_LAB's deferral `to_lab_app.c:64–68`; a guard comment in the module that owns the use; an application's or a mission's requirement). `rule`: no text; the record names a fixed rule of Kairos and the code it was applied to, and states no acceptance (the extraction of `cont_candidate` for `returned_and_tested`; the loop recognition of §7.1 source 4). A `rule` record never decides a status ("Caller-level Allowed"). A drop in the using code that no comment documents has no `rule` record: no fixed rule of Kairos reads a drop as accepted. The side belongs to the pair (record, κ): cSB4305.5 is `supplier` for K-ROUTE, whose user is the transmitting application, and `caller` for the K-ID instance at SB's `SEND_NO_SUBS` event site, whose user is SB's own event code.

  **Caller-level Allowed** [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4; QT-4 strict alternative, decided 2026-10-15). An Allowed entry is read as a horizon monitor if and only if it has at least one `caller` record. An entry whose records are all `supplier`, all `rule`, or a mix of the two decides no status: at the caller level it is `unspecified_by_reference`, and its not-ready occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED. **A rule alone never makes an occurrence accepted**, and an entry without an original-source basis stays unjudged. This removes labels that the former default produced (rule-backed TOLERATED, and VIOLATING that rested on a rule's REJECT); it removes no observation. The `consequence` of every not-ready occurrence keeps the code facts (state changes, returned codes, error returns and exits, the extracted `cont_candidate`) and the observed counts (§1.10), and the framework-level results stay as `fw_doc` and `fw_conf`. Supplier-only entries are kept as framework-level information. `appreq` and the precedence between it and the entries are given in the `appreq` bullet that follows. **In the statuses below, in §1.10 and in the outcome label, "Allowed" means the caller-level Allowed.**

- `appreq` [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4): the reference that states what this use must achieve for the calling application or the mission — an obligation addressed to the caller (K-ID: `cfe_evs.h:169–172`), an application's own requirement, a consumer's must-deliver statement, or a mission requirement — or `unspecified_by_reference` (the default). It is never taken from a supplier-side text or from a rule (§1.6 "Three judgements"). The **caller-level Allowed** is derived in this order: (1) with no `caller` entry, it is `none` when `appreq` is specified and `unspecified_by_reference` otherwise, and `Allowed = none` requires a specified `appreq`; (2) `rule`-only and `supplier`-only entries are not part of the caller level (QT-4); (3) a `caller` entry that accepts an outcome a specified `appreq` excludes, or two `caller` entries that accept incompatible continuations, are recorded as a **conflict** with both references. **The conflict is stated and the judgement is withheld** (QT-5, decided 2026-10-15): the versions, targets and scopes of both documents are checked first, and, while they do not resolve the conflict, no precedence is invented. The conflicting entries give the caller-level Allowed `unspecified_by_reference`, so their occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named in the verdict, never TOLERATED, with the outcome label `not-ready-unjudged` (not LOSS-UNDER-ORDER where the conflict involves a must-deliver `appreq`); (4) otherwise the entries decide. Whether an entry and `appreq` conflict is an authoring judgement recorded with both references. A change of `appreq` or of a recorded conflict after F1 is an `oracle refinement` (§7.2).
- `harm`: the effect class when the outcome is not allowed. Vocabulary: `event_request_rejected` (the event service refuses the request before it builds any packet, and the caller discards the status), `status_ignored`, `wrong_identity_stamped`, `app_error_exit`, `app_zombie` (record used forever, never cleaned), `service_task_absent` (a core task exits while the system continues), `processor_reset_requested`, `process_abort` (on pc-linux a reset request aborts after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`, §3.5), `panic` (core-sync failure), `task_never_runs`, `hang_or_deadlock`, `terminal_pending(panic | abort)`, `message_lost`, `unspecified_by_reference`. Two values have two uses that the record keeps apart. `task_never_runs` and `hang_or_deadlock` can be the harm of a VIOLATING occurrence: an Allowed analysis assigns them where the caller-level Allowed is specified, that is, where an entry with a `caller` record or a specified `appreq` decides the occurrence. K-SELF.es at TIME's `CFE_ES_CreateChildTask` calls (`cfe_time_task.c:160, 173`; §3.3, App. A.2 item 4) is not such a case under the strict alternative: its `returned_and_tested` entry cites no continuation, so the occurrence is NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with harm `unspecified_by_reference`, and the code fact that the returned error goes back to the caller, after which the child task never runs [I], is recorded as its consequence (QT-4, Q15). A VIOLATING occurrence with such a harm needs a `caller` record or a specified `appreq`, as in litmus LP-ALLOW. K-SPAWN.osal and K-LOCKBAL, whose `none` rests on supplier-side texts only, are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with harm `unspecified_by_reference` and the effect recorded as their consequence. **Since the PI's decision of 2026-10-15 (QT-9) they belong to the implementation-defect family:** no researcher-authored `appreq` is added to turn them into readiness violations (violations of a caller requirement), and only the part that is linked to an order violation is reported, on its own basis. They can also be the harm of a **progress result** (§1.8): a dead actor on the way to the use, inside the use, or holding the use's horizon open. That is a progress result of the instance that waits. `terminal_pending(panic | abort)` is a progress harm only: the waiting actor is dead, but a process end that the stop input does not cause stays reachable from every state, so the process ends by a panic (`panic`) or by another abort, a restart request or `main`'s return (`abort`) rather than hanging; the two values keep §3.3's distinction between `panic` and `process_abort`.
- `prov`: provenance records `(kind ∈ {api_doc, requirement, code_admission, code_pattern, issue, researcher}, side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}, path:line or requirement ID, sha256 of the text)`. `requirement` is a cFS functional requirement (`cfe/docs/cFE_FunctionalRequirements.csv`, an application's `*_FunctionalRequirements.csv`) [D, decided 2026-10-15: QT-2 approved; §10.1]. `side` is defined under "Three judgements" above; it is recorded for the pair (record, κ).
- `authored`: authoring time, author, and which design cases (C1, C2, C3, C5, R2) the author had inspected for that API ([R06 §5.3 item 4]).
- `guard`: version guard (e.g. `cfe ≥ 6.6.0`) for profile reuse across revisions (§7.6).
- `scope` [D] (fixes [X1-M6], [X2-04]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.1): every readiness specification carries the implicit scope conjunct **"no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP of `es.shutdown` precedes the occurrence's decision event"**, decided when the occurrence's readiness is decided (§1.6 Instances); conjunct LPs after the decision do not matter. For a single-conjunct readiness specification this is "precedes the use's LP". Scope is part of the instance, `i = (κ, t, key, scope)`: occurrences outside the default scope belong to instances of the separate family **K-TEARDOWN** (§3.5), the complement of the default scope, classified by the same statuses and reported apart; their progress is `unknown(teardown_scope)` (§1.8). The exclusion of states after a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP applies to default-scope instances only.

**Naming and source tiers** [D] (decision T1 = (a), 2026-10-15 [D]; research note `Architecture/background/08_terminology.md`; the old-to-new mapping is Appendix C, ADR-052). The judged object κ is called a **readiness specification** (준비 상태 명세); in a paper its first use reads "provenance-tracked readiness specification". The bare word "specification" is not used for κ, because it is also the name of this document. `Req` is the **readiness precondition** of κ: a lifecycle or value requirement on the key-selected resource, which no party is said to have promised. An in-scope occurrence at which the readiness precondition does not hold and whose outcome the caller-level Allowed does not accept (`VIOLATING`) is a **readiness violation**, a subclass of order violation where a provider of the state exists; it is not called a "contract violation". An occurrence whose caller-level Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference` is `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` (or LOSS-UNDER-ORDER for route and capacity specifications) and is not called a violation. The word **contract** is kept for documented API semantics only: the OSAL API contract of the model level M_api (§1.4) and the source tier `contract-backed` below; it names no object that Kairos authors. The class refinement formerly named `tolerated` is `allowed-not-ready` (it avoids the clash with "tolerating concurrency bugs", `background/08_terminology.md` §6.5; in prose, "an allowed not-ready outcome", never "Kairos tolerates"); the occurrence status `TOLERATED` keeps its name in this revision (Appendix C).

The **source tier** of an instance (Korean 근거 등급) says what kind of source stands behind it. It is derived at report time from the `prov` records of κ and its caller-level Allowed, with no extra authoring: `kz report` reads them from the Φ entry that the verdict names, and no field is added to the records of §2.3. A mixed entry takes the weakest tier of its parts, in the order listed. The tiers [D] (taken from `background/08_terminology.md` §5.4 under T1; the definitions of `code-derived` and `unreferenced` adapt it to the QT-4 strict alternative [I]):
- `contract-backed`: the caller-level part rests on a documented API obligation on the caller (`api_doc`, side `caller`; that a text states an obligation is the author's judgement, recorded), whether or not the supplier also documents an error return. Example: R2's K-ID, `cfe_evs.h:169–172`;
- `requirement-derived`: some caller-level part rests on a cFS functional requirement (kind `requirement`, QT-2), and none on anything weaker;
- `admitted`: some part rests on a `code_admission` (e.g. CF's comment `cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`);
- `issue-derived`: some part rests on an `issue`;
- `code-derived`: the readiness specification was generated by a fixed rule from the program text (a `code_pattern` record of side `rule`: the loop recognition of §7.1 source 4, the extraction of `cont_candidate`) and no `caller` record or specified `appreq` speaks for its caller-level part. A `rule` record decides no status (QT-4 strict alternative), so the caller-level Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference` and the occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED;
- `researcher`: some caller-level part rests on a `researcher` record;
- `unreferenced`: no record of any kind speaks for the caller-level part, i.e. the caller-level Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference` and no `rule` record generated the readiness specification.

"Reference-derived" is reserved for the first four tiers; a `researcher` entry is not a reference, and a `code_pattern` entry is generated by a rule from the program under analysis. The order of the tiers follows `background/08_terminology.md` §5.4; whether it is the right order is open there (08 §7 Q4) and is not decided here. **Reporting rule:** census counts, verdicts and, in the extended evaluation, recall are reported per source tier; every bad occurrence with a specified caller-level Allowed is reported as a readiness violation with its tier; "contract violation" is not used.

**Instances.** An instance is `i = (κ, t, key, scope)` for a context-qualified template `t` matching `U` and `scope ∈ {default, teardown}`. Statuses are defined on **finite executions** ρ (§1.8). Take an occurrence `u` of `t` in ρ, executed by actor instance `a`:
- `u` **begins at its call**, the execution by `a` of t's call node. A registry conjunct's LP is the read inside the call's extent. A value conjunct's LP is its capture point: the last execution by `a`, before the call, of a capture site that the `derives` fact links to the call's argument (or the call itself, §1.4); the capture is evaluated when it executes and is attached to the call it feeds. A capture that no call follows begins nothing.
- `u` is **decided** at the earliest of: its call, if an attached capture is false there; the first later conjunct LP at which the conjunct is false; the last conjunct LP, when every conjunct holds; the return of the call. If the return of its call is the earliest of these, i.e. no attached capture is false at the call, no conjunct LP that the call passed has its conjunct false, and some conjunct LP was not passed (e.g. on an argument check before a name search), `u`'s readiness is not evaluated: its status is `NOT_EVALUATED`. An occurrence already decided by a false capture or a false conjunct keeps that decision, whatever conjunct LPs its call skips afterwards. Its **decision event** is the call, the deciding conjunct LP, or, when the call returns first, the last conjunct LP it passed (or the call). Between its call and its decision, `u` is **open**. Every occurrence is decided at the latest by the return of its call, so an actor has at most one open occurrence of `t`.
- Its **ordinal** is `(a, k)`: it is the k-th occurrence of `t` with key `key` begun by `a` in ρ, where `a` is named across executions by `(τ, generation, n)`, n counting the executions of τ's spawn sites by its spawner (§1.2).
- It **belongs to** the instance `(κ, t, key, default)` iff no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes its decision event in ρ, and otherwise to the K-TEARDOWN instance `(κ, t, key, teardown)`; "in scope for i" means "belongs to i". Conjunct LPs after the decision do not affect scope. Both instances have the statuses below; they are classified and reported apart.
- An occurrence whose decision event follows a process-terminal step (§4.3) has no status and belongs to no instance; real runs have no events after such a step.
- For a conjunct `P_j`, `state_ρ(r, P_j, u)` is the state of `A_k` after the lifecycle effects on `r` (same generation) whose LPs precede the LP of `P_j` at `u` in ρ.

The **status** of an occurrence that belongs to i and is decided in ρ is:
- `NOT_EVALUATED` iff the return of its call decides it, i.e. its call returned before one of its conjunct LPs with no capture and no passed conjunct false (above): it is neither ready nor not ready, is no witness, is never an ACCEPT of `retry_until_ready`, and is counted by no statement of §1.10;
- otherwise `READY` iff every conjunct holds at its LP;
- otherwise one of the following, by the horizon monitor μ of `Allowed` (above):
  - `TOLERATED` iff μ = ACCEPT in ρ;
  - `VIOLATING` iff μ = REJECT in ρ, or `Allowed = none`;
