  - `PENDING` iff μ = OPEN in ρ;
  - `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` iff `Allowed = unspecified_by_reference`.

`READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` and `NOT_EVALUATED` are final: they hold in every extension of ρ (L-STATUS, §6.2). `PENDING` may become `TOLERATED` or `VIOLATING` in an extension, or stay `PENDING` in every extension. **A `PENDING` occurrence is never counted as `TOLERATED`.**

A finite execution is **bad** for `i` iff it contains a `VIOLATING` in-scope occurrence, or, where Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference`, a `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` one. Every extension of a bad finite execution is bad. A finite execution is a **good witness** for `i` iff it contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence. Witnesses record the ordinal of that occurrence. The outcome label says which meaning was used. A permanent wait, before the use, inside it or inside a horizon, is never a readiness violation: it is a **progress** result (§1.8).

**Readiness-violation chains** ([v0.1] §8; [R06 §3]): `i₁ ⇝ i₂` when an occurrence of `u₂` exists only because of `u₁`'s not-ready outcome (control or data dependence; for traces, frame containment plus Φ `emits` annotations). A chain records **every** branch of the consequence, not one (fixes [X2-02]). Example: 17 of R2's 24 identity uses are `SEND_NO_SUBS` events in ES's context ([F], [R2g §2]); they come from ES's EVS packets, MsgId 0x808, transmitted before any route ([I], [R2g §2]).

**Progress chains** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Readiness-violation chains link occurrences, and a stall state has no occurrence of the waiting use. A second kind of chain therefore links progress results: `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` iff some stall state of `i₂` (§1.8) has a wait-for root that (P1) waits inside `u₁`'s open occurrence or holds open the horizon of a PENDING occurrence of `u₁`; or (P2) can be satisfied only by events that the certificate view dominates by the READY outcome of `u₁`'s call, while the finite execution contains an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready on that path (e.g. a K-SELF.es raise skipped inside the core App-record window); or (P3) waits on a lock leaked by, or an actor lost on, the not-ready branch of an occurrence of `u₁` (K-LOCKBAL, K-SPAWN). The conditions are decidable on the stall state, its finite execution and the certificate view. The root instance is reported and the dependent progress results are attached to it, as for readiness-violation chains (App. A.2 item 4). A stall whose wait-for root satisfies none of (P1)–(P3) (a cycle of predicate waits, a provider that simply never gives) has no chain root; it is grouped by its wait-for root set only (§1.8), and an unchained instance with timeout-masked-liveness carries `tml-root` itself. Progress chains are stored per cell (`kz_verdict_cell.progress_chain`), apart from readiness-violation chains.

### 1.7 The analysis basis B

`B = ⟨Build, Config, Init, Env, Input⟩`. Every item is recorded with its value, its source and its policy status (fixes [X2-08], [X3-M3]):

- **Build**: commits; gcc 13.3.0 and the recorded flags ([R06 K1]); `link.txt` archive semantics ([R07 §3.4]); generated files by hash, including `cfe_static_module_list.c` (core order, [R02 §1.3]) and the generated module version table. Whether R2's per-boot count depends on that table is a configuration question that Kairos's own recorder must check on a basis without it [I]; 13 of the 24 occurrences seen under gdb come from the version-event callback that iterates over the configured modules ([M], [R2g §6]); that this iteration follows the table's content is code reading [I], and no gdb record covers a basis without the table.
- **Config**:
  - the **startup-script selection rule**, evaluated by `kz-static basis`: on a PROCESSOR reset ES first tries the volatile `/ram/cfe_es_startup.scr` (`CFE_PLATFORM_ES_VOLATILE_STARTUP_FILE`) and falls back to the non-volatile script (`cfe_es_apps.c:130–168`, re-read); on POWERON it uses the generated script (2 libraries + 15 apps, [R03 §6.1]). The path actually opened is recorded by the closure boot (O5);
  - built tables (`*.tbl`) cross-checked byte for byte against their source initializers through the gcc DWARF layout ([A §4.1 step 4]); platform constants constant-evaluated in their TU; `osconfig.h` limits; the PSP module list.
- **Init** (the persistent state before the run): boot record, CDS and reset-area SysV segments, user-reserved memory, `/dev/shm/osal:RAM`, `EEPROM.DAT` ([R01 §5.2–5.3]); **decoded CDS contents and critical-table images**, since critical tables are restored from CDS on PROCESSOR resets (`cfe_tbl_api.c:117–131`, re-read). A snapshot S0 is taken per run. The **Init hash is content-only** (no mtimes; `EEPROM.DAT`'s mtime changes every boot, [BM]).
- **Env**: kernel, recorded per run, because it drifts between VM sessions: `6.18.44-fc-v80` for the gdb reference runs (`env/logs/r2_g1_gdb_operational_esrestart.log.meta` L12), `-fc-v114` in [R07 §1] and in the S1-R2 smoke run (`env/logs/s1r2_p1_perf_operational_esrestart.log.meta` L13), and `6.18.44-fc-v139` read by `uname -r` on 2026-10-16 [M, here]; the reference group A is re-measured on the kernel in use before the S1 observation-effect comparison, and results from another kernel are not added to it (D11, §8.6); 4 vCPU; scheduling policy as realized (`SCHED_OTHER` for all 27 threads, [M] [BM §0]); affinity (the PSP pins `CFE_*` tasks to CPU 0, `cfe_psp_start.c:184–189`, not yet measured, [R06 K5]); **euid** (decides queue truncation, §1.3); **CWD** (fixes the `/cf` mapping `cfe_psp_start.c:378`, the SysV key files and `EEPROM.DAT`); effective queue depth (`msg_max` 10); platform capabilities relevant to providers (`/proc/schedstat` absent, [R01 §2.6]); **stratum** cold (first run after a VM reboot) or warm ([BM]: 2570 ms vs 1613–1633 ms to OPERATIONAL; 849 ms vs 2.2–3.3 ms load span).
- **Input**: external commands with **event-based** triggers; the **reset-type input** (`-R` on argv, else the boot record's `NextResetType`, `cfe_psp_start.c:411–434`, re-read); the stop method (PI decision D17).

**INIT** [D] (fixes [X1-m1], [X2-12]). INIT is the **program-entry state**: the static-initializer image of the closed program for process-scope locations, and the decoded S0 for persistent-scope locations. EarlyInit stores (e.g. `memset(&CFE_ES_Global, 0, …)` at `cfe_es_start.c:82`; `memset(&CFE_SB_Global, 0, …)` at `cfe_sb_init.c:50`, re-read) are ordinary W/D writer entries, ordered against falsifiers and uses by MB, once-PWB and the frontier rule like any other writer.

**Run sequences and generations.** For cross-run specifications (K-PERSIST), the end state of run *n* becomes `B_{n+1}.Init`; the reset type of run *n+1* depends on how run *n* ended ([M] PO → PR → PO after signal stops, [R01 §5.2]). Within one run, restarts and reloads create generations (`B_restart`), and K-PERSIST is also checked across generations (CDS `ALREADY_EXISTS` within one run, `cfe_es_cds.c:342–348`).

**Normalization** (§4.13): a run that did not end through `CFE_PSP_Restart(POWERON)` leaves the SysV segments and a boot record with `NextResetType = PROCESSOR` ([X3-M3]). Before any further pooled run, one unobserved normalization boot is made; Init is then checked against the canonical S0.

Every verdict carries the hash of B. **A configuration variant is a new basis B′, never a "new bug"** ([R06 §5.4]).

### 1.8 Scheduling and timing semantics σ

**Transition systems and executions** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2; fixes [X1-C1]). For σ ∈ {σ_U, σ_U∞} and level M, `TS(B,σ,M) = (Q, q₀, T, en_σ, δ)`. Its states and transitions are those of §4.10 item 2 (effects at LP granularity and explicit local choices), built over the **certificate view** with no horizon. `en_σ(q)` is the set of transitions σ enables in state q; σ_U and σ_U∞ share Q, q₀, T and δ. A Φ delay op is a transition of its actor that changes only its control state (σ_U's yield). The ES kill timer is abstract: at each scan of a used external app with `AppState > RUNNING` (`cfe_es_apps.c:1114`), `ES_BG_TASK` either finds the timer not yet expired (`not-yet-expired`) or expires it (`:1126–1136`), and the expiry is one of two outcome-labelled transitions, `expire-stopped` when the `AppState` read in the same scan is STOPPED (stored by the app's `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `cfe_es_api.c:399`) and `expire-waiting` when it is WAITING (a commanded app that has not exited, `:1139–1146`); a timer of 0, which an app has from creation (`:819`) unless a command set it (`:1139–1146`), always expires at the scan. σ_R1[P_sched] is a **separate system** `TS(B,σ_R1,M)` whose states add a discrete clock and a ready/blocked status per actor: a delay op's transition is the actor's block step, which moves its control as σ_U's delay transition does; clock-advance and wake steps change only the added components; the clock advances to the earliest pending wake only when no actor is ready; timeouts are enabled as in σ_U. The projection π erases clock-advance and wake steps and forgets the added components (L-CONT). Analyses evaluate statuses and stall predicates on the product of `TS` with a deterministic, non-blocking **instance monitor** that records, per actor, the open occurrence, the last captured value per capture site, the PENDING occurrences with their horizon-monitor states and the occurrence ordinal per key, and whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP has occurred (L-MON, §6.2). The explorer's bounded models use the exploration view, and their results carry h in b.
- **Finite executions** `Pref(B,σ,M)`: the sequences `t₁…t_n` with `t_k ∈ en_σ(q_{k−1})` and `q_k = δ(q_{k−1}, t_k)`. The set is prefix-closed. `Pref_b` is the same set for a bounded model. **Reachable states** `Reach_σ(q)` are the states that finite executions reach from q.
- **Maximal executions** `Max(B,σ,M)`: finite executions that end in a state q with `en_σ(q) = ∅`, and infinite executions that satisfy σ's fairness condition. σ_U and σ_R1 have no fairness condition; σ_U∞ is strongly fair. In this document `Exec(B,σ,M)` denotes `Max(B,σ,M)`; for σ_U, statements over every execution in which a node occurs (MustBefore) read the same over Pref and over Max.
- **Real runs** (σ_E): finite LP-granular event sequences of the gcc binary under B. A run ends by the stop method (D17), by `abort()` (`cfe_psp_support.c:107, :120`), by the return of `main` after the teardown, by a harness kill or by a watchdog. A run is a finite execution; it need not be maximal, and no claim depends on whether it is.
- **Time-based transitions** `TB ⊆ T`:
  - the timeout transition of every timed wait that is not a **periodic wait**, i.e. every await with a timeout: soft barriers, polls including L1 summaries, and the OSAL RESERVED wait's timed wake. A periodic wait is a timed wait inside a star-closed loop whose timeout and success outcomes both continue the loop (e.g. `ES_BG_TASK`'s wait, `cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:185`); its timeout, like an OSAL timer expiry, paces periodic work and is not in TB;
  - the failing outcome of the **last attempt** of every delay-paced retry, taken at the attempt's deciding transition at level M: the failing outcome of the attempt itself when it is one transition at M (an L1 summary; a Φ op that M models as one transition, such as CF's lookup at M_api); otherwise the outcome-labelled branch that takes the attempt's stale failing result into the exhaustion path (for CF at M_impl, `cf_cfdp.c:1285` taken with `NAME_NOT_FOUND` in the last iteration). The attempt's own transitions are not in TB, so removing TB never leaves an actor holding a lock;
  - every transition that Φ declares time-based. Φ must declare every countdown or clock comparison whose expiry abandons a wait or acts on another actor, with its paired "not yet expired" outcome. Declared here: the **forced kill**, i.e. the outcome `expire-waiting` of the ES kill timer (`AppTimerMsec`, §3.8, §3.14), its expiry for an app whose main task has not reached its STOPPED store (`cfe_es_api.c:399`). The outcome `expire-stopped`, including the expiry after a voluntary `CFE_ES_ExitApp` (timer 0), only paces the app's cleanup and is not in TB, and `not-yet-expired` is never in TB. Not yet declared: HS's application monitor, whose countdown, decremented once per HS wakeup cycle, calls `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_ES_RestartApp` on a monitored app at zero (`hs_monitors.c:107–160`; inactive with the default `NOACT` tables, §4.3), and TIME's flywheel entry after `CFE_PLATFORM_TIME_CFG_START_FLY` seconds without a tone (`cfe_time_tone.c:1172–1186`). Until Φ declares them, σ_U∞ keeps them, and a σ_U∞ result of an instance in a basis with an active HS application-monitor entry, or of an instance whose readiness specification reads TIME's clock state, carries the sub-label `tb-undeclared` (§1.10).

  A TB transition of an actor is **paired** if the actor has, at the same control node, a non-TB transition whose enabling depends on shared state (the success outcome of a timed wait or of a one-transition last attempt); otherwise it is **unpaired**.
- **What each notion uses.**
  - Safety (statuses, bad finite executions, the safety statements and classes of §1.10, witnesses) is defined on finite executions.
  - Progress (dead actors and stall states, below) is defined on reachable product states.
  - No verdict depends on Max or on a fairness assumption. Max is used only to state what is not claimed (L-CONT).

| σ | Definition | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **σ_U** (untimed) | Any interleaving of enabled transitions. `OS_TaskDelay` is a yield. Every **timed wait** (an await with a timeout: soft barriers, polls, the OSAL RESERVED wait's timed wake) may take its timeout transition at any point after it starts. A **count-bounded retry** takes its exhaustion branch **only after the reference number of attempts** (exact unrolling, §4.3). Polls read the current value. Priorities and affinity impose nothing. `CFE_PSP_Panic` (→ `abort()`, `cfe_psp_support.c:116–121`) is terminal. `CFE_PSP_Restart` is terminal **for its caller** (it never returns: `OS_TaskDelay(CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY)` then `abort()`, `cfe_psp_support.c:60–107`, re-read); the other actors continue (§3.5) | Reference semantics for certificates and model classification |
| **σ_U∞** (TB-free) | σ_U without time-based transitions: `en_σU∞(q) = en_σU(q) \ TB`. No non-periodic timed wait times out, and the ES kill timer never kills an app that has not exited (`expire-waiting` is removed); an app that has exited is still cleaned up (`expire-stopped` stays), so restarts, reloads, deletes and the K-LIFE window still occur. Give-ups that Φ has not declared time-based stay (sub-label `tb-undeclared`). Periodic waits and timer ticks stay. The last attempt of a delay-paced retry (a count-bounded loop whose iterations are separated by a Φ delay op, recognized by pattern, §4.3) has no failing outcome: when that attempt is one transition at level M it is enabled exactly when it would succeed; when it is several transitions at M (its test reads several values outside one critical section and outside one atomic read, so that L1 does not summarize it, §4.3; or it calls a Φ op that M splits at its LPs, such as an OSAL lookup at M_impl), its actor runs the attempt and is then **stranded** on the stale failing result, and deadness tests on it and on the actors it could enable are undecided (`unknown(unmodelled)`). `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M)` is therefore exactly the set of finite executions of σ_U that contain no time-based transition (L-CONT). An actor whose awaited predicate can never become true is **dead** (below); that is a progress result, not a class outcome. A poll that L1 summarizes (e.g. `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, whose test reads lie inside one ES-lock critical section) is one transition at M, so its actor is dead rather than stranded; with L1 switched off it is stranded. For Max only, scheduling is strongly fair (Musuvathi & Qadeer PLDI 2008 [V-abs, R05 §2.1 L111]); no verdict depends on it [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2; replaces the quiescence rule of [X1-M5]) | Separates "violable only through a time-based transition" from "violable in the ordering logic", and exposes permanent waits that time-based transitions mask |
| **σ_R1[P_sched]** (RT, one core) | Fixed-priority preemptive scheduling on one CPU, **parameterized by a cited target scheduling profile** `P_sched` (fixes [X2-07]): policy and timeslice; the priority-mapping function; the startup/root-thread priority; priority inheritance on mutexes; per OSAL backend. Delays **block** with a discrete-time wake order computed from constant-evaluated delay arguments (fixes [X1-m5]); computation between blocking points takes zero time (assumption A-ZT, recorded on every σ_R1 verdict that depends on a delay). Timeouts as in σ_U. Model-only by default | Platform classes where priorities order (C3 at 6.4.1, [LED §1a]) |
| **σ_E** (ENV) | The executions of the real binary under the recorded Env. Not modelled; sampled by observation and confirmation runs | Empirical witnesses and frequencies |

**The POSIX scheduling profile** `P_sched^posix` [F] (for any RT realization or σ_R1 claim about OSAL POSIX): when RT scheduling is permitted, OSAL selects `SCHED_RR` unless FIFO's priority spread exceeds `OS_MAX_TASK_PRIORITY` (255), which never holds on Linux (1..99) (`os-impl-tasks.c:348–365`, re-read); the root thread, which runs `CFE_ES_Main`, gets the highest priority (`:391–409`); OSAL priorities are compressed many-to-one by `OS_PriorityRemap` (`:78–106`); mutexes use `PTHREAD_PRIO_INHERIT` (`os-impl-mutex.c:82`). Profiles for RTEMS and VxWorks (6.4.x, H-V) are `unspecified_by_reference` until their backend sources are part of a basis (D13); no σ_R1 verdict is issued for them before that. [I] Under `P_sched^posix` the core App-record window cannot open on one CPU while the startup thread is runnable, because the startup thread outranks every task.

**Dead actors and stall states** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1; replaces the quiescence-based `stall` of [X1-M5]).
- **Terminated and dead.** In `TS(B,σ,M)`, an actor instance `a` is **terminated** in q if it is at its exit or after a terminal operation (T-TERM, §4.3). Deadness is evaluated per instance i on the reachable product graph `G_i` in which every state excluded for i (below) is a **sink**: paths stop at the first excluded state, and transitions out of excluded states do not count. `a` is **dead for i** in a non-excluded state q iff it is not terminated and no non-excluded state of `Reach_{G_i}(q)` enables a transition of `a`.
  - Deadness is a property of q, σ and i, not of a schedule. It needs no fairness, and an actor that is enabled but starved is never dead. An actor that only the teardown after a shutdown request, or only the model's continuation after a terminal step, could revive is dead.
  - Under σ_U a timed wait is never dead, because its timeout stays enabled. Under σ_U∞ a non-periodic timed wait is dead once its predicate can no longer become true.
  - Under σ_R1, deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation: `a` is dead iff no reachable σ_R1 state has a transition of `a` that σ_U enables in the projected state. Priority starvation never makes the starved actor itself dead; an actor waiting on a starved giver can be dead under unbounded σ_R1 (a higher-priority zero-time loop under A-ZT starves the giver forever).
  - Under σ_U∞, an actor every one of whose σ_U-enabled transitions is an unpaired time-based transition is **stranded** (the failing-result branch of a last attempt that is several transitions at M). A deadness test on a stranded actor, or on an actor that a stranded actor's effects could enable (as for censored actors, §4.10 item 8), is undecided (`unknown(unmodelled)`).
  - Deadness of an actor is the same in a TS state and in every product state over it (L-MON).
- **Ahead, per occurrence.** `u` is **ahead** of `a` in a product state if a path of `Sk^cert`, using intra-actor edges and spawn edges, leads from `a`'s control node to the call node of `u`'s template (if `a` has no open occurrence) or to a remaining conjunct LP of its open occurrence. Earlier occurrences are irrelevant. The local choices on that path are its **ahead support**.
- **Process-terminal steps and excluded states.** Φ marks a terminal declaration `process_terminal` iff its terminal step ends the process. On pc-linux these are: `abort`; `CFE_PSP_Panic` (`abort()` at `cfe_psp_support.c:120`); the restart requester's step after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` (`abort()` at `:107`); and the PSP main thread's return from `main` after the teardown (`bsp_start.c:247`). A reachable product state is **excluded** for i if its finite execution contains a process-terminal step, or, for an instance i whose scope is `default`, a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP (scope, §1.6). The model lets the other actors continue after a terminal step (T-TERM cuts only the caller); those continuations are sinks for deadness and the qualifiers, and no status or horizon verdict is decided in them (§1.6). Instances of K-TEARDOWN have progress `unknown(teardown_scope)`.
- **Stall states.** A non-excluded reachable product state is a **stall state for i** iff one of the following holds:
  - **pre-use stall**: some actor instance dead in it has no open occurrence of `u` and has `u` ahead;
  - **in-use stall**: some actor instance dead in it has an open in-scope occurrence of `u` (it is inside the call, before the decision);
  - **horizon stall**: it holds a `PENDING` in-scope occurrence of `u` whose monitored actor (§1.6) is dead, or does not exist yet while every existing actor that can still spawn it is dead.

  The dead actors named by the clauses are the stall's **witnessing actors**; the record keeps the set of clauses that hold.
- **Kinds and qualifiers**, recorded on every stall witness:
  - kind `deadlock-cycle`: the dead actors contain a cycle of untimed acquisitions of holder-released objects (mutexes, OSAL type locks), each held by the next actor of the cycle; kind `leaked-lock`: a witnessing actor waits, untimed, to acquire a holder-released, non-recursive object (OSAL type locks are `PTHREAD_MUTEX_NORMAL`, `os-impl-idmap.c:222`) whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and either is terminated or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release of the object that does not first acquire it again (K-LOCKBAL); otherwise kind `other`;
  - `terminal-pending`: on `G_i` without the transitions that inject B.Input's stop event (D17), a **process end**, i.e. a process-terminal step or a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP (which on pc-linux is always followed by the requester's `abort()` or `main`'s return, §3.5), stays reachable from every state reachable from the stall state; the process ends by a panic or an abort that the stop did not cause. The qualifier is computed on the closed cone, so it is relative to the cone (G11);
  - `idle`: every witnessing actor is blocked at an **idle wait**, the first blocking wait of an iteration of a star-closed message or periodic loop (e.g. `ReceiveBuffer`, `cfe_time_tone.c:1266`, `sch_lab_app.c:98`);
  - `choice-dependent`: the **choice support**, i.e. the over-approximating local choices (a star-closure iterate/exit, a havoc value, or a data branch the certificate view leaves unresolved) made by the actors of the stall's wait-for closure (the witnessing actors, the actors they wait on transitively and the holders of the objects they wait on) on the finite execution, together with the ahead support, is non-empty;
  - the **wait-for graph** has an edge from each dead actor to every actor that could satisfy its wait (the holder of an object it acquires; every actor whose `Sk^cert` region contains a give, put or send to an object it takes or receives on; every actor whose region writes the support of a predicate it awaits; the actor it joins; its spawner if it is not yet spawned), restricted to the part reachable from the witnessing actors; a **wait-for root** is a terminal strongly connected component of that graph (a lock cycle for `deadlock-cycle`, or a cycle of predicate and object waits), or a node without outgoing edges together with the wait it leaves unsatisfied; for `leaked-lock`, the leaked object with its holder. The record keeps the stall's **root set**;
  - a stall witness is **confirmable** iff its kind is `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` and it is neither idle nor terminal-pending; a **hang state** is a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending.
- **Harm of a stall**, in this order of precedence:
  - `terminal_pending(panic)` or `terminal_pending(abort)` if the stall is terminal-pending, after the process end that stays reachable (`CFE_PSP_Panic`, or another abort, a restart request or `main`'s return);
  - otherwise `task_never_runs` if every witnessing actor lies on the spawn chain to `u`'s actor (or, for a horizon stall, to the monitored actor) and that actor instance does not yet exist;
  - otherwise `hang_or_deadlock`.

  The record lists every dead actor and the object or predicate it waits on.
- **Progress chains.** `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` iff some stall state of `i₂` has a wait-for root r such that (P1) an actor of r is inside an open occurrence of `u₁` or is the monitored actor of a PENDING occurrence of `u₁`; or (P2) every event that could still satisfy r's wait (a write to its predicate's support, a release, give, put or send on its object, its actor's spawn) is dominated in the certificate view by the READY outcome of `u₁`'s call, and the finite execution contains an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready on that path; or (P3) r's object is a lock acquired in an occurrence of `u₁` decided not ready (K-LOCKBAL), or r waits on an actor that terminated on the not-ready branch of an occurrence of `u₁` (K-SPAWN). Each condition is read from the product state, its finite execution and the certificate view, so chains are decidable. The root instance is reported; dependents are attached (as in App. A.2 item 4). A stall whose wait-for root involves no not-ready outcome (a cycle of predicate waits; a provider that simply never gives) has no chain root and is grouped by its wait-for root only. Progress chains are stored per cell, apart from readiness-violation chains (§1.6).

**Progress result** per cell (σ, M) and bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). It takes one of three values:
- `stall-witness(S)`, S the non-empty set of {pre-use, in-use, horizon} clauses that hold, when a stall state for i is reachable. The witness is the finite execution to that state, with its kind, qualifiers, wait-for root, harm and realization: `model(b)` from the explorer, or `realized` from a run with a wait-for fact (§4.13).
- `no-stall-within(b)`, when `exh` shows that no stall state for i is reachable within b and no deadness test was undecided (§4.10).
- otherwise `unknown(reason)`, with reason `not_checked` when no route ran; `bound` (censored), `budget`, `unmodelled` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure), `teardown_scope`, `model_gap`, `model_fault`.

Certificates, prediction, observation without a wait-for fact and transfer between σ never set it, with one exception: a stall state of kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` is a stall state in every σ in which its finite execution is admissible (L-DEAD (c), (d)). No value means "live", "deadlock-free" or "safe". The progress result does not cover executions in which `u`'s actor instance, or an actor on its spawn chain, terminates without an occurrence (a task lost to F7, `osapi-task.c:128–137`; the timebase thread's silent return), or that end in a panic; their only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence that led to them, with the observed consequence on its chain (`consequence`: task lost, panic, process abort) and the harm that its caller-level Allowed gives (`unspecified_by_reference` under the defaults for K-SPAWN.osal and the K-SELF.es raise, §1.6, §3.3), and a σ_U panic reached through a spontaneous timeout has no such root and leaves no record (§6.4 item 34). Stall witnesses from a model are relative to that model, for two reasons:
- `Sk^cert` over-approximates paths (A-SKEL) and adds local choices, such as a star-closed loop's exit (hence the `choice-dependent` qualifier);
- deadness is preserved neither when behaviour is added nor when it is removed (L-DEAD).

A model stall witness is therefore a candidate until it is confirmed, only confirmable kinds can be confirmed, and `no-stall-within(b)` is a statement about the model only (G11).

**Containment, per model level** (L-CONT, §6.2; fixes [X1-C2]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). Under A7-impl (Φ_L-1 semantics over-approximate kernel, libc and OSAL POSIX on the M_impl paths), A7-api (the remaining OSAL primitives are linearizable at their LP on the real backend, A10), A3, A4, A9 and A-SKEL:
- `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M) = {ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M) : ρ contains no time-based transition}`, for each M;
- `π(Pref(B,σ_R1,M)) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, for each M. Here π erases σ_R1's clock-advance and wake steps, keeps every other step (a delay's block step is σ_U's delay transition), and projects states onto σ_U's components;
- the projection of every finite real run of B onto LP-granular events is in `Pref(B,σ_U,M_impl)`.

**Maximal containment is not claimed.**
- `Max(B,σ_U∞,M) ⊄ Max(B,σ_U,M)` in general. In litmus LP-TW (App. A.8), the σ_U∞ execution that ends with the waiter dead is maximal in σ_U∞, while σ_U still enables its timeout.
- Real runs are finite executions, and their end states may still enable σ_U transitions.
- For σ_R1, maximal containment modulo π is an unproved conjecture [I] and is not used.

The prefix version of `Pref(B,σ_E) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M_api)` is **not** claimed either: M_api excludes real POSIX behaviours (`INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` in RESERVED windows, the type-lock leak, silent task loss). For a cone that contains no M_impl-relevant operation, the two levels have the same projected finite executions (Lemma L-MLEVEL, §6.2) [I].

**Transfer rules** [D] (revised by EXT-20261012 §4.2):
1. Rule 1 has two parts.
   - (a) **Safety down, statement by statement.** A statement `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` or `¬NR_i` established under σ_U at level M and bound b holds, as the same statement, under σ_U∞ at the same M and b, and under σ_R1[P_sched] at the same M either with b = ∞, or with a bounded b when the σ_R1 model's cone contains the σ_U model's cone at the same h (L-CONE-R1). The receiving statement carries the flag `SAFETY_TRANSFERRED`; whether a transferred `¬OK_i` is deciding is recomputed in the receiving σ (§1.10); the receiving cell's class follows from its record, so transferred and locally established statements combine without order dependence.
   - (b) **Existence per σ.** Reach, witnesses (hence `allowed-not-ready`, `schedule-dependent` and `ordinal-split`) and the progress result are never transferred down, **except** stall states by L-DEAD (c), (d). Each is computed in its own σ; otherwise the cell reads `reach = assumed` and `progress = unknown(not_checked)`. If `exh` shows that no finite execution of σ′ reaches the use within b, the cell's reach at b is `unreached(b)`, and a transferred universal class reads "… if reached; not reached within b". The v1 label `inactive_σ′` is withdrawn; `inactive` is a static class only.
2. **Transfer to σ_E** is allowed only for safety statements with **b = ∞**, and only from M_impl, or from M_api when (i) the instance's cone and certificate contain no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL over prefixes, sub-label `mlevel-lemma`), or (ii) the M_impl result exists and agrees. The σ_E cell then states, for the **decided** in-scope occurrences of every finite real run of B, each safety statement that is established there (§1.10 safety record), in exactly the form in which the certificate lemmas prove it (L-FO, L-PR, §6.2): `¬Bad_i`: none is `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`; `¬OK_i`: none is `READY` or `TOLERATED`; `¬NR_i`: none is decided not ready (`TOLERATED`, `PENDING`, `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`), i.e. every decided one is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED`. An occurrence that is still open when a run ends has no status and lies outside all three statements. A `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrence (its call returned before one of its conjunct LPs and no conjunct was found false, e.g. an early return on an argument check) is neither ready nor not ready; all three statements allow it, so a real run that contains one is no counterexample to the cell. The cell does not state that every in-scope occurrence of a real run is `READY`, that any occurrence is reached, or anything about progress. Otherwise the σ_E cell stays `unknown(model_level)`, and the M_api result is reported as a portability claim.
3. **Witnesses up.** The following are good or bad witnesses under σ_U at the same M, with their occurrence ordinal, when they contain an in-scope occurrence with a final status (`READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`):
   - a finite execution of σ′ (σ_U∞, or σ_R1 after π);
   - a real run at M_impl, or at M_api under L-MLEVEL or with an M_api-conformance check of the prefix of every actor of the instance's closed cone (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2; §4.7 item 6).

   `PENDING` and `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences are not witnesses. A run witness is paired with a model witness at the same ordinal only through the map from run identities to canonical names (§1.2). **Stall witnesses do not transfer** (LP-TW: a σ_U∞ stall exists while σ_U has none), except by L-DEAD (c), (d).
4. Nothing transfers *into* σ_E as a frequency, an existential class, a reach value or a progress result. A σ_E `schedule-dependent` class needs two distinct natural runs with a bad and a good occurrence at the same ordinal (§1.10).
5. A blanket "no transfer across σ" ([A §6.3 item 3]) is dropped as too weak; B's transfer of a trace-local certificate ([B §4.3]) is dropped as too strong.

**Refinements, derived from the per-σ results and stored with their own bound** [D] (fixes [X1-M5], [X1-m3]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). `Bad_i(σ)` and `OK_i(σ)` denote the existence of a bad finite execution and of a good witness (§1.10), at the cell's M and b.
- **timeout-dependent** (refines schedule-dependent under σ_U): `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent`, `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` established, and the use reached under σ_U∞ (reach `model(b)` or `proved`). By L-CONT the second condition is equivalent to: every bad finite execution of σ_U contains a time-based transition. Without σ_U∞ reach the flag is not set; `timeout-rescued` and the σ_U∞ reach then show that the use is reached only through a time-based transition.
- If `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds, the readiness violation lives in the ordering logic, and no refinement applies.
- **timeout-rescued** (good-side flag): `OK_i(σ_U)` and `¬OK_i(σ_U∞)` established. Equivalently, every good witness of σ_U contains a time-based transition: the instance is good only because some wait gave up.
- **timeout-masked-liveness** (progress refinement of the σ_U cell, **per instance**): some product state reachable under σ_U∞ is a **hang state** for i under σ_U∞ but not under σ_U. Every σ_U escape from it passes through a time-based transition, which turns a permanent wait into a readiness violation, a panic or another continuation.
- **tml-root**: `tml-root(i₁)` iff no progress chain leads into `i₁` and some `i₂` with `i₁ ⇝_p* i₂` (`i₂ = i₁` included) has timeout-masked-liveness. It marks a progress-chain root with TML members, and an unchained instance with TML itself. The report shows each `tml-root` instance and the number of its chained members; each member's own cell carries timeout-masked-liveness and the flag `CHAINED`, and members are not reported one by one. The root's own σ_U∞ progress can be `no-stall-within(b)`. Example: a raise skipped inside the core App-record window makes `MainTaskSyncDelay` unsatisfiable (§3.3); under σ_U∞, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`, whose test reads lie inside one ES-lock critical section, §4.3), the startup thread is dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835`, and the core tasks already created are dead in their `CORE_READY` polls; under σ_U the startup thread panics. The instances behind it carry timeout-masked-liveness and are chained to that window's K-SELF.es instance, which carries `tml-root`.
- **Without the explorer, no refinement is established.** `timeout-dependent` and `timeout-rescued` need universal σ_U∞ statements, which neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide; prediction can only show `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` or `OK_i(σ_U∞)` and so rule a refinement out.
- **Correspondence with v1** [I]. v1's `timeout-dependent` required that the σ_U∞ continuations from each pre-timeout state reach only good outcomes; the new refinement keeps the "not bad" part, and the "no stall" part is the progress refinement. Every v1 `timeout-masked-liveness` instance whose v1 stall state is a hang state under σ_U∞ has the new refinement; v1 also counted states in which the use's actor waits at an idle wait, which the new refinement excludes. The converse does not hold, because the new one also covers hang states that no bad σ_U execution of the instance passes through, which progress chains attach to their root.

Confirmation runs in which a **time-based transition** (§1.8: the timeout of a non-periodic timed wait, the failing last attempt of a delay-paced retry, the forced kill) occurs during a hold, or after it and before the witness's deciding events, on an actor of the instance's closed cone, are labelled `timeout-exercised` (naturalness axis), never natural σ_E. It is decided from the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 (c), not from return statuses alone: a retry that exhausts because the hold keeps its provider back (C1, App. A.4 step 5) sees `NAME_NOT_FOUND`, not a timeout status, and an OSAL-internal timed wake is never returned. Timeouts of periodic waits do not count.

### 1.9 Ordering relations

Φ's HB rules are inference rules `e₁ → e₂` with a matching condition, a stratum, a robustness tag and a code citation.

**Strata** ([C §0]) [D]:

| Stratum | Meaning | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| **S** (structural) | Holds in every execution that contains both endpoints on the given path: program order within an actor (inside one handler for loopers), task create → child start, join, script sequencing, and **guarded observation edges** whose writer set is complete, whose predicate is extracted from the code, and whose only alternative continuation is terminal | MustBefore and certificates |
| **O** (observed synchronization) | Holds in the observed execution only: outermost mutex release → next acquire, semaphore give → take, delivered queue/SB message, poll exit by condition with a soft alternative, timer add/set → callback, TBL request and flag protocols, FS request → completion, outcome-implied reads-from | HB of a trace; reorderable in prediction, subject to the primitive's semantics |
| **C_σ** (schedule constraint) | Fixed-priority preemption on one CPU, affinity, under P_sched | Feasibility under σ_R1 only; never an HB edge ([R04 §4 row 20]; [R05 §2.7]) |

**Looper rule** [D] (fixes [X1-M7](b)). On an actor that loops over `ReceiveBuffer` and dispatches (a *looper*), program order holds **inside one handler** and from the loop's init prefix to every handler. **Between two handlers on the same looper there is no HB edge**, neither in VC nor in VC_R; shapes and the UNORDERED route are computed without it (CAFA PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; DCatch Rule-Pnreg ASPLOS 2017 [P]; [R04 §2.7, §4 row 8]). The sequential execution of handlers survives only as a **prediction constraint**: handlers of one looper do not overlap, and handlers of messages from the same pipe run in dequeue order, which is the FIFO order of their enqueues.

**Trace-side image: robustness tags** ([B §4.2]):
- `unconditional` (S-rules that need no observation);
- `obs-fatal` (S, guarded observation with terminal escape);
- `obs-soft` (O, log-and-continue escape);
- `schedule-chosen` (O: locks, semaphores, messages);
- `assumed` (holds only under assumption A4: volatile flags, seqlock, TBL result flags, the SB subscription-reporting flag).

**Relations computed:**

1. **MustBefore** (static; §4.4). `a ∈ MB(n)` iff in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` in which `n` occurs (for σ_U, the same relation as over `Exec(B,σ_U,M)`, since every finite execution extends to a maximal one and MB is prefix-closed), every occurrence of `n` is preceded by an occurrence of `a`. It is a greatest fixpoint over the certificate view, using S-rules only, computed per M.
2. **Precedes-whenever-both-occur** `PWB(a, b)`: in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` containing both, every occurrence of `a` precedes every occurrence of `b` (for σ_U, the same relation as over `Exec`). Certified by the **frontier rule** (`once(actor(a))`, `∃ m` in `a`'s actor with no skeleton path `m →* a` and `m ∈ MB(b)`) or by the **once rule** (`once(a) ∧ a ∈ MB(b)`), §4.5.
3. **HB_π** on a trace π: the transitive closure of S- and O-edges (looper rule applied), computed with vector clocks (Fidge 1988; Mattern 1989 [V-abs]). Two clocks per event ([B §4.2]):
   - `VC` over all definite edges;
   - `VC_R` over the edges tagged `unconditional` or `obs-fatal`.

   `a ≺_R b` is a cheap trace-side prefilter for a static certificate, never an Exec-level claim ([J1 X5]).
4. **C_σ** constraints only restrict σ_R1 feasibility, and only in the explorer (never in trace prediction, §4.9).

Rules not in Φ are listed as **unmodelled**. A loop that matches the poll pattern (§4.3) but whose predicate cannot be extracted, or a protocol over app-private memory with no Φ rule (e.g. FM's parent–child queue, `fm_child.c:65–100, 162`), becomes an `unmodelled-sync` mark, and every instance whose decision depends on it is `unknown(unmodelled)`. This is the KCSAN lesson that an HB model missing one ordering mechanism floods false positives (`kcsan.rst` L366–373 [V-doc, R04 §2.4]).

### 1.10 Classes, refinements, axes, shapes, evidence and the verdict record

Fix instance `i`, basis B, semantics σ, level M and bound b. Statuses are those of §1.6, evaluated on `Pref_b(B,σ,M)`; "in-scope" means belonging to `i`, and `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences are counted below by nothing. For an occurrence ordinal o, write:
- `Bad_i` (`Bad_i(o)`): some finite execution contains a `VIOLATING` (or, with Allowed unspecified, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`) in-scope occurrence of `u` (at ordinal o);
- `OK_i` (`OK_i(o)`): some finite execution contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence (at ordinal o);
- `NR_i`: some finite execution contains an in-scope occurrence decided not ready (`TOLERATED`, `PENDING`, `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`);
- `Reach_i`: some finite execution contains an in-scope occurrence whose readiness is decided and evaluated (not `NOT_EVALUATED`).

**Safety record** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Per cell and per bound, the record holds the statement `¬Bad_i` if established, the statement `¬OK_i` if established, and the statement `¬NR_i` ("no in-scope occurrence is decided not ready", i.e. every decided one is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED`) if established, each with its route (certificate, `exh`, transfer); `¬NR_i` comes only from a PROTECTED certificate or a transfer of one, and implies `¬Bad_i`. With `¬OK_i` the record keeps its **decision route**: `¬OK_i` is **deciding** iff the record holds a bad witness in that cell at that bound, or the statement comes from a FIXED_ORDER certificate (or a transfer of one) whose badness item decides every not-ready occurrence at its decision event (`Allowed = none`; `unspecified_by_reference`; an outcome outside the entry's codes). Such a certificate decides in every σ. A certificate whose REJECT events lie after the decision (a retry's exhaustion, an error exit or return, a next operation, a termination) decides in no σ by itself: the path to the REJECT can be blocked by an untimed wait, by a transition that σ_U∞ removes, or by starvation under σ_R1. The record also holds the bad and good witnesses with their ordinals. The **class is a function of the record**:

| Class (safety axis) | Record | Proof obligation |
|---|---|---|
| `inactive(B)` | no execution reaches the use | static: the template is unreachable in the **certificate view** under B constants and message provenance (path condition as proof). An unobserved use is **not** inactive; it is `unknown(not_reached)` |
| `protected(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬Bad_i` established, `¬OK_i` not: every in-scope occurrence that is reached is `READY`, `TOLERATED` or `PENDING`, never `VIOLATING` or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`; with `¬NR_i`, every one is `READY` (a reached occurrence is decided and not `NOT_EVALUATED`, `Reach_i` above; `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences are counted by no statement, so `¬NR_i` is not "every occurrence is `READY`") | certificate (§4.5, b = ∞; L-PR proves `¬NR_i`), `exh` with `Q_bad = no` within b (or `dpor`, labelled unverified, T-JACM, G5), or transfer (§1.8 rule 1(a)) |
| ↳ refinement `allowed-not-ready` | protected, and some finite execution contains a `TOLERATED` occurrence | `exh` within b only. A PROTECTED certificate proves `¬NR_i`, so it never supports `allowed-not-ready`; a decided not-ready occurrence (`TOLERATED` or `PENDING` included) against it is a contradiction or an O10 disagreement (below) |
| `fixed-order(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬OK_i` established **and deciding**, `¬Bad_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is `READY` or `TOLERATED`, and a bad witness exists or not-ready occurrences are decided bad at their decision | certificate (L-FO with the badness item), `exh` with `Q_ok = no` and a bad witness within b (or `dpor`, labelled unverified, T-JACM, G5), or transfer with a decision route in the receiving σ. **Never** from k-of-k failing runs ([R06 S9]). That every execution reaches a decision is not claimed |
| `no-final-status(B,σ,M,b)` | `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` both established: every reached in-scope occurrence is `PENDING` (or `NOT_EVALUATED`), or none is reached | the two statements by any of their routes (e.g. a transferred `¬OK_i` and an `exh` `¬Bad_i`) |
| `schedule-dependent(B,σ,M,b)` | a bad witness and a good witness **at the same ordinal**: two distinct finite executions | the two witnesses in the same cell and bound. The record keeps the weaker evidence per axis. A stall witness is never one of the two |
| ↳ refinement | `timeout-dependent` (§1.8) | derived from the σ_U and σ_U∞ cells, with its own bound; needs `exh` |
| `ordinal-split(B,σ,M,b)` | bad and good witnesses, but no established pair at a common ordinal | the witnesses with their ordinals. It does **not** state that any single occurrence depends on the schedule |
| `unknown(reason)` | nothing established; or `¬OK_i` alone without a decision route (reason `no_decision_route`; the statement is still shown); or witnesses of one side only and no statement, i.e. bad witnesses without `¬OK_i` or good witnesses without `¬Bad_i` (reason `one_sided`; the witnesses are shown) | reason ∈ {`not_reached`, `model_gap`, `bound`, `budget`, `key_unresolved`, `loss`, `unmodelled`, `writer_incomplete`, `horizon` (a trace ended while a horizon monitor was OPEN), `horizon_static` (the census's static case, no trace: FIXED_ORDER item 5 found only some continuations within the horizon disallowed, so the class stays open, §4.5, §4.6), `no_decision_route`, `one_sided`, `unordered`, `O-ordered-only`, `conditional-observation` (the open routes of the census, §4.6, while no route has decided), `conformance`, `model_fault` (incl. a `contested` bound), `model_level`} |

**Universal statements, existence and bounds** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2).
- **Scope of the universal statements.** `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` quantify over the reached occurrences, so they hold vacuously when no occurrence is reached, and both hold when every reached occurrence stays `PENDING`. They are **not exclusive**; `no-final-status` names the case where both are established. Neither says anything about executions that do not reach the use. A permanent wait before the use is covered by the progress result; an execution in which the use's actor instance terminates without an occurrence, or the process panics, is covered by neither axis: its only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence, if one led to it, with the observed consequence on its chain (`consequence`: task lost, panic, process abort) and the harm that its caller-level Allowed gives (`unspecified_by_reference` under the defaults for K-SPAWN.osal and the K-SELF.es raise, §1.6, §3.3), and a σ_U panic after a spontaneous timeout has none (§6.4 item 34).
- **Existence** is recorded on the cell's **reach** axis and is never part of a class. With `reach = assumed`, a universal class reads "… if reached". With `reach = unreached(b)`, it reads "… if reached; not reached within b".
- **Per bound.** The record, the class, reach and progress are kept per bound: b = ∞ for certificates, transfers from them, runs and confirmed model paths; each completed h for `exh`. A statement at b = ∞ holds at every b; a witness within b holds at every larger finite bound. The b = ∞ record holds no unconfirmed explorer witness: a model witness enters it only when a run confirms its path, as a realized witness or a confirmed model path (as for reach, below). So an `exh` bad witness never makes a certified `¬OK_i` deciding at b = ∞ (L-PATIENT variants 2 and 3, LP-EARLY, App. A.8). One class is computed per (cell, bound), and the report renders the class at b = ∞ with every bounded class that adds information.
- **Contradictions and disagreements** (§4.12 item 5). At the same bound: (1) a realized bad witness against `¬Bad_i`, a realized good witness against `¬OK_i`, or an observed decided not-ready occurrence against `¬NR_i`, where the statement comes from a certificate or a transfer of one, is a contradiction (§4.14, suspension); (2) an `exh` witness against such a statement, or an `exh` result in one σ against a transferred `exh` statement from another, goes to obligation O10 (directed check; the explored bound becomes `contested`, the certificate's own record at b = ∞ is unchanged and carries a persistent contested mark for the Φ version); (3) a realized witness against an `exh`-only statement is a model-bound conflict (O6, the bound becomes `contested`). None is a lattice step. Evidence outside a bound never contradicts a statement within it.
- **Templates with several occurrences per execution** (§1.2). Classes quantify over occurrences, and witnesses record their ordinal (per key, over canonical actor-instance names). Where a template has at most one in-scope occurrence per execution, `¬Bad_i` coincides with v1's "no execution in `E_i` is bad" (without v1's `E_i ≠ ∅` and `S_i = ∅`), and every pair of witnesses is at the same ordinal.

The good-side flag `timeout-rescued` (§1.8) may accompany protected, schedule-dependent or ordinal-split (every class with a good witness under σ_U).

**Orthogonal axes** [D] (fixes [X1-m3]):
- **provider** ∈ {`present`, `none` (a conjunct is INIT_FALSE and its complete writer set is empty), `env-absent` (the only writers are Φ effects guarded by an `env` predicate that B.Env records false, with outcome agreement O4; e.g. HS `SET_RUNNING`, App. A.6), `unknown`}. A use with provider `none` is classified by its outcomes like any other. It is fixed-order if no reached occurrence can be `READY` or `TOLERATED` and a decision route exists (§1.10). A retry whose only REJECT is its exhaustion, which lies after the decision, therefore has `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown at b = ∞ in every σ from the certificate alone; with `exh` it is fixed-order within b under σ_U (the exhausting execution is a bad witness) and `no-final-status` within b under σ_U∞ when the exhaustion is time-based. It can be protected with `allowed-not-ready` only through `exh`: no certificate route exists for an allowed not-ready outcome, and FIXED_ORDER item 5 leaves such an instance open (§4.5);
- **reach**, per cell (σ, M) and per bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2): at b = ∞, `proved(run id | confirmed model path)` or `assumed`; at a bounded b, `model(b)` (`exh`, or `dpor` labelled unverified, found a decided in-scope occurrence within b; not confirmed) or `unreached(b)` (it found none).
  - `proved` needs a finite execution **of that σ at that M**, realized on B, that contains a decided in-scope occurrence:
    - for σ_U at M_impl, any run (L-CONT) or a model path confirmed by a run;
    - for σ_U at M_api, a run only if the instance's cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL, sub-label `mlevel-lemma`), or if the run's prefix up to the occurrence passes an **M_api-conformance check on every actor of the instance's closed cone**: every OSAL call those actors made returned an outcome that M_api allows, and none of the M_impl-only behaviours (`OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`, a lost task, a leaked type lock) occurred on them; OSAL calls of actors outside the cone do not matter (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2). The check needs the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6;
    - for σ_U∞, the σ_U condition at that M, and a prefix whose transitions **by the actors of the instance's closed cone** contain no time-based transition: no timeout outcome of a non-periodic timed wait, no exhausted delay-paced retry, no forced kill, and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake (`OS_WaitForStateChange` returning by timeout); TB transitions of actors outside the cone do not matter (L-REACH-CONE, §6.2). The check needs the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6, and until they exist run-based σ_U∞ reach at M_impl is `assumed`;
    - for σ_R1, a confirmed model path only on a native σ_R1 arm (S10); otherwise `assumed` or `model(b)`;
    - for σ_E, an observed occurrence.
  - `unreached(b)` and `model(b)` come only from `exh` within b, or from `dpor` within b, labelled unverified (T-JACM, G5; §4.10 item 7, §4.12 item 2). A proof whose witness lies within b (a run whose conforming model prefix lies within b) meeting `unreached(b)` contests that bound (`contested`, §4.14).
  - Otherwise reach is `assumed`. A class with `reach = assumed` reads "… if reached";
- **scope** ∈ {`in-scope`, `teardown`} (§1.6).
- **progress**, per cell (σ, M) and per bound [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1), ∈ {`stall-witness(S)` with S a non-empty set of {pre-use, in-use, horizon}, `no-stall-within(b)`, `unknown(reason)`}. A stall witness carries its kind (`deadlock-cycle`, `leaked-lock`, `other`), its qualifiers (`idle`, `terminal-pending`, `choice-dependent` with the choice support), its wait-for root set, the `confirmable` flag, its realization (`model(b)` | `realized`), harm (`hang_or_deadlock` | `task_never_runs` | `terminal_pending(panic | abort)`), its progress chain, and the outcome label `liveness` (§1.8). The default is `unknown(not_checked)`; K-TEARDOWN instances have `unknown(teardown_scope)`; a bound refuted by evidence becomes `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`, §4.14).
  - The progress result never changes the class, and the class never implies a progress value. `protected` together with a stall witness is a consistent record (litmus LP-DL), not a contradiction.
  - **Rendering rule.** A universal class is always rendered together with the cell's reach and progress values and their bounds, and a stall witness with its kind, its qualifiers and its wait-for root; a statement that rests on a certificate, or on a transferred one, is also rendered with its lemma status (§6.2, PS3). Example: "protected — every decided occurrence READY or NOT_EVALUATED (certificate, b = ∞); reach proved; progress not checked; lemma status: unproved". `terminal-pending` is rendered as relative to the closed cone.
  - **Counting rule.** A stall witness counts as a detection or as Reported (§8.5) only if it is `realized`, or a model witness that is confirmable and not choice-dependent. Counted witnesses are grouped by wait-for root set, and each group counts once per cell, with the instances it blocks attached. Other model stall witnesses are listed as progress candidates.

**Shape** ([A §1.7]) of a bad finite witness ρ with occurrence `u`, where `p*` is the first transition that would make the failing conjunct true; computed with the looper rule (no between-handler edges) and API-level edges only. Witnesses are finite executions (§1.8), so the shape is defined on ρ and on a **recorded extension** of it (the rest of the run that contains ρ, or the explorer path ρ belongs to) [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.2):
- **WRONG-WAY**: `p*` occurs after `u` in ρ or in its recorded extension, and `u →hb p*`;
- **UNORDERED**: `u ∥hb p*`;
- **ABSENT**: no extension of ρ can contain `p*`. This is shown either statically (provider `none`; or `p*`'s node is once and has already been passed on ρ without producing `p*`, e.g. SB's `GetAppID` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` after its failure branch), or by `exh` from ρ's end state within b with no censored or stranded actor that could produce `p*` (the statement is then bounded by b). It is never inferred from a run that merely ends;
- **NOT-OBSERVED**: `p*` occurs neither in ρ nor in its recorded extension, and ABSENT is not shown;
- **STALE**: the conjunct held earlier, then a disabling transition occurred before `u` (generation mismatch).

**Evidence**, two axes ([R06 S1]; fixes [X1-M11], [X1-M4]):

| Axis | Values |
|---|---|
| **realization** | `certificate(id)`; `model(b)`; `predicted` (trace-sound under A-COMM, §4.9); `predicted-modelled` (A-COMM failed: model-based); `predicted-extrapolated` (summary-guided extrapolation: model-based); `realized` (the order and outcome occurred on the gcc binary) |
| **naturalness** (realized only) | `natural` (no hold, no gate); `held-F0`; `gated-F1`; `timeout-exercised` (a time-based transition of a cone actor occurred during or because of a hold, §1.8; periodic waits excluded); flag `derived` (the schedule was constructed) |

"Weaker" is decided per axis: realization `model(b)` ≈ `predicted-modelled` ≈ `predicted-extrapolated` < `predicted` < `realized`; naturalness `timeout-exercised` < `gated-F1` ≈ `held-F0` < `natural`.

**σ_E cells.**
- **schedule-dependent.** `schedule-dependent(σ_E)` needs a natural bad witness and a natural good witness **at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct realized runs whose occurrences at that ordinal have final statuses, each with its pool key. Bad and good occurrences at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)`, also from a single run. Realized but held or gated witnesses give the label `σ_E(held)`.
- **Universal classes.** No σ_E protected, fixed-order or no-final-status class is ever derived from runs. Such σ_E cells come only from transfer rule 2 of §1.8 (b = ∞), and they state, over the decided in-scope occurrences of every finite real run, the same safety statement that the certificate proves (`¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` or `¬NR_i` as defined above; a `NOT_EVALUATED` early return is allowed by all three, and `¬NR_i` is not "every occurrence is `READY`").
- **Counts.** Observation counts are written `no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown`, never "timing-protected".
- **Progress.** The σ_E progress result is `stall-witness(realized)` only with a wait-for fact (§4.13) that itself shows permanence, i.e. a `deadlock-cycle`, or a `leaked-lock` (K-LOCKBAL) whose holder has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock. Otherwise the result is `unknown(not_checked)`. It is never `no-stall-within(b)`.

**Outcome label** of the safety result: `violation` (a readiness violation: Allowed specified) / `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` (route or capacity specifications whose caller-level Allowed is unspecified and that have no must-deliver `appreq`) / `not-ready-unjudged` (other specifications with Allowed unspecified, and route or capacity specifications whose Allowed is unspecified because a must-deliver `appreq` conflicts with a `caller` entry, §1.6 `appreq` step (3)); `NONE` while no cell has a class that shows a not-ready outcome. The label names the meaning by Allowed, so it is one field per verdict; a cell renders it only when its class is fixed-order, schedule-dependent or ordinal-split. A `fixed-order` class carries its label (`violation`, `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` or `not-ready-unjudged`) only with a decision route (§1.10): App. A.6 is fixed-order with `not-ready-unjudged`. `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)` and renders no label. The label `liveness` belongs to the progress result (§1.8).

**Framework-level judgement** [D] (§1.6 "Three judgements"). Two verdict fields, never combined with the outcome label; their encoding is fixed at S0:
- `fw_doc`, **per instance** (a property of the op's outcome map): the set of documentation statuses of the not-ready result codes that the instance's outcome map lists at either model level, each `documented-not-error` (a supplier-side reference documents the result and says that it is not an error: cSB4305.5's rationale, `cFE_FunctionalRequirements.csv:352`), `documented-result` (documented without such a statement: an `@retval`; `\retval CFE_EVS_APP_ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, `cfe_evs.h:199`, whose code has error severity, `cfe_error.h:115–119, 267`) or `undocumented` (`OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` from `OS_CountSemGetIdByName` at M_impl, outside `osapi-countsem.h:157–160`); empty if the map has no not-ready code. Which code occurred where is in the witnesses and run records;
- `fw_conf`, **per (σ, M) cell**, ∈ {`as-documented`, `deviates`, `unknown`}: whether, in the cell's evidence, the result code (O4(a)) and the documented side effects (§3.9 counters; the event attempts and their outcomes in the trace) occurred as documented. M_api assumes conformance to the API contract (§1.4), so an M_api cell has `unknown`; `as-documented` and `deviates` come from M_impl cells and runs. Each run's conformance is in the conformance record (§4.11), reached through the cell's `runs` reference; a deviation is named there and suspends nothing.

A `documented-not-error` result can carry `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER` or `violation` at the caller level, and a `violation` can rest on a `documented-result` that occurred `as-documented` (R2).

**Consequence** (the observed consequence of §1.6; [A §1.3], effect-oriented as in ConMem and Portend [V-abs, R04 §2.6, §2.10]), recorded for every not-ready occurrence whatever its Allowed, from the trace or the model continuation, with the observed counts that the run oracles give (counter deltas, event attempts and their outcomes, §3.9), read through the cell's `runs` reference: crash or undefined behaviour; deadlock (as the consequence of a VIOLATING occurrence; a permanent wait of the instance itself is its progress result, §1.8); process abort; panic; task lost (a task or thread that never runs its entry, or a child never created; the effect that the harm value `task_never_runs` names); app error exit; app zombie; core service task absent; status ignored and effect lost silently; event then continue; delayed but recovered; message lost (no route, queue full or MsgLim reached); event rejected (an event attempt that EVS refuses, e.g. with `ILLEGAL_APP_ID`).

**Sub-labels**: `portability-hazard`, `implementation-defect-candidate`, `portability-hazard-evidence` (§1.4); `assumed-visibility` (§3.14); `A-ZT` (σ_R1 delay semantics); `tb-undeclared` (a σ_U∞ result that depends on a give-up that Φ has not declared time-based, §1.8). The sub-labels are a bitset (`sublabels`, §2.3). Three rendered labels are not sub-labels and are not stored in the bitset: `trusted:<n>`, kept for continuity, is the number of part-(iii) entries of kind `DECLARED_TRUSTED` (`KZ_TRUST.kind`, §2.3) in the certificate's trust ledger; `ledger:<i>/<ii>/<iii>/<iv>` gives the sizes of the four parts (§4.5.1), read from the `TRUST_LEDGERS` record (`n_rechecked`, `n_extracted`, `n_trusted`, `n_refutable`, where `n_trusted` is the size of the whole of part (iii)); `lemmas:<p>/<d>/<u>` gives the numbers of `LEMMA` entries of part (iii) whose proof status is `proved`, `depends-on-stated-assumption` and `unproved` (§6.2), counted from the ledger's `KZ_TRUST` entries.

**The verdict record** (`kz_verdict` plus per-(σ, M) `kz_verdict_cell` records, §2.3) holds the following:
- instance id; provider and scope;
- per cell:
  - class and refinements, and the safety record they derive from: the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` (with its decision route) and `¬NR_i` with their routes, and the witnesses with their ordinals;
  - bound reference: entries of the section `BOUNDS` (§2.3, `KZ_BOUND_FIELDS`), one per bound, each with the bound and its policy status (including h where the exploration view was used), the per-bound class (including `contested`), reach and progress values (including `contested`), the safety routes and the decision route, the refinements, the progress flags, reason and harm, and the bad, good and stall witnesses as ranges of witness records that carry their ordinals, and the progress chain at that bound, so that the fixed-point test can compare per-bound statements, routes, refinements, progress and witness ordinals (§4.14);
  - reach;
  - the framework-level conformance `fw_conf` of this cell (§1.10);
  - realization and naturalness sets; `n of N` with pool key;
  - the progress result: value, set of stall clauses, kind, qualifiers, wait-for root set, reason, harm, realization, bound, stall witness, progress chain and the flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`;
  - a reference to the trust ledger;
  - the proof status of the cell's statements (`proof_status`): the weakest status among the `LEMMA` entries of part (iii) of the cell's ledger that its statements use (§6.2 "Proof status"), and `NA` if there is none;
- shape; caller level: outcome label and harm; framework level: `fw_doc`; observed: consequence with references to its observed counts; readiness-violation chain links; unknown reason;
- B, Φ and tool hashes;
- typed references to the certificate (with its contested mark, if O10 set one for this Φ version; stored in the certificate record, `KZ_CERT_FIELDS`, §2.3, which also carries the certificate's `proof_status`), witnesses and runs.

**There is never a "safe" field, and no field means "live" or "deadlock-free".**

### 1.11 Why plain data-race HB detection is insufficient, and what is borrowed

| Phenomenon in cFS | What prior work does | What Kairos adds (no new mechanism claimed) |
|---|---|---|
| **R2**: all 24 violating occurrences per boot (one cause at 3 static sites, [R2g §6]) are HB-ordered **before** the publication by core serialization ([R2g §2]) | FastTrack (PLDI 2009 [V-abs]), TSan and Djit+ report unordered conflicting accesses; CP, WCP, SHB, M2 and SyncP (all [V-abs]) report reorderable pairs only ([R04 §3 L4]). DefUse (OOPSLA 2010 [V-abs]) learns def-use invariants from runs; [I] it would learn R2's wrong definition (the EarlyInit value) as normal, because every run reads it. GPredict (ICSE 2015 [V-abs]) predicts violations of user-written generic properties under a causal model ([R04] L249–251). [R04 L553]'s "no notion of a legal error outcome" is an inference of [R04 §5] at abstract level: [R04] records no built-in vocabulary of documented not-ready outcomes for GPredict (abstract-level, [I]); the tool and its property formalism (adapted from MOP, `background/terminology/T4_term_census.md` #52, P) are unverified (P-PAPERS) [U]; we infer that the finite-horizon Allowed entries can be encoded in a GPredict-style property [I]; since revision 1.2 that is a design-rationale inference about related work, not an experiment (§8.3) | A reference-derived readiness specification (R2's K-ID, source tier `contract-backed`) whose caller-level Allowed outcomes (§1.6 "Three judgements") carry provenance and reference horizons, checked on **ordered** pairs, and a σ- and M-qualified PWB certificate that lifts "ordered the wrong way" to `fixed-order` ([R04 §5 G2]) |
| **C1 / C5** and both App-record windows: create/lookup or publish/`GetAppID` under the same lock | HB orders them through the lock; lockset sees consistent protection ([R04 §2.3, §3 L5]). **RVPredict (PLDI 2014) and GPredict-style constraint encodings already treat the key as the conflict object and the lock as atomicity** ([R04 L259, L492]); ExceptioNULL (FSE 2012 [V-abs]) predicts "use before initialized" from one execution with static lockset pruning and SMT ([R04 L253–255]) — the closest predictive prior work for readiness | Explicit-state search instead of a solver (an engineering choice, not novelty); framework typestate, which has prior art (predictive typestate checking, ASE 2008; framework-lifecycle typestate checked over traces, Lifestate, ECOOP 2019; see Defensible contribution), to which Kairos adds only the not-ready outcomes that the caller's, consumer's or mission's references accept (the caller-level Allowed of §1.6; the framework's documented results are reported apart), each with a reference horizon and provenance, pending a check of Joshi & Sen and Lifestate (P-PAPERS), and expressible in a GPredict-style property as far as the inference of the first row goes [I], so not claimed as an expressiveness gap; per-σ classes with same-ordinal pairing; certificates only where an S-edge orders the pair, which is expected neither for an app provider in C1's B′ nor for C5's lock-scope fix (MustBefore has no lock rule, §4.4) [I]; held confirmation. Kairos's predictive soundness is **weaker** than RVPredict's on its sparse API-level traces (A-COMM, §4.9) |
| **No-subscriber loss**: a late subscribe makes a transmit a loss that the sender cannot see (a success return). SB counts it (`NoSubscribersCounter`) and attempts a `SEND_NO_SUBS` event, which SB's default filter passes four times and then stops (`CFE_EVS_FIRST_4_STOP`, `cfe_sb_internal_cfg.h:225`); in R2's window the event is rejected by EVS [M] and the window's counter increments are cleared later by SB's own initialization [I] (App. A.1 step 13a), so there the loss leaves no trace that survives the window [I] | **InitRacer's "late event handler registration"** (OOPSLA 2017 [V-abs]; [R04 L69, L367, L529]) | The cFS instance of that taxonomy, with registries as keys and LOSS-UNDER-ORDER when no `appreq` requires delivery; the framework-level judgement (cSB4305.5: not an error) is reported beside it, never merged (§1.10) |
| **Capacity loss**: queue full drops silently ([M] 4–49 drops per boot, [BM §0]) | Event-driven and distributed HB models assume delivery ([R04 §5 G5]) | Capacity as typestate (queue depth and MsgLim); prediction and exploration ask whether a feasible order changes the outcome. G5 is genuinely open only for capacity |
| Soft, opt-in, timed barriers (`WaitForStartupSync` returns void, `cfe_es_api.c:616–619`) | EventRacer and Tian et al. recognize ad hoc synchronization but not timeout-conditional edges ([R04 §5 G3]) | Conditional O-edges, guarded S-edges, σ_U/σ_U∞ split, timeout-dependent refinements. **The novelty of the G3 formalization is an open literature check**: R04 records that a targeted search was not done ([R04 §8 item 5]) |
| Executing task ≠ owner ≠ service ≠ stamped AppId (G6) | thread identity; CAFA/DroidRacer separate thread from event; DCatch abstracts RPC pools | four identity fields, on-behalf frames, capture points |
| Configuration decides reachability and capacity (G7) | configuration taken as given | B with configuration closure; `inactive`; provider axis |
| Priority order only on RT targets (G8) | no edge, but no verdict either | σ_R1[P_sched] as a schedule constraint, verdict per σ |

**Related work and the experimental comparison** [D] (revision 1.2; ADR-046). The works named in this section (the thread data-race detectors FastTrack, TSan, Djit+ and Eraser-style lockset tools; the predictive analyses CP, WCP, SHB, M2, SyncP, RVPredict, GPredict and ExceptioNULL; DefUse; DCatch) are **related work** for positioning. They are not experimental comparators, and no re-implementation of them is built for a comparison. The experimental comparison is among existing process-level race work (RacePro, SimRacer, SIMEXPLORER, RRF, DESCRY, SimEvo, ReDPro, SysPro; §8.3). This changes nothing in what the table above says about what each prior work does, which rests on [R04]. The roadmap of 2026-10-15 (`plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.3, §11.5) and the resolved-decisions record (§4.4) state that the author-public texts of RacePro and GPredict were read in the review before the decision; this specification relies on them no further than [R04] does, and its statements about GPredict's formalism stay [U] until its §II.B is read here (P-PAPERS, approved on 2026-10-15 with immediate start).

**Established theory used.** No mechanism is claimed as new ([R04 §5], last paragraph):

| Element | Source (status) |
|---|---|
| HB as a partial order; vector clocks | Lamport 1978; Fidge 1988; Mattern 1989 [V-abs] |
| Feasible vs apparent races; order violations as a major class | Netzer & Miller LOPLAS 1992 [V-abs]; Lu et al. ASPLOS 2008 [V-abs] |
| Framework/event HB rules (looper, post/begin, RPC/event) | DroidRacer PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; CAFA PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; DCatch ASPLOS 2017 [P] |
| Initialization races (late registration, access before definition) | InitRacer OOPSLA 2017 [V-abs] |
| Typestate | Strom & Yemini 1986 [V-me] |
| Def-use invariants; generic predictive properties; predicted use-before-init | DefUse OOPSLA 2010 [V-abs]; GPredict ICSE 2015 [V-abs]; ExceptioNULL FSE 2012 [V-abs] |
| Sound prediction with control-flow abstraction; candidate + vindication | Maximal causal models RV 2012 [V-abs]; RVPredict PLDI 2014 [V-abs]; Vindicator PLDI 2018 [V-abs] |
| Ad hoc synchronization; race coverage | Xiong et al. OSDI 2010 [V-abs]; Tian et al. ISSTA 2008 [V-abs]; EventRacer OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs] |
| Atomicity / serializability | Velodrome PLDI 2008 [V-abs]; AVIO ASPLOS 2006 [V-abs]. Left/right-mover reduction (L3): citation open literature check |
| Systematic exploration | DPOR POPL 2005 [V-abs; guarantee wording UNVERIFIED]; source/optimal DPOR POPL 2014 [V-abs; JACM extension to disabling P]; BPOR OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs]; fair SMC PLDI 2008 [V-abs; strongly fair scheduler]; PCT ASPLOS 2010 [V-abs; mechanism P]; SCTBench PPoPP 2014 [V-abs] |
| Static action HB; environment models; harnesses | SIERRA ASPLOS 2018 [V-abs]; SDV EuroSys 2006 [V-abs]; Holzmann & Joshi SPIN 2004 [V-abs]; FlowDroid PLDI 2014 [V-me] |
| Points-to and indirect calls | Andersen 1994 [V-abs]; SVF CC 2016 [V-abs]; MLTA CCS 2019 [V-abs] |
| Triggering and confirmation; outcome triage | CTrigger ASPLOS 2009, RaceFuzzer PLDI 2008, RacePro SOSP 2011, Portend ASPLOS 2012, DataCollider OSDI 2010 [all V-abs] |
| Log/order mining and potential communications | CloudRaid ESEC/FSE 2018 [V-abs]; Snowboard SOSP 2021 [V-abs] |
| Belief deviance | Engler et al. SOSP 2001 [V-me] |
| Soundiness | Livshits et al. CACM 2015 [V-abs] |

**Defensible contribution** [I] (after [X1 §4]; revised by EXT-20261012 §3 and §5.1–§5.2): a σ- and M-qualified must-order certificate that lifts an observed HB-ordered readiness violation to a universal fixed-order class, as a safety statement re-validated relative to a stated trusted base (trust ledger); caller-level Allowed outcomes with provenance and reference horizons (§1.6 "Three judgements"), and per-conjunct LPs; combined with lock-reversing prediction, held confirmation and an evaluation that reports detection, classification and evidence separately on the five design cases, compares with existing process-level race work by the six analysis questions of §8.3 (a difference only where an existing tool's shortage is confirmed), and, in the separate extended evaluation (D8/P-RECALL), measures misses on history-derived mutants and pairs; organized around the cFS semantics G1–G8. Every individual mechanism has prior art, and so do three closer lines of work: checking user-written properties on predicted traces (GPredict, ICSE 2015 [V-abs]); predictive typestate checking of multithreaded programs (Joshi & Sen, ASE 2008; bibliographic record V per `background/08_terminology.md` L754, content known from the abstract and the external review); and framework-lifecycle typestate and protocol specifications checked over traces (Lifestate, Meier, Mover and Chang, ECOOP 2019; V per `background/08_terminology.md` L758, content known from its abstract). None of the three papers has been read in full here (P-PAPERS). The candidate contribution is therefore the cFS-specific extraction, the classification and the evidence, not the use of a specification or of framework typestate. The only difference claimed from Joshi & Sen and Lifestate is that each not-ready outcome that the caller's, consumer's or mission's references accept carries a reference horizon and provenance, while the framework's documented results are reported apart (§1.6); this is pending a check of both papers (`background/08_terminology.md` L559–561; P-PAPERS). It is not a claim that other specification-based techniques cannot express Allowed outcomes (§6.4 item 37). It must be shown beyond R2: on C1, C2, C3 and C5 the classification differentiators need the explorer (S7) and the case bases (D22 for C1, decided and not yet built; P-C2 for C2, D13 for C3 and C5; §9.3, per-case paths and controls), so a result on R2 alone is a single-case result (§8.8).

---

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Layers and principles

```
L5  Orchestration & reporting      kz loop (feedback driver), kz report, kz dump, kz proofcheck (independent inference relative to its trusted base)
L4  Real-binary observation/control kz observe (F0: perf-uprobe + eBPF raw loader, attach cookies |
                                   perf-sample bring-up backend), kz hold (F0: ptrace + debug-register gates),
                                   kz gate (F1, PI-gated)
L3  Analyses (three interpreters)   abstract: kz-static static (census, MustBefore, PWB/once, certificates)
                                   concrete: kz trace (identity, HB, per-conjunct LPs, routing), kz predict, kz conform
                                   operational: kz explore (gated, late; exh and dpor engines)
L2  Formal core                     libkz-core: KEM types, Φ parser, typed core expression evaluator (one evaluator,
                                   three abstract domains), rule engine, typestate, vector clocks, verdict lattice,
                                   provenance, KZ container I/O  (no LLVM dependency)
L1  Static closure & skeletons      kz-static basis, extract (Clang AST facts, derives, writer extraction S3a),
                                   link (IR call graph, DWARF/MC binder, anchor audit), modref (S3b, conditional),
                                   skel (certificate view + exploration view)   (libkz-llvm)
L0  Inputs                          basis B (build tree, configuration, Env, Init, Input), profile Φ_cFS (text)
```

Principles [D]:

1. **One formal core.** Every component reads and writes KEM types through `libkz-core`. No component has a private notion of event, resource, edge, predicate or verdict. Φ predicates have one evaluator. The one deliberate exception: `kz proofcheck` re-derives extracted await predicates and decides `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own symbolic procedure (§4.5), so that this step does not reuse the producer's evaluator; only `C`'s syntax tree comes from the shared Φ parser. Φ conjuncts and guards are otherwise evaluated by the `libkz-core` evaluator everywhere, the checker included: the checker's INIT step and the falsifier condition of T-OBS call it. That evaluator is therefore part of the checker's trusted base, and those steps are marked "re-validated via T5" in the trust ledger (§4.5, §4.5.1).
