7a. **Horizon completion** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1). A predicted reversal makes u's outcome not-ready, so u's continuation in the witness is not in π.
   - If the horizon monitor decides at the use (Allowed `none`, `documented_drop`, `unspecified_by_reference`, or an outcome outside the entry's codes, e.g. `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` for CF), the predicted occurrence has a final status, and the witness is a bad witness with the label of step 4.
   - Otherwise the occurrence is `PENDING` in the predicted prefix: **predicted not-ready evidence**, not a witness, and no class follows from it. It becomes a witness only by horizon completion. Either the monitored actor's continuation from `u` to the monitor's decision event is taken from `Sk^cert` (the path check of §1.6), or, for retry and poll loops, step 7's extrapolation reaches the effective bound. The witness is then `predicted-extrapolated` (model-based), and is admitted only where that region passed O2. The explorer completes the remaining cases.
8. **Budget**: a deterministic work-unit cap (search nodes), a **deviation** (reason: finite resources; effect: `unknown(budget)`; necessity: no reference defines it). Results are reported as an **anytime curve**: the decided share against work units (fixes [X3-M9], [X3-M10]).
9. **Result**: a witness with its realization label, predicted not-ready evidence (`PENDING`; no class), or "not predictable from π within the cap". **None of them is a protected verdict.**
10. **Optional SMT cross-check** after PI decision P-Z3: an order-variable encoding in the style of Alglave, Kroening and Tautschnig (CAV 2013 [V-abs]) and Wang et al. (FM 2009 [V-abs]), emitted as SMT-LIB2 for small cones; never on the critical path.

Complexity: prediction with even one synchronization reversal is NP-complete and W[1]-hard (SyncP [V-abs]); the explicit search is exponential in |C| in the worst case.

### 4.10 Bounded explorer (`kz explore`; stage S7, gated)

1. **Closed model per candidate.**
   - The **closed cone** ([A §4.5], `proposal_A-static-model.md:674`) is built by repeating the following to a fixpoint (EXT-20261012 §4.1):
     - actors containing `u`, `W`, `D` or support objects;
     - every actor that writes a cone location, synchronizes on a cone object, or writes the support of a cone predicate;
     - **cone objects and cone predicates also include the synchronization objects (mutexes, semaphores, queues, pipes) and the awaited predicates that cone actors acquire, take, get or await** on their `Sk^cert` paths (i) to their cone operations, (ii) through the end of every monitored horizon of the instance (§1.6), and (iii) for the actors on the spawn chains, to their spawn sites; **every monitored actor of the instance's Allowed entry and its spawn chain are cone actors**, also when the monitored actor does not exist when the occurrence is decided (e.g. TO_LAB for `defer_by_design`); Without (i), an actor that takes the same locks in the opposite order is outside the cone and a deadlock is missed (litmus LP-DL); without (ii), a wait after a not-ready use on an object given outside the cone becomes a false horizon stall; without (iii), the startup thread's wait at `cfe_es_start.c:835` loses the writers of its predicate and becomes a false pre-use stall; without the monitored actor, ACCEPT cannot occur in the model and a deferred occurrence would stay PENDING;
     - the spawn chains that reach them.

     The closed cone is computed by `static` and `plan` at S3a (§2.4 step 4) on `Sk^cert`, so it exists without the explorer; the explorer, L-MLEVEL, the per-cell reach checks, the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 and the naturalness label `timeout-exercised` reuse it [D] (EXT-20261012).

     The full synchronization skeleton of the cone actors is kept. **Under σ_R1, every actor whose priority is at least the minimum cone priority is kept**, because it interferes through the CPU (fixes [X1-m5]); a bounded σ_R1 model built this way contains the σ_U cone at the same h (L-CONE-R1). Expected size in cFS [I]: the closed cones of the five kill-criterion instances (item 9) contain the startup thread and every core main task created before the instance's actor, because the startup thread awaits each core task's raise on its path to the next spawn and every ES API call takes the ES shared-data lock; the C1-derived, K-PHASE and `SBNSubPipe` cones also contain most script apps. This is not measured; S7's anytime curves will show it (D21, decided: the closed cone, §10.1).
   - B constants; Φ semantics at level M; awaits per σ; retries unrolled to effective bounds; inputs from B.Input; the exploration view `Sk^h` (h in b).
   - **Trace-anchored windows** (optional, [B §4.4]): the prefix is fixed to an observed run up to the latest common S-predecessor of {p, u}; verdicts are labelled "within window W of run r".
2. **Transition system.** A state is (actor control states, resource states, synchronization states, relevant locals, a discrete clock and ready/blocked status under σ_R1) together with the **instance monitor state** of §1.8: whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP occurred, and per actor its occurrence ordinal per key, the last captured value per capture site, its open occurrence (at most one per template) and its PENDING occurrences with their horizon-monitor states. The visited set of `exh` holds these product states. A transition is one effect at its LP, or one of the following explicit local choices:
   - a havoc value (finite domain from value analysis, else `unknown`);
   - an await outcome: success is enabled iff `Pred_code` holds; timeout is always enabled under σ_U, and under σ_U∞ only for periodic waits;
   - a retry exhaustion, i.e. the failure of the last unrolled attempt at its deciding transition at level M. Under σ_U∞ it is never enabled for delay-paced loops: a last attempt that is one transition at M waits until it succeeds; one that is several transitions completes and can strand its actor (§1.8);
   - the ES kill timer at each scan: `not-yet-expired`, `expire-stopped` or `expire-waiting` (§1.8); under σ_U∞ only `expire-waiting`, the forced kill, is removed;
   - a star-closure iterate or exit within h ([J1 X6]).

   An actor at a `TRUNC` node is **censored**: it is neither terminated nor dead (item 8). The record of every explored path keeps its **choice support**: the havoc values, star-closure choices and unresolved data branches it takes.
3. **Dependence** `D(t₁, t₂)` holds iff both belong to the same actor; or they touch the same key, location or object and at least one changes it; or one may enable or disable the other (lock/unlock of the same lock; give/take of the same semaphore; put/get on the same queue incl. capacity and MsgLim; an await versus any write to its predicate's support; a spawn versus the child's first transition; a join versus the victim's last transition). Under σ_R1, every transition that can change the readiness of a higher-priority actor is dependent with all lower-priority transitions (conservative, [A §4.5]). **Monitor dependences** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1; the instance monitor reads orders that the rules above treat as independent): (i) the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP is dependent with every call and every conjunct LP of the instance's template, because scope is decided by their order (§1.6); (ii) every deciding event of an occurrence (its call and its conjunct LPs) is dependent with every transition of its monitored actor when that actor is not the using actor, and, while the monitored actor does not exist, with the transitions of its spawn-chain actors, because the horizon monitor and its static REJECT read their order; (iii) for default-scope occurrences, the TEARDOWN LP is dependent with every transition of a monitored actor, because the horizon monitor freezes at it (§1.6). `dpor`'s guarantee covers only monitor properties that are invariant under this extended dependence (L-DPOR).
4. **Two engines** [D] (fixes [X1-M9]):
   - **`exh`**: stateful exhaustive search over the finite closed model with a visited-state set and no partial-order reduction. Its correctness is that of reachability on a finite graph: every reachable state within b is visited. Results from `exh` carry G5 relative to the model (§6.3).
   - **`dpor`**: source-DPOR with sleep sets (Abdulla et al., POPL 2014 [V-abs]), with blocking handled by the enabling/disabling dependences of item 3. Its correctness for systems with disabling is the theorem of the JACM 2017 version (source-DPOR extended to disabling), whose details are **status P** in [R05 §2.2]. Until that theorem's statement is verified against the paper (PI decision P-PAPERS), **G5 for `dpor` results is labelled unverified**. The differential test against `exh` on every model small enough for `exh` is **validation evidence only**, not a proof. Optimal DPOR (wakeup trees, "one execution per class") is not adopted and not claimed.
   - Every verdict records its engine.
5. **Reductions**, each switchable and ablated:
   - **L1** (await summarization with code-extracted predicates; §4.3, §6.2);
   - **L2**: a critical section on lock ℓ is removed only if (i) ℓ is not a cone object and no other cone actor acquires ℓ, and (ii) the body touches no cone location or cone object and **contains no blocking operation** ([A §4.2], `proposal_A-static-model.md:615`). The section's own acquire and release count as touching ℓ, and nested acquisitions count as blocking operations. A section that can take part in a lock-order cycle, including one with an empty body such as τ_B's inner `m1` section in litmus LP-DL, is therefore kept;
   - **L3** (**critical sections as mover-reduced atomic blocks**, fixes [X1-M10]). A critical section on lock ℓ becomes one atomic transition only if a static check shows: the acquire is a right mover and the release a left mover; every access inside to a location protected by ℓ is a both-mover; **at most one non-mover** inside (a blocking call, an operation on another synchronization object, or an access to an unprotected shared location); **the lock is released on every path** (paths that leak the lock, such as K-LOCKBAL under M_impl, fail the check); and **no nested blocking acquire**. `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask` (cancel and join under the ES lock, `cfe_es_api.c:1348, 1383`) fails the check and stays fine-grained, so K-LOCK.cancel executions are not removed. Critical sections that fail stay acquire/release transitions. The left/right-mover reduction theorem is used as proof obligation L3; its citation is an open literature check (not verified in R04/R05).
6. **Bounds.** Horizon h and work-unit caps: h is not chosen; the explorer reports results for each h it completes within the work-unit cap, which is a **deviation** (finite resources), with anytime curves (decided share against work units). Wall time and peak memory are costs only. No preemption bound by default; if one is introduced, BPOR (Coons et al. OOPSLA 2013 [V-abs]) is used, because naive DPOR plus a bound is unsound within the bound, and the bound is swept from 0 upward until the cap (SCTBench norm, [R05 §2.1]).
7. **Four queries** ([R05 §2.3]; revised by EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2), over the finite executions and reachable product states of the closed model at σ, M and b:
   - `Q_reach`: some finite execution contains a decided in-scope occurrence of `u` whose readiness is evaluated (not `NOT_EVALUATED`);
   - `Q_bad`: some finite execution contains a `VIOLATING` (or `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`) in-scope occurrence; also answered per occurrence ordinal;
   - `Q_ok`: some finite execution contains a `READY` or `TOLERATED` in-scope occurrence (a `NOT_EVALUATED` one is neither); also answered per occurrence ordinal;
   - `Q_stall`: some reachable product state is a stall state for i (§1.8), with its kind and qualifiers.

   The results are read as follows. Each query sets its own component of the cell's safety record at bound b, and the class follows from the record (§1.10).
   - **Safety record**:
     - `Q_bad` = no → the statement `¬Bad_i` within b; `Q_ok` = no → the statement `¬OK_i` within b; `Q_bad` = yes → bad witnesses; `Q_ok` = yes → good witnesses, each with its ordinal;
     - **safety-side censoring**, symmetric (a bounded statement is withheld when the continuation of a censored or stranded actor could refute it): `Q_ok` = no establishes `¬OK_i` only if no such actor's region beyond the cut contains (i) a writer of a conjunct of the instance (a `W` entry), (ii) the ACCEPT event of a monitored actor that holds a PENDING occurrence, or (iii) a call of the use whose conjuncts can hold there (true at INIT or with a writer in the model); `Q_bad` = no establishes `¬Bad_i` only if no such region contains (i) a disabler (a `D` entry) of a conjunct, (ii) the REJECT event of a monitored actor that holds a PENDING occurrence, or (iii) a call of the use whose conjuncts can fail there (false at INIT or with a disabler in the model). Otherwise the statement is `unknown(bound)` for that side (litmus L-H2);
     - hence (no, no) gives `no-final-status` within b, (yes, no) fixed-order (the bad witness is its decision route), (no, yes) protected (with `allowed-not-ready` if a `TOLERATED` occurrence exists), and (yes, yes) schedule-dependent if some ordinal answers yes to both, else `ordinal-split`.
   - **Reach**: `Q_reach` = yes → `model(b)`; `Q_reach` = no → `unreached(b)`.
   - **Progress**:
     - `Q_stall` = yes → `stall-witness`, recording the shortest finite execution to a stall state, its kind, qualifiers and choice support, the witnessing actors and what they wait on, the wait-for root set and the progress chain; a stall state found is a witness even when other deadness tests are undecided;
     - `Q_stall` = no, with no undecided deadness test → `no-stall-within(b)`;
     - an undecided test → `unknown(bound)` (censored, or in a censored actor's closure) or `unknown(unmodelled)` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure).

     A stall never decides the safety record. In particular, a stall without a bad occurrence never yields fixed-order.
   - **Budget**: when the cap is exhausted, the components not yet decided get `unknown(budget)`.

   The queries run under σ_U and σ_U∞ (refinements, §1.8), and under σ_R1[P_sched] when requested. **Each σ's results are computed independently.** Refinements are derived afterwards from the per-σ results and stored with their own bounds; timeout-masked-liveness also compares the σ_U and σ_U∞ hang states at the same product states.

   `dpor` (item 4) decides `Q_reach`, `Q_bad` and `Q_ok` under T-JACM, and only with the extended dependence of item 3 (the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP against the instance's calls and conjunct LPs; an occurrence's deciding events against the transitions of a monitored actor other than the using actor and of its spawn chain; the TEARDOWN LP against a monitored actor's transitions), because scope and those monitors read the order of transitions that the plain dependence treats as independent. It does not decide `Q_stall`, because the deadness test needs the full reachable graph. A `dpor`-found candidate stall state is confirmed by `exh` from that state; otherwise the progress result stays `unknown(not_checked)`.
8. **Monitor**. Per finite execution, the monitor checks:
   - the readiness precondition `Req` at every conjunct LP, and the scope of each occurrence at its decision;
   - the horizon monitors of Allowed (§1.6), on the monitored actor of each entry, frozen at the first process-terminal step (occurrences decided after it have no status) and, for default-scope occurrences, at the TEARDOWN LP;
   - the shape (looper rule, API-level edges).

   Per reachable product state, it runs the **deadness test** of §1.8: an actor is dead for i iff it is not terminated and no non-excluded reachable state enables it, on the visited product graph with the states excluded for i made sinks (under σ_R1, with σ_U's enabling relation). The test is computed by backward reachability over that graph, so it is available in `exh` only.
   - **Censoring (transitive).** A censored actor is treated as able to perform every Φ effect of its `Sk^cert` region beyond the cut on every object and location that region names: writes, release, give, put, flush, spawn, exit and object deletion. A **stranded** actor (σ_U∞; its only σ_U transitions are unpaired time-based ones, §1.8) is treated as able to perform every Φ effect of its region after its stale test, as if the test had succeeded. An actor is potentially enabled if some reachable state contains a censored, stranded or potentially enabled actor whose effects could enable it (write its awaited predicate's support; release, give, put to or flush an object it waits on; spawn it; exit while it joins). A deadness test on a stranded or potentially enabled actor is undecided: `unknown(unmodelled)` when the closure starts at a stranded actor, `unknown(bound)` otherwise.
   - **Kinds.** A cycle of dead actors over holder-released objects, each acquired without a timeout, is reported as a `deadlock-cycle`. An untimed acquisition of a holder-released, non-recursive object whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it, and is terminated or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release that does not first acquire the object again, is reported as a `leaked-lock`; this is how OSAL F1 (K-LOCKBAL) shows up under M_impl: not as a cycle, but as dead actors behind a lock that no actor can release, including the leaker itself when it re-enters an API that takes the lock. Stall witnesses record their wait-for root set, computed on the wait-for graph of the stall state (§1.8).
   - **Qualifiers.** `terminal-pending` (a process end, i.e. a process-terminal step or a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP, stays reachable from every successor state without B.Input's stop event; relative to the cone), `idle` (every witnessing actor at an idle wait) and `choice-dependent` (non-empty choice support of the wait-for closure, including the path ahead) are computed and recorded.
   - **Exclusions.** States after a process-terminal step are excluded for every instance; states after the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP are excluded for instances of the default scope (§1.8).
9. **Kill criterion** ([J3-M3]). At the S7 review, the explorer is run on the development-set cones that lack a certificate: both App-record windows, the C1-derived lookup, the K-PHASE CF instance and the `SBNSubPipe` K-CAP instance. It runs up to the work-unit cap of D15 on the closed cone of item 1 (D21, decided 2026-10-15: the closed cone), whose expected size is stated there [I]. D15 (decided 2026-10-15): caps are in deterministic work units (predicted nodes, search states, transitions), each cap is frozen after that stage's pilot, no value is chosen here, and exceeding a cap is reported `unknown(budget)`. Two of the five development cones, the C1-derived lookup and the K-PHASE CF instance, exist only in C1's basis (D22, decided: the public BP basis, which has to be built first; the lookup is `inactive` in the default B, App. A.4 step 1). P-D18GATES (b), decided 2026-10-15, separates a **preliminary** from a **final** judgement: a preliminary review may measure the explorer's cost on the cones that exist at that time, including the reduced set (both App-record windows and `SBNSubPipe`, none of them a design case's instance), with that set named; the **final** keep-or-freeze judgement includes the relevant scope of every prepared case of the five (the C1 cones, C2's role-derived pair under P-C2, C3's and C5's instances on the 6.4.x pair under D13), and a case that is not prepared is recorded as a limitation, never filled by the result of other code. If none of these cones is available at the final review, or if `dpor` and `exh` disagree on any small model, the explorer is frozen, and its outputs are then reported only as an ablation. If the explorer is frozen or cut (ADR-026), no route sets a **model** progress result and no refinement is established. Every cell then keeps `progress = unknown(not_checked)`, except σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact (§4.13) and the M_impl stall states that L-DEAD (c)/(d) carry from such a realized `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, and every protected verdict is reported as a safety statement only. If the explorer is frozen or cut, the cells that become unanswerable are published per case with that decision: C1's refinements and progress results, C2's refinements, and the fixed sides of C3 and C5 (§9.3).
10. **Fidelity.** Explorer verdicts carry `model(b)` realization. They rest on O1, O2, O6 and O8, and, where the instance also has a certified or certificate-transferred statement, on O10 (§2.5), and are never labelled realized.

### 4.11 Conformance (`kz conform`)

- **O1/O2, per actor, on every trace** ([J3-M9]). Each actor's decoded event sequence is run through its `Sk^cert` automaton by NFA simulation over the explicit local choices; linear in events × automaton states. A failure gives the shortest rejected prefix.
- **O8, global, from stage S5** (fixes [X3-M5]). The question is whether some model execution's projection equals all per-actor sequences, with outcomes and matched synchronization. It is answered by a **trace-constrained search built on S5's prediction engine** (the trace fixes each actor's sequence; the search looks for a consistent interleaving under Φ semantics), not by the gated explorer. Timestamps are only the search order heuristic ([A §4.8]). The sampling rule is pre-registered (the first trace of each pool, plus every trace with an O1–O7 or O9 failure); the work-unit cap is a deviation. Results: conforming, non-conforming (with the region), undecided. Before S5, O8 is reported as `NOT_RUN`.

### 4.12 Combining evidence into verdicts

1. **Cells.** Each instance has one cell per analysed (σ, M). σ_U first, then σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched] on request; σ_E last. Safety records, classes, reach and progress are computed per cell and per bound (§1.10); refinements are derived from the cells and stored with their bounds.
2. **Universal statements** (`¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i`, and hence the classes `protected`, `fixed-order` and `no-final-status`) need either a certificate (b = ∞ on `Sk^cert`), exhaustive exploration within b (`exh`, or `dpor` labelled unverified), or transfer (§1.8). A certificate proves one statement (L-PR, L-FO); `exh` decides both within b, and also reach and progress. When both routes apply to the same σ and M, their statements must agree within the explored bound: an `exh` witness within b against a certified or certificate-transferred statement goes to obligation O10 (§2.5, item 5), which contests the explored bound; only a realized witness is a contradiction (§4.14). Their classes can still differ, because `exh` may add the second statement within b (e.g. `no-final-status` within b where nothing is reached) while the certificate's class holds at b = ∞; the report shows both bounds. Only `exh` gives `allowed-not-ready` and model progress values; `exh` and `dpor` (the latter unverified, under T-JACM) give model reach values; a realized wait-for fact gives σ_E progress and, by L-DEAD (c)/(d), M_impl stall states. Observations are otherwise supporting evidence only.
3. **`schedule-dependent`** needs a bad witness and a good witness in the same cell and bound, **at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct finite executions with final statuses there. Bad and good witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split`. The record keeps the weaker of the two **per evidence axis** (realization, naturalness). A stall witness is a progress result and never substitutes for a bad witness.
4. **σ_E cells** come from one of two routes:
   - transfer rule 2: safety statements with b = ∞, only from M_impl or M-agreement;
   - **natural realized witnesses on both sides at the same occurrence ordinal**, i.e. two distinct natural runs, which give `schedule-dependent(σ_E)`; natural witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)`.

   Further rules for σ_E cells:
   - Realized witnesses with held or gated naturalness give `σ_E(held)`.
   - Frequencies (`n of N`) are pooled only over identical pool keys (B hash, plan hash, stratum) and are labelled `σ_E(plan P)`. Equivalence to unobserved runs is claimed only on the measured uninstrumented marginals (§8.6) (fixes [X3-M2]).
   - Absence of bad witnesses is written "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown" (fixes [X1-M11]).
   - The σ_E progress result follows §1.10.
5. **Contradictions** trigger O3/O4/O9: the rule is suspended, a model-fault record is written, the affected verdicts become `unknown(model_fault)`, and the loop ends for this Φ version. An M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary gives `portability-hazard-evidence` instead (§1.4).
   - **What is a contradiction.** Evidence from a run against a statement that comes from a certificate or from a transfer of one, in the same (σ, M) and **within the statement's bound**: a realized bad witness against `¬Bad_i`; a realized good witness against `¬OK_i`; an observed decided not-ready occurrence (TOLERATED and PENDING included) against `¬NR_i` (O4(b)). A witness outside the bound does not contradict a bounded statement.
   - **Model–certificate disagreements (O10, §2.5).** An `exh` bad witness against a certified or certificate-transferred `¬Bad_i`, an `exh` good witness against such a `¬OK_i`, or an `exh` decided not-ready occurrence against such a `¬NR_i`; also an `exh` result in one σ against an `exh` statement transferred from another σ (which L-CONT (a) says cannot happen). The disagreement is reported and the explorer's bound becomes `contested`. A directed check follows: (i) re-explore with L1–L3 switched off; if the witness disappears, the reduction is at fault and is disabled for the instance; (ii) otherwise re-run `kz proofcheck` on the certificate; if it rejects, the certificate is withdrawn; (iii) otherwise the explored bound stays `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`), the certificate's own record at b = ∞ is unchanged, the certificate record gets a persistent contested mark for this Φ version (`KZ_CERT_FIELDS`, §2.3), which the census reads, and a directed confirmation of the witness is planned. No component of any other bound is lowered. No Φ rule is suspended and the loop does not end unless the witness is realized, which makes it a contradiction (above). A test pairs a PROTECTED certificate built on a writer set with one disabler removed with an `exh` run that finds a TOLERATED or PENDING occurrence, and checks that O10 fires.
   - **What is not.** A stall witness against any class is **not** a contradiction, because the classes are safety statements.
   - **Model-bound conflicts.** A realized bad or good witness whose conforming model prefix lies within b, met by an `exh` statement `¬Bad_i` or `¬OK_i` within b; a realized stall witness whose conforming model prefix lies within b, met by `no-stall-within(b)` in the same cell; a reach proof whose witness lies within b, met by `unreached(b)`; or a realized prefix to a model stall state after which a witnessing actor is observed to proceed. Each shows that the closed model is too strict. It is reported as O6 (extended, §2.5), and the affected component at that bound moves to the top element `contested`, rendered `unknown(model_fault)` (§4.14). The class is unaffected, and this is not a suspension.
6. **Three judgements** (§1.6). Harm (caller level) comes from the caller-level Allowed and the observed or model continuation; with the caller-level Allowed unspecified, harm is `unspecified_by_reference`, and route/capacity verdicts carry LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, except where the Allowed is unspecified because a must-deliver `appreq` conflicts with a `caller` entry (then `not-ready-unjudged`, §1.10). `fw_doc` and `fw_conf` (framework level) come from the op's outcome map, O4(a), the §3.9 oracles and the trace. The consequence (observed) comes from the continuation and the oracles in every case, including an unspecified Allowed. No rule combines the three.
7. **M levels**: M_api always; M_impl for instances whose cone is M_impl-relevant. Disagreements give `portability-hazard` or `implementation-defect-candidate`.
8. **Perturbation qualifier** (fixes [X3-M2]): every realized witness carries its computed perturbation ratio (§4.13); no threshold is applied.
9. **Progress** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1), per cell and bound:
   - it comes from `exh` (§4.10 item 7) or from a realized hang with a wait-for fact (§4.13);
   - it never comes from a certificate, from a prediction, or by transfer between σ, except stall states of kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock` (L-DEAD (c), (d));
   - its default is `unknown(not_checked)`; K-TEARDOWN instances have `unknown(teardown_scope)`;
   - dependent progress results are attached to their root by progress chains, and the root is what is reported.

### 4.13 Confirmation on the real binary (`kz hold`, `kz gate`)

**Which verdicts get controlled confirmation** ([A §4.9]):

| Verdict | Action |
|---|---|
| fixed-order (certificate) | passive observation (reach and outcome). A good counterpart exists only under a changed basis B′ (e.g. another core order): a configuration-change experiment, labelled as such. For R2, such a basis is expected to withdraw the certificate without a protected verdict in its place (App. A.1 step 15) |
| protected | none beyond conformance for the safety statement |
| progress stall witness (`model(b)`) | directed confirmation only for **confirmable** witnesses (kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, not idle, not terminal-pending), where a hold can realize the prefix to the stall state. The oracle is the wait-for fact (below), never a timeout. The run ends with a harness kill and a normalization boot. The result is labelled `realized` with its naturalness, and `derived` when the schedule was constructed. If the stall is not realized, the witness stays `model(b)` with "not realized in run R"; this is not a refutation. It is a refutation (O6, contested bound) only if the prefix is realized and a witnessing actor is then observed to proceed. Witnesses of kind `other` (waits on semaphores, queues, messages or predicates) cannot be confirmed by this oracle and stay model candidates |
| schedule-dependent / timeout-dependent | directed confirmation of the bad witness; if natural runs show only the bad order, also of the good witness |
| ordinal-split | passive observation of the ordinals concerned; no directed confirmation, because no single occurrence is claimed to depend on the schedule. A same-ordinal pair found later moves the class to schedule-dependent |
| no-final-status | no confirmation of the class, which says that no reached occurrence has a final status; reach and the progress result are reported with it, and a confirmable stall witness is confirmed as in the progress row |
| unknown(no_decision_route) with `¬OK_i` shown | directed attempts at a bad witness in that cell (a run or a prediction that completes a not-ready occurrence's horizon to its REJECT), as evidence; the class becomes fixed-order only when a bad witness is found in that cell |
| unknown(model_gap, writer_incomplete, conformance) | directed attempts at predicted witnesses, as evidence; the class stays unknown until the model is fixed |
| unknown(budget) | F1 PCT runs only if the F1 arm is added later (D3: later); otherwise remains unknown |
| LOSS-UNDER-ORDER | observation through SB counters and put outcomes |

**Gate placement** ([J2-10], [J3-M10]). To force "event `a` of thread A after event `b` of thread B", hold A at a point before its first event of the pair where:
1. **A's program-lock set is empty**, by the observed lock events and the static lockset (incl. the ES lock, the SB lock, TBL locks, and the window between a `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask` lock and its delete);
2. the point is **not** inside a timebase callback (`handler_mutex` held), a binsem internal section (the internal 2 s `timedlock`, [R01 §6.3]), a signal handler, or before `OS_TaskPrepare` registration;
3. the point is not inside an OSAL RESERVED window, except in the **follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate experiment** (App. A.4 step 5; P-C1HOLD, decided 2026-10-15), which is not part of C1's validation and is not counted among the five cases;
4. the joint debug-register allocator (§4.7) has a free slot on A.

If no point qualifies, the rule is applied symmetrically: hold B before its enabling event.

**Attach strategy** (fixes [X3-m1]): threads that exist at plan time are seized individually (`PTRACE_SEIZE`). A gate on a thread created later (e.g. App. A.4's provider) needs auto-attach at clone (`PTRACE_O_TRACECLONE` on its creator); each new thread then stops once at creation. That stop is an unmeasured perturbation inside the app-start windows; S6 measures its cost, and runs that use it record it.

**Release** is event-based: when the awaited event (with its outcome) appears in the live stream of `kz observe`, or when the other party is blocked or has exited ([POL] L22).

**Watchdog** = the smallest reference timeout among the waits that the hold can make expire, read from Φ and B: e.g. `CFE_PLATFORM_ES_STARTUP_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT_MSEC` for a held startup thread whose child polls `GetTaskFunction`; `CFE_PLATFORM_CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC` for core serialization; **`CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` when the held schedule can lead to a restart request** (the requester aborts after it, §3.5); for CF, the retry count and the delay read from the selected CF version (never fixed to 25 or 100 ms for every version). The retry count times the delay is not by itself taken as a safe wall-clock waiting limit (P-C1HOLD, decided 2026-10-15): the observation effect and the real end time are checked in a pilot, and the wall-clock watchdog and the stop conditions are recorded before the main experiment. If the watchdog fires, the result is `inconclusive(await-timeout)`. A failure made by a hold is told apart from a natural run, and an observed timeout alone never decides a permanent deadlock. If a time-based transition (§1.8) of a cone actor occurs during the hold, or after it and before the witness's deciding events, as shown by the prefix probes of §4.7 item 6 (c) (timeout outcomes of non-periodic timed waits, retry exhaustion branches, forced kills, the return causes of OSAL-internal timed waits), the run's naturalness is `timeout-exercised`; periodic waits do not count.

**Normalization after abnormal exits** [D] (fixes [X3-M3]). A run that did not end with the console line "Exiting cFE with POWERON Reset status" (`cfe_psp_support.c:64`) — a panic, a PROCESSOR restart, a requester abort, a harness kill of a deadlock — leaves the SysV segments and a boot record that makes the next boot a PROCESSOR reset (`cfe_psp_start.c:411–434`). After every such run:
1. one **unobserved normalization boot** is made, ending with the CI POWERON restart already used as E11(a) (`.github/workflows/build-run-app-reusable.yml` L209–214; [BM] L29), which deletes the segments (`cfe_psp_support.c:62–69`) without any manual cleaning ([R2g]'s "not cleaned (policy)" practice is kept);
2. the persist snapshot after it must equal the canonical S0 (content hash, no mtimes); this is the check, because a POWERON restart does not reset every persisted file, file cache or shared file system (D17 (a), decided 2026-10-15);
3. the normalization boot is excluded from every pool;
4. cold runs (the first after a VM reboot) form their own stratum and are never pooled with warm runs.

**Mechanisms.**
- **F0 `kz hold`** (default): `PTRACE_SEIZE` by root; an **execution breakpoint in a debug register** (DR0–DR3, set through `PTRACE_POKEUSER`) at the gate, so the text is never modified ([B §4.6]). Only that thread stops. On release, the debug register is cleared. Measured unit costs: #DB trap ≈34 µs; gdb breakpoint ≈203 µs ([R07 §4.7]). The holder's stop/resume cost and its interaction with uprobes and signals (the timebase's real-time signal, `SIGUSR1`) are **unmeasured** S6 exit items.
- **F1 `kz gate`** (only when the arm is added later, D3): handwritten `__wrap_*` gates for the synchronization-skeleton APIs, in one C99 file built with the cFS flags; the controller enforces witness schedules or PCT. PCT gives probability at least `1/(n·k^(d−1))` per run for a depth-d bug ([V-abs; mechanism P]), with n and k measured per run and d increased from 1 within the PI-set run budget (a deviation), relative to the gated scheduling points.

**Oracles.** `kz trace` re-evaluates the confirmation trace with the same readiness specifications: the target use must show the not-ready state at its LP and the predicted outcome, and harm follows Allowed. For deadlocks and lock leaks (K-LOCKBAL) the oracle is a **wait-for fact**, never a timeout: the blocked thread's state, read from `/proc/<tid>/wchan` or futex tracepoints, while the lock's last acquirer has already returned from its API ([C §4.9]). A wait-for fact is also the oracle of a realized progress witness (§1.10). It establishes permanence only in two cases: a cycle over holder-released objects acquired without a timeout (`deadlock-cycle`); or an untimed acquisition of a non-recursive lock whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock (`leaked-lock`, the observable form of §1.8's holder condition). A holder that has returned and still runs elsewhere gives a `leaked-lock` only together with the static condition of §1.8 (no `Sk^cert` path to a release that does not first acquire the lock).

**Labels.** `realized` with naturalness `held-F0` | `gated-F1` | `timeout-exercised`; flag `derived` (kept apart from original-case results, [R06 S10]; [LED] X5); `refuted` (the order was realized and the outcome was good: the model or summary that produced the witness is wrong for that prefix; the witness is withdrawn and reported under O6, and, for an `exh` witness, the explored bound becomes `contested` (§4.12 item 5); no rule is suspended. A progress witness is refuted only if its prefix is realized and a witnessing actor then proceeds; a hold that does not realize the prefix refutes nothing (§1.10)); `inconclusive`.

**Perturbation accounting per run** (fixes [X3-M2]): holds (count, site, duration); probe hits by cost class; observer CPU time and placement; the uninstrumented-observable measures of §8.6; and, **per witness**, the **perturbation ratio** = (probe and hold time added on the involved threads between the two deciding events, from the run's own hits × the S1 class costs, plus hold durations) / (the code-derived separation that protects the other order: e.g. the 50 ms poll delay of `CFE_ES_GetTaskFunction`, `cfe_es_apps.c:559–596`; the ~100 ms core-serialization spacing, [R2g §2]; SCH_LAB's first tick at least 1 s after init). The ratio is reported with no threshold.

### 4.14 The feedback loop, its reproducibility and its termination (`kz loop`)

```
fix (B, Φ, tool);  K0 := static closure of B;  census0 := kz-static static(K0)
i := 0
repeat
   plan_i   := kz-static plan(open instances of census_i ∪ mandatory sync skeleton ∪ A-COMM probes)
   runs_i   := kz observe(B, plan_i) × n_i      # n_i: deviation (decided per purpose at S1 and frozen, D14); run IDs; normalization boots
   check O1–O5, O9 (+ O8 on the sample, from S5; + O6 (b) and O10 on the explorer's records, from S7); a suspension (O3/O4/O9) ENDS the loop for this Φ version, while O6 (b) and O10 only contest a bound
   K_{i+1}  := K_i ⊔ keys, targets and paths learned from runs_i                  # F1, F2
   census_{i+1} := kz-static static(K_{i+1}) ⊔ kz trace(runs_i)
                   [⊔ kz predict(runs_i)          from S5]
                   [⊔ kz explore(K_{i+1})          from S7, if not frozen]
   [for each witness w: π_w := kz hold/gate(B, holdplan(w)); census_{i+1} ⊔= kz trace(π_w)   from S6]  # F3
   write loop-i.kz: run IDs, B/plan/Φ/tool hashes, S0 hashes, deltas, obligation results, work units
   i := i + 1
until state(census_i) = state(census_{i-1}) and K_i = K_{i-1}
report the fixed point together with its run set ∪_i runs_i
```

- **Staged availability** (fixes [X3-M5]): at S4 the loop runs with `static` and `trace` only (O1–O7, O9); `predict` joins at S5 (and O8), `hold` at S6, `explore` at S7 if it passes its kill criterion.
- **The fixed-point test** (fixes [X1-m8]) compares `state(census)`. Per instance and per (σ, M) cell and bound, it compares the fields of the `BOUNDS` entry (§2.3): the safety record (statements with their routes, the decision route of `¬OK_i`, and the set of ordinals of the bad and of the good witnesses, read from the witness ranges), hence the class; the refinements; reach; the progress value with its set of clauses, kind, qualifiers, reason, harm and wait-for root set; the flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`; the certificate's contested mark; the provider axis; the **set** of realization and naturalness values present; the key resolution; and K. It excludes counts (`n of N`), pool keys, run IDs and plan hashes, because those change every iteration.
- **Lattice.** Per cell and per bound, the components are ordered as follows; they are independent across bounds.
  - **Safety record.** Each statement (`¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i`, `¬NR_i`) and the decision route of `¬OK_i` move from unset to set; witness sets grow by inclusion. The derived classes are therefore ordered `unknown < protected, fixed-order < no-final-status` and `unknown < ordinal-split < schedule-dependent`, with `unknown(no_decision_route)` (¬OK_i without a decision route) below fixed-order and no-final-status, and `contested` above every class of that bound (stored as `CONTESTED`); `inactive` is static. A certified (or certificate-transferred) statement and a contradicting realized witness at the same bound are a contradiction, handled by O3/O4 and a suspension; it is never a lattice step. An `exh` witness against such a statement moves the **explored** bound to the top `contested` (O10); the certificate's b = ∞ record is not lowered, and its persistent contested mark keeps the census from re-emitting a different value for the explored bound within the Φ version. An `exh`-only statement met by a contradicting realized witness within its bound moves that bound's record to the top `contested` (O6, model-bound conflict).
  - **Refinements and provider** move only from unset to set.
  - **Reach.** At b = ∞: `assumed < proved`. At a bounded b: `assumed < model(b)` and `assumed < unreached(b)`, both below the top `contested` (stored as `CONTESTED`, §2.3). A reach proof whose witness lies within b, met by `unreached(b)`, moves that bound to `contested` (O6). A proof outside b leaves `unreached(b)` in place, because the two bounds are kept apart.
  - **Progress.** `unknown(·) < stall-witness` and `unknown(·) < no-stall-within(b)`, both below the top `contested` (stored as `P_CONTESTED`, rendered `unknown(model_fault)`). A realized stall within b met by `no-stall-within(b)`, or a realized prefix to a model stall state after which a witnessing actor proceeds, moves that bound to `contested` (O6). `stall-witness[model b] < stall-witness[realized]`. A stall witness never moves the class.
  - **Evidence sets** are ordered by inclusion; **keys** `⊤ < symbolic < concrete`; **K** by inclusion.

  Within one Φ version, every non-suspending iteration moves up or stays, so L-LOOP's termination argument is unchanged. A test checks that no contested bound is lowered again within one Φ version.
- **Suspensions are non-monotone**, so a suspension ends the loop for that Φ version; the fix needs a human Φ edit, which produces a new Φ hash and a new loop.
- **Termination**: under fixed B and Φ, the universe of sites, keys (from code constants and finite tables), instances and evidence values is finite, and every non-final iteration strictly moves up the lattice or stops [I].
- **Reproducibility** ([J3-M7]; fixes [X3-M10]): every analysis is a pure function of stored artifacts (sorted iteration, deterministic search order, work-unit budgets, no wall-clock dependence), so re-running from stored traces reproduces the verdict store byte for byte; this is a test. A result produced under a wall-clock cap (only if D15 ever allows one) is labelled and excluded from the replay test. σ_E frequencies are pooled only over identical pool keys. The fixed point is reported **with its run set**.

### 4.15 Complexity and bounds

| Stage | Cost | Bounds and their sources |
|---|---|---|
| Closure and extraction | linear in TUs; [M] 10 s parse, 4.4 s bitcode, 0.35 s core link | — |
| S3a writer extraction | linear in AST size [I]; measured at S3a | — |
| S3b points-to (if built) | Andersen worst case cubic ([R05 §2.5]) | work-unit cap (deviation) |
| Skeletons | worst case **exponential in call depth** under full call-string inlining; per-(actor, function) summary fallback when the work-unit cap is reached (deviation) | poll loops summarized (L1); retry loops at effective constant bounds (CF: effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`; OSAL 5, `osapi-idmap.c:475–480`; `GetTaskFunction` ≤ 21, `cfe_es_apps.c:570–595`) |
| Message provenance | least fixpoint over handlers; at most one round per handler | — |
| MustBefore | `O(N·R·(d̄+w̄)·R/64)` plus cached PWB queries | — |
| Certificates | reachability per frontier; derivations | — |
| Trace HB and readiness checking | O(N × number of actors) | — |
| Prediction | NP-complete in general (SyncP); exponential worst case | work-unit cap (deviation); anytime curve |
| Exploration | `exh`: linear in reachable **product** states (model state with the instance monitor, §1.8) and their edges, exponential in the model; the deadness tests and the qualifiers add one backward reachability per actor and per instance over the visited graph, with excluded states as sinks; `dpor`: exponential worst case, with the extended dependence of §4.10 item 3; **no "one execution per class" claim** | h not chosen (results per completed h); work-unit cap (deviation); preemption bound none by default (BPOR and sweep if used); restart depth 1 per documented request |
| Conformance | O1/O2 linear; O8 a search (from S5) | work-unit cap (deviation) |
| Confirmation | one run per witness, plus a normalization boot after abnormal exits; PCT ≥ `1/(n·k^(d−1))` per run | watchdogs from platform constants; PCT depth d increased from 1 within the run budget (deviation) |
| Feedback loop | ≤ the lattice height under (B, Φ) | runs per iteration: deviation (D14) |

---

## 5. Observation and instrumentation design

### 5.1 Mechanisms and fidelity classes

Fidelity classes are those of [R07 §7]: F0 = the unmodified reference binary, run by the same cFS user with the same command, observed by a separate root process that attaches hooks to it; F1 = same sources and compiler, link or code-generation option changed; F2 = sources rewritten; F3 = another compiler.

| Mechanism | Class | Role | Unit cost ([M] [R07 §4.7, §5.1] unless stated) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| perf-PMU uprobes + eBPF, root-side, own raw loader, **attach cookies** | F0 | **production observation** of API-boundary and function-boundary events in `core-cpu1` and app `.so` files | 0.57 µs/hit at entry+4 (emulated `push %rbp`); 31.7 µs at `endbr64`, at `mov %rsp,%rbp` (+5) or any non-emulated instruction; BPF body **unmeasured** | callee-side probes also see the 88 intra-TU API sites that `--wrap` misses |
| **perf-sample uprobe backend** (sample flags `PERF_SAMPLE_TID`, `PERF_SAMPLE_TIME`, `PERF_SAMPLE_CPU`, `PERF_SAMPLE_REGS_USER`; `PERF_RECORD_LOST` for loss) | F0 | **zero-install bring-up backend** (fixes [X3-m2]); fd-scoped, cleans itself up | same trap path; sample cost unmeasured | measured to work without tracefs and with registration before the file is mapped ([R07] L340) |
| tracefs dynamic uprobe events | F0 | optional only; if used, the rules of §5.4 apply | same trap path | event definitions are kernel-global and outlive a private mount namespace [I] |
| HW write watchpoints (perf breakpoint PMU, per task) | F0 | readiness-field stores with no API boundary | ≈34 µs/hit; shares the 4 debug registers with gates (§4.7) | only for fields written a handful of times per boot; write-only except in declared experiments |
| ptrace + debug-register execution breakpoints | F0 | confirmation holds (`kz hold`) | #DB trap ≈34 µs; gdb breakpoint ≈203 µs; purpose-built holder **unmeasured** | no Yama; root on a uid-1000 process works |
| sched/syscall tracepoints | F0 | clone cross-check; system-call observation under stated conditions where the §8.3 analysis needs one (`baselines/availability.md` decision A10 is open) | not measured | exist on this kernel |
| `ld --wrap` gates | F1 + deviation | PCT and many-point schedules, **only if the arm is added later (D3: later)**; perturbation group W | handoff 1.1 µs same CPU / 10.5 µs across CPUs | apps relinked; misses intra-TU calls |
| Source probes | F2 | not planned | — | 12.4% of API sites are inside macro expansions ([R07 §4.6]) |
| Clang-built execution | F3 | never | — | — |
| `LD_PRELOAD` / `LD_AUDIT` | — | rejected: cannot interpose symbols defined in the PIE executable; refused by `run_cfs.sh:154–158` | — | [R07 §4.3] |
| gdb | F0 | reference observation only (the R2 records; ran with ASLR disabled, [R2g]) | +0.6 to +1.1 s to OPERATIONAL ([R2g §1]) | — |

The observer runs as root next to the README-mode `ubuntu` process. This needs a new root-side hook in `env/run_cfs.sh` (PI decision D1, [R07 §9 item 1]). **D1 was approved on 2026-10-15 with a corrected explanation.** The cFS user and the reference binary are kept. F0 is not read as "only time changes": that is not claimed, not even as an assumption. The observer's traps, CPU use, scheduling and any hold are effects to be checked, by the observation-effect comparison of §8.6, and an internal event that cannot be compared with an unobserved reference run gets no conclusion of "no effect". Every run records the cFS process's actual user ID, command, priority and scheduling policy, environment and initial state. Observation and control are different functions of the root-side tools, and runs are labelled accordingly: an observation-only run, and a run that uses a hold, stop or release (a controlled run, §4.13).

### 5.2 Probe placement and return-value capture

- **Function entries at entry+4** [D]. gcc emits `endbr64; push %rbp; mov %rsp,%rbp` at `-O0`; checked on nine core functions and one app entry ([B §A.2]) and on `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` ([X3, here]). At entry+4 the argument registers still hold the arguments.
- **Locks and queues without return probes** ([B §5.5]; [J3-M4]). Mutex order from `objseq` at `OS_MutSemGive` entry; queue edges from put entry and the get outcome where an edge or a readiness specification needs it.
- **Return values only at readiness use sites**, where an outcome decides an edge, and at the prefix sites of §4.7 item 6. The capture mechanism is **chosen per site by measurement in stage S1**:
  - (R-a) uretprobe on the callee. This kernel has the uretprobe syscall trampoline (`__x64_sys_uretprobe`, `arch_uretprobe_trampoline` in `/proc/kallsyms`, [X3, here]), so R-a may be cheaper than the #DB class [I];
  - (R-b) uprobe at the caller's instruction after the `call` (typically `mov %eax,-0x..(%rbp)`), inferred to be in the 31.7 µs class like the measured `mov` at +5 [I];
  - (R-c) uprobe at the callee's epilogue `leave`: class unmeasured;
  - (R-d) **deferred read**: out-pointer targets captured at entry are dereferenced at the same thread's next probe hit; valid only where the static check shows that no event of that thread between the return and the next probe can write the target. Used at sites that ignore the status, e.g. `CFE_ES_GetAppID(&CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` (fixes [X3-m3]);
  - (**R-e**) **the first emulated instruction after the call while the value is still in a frame slot** (fixes [X3-m3]). At `-O0`, gcc spills the result and tests it: e.g. after `call CFE_ES_GetAppID` in `CFE_EVS_TaskInit`, `mov %eax,-0x124(%rbp)`, `cmpl $0x0,-0x124(%rbp)`, `je` ([M] [X3, here], `0x2aca1`–`0x2acae`). Branches are emulated by x86 uprobes (`branch_emulate_op` present), so a probe on the `je` that reads the slot at `rbp-0x124` is plausibly in the 0.57 µs class [I].

  Each planned probe records its cost class. No overhead claim is made before S1.
- **Internal points** (publication stores not at an API boundary): an entry probe on the out-of-line local copy of the `static inline` helper, bound by DWARF compile unit (copies exist, §3.17); otherwise a write watchpoint; otherwise the nearest instruction of a cheap class.
- **Capture sites** (value conjuncts, §4.8 step 4): the call-entry probe of the consuming API usually brackets the capture point closely enough; the plan adds a probe at the capture load only where a decision needs it.
- **Site validator.** Every planned address is disassembled with LLVM MC and looked up in the calibration table (§5.10). Expensive sites are reported, never accepted silently.
- **App `.so` files: register by inode, system-wide, before launch** (fixes [X3-M1], [X3-m4]). Perf-PMU uprobes are attached to (file inode, offset) for every process, before `core-cpu1` starts; this was shown to count hits for a file mapped after registration ([R07] L340, tested on the main binary only; S1 tests it on an app `.so`). The v1 fallback (holding the startup thread at each `OS_ModuleLoad` return) is **removed from the default design**: it delays app loading, the very variable that changed the init interleaving in [BM] (849 ms load span instead of 2.2–3.3 ms; 10 apps initialized before the last was loaded). If it is ever used, its traces are excluded from σ_E pools and say so.

### 5.3 eBPF data path

- **Site keying by attach cookie, never by runtime address** [D] (fixes [X3-M1]). ASLR is on (`randomize_va_space` = 2, [M] [X3, here]; `mmap_rnd_bits` 28, [R07] L77); `core-cpu1` is a PIE and apps are `dlopen`ed at random bases. A map keyed by the probed runtime IP would have to be filled per module after mapping, racing first execution (all 15 apps load within 2.2–3.3 ms, [BM] L278). Instead each site's **attach cookie** equals its site id:
  - option 1: one perf uprobe fd per site, linked with `BPF_LINK_CREATE` carrying the cookie;
  - option 2: one `uprobe_multi` link per binary with per-offset cookies.

  The program reads the cookie with `bpf_get_attach_cookie`. The kernel symbols for both options exist (`bpf_perf_link_attach`, `bpf_get_attach_cookie_pe`, `bpf_get_attach_cookie_uprobe_multi`, [M] [X3, here]; presence only). S1 probes both functionally.
- **One generic BPF program per phase** (entry, point, return, watch). The cookie indexes a **site-descriptor array map**: registers to copy, stack slots and bounded dereferences, whether to read a bounded string (bounds `OS_MAX_API_NAME` 20 and `OS_MAX_PATH_LEN` 64 from the built `osconfig.h`, [B §A.2]), whether to assign an `objseq`, whether to capture a user stack. Descriptors are **data**; there is no per-site code generation ([C §5.3]).
- **Within verifier limits** [I] (fixes [X3-M1]): `pt_regs` is read only at constant offsets, so the program copies a fixed register set at constant offsets and then selects with bounded code; stack slots and dereferences are read with `bpf_probe_read_user`; strings with a constant maximum.
- **No libbpf** ([R07 §2.3]): helper prototypes are written by hand against the UAPI `linux/bpf.h` (present, [J3 §2.4]); the BPF ELF object (system clang-18 `-target bpf`) is parsed with glibc `<elf.h>`. Neither needs an install.
- **Per-thread `tseq`** in task-local storage (or a map keyed by tid). **Per-object `objseq`** by atomic add on a per-object map entry, assigned only at in-critical-section points [I].
- **Buffers**: per-CPU ring buffers (or a perf event array). Nothing is shared with the traced program in user space; the traced threads make no extra system calls and take no user-space lock ([R06 F10]).
- **Stacks**: `bpf_get_stack` with `BPF_F_USER_STACK` (frame-pointer unwinding of gcc `-O0` code), at sites that need on-behalf frames; depth from the maximum static call depth, capped by `perf_event_max_stack`; truncation is flagged.
- **Feasibility** [U]. Raw `bpf()` load and attach to a uprobe were probed ([R07 §4.7]). Ring buffers, task storage, `bpf_get_stack`, attach cookies and `uprobe_multi` were **not** probed. S1 tests each one. The fallback is the perf-sample backend.

### 5.4 The tracefs option (only if used)

tracefs is no longer the bring-up path (fixes [X3-m2]). If it is used anyway: a **dedicated tracing instance**; an explicit cross-CPU trace clock set for that instance (the default "local" clock is per CPU [I]); every event definition removed at exit; the use recorded in RUNMETA as a **host-state change**. Per-thread order then comes from timestamps under assumption **A-CLK-PO** (clocksource `tsc` with `constant_tsc` and `nonstop_tsc` on all four vCPUs, [B §A.2]). Cross-thread order comes only from matched protocol keys; lock order inferred from timestamps is `clock-ordered` evidence, excluded from VC, VC_R, certificates and prediction constraints ([J2-4]).

### 5.5 Event record and trace container

The raw record `kz_raw_ev` (64 bytes, §2.3) carries a timestamp (evidence only), tid, `tseq`, site id (= cookie), phase, flags, `objseq` and four decoded values. Strings and stacks go into side sections keyed by `(tid, tseq)`.

A `.kzt` container has these sections: `SITES` (copied from the plan, with the binary build-id, inode and offsets); `RAW_EVENTS`, `STRINGS`, `STACKS`; `LOSS`; `RUNMETA` (B hash, plan hash, S0 hash, stratum, kernel version, backend, holds, observer CPU time and placement, harness version); `PERTURBATION` (per-run accounting, §4.13).

`kz trace` decodes `RAW_EVENTS` into typed `EVENTS`: actor instance, operation, resource, outcome, identity quadruple, template link and per-conjunct LP intervals.

### 5.6 Loss accounting

Ring-buffer reservation failures increment per-CPU, per-site counters (perf backend: `PERF_RECORD_LOST`). Per-thread `tseq` gaps identify the lost records of each actor. Any loss marks the affected actor's interval as incomplete, and every verdict whose evidence depends on that interval becomes `unknown(loss)`. **Loss is never treated as the absence of events.** Buffer size is set from a calibration run's high-water mark and recorded ([B §5.2]).

### 5.7 Identity bootstrap without `GetAppID`

No in-process lookup is ever made by the instrumentation; all joins are offline. An instrument that called an OSAL name lookup could trigger the F1 lock leak it is meant to detect ([R01 §6.3]); calling `GetAppID` early would itself hit the App-record window ([v0.1] §8.4).

1. Every record carries the kernel tid.
2. Creator side: `OS_TaskCreate` entry (name, entry pointer, priority) and its out-parameter.
3. Child side: `OS_TaskEntryPoint(osal_id)` (exported, `osapi-task.c:124`) binds tid ↔ OSAL id before `OS_TaskPrepare`; `OS_PthreadTaskEntry` (`os-impl-tasks.c:131`) is a `.symtab` fallback ([J3 §2.4]).
4. ES side: `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` arguments (`ParentAppId`), the task-record publication, and the App-record publications (AST-bound sites, §3.17). These joins also check A-OWNER and A-FRESH (O9).
5. Timebase thread: `OS_TimeBase_CallbackThread(timebase_id)` (`osapi-timebase.c:365`); callbacks attributed through `OS_TimerAdd` records.
6. Raw threads by creation site; PSP-set kernel thread names (15 characters, `cfe_psp_start.c:197–201`) are an independent second check.
7. Generations from OSAL and cFE serials; across runs, `(type, name, creator, creation ordinal)`.
8. `svc` frames by symbolizing user stacks against the gcc binary; `stamp` from arguments, using Φ's parameter roles.

### 5.8 Causal tokens versus timestamps

Cross-thread order comes **only** from matched synchronization: `objseq` for mutexes; descriptor plus FIFO for queues; descriptor plus pipe for SB; outcome-implied reads-from for registries; the recorded exit status for polls; OSAL ID for task creation. Semaphore matching uses the conservative join. Where a protocol key is insufficient, a timestamp window may be used as *matching evidence*: the edge is then ambiguous and the evidence weakened. **`CLOCK_MONOTONIC` values never become HB edges** ([R04 §2.1, §4 row 16]; v0.1 §9.3).

### 5.9 Safety rules for observation and control

- eBPF runs in kernel context. It calls no OSAL API, takes no user lock, never prints, and cannot be cancelled mid-record, which removes by construction the in-process hazards of [R01 §6.3].
- What remains is the observer's effect on execution: timing, CPU competition and scheduling, which §8.6 checks and which is not assumed to be only a change of time (D1). A trap inside an OSAL table-locked region lengthens the hold and could consume bounded RESERVED-wait attempts (§3.2: by broadcast **or** by timeout). The planner prefers entry probes outside OSAL internal locks and flags any probe inside an idmap critical section, a timebase callback or the binsem internal section.
- Holds follow §4.13. Observer CPU time and placement are recorded in every run, because the drainer competes for the 4 vCPUs during the startup burst ([BM §1.3] could not separate this effect for a root-side hook).

### 5.10 Cost model and measurement obligations

Per thread `i`: `ΔC_i = Σ_j n_ij · c_j` (v0.1 §11.2); per confirmation run add `Σ_holds (c_hold + hold duration)`. **Six costs are reported separately**: analysis, exploration, recording, control, profile authoring (person-hours), and triage (rater hours, §8.5) ([R06 R4]; [J1-15]; [X3-M7]).

| Quantity | Value | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `c_trap` at entry+4 (`push %rbp`) | 0.57 µs | [M] |
| `c_trap` at `endbr64`, `mov %rsp,%rbp` (+5) or another non-emulated instruction | 31.7 µs | [M] |
| R-b post-call `mov %eax,-0x..(%rbp)` | inferred 31.7 µs class | [I]; S1 |
| R-e emulated branch after the spill | inferred 0.57 µs class | [I]; S1 |
| R-a uretprobe (syscall trampoline present); R-c `leave` | — | unmeasured; S1 |
| `c_bpf` (program body, cookie lookup, string fetch, stack capture) | — | unmeasured; S1 |
| `c_wp` (HW watchpoint hit) | ≈34 µs | [M] |
| `c_hold` (gdb breakpoint) | ≈203 µs | [M]; purpose-built holder and auto-attach at clone unmeasured (S6) |
| ptrace × uprobe and ptrace × signal interactions | — | unmeasured; S6 |
| gate handoff (F1) | 1.1 µs same CPU / 10.5 µs across CPUs | [M] |
| `n_ij` per boot | — | unmeasured for the full sync skeleton; for scale only, from the gdb records: `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` was hit 251 / 257 / 257 times per boot (all callers; 223 / 229 / 229 of them from SB, [R2g §7]); the hit counts of the other probe sites are not known |
| static API call sites | 3,762 | [M] static count ([R07 §4.2]) |

Arithmetic on measured values only, not a prediction [I]: for the one site `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` alone, 251–257 hits at 0.57 µs are about 0.14–0.15 ms per boot, and at 31.7 µs about 8 ms; the cost of the other sites is not known. OPERATIONAL is about 1.6 s ([M] 1613–1636 ms, [R2g §1]). The startup burst (`SBNSubPipe` overflow about 47–49 ms after OPERATIONAL, [BM §1.3]) is where probe time concentrates and where the per-witness perturbation ratio (§4.13) will be largest.

**Calibration table.** S1 measures each instruction class with a micro-benchmark on this VM, by the method of [R07 §4.7]: `push`, `mov`, `leave`, the emulated branch (R-e), uretprobe, the BPF body with and without string and stack capture, cookie lookup, the perf-sample path, and the holder's stop/resume. The table is versioned by kernel. **Group A is re-measured on the kernel in use at S1** (recorded per run; `-fc-v114` in [R07 §1], `-fc-v139` on 2026-10-16, §1.7 Env) before the S1 observation-effect comparison and before any other comparison (D11, decided 2026-10-15); results on an earlier kernel are not pooled with the new ones, and the calibration table is re-measured when the kernel changes.

### 5.11 Untested items and fallbacks

| Item | Status | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| inode-registered uprobes on an app `.so` before `dlopen` | untested on a `.so` ([R07 §9 item 6]); works on the main binary | none needed by design; the startup-thread hold is excluded from pools if ever used |
| attach cookies (`BPF_LINK_CREATE` / `uprobe_multi`) | kernel symbols present; not functionally probed | perf-sample backend (site from the event's perf fd) |
| ring buffer, task storage, `bpf_get_stack` | not probed | perf-sample records with registers and user stack |
| return-capture mechanisms (R-a…R-e) | unmeasured | calibration decides per site; deferred read only where statically valid |
| `c_bpf` with stack capture | unmeasured | capture stacks only at sites that need frames |
| purpose-built ptrace holder cost; auto-attach at clone; ptrace × uprobe interaction | unmeasured | gdb-equivalent bound (≈203 µs per stop) for planning only |
| BPF ELF loading without libbpf | design inference | a maintained public library only if a needed function is shown missing, with the environment change recorded (P-LIBBPF) |
| out-of-line copies of `static inline` helpers at `-O0` | **closed**: copies exist ([X3, here]) | — |
