## 6. Guarantees

All statements are relative to the basis B, the profile Φ (hashed, every rule cited), the model level M, the semantics σ, the bound b, the skeleton view, and the assumptions A below. Terminology follows [R05 §0.5]: "over-approximate relative to M and A", "complete up to b"; never a bare "sound". This is "soundy" in the sense of Livshits et al. (CACM 2015 [V-abs]).

### 6.1 Assumptions A (the soundiness manifest)

| Id | Assumption | Effect if violated | Mitigation / visibility |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Rule validity: every Φ rule, effect and binding is correct for the pinned revision | wrong edges or effects | citations; O3/O4/O7 refute; certificates list the rules used |
| A2 | Binding correctness: Clang sites and AST patterns map to the gcc binary's instructions | wrong probes or anchors | anchor audit; DWARF compile-unit binding |
| A3 | Closure: actors come only from the startup script **selected by B**, the generated object table, `CreateChildTask` sites, OSAL timebase/timer creation and OSAL-internal threads, PSP threads and environment inputs; `dlsym` targets are resolved by configuration; residual indirect calls are `unknown_effect`; code outside the compile DB is out of model | missed actors or targets | closure boot incl. the opened script (O5); `unknown_effect` → T-SPAWN ∅, writer sets DISABLED |
| A4 | Memory model: sequential consistency at event granularity on x86-64 TSO at `-O0`; ad hoc flag synchronization is an assumed-visibility edge | weak-memory effects missed | edges tagged `assumed`; sub-label `assumed-visibility` on every result that uses the assumption; D10 (decided 2026-10-15): no guarantee beyond that scope, and `unknown` where it is unclear that the assumption holds |
| A5 | API-level edges only for shapes; OSAL-internal locks constrain interleavings only under M_impl | — | ablation 11 |
| A6 | Observation completeness for planned sites, with loss accounting | missed events | `tseq` gaps; `unknown(loss)` |
| A7-impl | Φ_L-1 semantics (pthread mutex, condvar incl. timed wait, `sem_*`, `mq_*`, `clock_nanosleep`, thread create/cancel/join) over-approximate kernel, libc and OSAL POSIX behaviour on the M_impl paths at LP granularity (equal-priority FIFO for `mq` is UNVERIFIED, [R04 §4 row 7]; OSAL always uses priority 1) | containment fails | litmus suite on the real OSAL (§9.2); O4 |
| A7-api | Every OSAL primitive **not** covered by M_impl (A10) is linearizable at its LP on the real backend | containment fails for those primitives | litmus tests; O4 |
| A8 | Out of model: inline assembly, signals beyond the PSP exception path and `SIGUSR1` shutdown, ISRs, DMA, weak-memory effects | — | listed |
| A9 | Inputs: B.Input is the complete set of external events of the run (message provenance relies on it) | ground-driven paths latent; handlers wrongly folded | scenario families as B′; O5 (an observed message outside `Prov(p)` is a closure failure) |
| A10 | M_impl covers the OSAL idmap, task, binsem and queue paths only | other primitives at API level in both | listed; A7-api |
| A11 | Table values: from `.tbl` on POWERON; **from the decoded CDS image for critical tables on PROCESSOR resets**; runtime table loads only via B.Input | wrong table contents | table cross-check; CDS decoding in `basis` |
| A12 | One process; SBN peers and cpu2 out of model | cross-instance readiness specifications not covered | listed ([R03 §9 item 8]) |
| A-SKEL | `Sk^cert` over-approximates each actor instance's projected behaviour under B. This supports safety statements. It does not make progress results sound for B, because deadness is preserved neither by over- nor by under-approximation (L-DEAD, G11) | spurious or missing model behaviour | O1, O2, O8 |
| A-WS | Writer-set completeness: S3a (and S3b) find every writer of every support location, given A-TYPE, and every `DECLARED_TRUSTED` guard is correct | unsound T-OBS | O7; trusted entries listed per certificate; O3/O4/O9 refute; ablation 17 |
| A-TYPE | Typed access: every access to a support location goes through its declared type | untyped writes missed | the untyped-write and address-escape listings (§4.2 step 3): each hit disables the location |
| A-EXTRACT | Extraction faithfulness: the facts in `facts.kz` are faithful to B's source as compiled. These are the AST copy chains, writer sites and their transition classification, serialized loop-exit expressions with mod sets and lock regions, the INIT image, constant evaluations and ALLOCS | certificates re-validated on wrong facts | O4, O7, anchor audit, litmus suite, program mutants (§8.5); listed in part (ii) of every trust ledger |
| A-CORE | The shared `libkz-core` components (Φ parser, core-expression evaluator, rule engine, container I/O) are correct | producer and checker share an error | evaluator agreement property test (§2.3), golden tests (S0); listed in part (iii) of every trust ledger |
| A-FRESH | Distinct reservation nodes allocate distinct, previously unused records | wrong key separation | `DECLARED_TRUSTED` per use; O9 |
| A-OWNER | The App record resolved for a task created by `StartAppTask(…, ParentAppId)` is the record of the reservation whose `PendingAppId` flowed to `ParentAppId` | wrong ownership | `DECLARED_TRUSTED` per use; O9 |
| A-COMM | Per predicted witness: every location read in a cone actor's unobserved code that another cone actor may write is observed, constant, or read through a kept outcome | a "predicted" witness infeasible | checked per witness (§4.9); failure → `predicted-modelled` |
| A-API | The abstract trace is faithful enough for SHB/WCP/SyncP soundness to transfer | prefilter proposals wrong | always re-validated (§4.9 step 6) |
| A-CLK-PO | tracefs option only: a cross-CPU-consistent clock for per-thread order | wrong program order | recorded; the eBPF and perf-sample backends remove it |
| A-ZT | σ_R1 only: computation between blocking points takes zero time | σ_R1 orders that depend on execution time are wrong | sub-label on every σ_R1 verdict that depends on a delay |
| L1, L2, L3 | Lemma obligations (await summarization with extracted predicates; removal of a critical section whose lock is not a cone object and is acquired by no other cone actor, and whose body touches nothing in the cone and contains no blocking operation; mover-reduced critical sections) | model behaviour changed, including its stall states | each switchable per instance; ablated; L1's preconditions checked by `kz proofcheck` on the extracted mod sets and lock regions (A-EXTRACT); proofs written before F1; whether L2 and L3 preserve stall states is part of their obligations (litmus LP-DL checks that L2 keeps every section of a lock-order cycle), and L1's obligation includes that the summarized poller is dead exactly where the unsummarized one is stranded on a test whose predicate can never become true (§6.2) |
| T-JACM | The correctness theorem of source-DPOR with sleep sets extended to disabling (JACM 2017), status P | `dpor` results unverified | `dpor` verdicts labelled unverified; `exh` mode available; differential test as validation |

### 6.2 Lemmas and proof sketches

The model at level M consists of per-actor certificate-view skeleton automata plus Φ semantics at level M, taken as the transition systems `TS(B,σ,M)` of §1.8. Every safety lemma below is stated over finite executions `Pref(B,σ_U,M)`. Its proof is an induction over positions in a finite execution, so it uses neither maximality nor fairness.

**Proof status** [D] (PI decision D20, additional completion conditions, 2026-10-15; defined here and nowhere else). Every lemma and every guarantee of §6.2 and §6.3 carries one of three values, listed from the strongest to the weakest:
- `proved`: this specification contains the complete proof. It uses only the definitions of §1 and lemmas that are themselves `proved`; no assumption of §6.1, no extracted fact and no unwritten step.
- `depends-on-stated-assumption`: the argument is complete for the statement under assumptions that §6.1 lists by id (an `A-…` entry, or `T-JACM`); every other lemma it uses is `proved` or `depends-on-stated-assumption`; the table row names the assumptions. The statement holds relative to them only. An assumption can be refuted by observation (§4.5.1 part (iv)) and is never proved by one.
- `unproved`: anything else. The text is a sketch with a step that is only asserted, or the lemma is marked as an obligation, [I] or "to be written", or it uses an `unproved` lemma.

The status of a statement that uses several lemmas is the weakest of theirs (**weakest link**). A guarantee that is a requirement on the implementation (G9, G10, the replay part of G8) is not a theorem: it has no proof, is `unproved`, and is checked by tests, as its row says. A lemma with parts (L-CONT, L-TRANS, L-DEAD) has one status per part.
- **(PS1)** A status is a property of this text. It is raised only by an edit that adds the written proof and updates the table in the same change. A tool run, a passed test, a passed obligation (O1–O10) or an entry in a ledger never raises it, and any review that finds a gap lowers it. The statuses in the table at the end of this section are the author's classification of this revision; no independent review is recorded yet [I], and V0-P (§9.3) is where one is recorded.
- **(PS2)** A lemma that a trust ledger or §9.3 lists only as an obligation is `unproved`. No verdict, report, ledger rendering, README or paper sentence prints "proved" for a lemma, or "verified" or "certified" for a statement that rests on one, unless the lemma's status is `proved`.
- **(PS3)** Every certificate, every certificate-based verdict cell and every trust ledger carries the proof status of its statements: the weakest status among the `LEMMA` entries of the part (iii) it used (`KZ_CERT.proof_status`, `KZ_VERDICT_CELL.proof_status`, `KZ_TRUST.proof`, §2.3). Reports render it next to the safety statement and name the lemmas that are not `proved`, e.g. "fixed-order — ¬OK_i, deciding (certificate, b = ∞); lemma status: unproved (L-MB, L-ONCE, L-PWB, L-FO, L1)". "Accepted by `kz proofcheck`" is always followed by "relative to its trusted base" and this status.
- **(PS4)** `proved` is a status of a lemma, not of a verdict. A certificate whose lemmas are all `proved` is still relative to the extracted facts, the skeleton, the closure lists, Φ, `libkz-core` and the assumptions in its ledger; the status never turns a statement about the model into a statement about the binary.

**L-MB (MustBefore soundness)** [proof status: `unproved`]. For every finite execution `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, every node `n`, every occurrence `n_k` of `n` in `ρ` and every `a ∈ MB(n)`, an occurrence of `a` precedes `n_k` in `ρ`. The statement is conditioned on occurrences. It says nothing about finite executions in which `n` does not occur, which is why no certificate built from MB facts can establish progress.

*Sketch.* Induction on the position of `n_k` in `ρ`; the fixpoint equations hold for the computed MB. Case by the equation that kept `a` in MB(n):
- **Base cases.** Entries with `MB = ∅` contain nothing to prove (fixes [X1-m2]: no empty meet yields ⊤).
- **(T-PO)** The actor reached `n_k` from some `Sk^cert` predecessor occurrence `p_j`, which is earlier: in `Sk^cert` every loop is either unrolled exactly to a reference bound or star-closed, so **every** actual predecessor of `n_k` (also in any iteration of a message loop) is a skeleton predecessor (A-SKEL; this is where the h-cut of v1 broke the argument, [X1-C1]). Terminal ops have no successors. Since `a ∈ MB(p) ∪ {p}`, the hypothesis on `p_j` gives an earlier occurrence of `a`.
- **(T-SPAWN)** An actor's entry is preceded by one of its spawn sites (A3: the list is complete). `a` is in the meet over all spawn sites.
- **(T-OBS)** `o_k` observed `Pred_code` true; by L1, the observation is equivalent to an atomic test at the await's success point, and `Pred_code ⇒ C`. `C` was false right after the latest occurrence `f_j` of `f` before `o_k` (falsifier obligation; `f` dominates `o` modulo T-TERM). So some write `w_l` between `f_j` and `o_k` made `C` true. By A-WS, `w ∈ W_C`. It is not removed from `W_o`: (OWN-NOPATH) if `w` were in `o`'s once actor with no path `w →* o`, its occurrence between `f_j` and `o_k` in that actor's unique run would give such a path; (PWB-before-falsifier) `PWB(w, f)` would put `w_l` before every occurrence of `f`, contradicting `f_j < w_l`; (PWB-after-observation) `PWB(o, w)` would put `w_l` after `o_k`; (KEY-SEP) a `NO_KEY` writer cannot change `C`'s key. Each PWB fact used was derived from the same fixpoint and is justified by the induction hypothesis at earlier occurrences ([X1 §1]). So `w ∈ W_o`, and `a ∈ MB(w) ∪ {w}` gives an occurrence of `a` before `w_l`, hence before `o_k`.

Locks contribute nothing. ∎

**L-ONCE** [proof status: `unproved`]. If `once(a)` and `a ∈ MB(b)`, then `PWB(a, b)`, over the finite executions `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` (§1.9).

*Sketch.* By L-MB, every occurrence of `b` is preceded by an occurrence of `a`. `a` occurs at most once (its actor has at most one instance, and `a` lies outside every star-closed region, so it occurs at most once in that instance's run). That single occurrence therefore precedes every occurrence of `b`. ∎

**L-PWB (frontier)** [proof status: `unproved`]. If `once(actor(a))`, `m` is in `actor(a)`, `m ∉ Anc(a)` and `m ∈ MB(b)`, then in every finite execution of `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` containing occurrences of `a` and `b`, every occurrence of `a` precedes every occurrence of `b`.

*Sketch.* By L-MB, an occurrence of `m` precedes each occurrence of `b`. If some occurrence of `a` came after an occurrence of `m`, the actor's unique run (once) would contain `m … a`, and by A-SKEL `Sk^cert` would contain a path `m →* a`, contradicting `m ∉ Anc(a)`. So every `a` precedes every `m`, hence every `b`. Without once, `m` and `a` could lie in different instances, which is why once is a premise (fixes [X1-M2]). ∎

**L-SCOPE** (restated by EXT-20261012 §4.1) [proof status: `unproved`]. If `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB(d)`, and an occurrence of `d` precedes the decision event of an occurrence `u_k` of a use in a finite execution ρ (in particular, its call or one of its conjunct LPs at or before its decision), then `u_k` is out of scope.

*Sketch.* By L-MB, a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes that occurrence of `d`, hence the decision event. Scope is decided at the decision event (§1.6), so `u_k` is out of scope. ∎ A conjunct LP after the decision does not affect scope. The lemma is what PROTECTED (a)/(b), L-PR and L-FO need when they drop a SCOPE disabler or writer for one conjunct: a conjunct LP at or before the decision is covered by the lemma, and an occurrence whose conjunct LP lies after its decision was decided by another false conjunct, or its call returned before that LP and it is `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts (§1.6). Scope decided at the first conjunct LP fails for a `d` between two conjunct LPs, and scope decided over every conjunct LP fails for code that does not short-circuit (litmus LP-SCOPE2). It is listed in trust ledger part (iii) wherever SCOPE is used.

**L-DERIVE** [proof status: `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A-EXTRACT, A-TYPE, A2]. If `derives(arg, loc, c)` holds, the value the API checks at its slot LP equals the value of `loc` at `c`.

*Sketch.* The argument is a copy chain from the load at `c` through stores, loads of thread-local temporaries, parameter passing and casts without arithmetic; none of these reads shared memory again. ∎ Hence a value conjunct evaluated at `c` decides the API's outcome (fixes [X1-M1]).

**L-FO (fixed-order certificate)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Suppose a FIXED_ORDER certificate (§4.5) exists for instance `i` at level M, with conjunct `P_j` and LP node `ℓ_j`. Take any `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)` and any in-scope occurrence `u_k` of the use decided in ρ. If `ℓ_{j,k}` lies at or before the decision of `u_k`, then `P_j` is false at `ℓ_{j,k}`; otherwise either `u_k` was decided earlier by another false conjunct, or its call returned without passing `ℓ_j`, and then `u_k` is `NOT_EVALUATED` (§1.6). So `u_k` is not READY, and, with the badness condition, no extension of ρ makes it `TOLERATED`. Hence `¬OK_i(σ_U,M,∞)`. The badness condition also names the REJECT events that decide not-ready occurrences; those that lie at the occurrence's decision are its decision route (§1.10). Two things are not claimed: that some occurrence is reached, and that every execution reaches a decision rather than leaving occurrences `PENDING`. These are reach and progress matters.

*Sketch.* Suppose `P_j` true at `ℓ_{j,k}` at or before the decision. `P_j` was false at INIT, so some writer `x ∈ W_j` (complete by A-WS; `NO_KEY` writers cannot change the key's state) occurred before `ℓ_{j,k}`. A SCOPE-excluded writer would precede the decision and make `u_k` out of scope (L-SCOPE). By L-PWB or L-ONCE, every occurrence of `ℓ_j` precedes every occurrence of `x`. Contradiction. For a value conjunct, L-DERIVE transfers the result to the API's check. No dominance of the use is needed, so early returns are covered ([J2-1]). ∎

**L-PR (protected certificate)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Suppose a PROTECTED certificate (§4.5) exists for `i` at level M. Take any `ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`, any in-scope occurrence `u_k` of the use, and any conjunct `P_j` whose LP `ℓ_{j,k}` lies in ρ at or before the decision of `u_k`. Then `P_j` holds at `ℓ_{j,k}`. Hence no in-scope occurrence is decided by a false conjunct, every decided in-scope occurrence is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED` (`¬NR_i(σ_U,M,∞)`), `¬Bad_i(σ_U,M,∞)` holds, and no occurrence is `TOLERATED` or `PENDING`. Nothing is claimed about finite executions that do not reach the use, about stall states, about reach, or about maximal executions.

*Sketch.* Case (a) is immediate: every disabler is NO_KEY, MB-unreachable, or SCOPE-excluded, and a SCOPE-excluded disabler that occurs before `ℓ_{j,k}`, hence before the decision, makes `u_k` out of scope (L-SCOPE). Case (b): by L-MB, `x*` occurred before `ℓ_{j,k}`, and as a `MUST_KEY` must-writer it established `P_j` **for this key** (fixes [X1-M3](a)). Every remaining disabler `d` satisfies either `PWB(d, x*)` (it occurred before every `x*`) or `PWB(ℓ_j, d)` (it occurs after every `ℓ_j`), or is SCOPE-excluded as above. Hence no disabler of the key lies between the last `x*` and `ℓ_{j,k}` in an in-scope occurrence, and `P_j` holds there. Applied to the first conjunct LP of `u_k` at which the conjunct would be false, this shows that no in-scope occurrence is decided not ready. ∎

**L-CONT (prefix containment, per level)** (fixes [X1-C2]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2). One proof status per part, as marked.
- **(a)** [proof status: `proved`] `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M) = {ρ ∈ Pref(B,σ_U,M) : ρ contains no time-based transition}`.
  *Sketch.* Both systems have the same states and successor function, and `en_σU∞(q) = en_σU(q) \ TB` in every state (§1.8). Induction on length gives ⊆, and, for σ_U finite executions without time-based transitions, ⊇. Strong fairness constrains only infinite executions. ∎
- **(b)** [proof status: `unproved`] `π(Pref(B,σ_R1[P_sched],M)) ⊆ Pref(B,σ_U,M)`. σ_R1 is a separate system (§1.8); π erases its clock-advance and wake steps, keeps every other step (a delay's block step is σ_U's delay transition), and forgets the clock and the ready/blocked status.
  *Sketch.* σ_R1 enables a σ_U transition only if that transition is enabled in the projected σ_U state: priority and blocking only remove choices, timeouts are enabled as in σ_U, A-ZT adds no effect, and the erased steps change no σ_U component. ∎ [I]
- **(b′) L-CONE-R1** [I] (obligation) [proof status: `unproved`]. If a bounded σ_R1 model's cone contains the σ_U model's cone at the same h, erasing from a σ_R1 execution the erased steps of (b) and the transitions of actors outside the σ_U cone gives a finite execution of the σ_U model. *Sketch:* by the cone closure (§4.10 item 1), those actors write no location of the σ_U cone and synchronize on none of its objects; they interact with it only through the CPU, which σ_U ignores. ∎
- **(c)** [proof status: `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A7-impl, A7-api, A-SKEL, A3, A4, A9] The projection of every finite real run of B onto LP-granular events is in `Pref(B,σ_U,M_impl)`.
  *Sketch.* By A7-impl, every real step on an M_impl path is a step of Φ_L-1 at LP granularity, including timed-wait timeouts and broadcast wakes. By A7-api, the other primitives are atomic at their LPs. A real timeout is one of σ_U's timeout transitions. By A-SKEL, each actor's real sequence is a `Sk^cert` path. By A3 and A9, no actor or input is missing. By A4, interleaving at event granularity is adequate. Induction on the run's length then gives a step simulation. It says nothing about the run's end state. ∎
- **(d) Not claimed** (negative statements; no proof status).
  - `Max(B,σ_U∞,M) ⊆ Max(B,σ_U,M)` is false. In litmus LP-TW, the finite σ_U∞ execution that ends with its waiter blocked is σ_U∞-maximal, while σ_U enables the waiter's timeout.
  - That real runs are maximal in (σ_U, M_impl): this fails for runs ended by `abort()`, a kill or the stop, whose end states still enable σ_U transitions.
  - Maximal containment of σ_R1 modulo π [I] is plausible under §1.8's clock rule. It is not proved and not used.
- **(e)** σ_E against M_api is **not** claimed: the real POSIX backend has executions (e.g. `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`) that M_api excludes by design. L-MLEVEL (below) gives the conditional version.

**Corollary (reverse prefix inclusion)** [proof status: `proved`, from L-CONT (a)]. Every finite execution of σ_U without time-based transitions is a finite execution of σ_U∞. In particular, the state just before the first time-based transition of a σ_U execution is σ_U∞-reachable.

**L-REACH-CONE (TB-freedom relative to the cone)** [I] (obligation) [proof status: `unproved`]. Let C be the closed cone of instance i (§4.10 item 1). If the transitions of C's actors in ρ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U,M)` contain no time-based transition, then some ρ′ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U∞,M)` has the same projection onto C's actors. *Sketch:* delete from ρ, for each actor outside C, its transitions from its first time-based transition on, and then, repeatedly, every transition of an actor outside C that a deleted transition enabled; by the cone closure no actor outside C writes a location of C, synchronizes on an object of C, spawns an actor of C or writes the support of a predicate of C, so C's transitions stay enabled. ∎ Run-based σ_U∞ reach (§1.10) uses it.

**L-MAPI-CONE (M_api conformance relative to the cone)** [I] (obligation) [proof status: `unproved`]. Let C be the closed cone of instance i and ρ the LP-granular projection of a finite real run. If every OSAL call made by C's actors in ρ returned an outcome that M_api allows, and no M_impl-only behaviour (an `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` return, a lost task, a type lock left held by a returned API) occurred on C's actors, then some ρ′ ∈ `Pref(B,σ_U,M_api)` has the same projection onto C's actors. *Sketch:* map each OSAL call of C's actors to its M_api transition at its LP with the observed outcome; an actor that takes a type lock that a cone actor also takes synchronizes on a cone object, so by the cone closure it is in C; delete the transitions of actors outside C from their first non-conforming one on, and, repeatedly, every transition of an actor outside C that a deleted one enabled; C's transitions stay enabled. ∎ Run-based M_api reach and M_api run witnesses (§1.8 rule 3, §1.10) use it.

**L-STATUS (statuses are determined by the prefix)** [proof status: `unproved`]. The status and the scope of an in-scope occurrence in a finite execution ρ (§1.6) depend only on the effect events of ρ. The state at each conjunct LP is computed from the lifecycle effects that precede it; scope asks only whether a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes the occurrence's decision event, which is in ρ once the occurrence is decided; and the horizon monitor reads only the monitored actor's events (its spawn, if it did not exist at decision) and the resource's effects. Hence:
- (a) the status is invariant under π, and is the same whether ρ is read as a finite execution of σ′ or of σ_U;
- (b) `READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` and `NOT_EVALUATED`, and the scope of a decided occurrence, are final in every extension of ρ;
- (c) every extension of a bad finite execution is bad. ∎

**L-MON (instance monitor)** [proof status: `unproved`]. The instance monitor of §1.8 is deterministic and never blocks a transition. Hence (a) product executions project exactly onto `Pref(B,σ,M)`, and the monitor state after ρ is a function of ρ; (b) an actor is dead in a product state iff it is dead in the underlying state; (c) the status and the scope of an occurrence decided by a transition t from a product state `(q, m)` are a function of `(q, m, t)`, so `Bad_i(o)`, `OK_i(o)` and `NR_i` are properties of the edges of the product graph, and the stall predicates of §1.8 are functions of the product state. Decided statuses are not part of the monitor state. *Sketch:* every TS path lifts to exactly one product path; deadness reads only enabledness; a deciding transition reads the conjunct values and horizon states held in m and its own effect. ∎

**L-TRANS (transfer soundness)** [proof status: `unproved`].
- **(1a)** If `¬Bad_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬Bad_i(σ′,M,b)`, if `¬OK_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬OK_i(σ′,M,b)`, and if `¬NR_i(σ_U,M,b)` then `¬NR_i(σ′,M,b)`, for σ′ = σ_U∞, and for σ′ = σ_R1[P_sched] with b = ∞ or, with a bounded b, under L-CONE-R1. A σ′ finite execution with a `VIOLATING` (resp. `READY`/`TOLERATED`, resp. a decided not-ready) occurrence would be, after π and, for bounded σ_R1, after the cone projection, a σ_U finite execution with the same status (L-CONT (a)/(b)/(b′), L-STATUS (a)). For σ_U∞, the bounded models of σ_U and σ′ share `Sk^h`, the cone and b.
- **(2)** With b = ∞ and M = M_impl, the same argument with L-CONT (c) gives the σ_E statement for every finite real run: the same `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` or `¬NR_i`, over the decided in-scope occurrences of the run (the status of a run's occurrence is the status of its projection, L-STATUS (a)). A `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrence in a real run contradicts none of the three. For M_api it needs L-MLEVEL or the agreeing M_impl result.
- **(3)** By the same lemmas, a good or bad witness of σ′ is a witness of σ_U at the same M, and so is a real run at M_impl, or at M_api under L-MLEVEL or with an M_api-conformance check of its prefix.
- **(Negative)** The following do not transfer down: `Reach_i`, `OK_i` and `Bad_i` as existence claims, witnesses, `allowed-not-ready`, and stall states. Removing transitions can remove reaching finite executions and can make actors dead (LP-TW). A stall state does not transfer up either: adding transitions can revive a dead actor (LP-TW under σ_U). The only exceptions are L-DEAD (c), (d). A frequency is a measure on σ_E and is not defined by σ_U. ∎

**L-MLEVEL** [I] (restated over prefixes by EXT-20261012 §4.2) [proof status: `unproved`]. If an instance's closed cone and certificate contain no M_impl-relevant operation, then the projections of `Pref(B,σ,M_api)` and `Pref(B,σ,M_impl)` onto the cone's events coincide. Transfer rule 2(i), transfer rule 3 at M_api, per-cell reach at M_api and G2's M_api branch use this prefix form.

*Sketch.* The two levels differ only in the transitions of M_impl-relevant operations (§1.4). If none occurs in the cone, the cone's transitions and their enabling conditions are identical at both levels; actors outside the cone affect the cone only through cone objects (by the cone's closure), which are not M_impl-relevant. ∎ The lemma is an obligation to prove in the paper; until then transfer rule 2(i) is applied with the sub-label `mlevel-lemma`.

**L-REF (refinements are well defined)** (fixes [X1-M5]; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2) [proof status: `unproved`]. At the same M and b:
- `timeout-dependent` is `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent ∧ ¬Bad_i(σ_U∞) ∧ Reach_i(σ_U∞)`;
- `timeout-rescued` is `OK_i(σ_U) ∧ ¬OK_i(σ_U∞)`;
- `timeout-masked-liveness` is, per instance: some product state reachable under σ_U∞ is a hang state for i under σ_U∞ (a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending) and not a hang state for i under σ_U;
- `tml-root(i₁)` is: no progress chain leads into `i₁`, and some `i₂` with `i₁ ⇝_p* i₂` (`i₂ = i₁` included) has `timeout-masked-liveness`; an unchained instance with TML therefore carries it itself.

**Well-definedness.** Each refinement is a Boolean combination of the per-σ queries of §4.10 item 7, of progress chains, and of deadness and reachability tests on the shared product state space. Progress chains are decidable: their conditions (§1.8: the root inside the use or horizon; the root's satisfiers dominated by the READY outcome of the use's call; a lock leaked or a thread lost by a not-ready occurrence) are read from the product state, its finite execution and the certificate view. None uses maximal executions, fairness or quiescence. Each is therefore defined whenever the per-σ results exist, and `exh` decides each within b. An undecided deadness test gives `unknown(bound)` (censored) or `unknown(unmodelled)` (stranded, or in a stranded actor's closure) for `timeout-masked-liveness`. Without the explorer, none is established: the first two need universal σ_U∞ statements, which neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide, and the last two are progress results.

**Agreement with v1.** v1's `timeout-dependent` required (i) that every bad σ_U execution takes a timeout transition and (ii) that the σ_U∞ continuations from the state just before its first timeout transition reach only good outcomes, i.e. neither bad outcomes nor stalls. By L-CONT (a), `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds iff every bad finite execution of σ_U contains a time-based transition, which is (i). The "not bad" half of (ii) follows, because that state is σ_U∞-reachable by a prefix that is not bad; the "no stall" half is the progress refinement. Every v1 `timeout-masked-liveness` instance whose v1 stall state is a hang state under σ_U∞ has the new progress refinement; v1 also counted states in which the use's actor waits at an idle wait, which the new refinement excludes. The converse does not hold (progress chains attach the extra instances to their root).

**The v1 gap is closed for last attempts that are one transition at level M.** Under σ_U∞, such an actor is never left without an enabled transition while its predicate can still become true: that attempt is enabled exactly when it would succeed. An actor at a last attempt that is several transitions at M (an OSAL lookup at M_impl, a test that reads several values) completes the attempt and can be stranded; its deadness tests, and those of its closure, are undecided rather than wrongly decided. ∎

**L-DPOR (explorer correctness statement)** (fixes [X1-M9]) [proof status: `exh` `unproved`; `dpor` `unproved`, which also rests on T-JACM]. Local nondeterminism (havoc values, await outcomes, retry exhaustion, star-closure choices) is made into explicit transitions of the acting actor, so each actor is deterministic given its choice transitions; dependence is as in §4.10 item 3.
- **`exh`**: every **product state** (model state together with the instance monitor state, L-MON) reachable in the finite closed model within b is visited (finite-graph reachability with a visited set of product states), and every enabled edge from it is examined. `Q_reach`, `Q_bad` and `Q_ok`, also per occurrence ordinal, are properties of deciding edges, whose status is a function of the source product state and the transition (L-MON (c)), so they are decided exactly within b; deduplication by product state neither loses nor invents a deciding edge. The deadness test and the qualifiers are backward reachability properties over the visited product graph with excluded states as sinks, so `Q_stall` is also decided exactly within b, except where a test is undecided: censored by a `TRUNC` region, stranded, or in the closure of either (§4.10 item 8). A visited set over model states alone would not suffice, because the stall predicates and the deciding edges depend on the occurrence history held in the monitor state. No external theorem is needed.
- **`dpor`**: every equivalence class with a bad (or good) occurrence of `u` within b is represented **if** the JACM 2017 theorem for source-DPOR with sleep sets extended to disabling holds for this transition system class. This is assumption T-JACM, status P in [R05 §2.2]. The equivalence is that of the **extended dependence** of §4.10 item 3: the SHUTDOWN_REQ LP is dependent with the instance's calls and conjunct LPs, and an occurrence's deciding events are dependent with the transitions of a monitored actor other than the using actor and of its spawn chain, and, for default-scope occurrences, the TEARDOWN LP with a monitored actor's transitions. Without these, two equivalent interleavings could give an occurrence different scopes or horizon verdicts. That every status, scope and horizon verdict is invariant under the extended dependence is an obligation [I]; `dpor` claims cover only monitor properties that are. `dpor` verdicts are therefore labelled **unverified** until the theorem's statement is checked (P-PAPERS). With a preemption bound, BPOR ([V-abs]) is used and the claim is "under bound p". Under σ_R1, the conservative priority dependences keep coverage under the scheduler restriction [I]. `dpor` makes no claim about stall states, since deadness is not a property of single executions; candidates are re-checked by `exh` (§4.10 item 7).
- The differential test (`dpor` vs `exh` on every model small enough for `exh`) is validation evidence, not proof. No "one execution per class" (optimality) claim is made.

**L-DEAD (stall states)** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1) [proof status: (a) `unproved`; (b) `proved` by the example LP-TW (a); (c) and (d) `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A1, A7-impl].
- **(a)** In a finite transition system, deadness of an actor for instance i in a product state is decided by one backward reachability per actor over the reachable product graph with the states excluded for i made sinks, and `terminal-pending` (`AG EF` of a process end, on that graph without B.Input's stop event) by another; idle waits, kinds, wait-for roots and choice support are read from the state, the path and the path ahead. A stall state for i is therefore decidable. In a bounded model, a test on a stranded or potentially enabled actor (§4.10 item 8) is undecided.
- **(b)** Deadness is relative to the transition system. Adding transitions can revive a dead actor, and removing transitions can make one dead. A stall state of one σ is therefore, in general, neither a stall state of a σ with more transitions (LP-TW: σ_U∞ against σ_U) nor implied by a stall state of one with fewer.
- **(c)** **Exception: deadlock cycles.** Suppose the dead actors in q contain a cycle in which each actor waits, without a timeout, to acquire an object that only its holder can release (a mutex or an OSAL type lock), held by the next actor in the cycle, and the stall's witnessing actor belongs to the cycle or waits, without a timeout, to acquire such an object held by a cycle member, directly or through a chain of such waits. Then no transition of any σ releases any of these objects, because every holder is blocked untimed, and the witnessing actor is dead in q under every σ in which q's finite execution is admissible. The stall state transfers; its qualifiers are recomputed per σ.
- **(d)** **Exception: leaked locks.** Suppose a witnessing actor waits, without a timeout, to acquire a holder-released, non-recursive object whose holder has returned from the API that acquired it and is terminated, or has no `Sk^cert` path to a release of the object that does not first acquire it again (K-LOCKBAL). Then no actor can release it under any σ, and the waiting actor is dead in every σ in which the finite execution is admissible.

*Sketch.* (a) follows from the definitions and L-MON. (b) is shown by LP-TW. (c): only a holder releases such an object, and every holder's only next transition is a blocked, untimed acquisition; an untimed wait on a cycle member's object inherits this. (d): only the holder can release the object; a terminated holder never does; any other holder must first acquire the non-recursive lock it already holds, which blocks it forever. Neither (c) nor (d) depends on which transitions σ enables. ∎

**L1 (await summarization with extracted predicates)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Suppose the poll loop satisfies the preconditions of §4.3: the body's mod set contains no relevant location other than the counter and the budget, and all test reads happen inside one critical section of one lock, or in one atomic read. Then every exit by condition of the real loop corresponds to an `await(Pred_code)` success transition at the test's atomic read point, and every exit by budget corresponds to its timeout transition.

*Sketch.* Between tests the loop changes no relevant state. The test is evaluated atomically, so its value equals `Pred_code` in the state at the read point, and `Pred_code` is extracted from that same exit branch. ∎

Under σ_U∞, the summary's timeout transition and the unsummarized loop's budget exit are both time-based transitions, and both are removed. The summary therefore does not change σ_U∞'s finite executions on the loop's relevant events, up to the unsummarized loop's failing test reads, which change no relevant state (stuttering) [I]. **It changes deadness** (EXT-20261012): under σ_U∞ the summarized poller is dead in a state where `Pred_code` can never become true, while the unsummarized one is stranded there on its stale failing test, so its tests and their closure are undecided. The obligation therefore includes: the summarized poller is dead exactly in the states where the unsummarized one is stranded with a predicate that can never become true, so the summary turns an undecided test into a decided one without inventing a stall. `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`) satisfies the preconditions (all reads inside one ES-lock critical section, `:878–888`) at both M; with L1 switched off, the progress results that rest on it are `unknown(unmodelled)`. `kz proofcheck` re-derives the predicate from the same serialized exit trees that the extractor produced (§4.1 step 8); those trees are extracted facts (trust ledger part (ii)). The lemma is still to be written in full before F1.

**L2, L3** [proof status: `unproved`].
- **L2** is stated as in [A §4.2] (`proposal_A-static-model.md:615`), with an added lock condition. Take a critical section on lock ℓ such that (i) ℓ is not a cone object and no other cone actor acquires ℓ, and (ii) its body touches no cone location or cone object and contains no blocking operation, counting its own acquire and release as touching ℓ. Removing it changes neither the reachable states and outcomes on the cone under σ_U, nor the cone's stall states, modulo its actor's intermediate control nodes [I]. *Argument:* the acquire succeeds whenever the actor reaches it, because no other cone actor holds ℓ; the body neither blocks nor changes anything another cone actor reads or waits on; the release enables no other cone actor. The removal is therefore a stuttering change for its actor, which is never dead inside the section. Condition (ii) alone is not enough. In litmus LP-DL, τ_B's inner `m1` section has an empty body; removing it breaks the lock-order cycle and with it the deadlock, so the stall-preservation claim fails under (ii) alone. Condition (i) keeps that section. The argument is a proof obligation before F1.
- **L3** (mover reduction) is stated with the conditions of §4.10 item 5: right-mover acquire, left-mover release, both-mover protected accesses, at most one non-mover, release on every path, no nested blocking acquire. Whether L3 preserves stall states is part of its proof obligation.
- Until they are proved in the paper, both lemmas are listed as assumptions, are switchable per instance, and are ablated (§8.4). The citation of the reduction theorem is an open literature check.

**L-PRED (prediction)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Every `predicted` witness is a linearization of Pred(π) that passed the A-COMM check. Under A4, A7 and Φ's primitive semantics it is feasible for every program that produces π **and** whose unobserved shared reads in the cone are covered by A-COMM (maximal causal models, RV 2012; RVPredict PLDI 2014 [V-abs], whose guarantees concern traces that contain the shared accesses). A `predicted-modelled` witness has no such guarantee and ranks with model evidence; a `predicted-extrapolated` witness is additionally relative to A-SKEL for the extrapolated loop (fixes [X1-M4]), and so is a witness completed by **horizon completion** from `Sk^cert` (§4.9 item 7a; EXT-20261012 §4.1). **Predicted not-ready evidence** (a `PENDING` occurrence in a predicted prefix) is not a witness and supports no class.

**L-LOOP (termination)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Under fixed (B, Φ, tool), the loop's comparable state (§4.14) lives in a finite lattice, every non-final iteration moves strictly up or stops, and a suspension ends the loop. A Φ edit starts a new loop. ∎
