### 6.3 Guarantee statements

Each guarantee carries a proof status (§6.2 "Proof status" and the table at its end): the weakest status of the lemmas it cites. A guarantee whose status is not `proved` says what holds relative to its stated assumptions if its lemmas hold; it is not a proved theorem.

- **G1 (census over-approximation, soundy)** [proof status: `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A1, A3, A-SKEL, A-WS, A-EXTRACT]. For B, Φ, M and A, every use of a readiness specification reachable in `Sk^cert` yields an instance with a class or `unknown(reason)` per analysed (σ, M). `inactive` is issued only from `Sk^cert`. Instances outside the closure are counted and listed. Claim template C-1 of [R05 §5.2].
- **G2 (certified verdicts, per M; safety only)** [proof status: `unproved`: L-FO, L-PR, L-CONT, L-TRANS, L-MLEVEL]. A fixed-order or protected verdict with realization `certificate` at level M states `¬OK_i` (deciding by itself only when the certificate's REJECT is at the occurrence's decision; otherwise the cell shows `unknown(no_decision_route)` until a bad witness) or `¬NR_i` (no reached occurrence decided not ready, hence `¬Bad_i`), respectively, over every finite execution of `TS(B,σ_U,M)` under A, by L-FO / L-PR.
  - **Other σ.** By L-CONT and L-TRANS, the same safety statement holds over the finite executions of σ_U∞ and σ_R1 at the same M and b; whether a transferred `¬OK_i` keeps its decision route is decided per σ (§1.10).
  - **σ_E.** **It holds for every finite real run of B (σ_E) only if M = M_impl, or if M = M_api and either the cone contains no M_impl-relevant operation (L-MLEVEL) or the M_impl verdict agrees.** What holds is the statement as L-FO or L-PR proves it, over the decided in-scope occurrences of the run (§1.8 rule 2): `¬NR_i` allows `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrences and does not say that every occurrence is `READY`. Otherwise it is a claim relative to the OSAL API contract.
  - **Not stated.** It states nothing about reach except through the reach axis. It states nothing about progress: permanent waits, deadlock, lost providers or spawns, horizons that never close. It states nothing about maximal executions.
  - **Checking.** Each certificate passed `kz proofcheck`, names its view (`Sk^cert`) and M, and carries its trust ledger (§4.5.1). `kz_verdict` stores per-M results.
- **G3 (observed readiness violations)** [proof status: `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A1, A2, A6, O4]. An "observed readiness violation" is reported only when, in the recorded trace, the state at some conjunct's LP computed from HB-ordered lifecycle LPs is outside the readiness precondition `Req`, the program's own outcome code agrees (A2, A6, O4), **and** the occurrence's status in the trace is `VIOLATING` (its horizon monitor REJECTed in the trace, or `Allowed = none`) or, with Allowed unspecified, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED` (EXT-20261012 §4.1). A `PENDING`, `TOLERATED` or `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrence is not an observed readiness violation; a trace that ends while the monitor is OPEN gives `unknown(horizon)`. No claim that the readiness specification is the "right" requirement; its provenance and source tier are shown.
- **G4 (predictive soundness relative to the trace model)** [proof status: `unproved`: L-PRED]. Every `predicted` witness is in Pred(π) and passed A-COMM (L-PRED); `predicted-modelled` and `predicted-extrapolated` witnesses, including those completed by horizon completion (§4.9 item 7a), are model evidence. Predicted not-ready evidence (`PENDING` in the predicted prefix) is neither a witness nor a class. Prediction is not maximal; "not predictable" is never "protected".
- **G5 (bounded model classification)** [proof status: `unproved`: L-DPOR, L-DEAD (a), L-MON; T-JACM for `dpor`]. Within the closed model (the closed cone of §4.10 item 1 and the reductions in use), σ, M and b, `exh` decides the safety record (hence the class, including `no-final-status` and `ordinal-split`), the reach value and the refinements exactly, on product states and their deciding edges, except timeout-masked-liveness and `tml-root` where a deadness test is undecided (`unknown(bound)` or `unknown(unmodelled)`, L-REF). It decides the progress result exactly too, except where a deadness test is undecided: censored or in a censored actor's closure, giving `unknown(bound)`, or stranded or in a stranded actor's closure, giving `unknown(unmodelled)` (L-DPOR, L-DEAD, L-MON). Statements that safety-side censoring withholds are `unknown(bound)`. `dpor` classes hold if T-JACM holds and the monitor properties used are invariant under the extended dependence of §4.10 item 3, and are labelled **unverified** until then; `dpor` sets no progress result. This is C-2 of [R05 §5.2]. Fidelity evidence: O1, O2, O6, O8; disagreements with certificates go to O10.
- **G6 (confirmation)** [proof status: `depends-on-stated-assumption`: A2, A6, the recorder's oracle]. A `realized` verdict was reproduced on the gcc-built binary of B under the declared control, with the readiness oracle evaluated on the confirmation trace, its naturalness label, and its perturbation ratio (C-3 of [R05 §5.2]; precedents RacePro, DESCRY, CTrigger [V-abs]). Realized reports contain no false alarm relative to the recorder's oracle. A σ_E `schedule-dependent` class needs natural bad and good witnesses at the **same occurrence ordinal**, in two distinct runs; natural witnesses at different ordinals give `ordinal-split(σ_E)` (§1.10).
- **G7 (PCT, only if the F1 arm is added later, D3)** [proof status: `unproved`]. Each F1 run finds a depth-d bug, relative to the gated scheduling points, with probability at least `1/(n·k^(d−1))`, with n and k measured (C-4 of [R05 §5.2]).
- **G8 (loop termination and reproducibility)** [proof status: `unproved`: L-LOOP; the replay is a tested requirement]. Under fixed (B, Φ, tool) the loop terminates (L-LOOP). Re-running from stored artifacts reproduces the verdict store byte for byte, because every budget is a deterministic work-unit count.
- **G9 (inconsistency surfacing)** [proof status: `unproved`; a requirement on the implementation]. Every contradiction between layers (O1–O10, CC) is reported and suspends or downgrades the rule or model involved; a suspension ends the loop for that Φ version. None is resolved silently.
- **G10 (transparency of trusted facts)** [proof status: `unproved`; a requirement on the implementation]. Every certificate and every verdict cell carries a trust ledger (§4.5.1) that lists:
  - (i) the inference steps that `kz proofcheck` re-validated;
  - (ii) the extracted facts those steps read, with the hashes of `facts.kz`, `skel.kz` and `anchors.kz`;
  - (iii) the Φ entries, `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries (A-FRESH, A-OWNER, relational guards), assumptions and lemma obligations it relies on;
  - (iv) the observation obligations that can refute an entry of (ii) or (iii), with their status and the exercised-path limit;
  - (v) the proof status of each lemma among the entries of (iii) (§6.2), and the weakest status of each certificate-based statement.

  The census reports the number of conjuncts with T-OBS disabled.
- **G11 (progress, bounded and model-relative)** [D] (EXT-20261012 §4.1) [proof status: `unproved`: L-DEAD (a), L-MON, L-DPOR].
  - **`no-stall-within(b)`** is issued only by `exh`. It states that no stall state for i (§1.8) is reachable in the closed cone model at σ, M within b. The statement is relative to the closed-cone rule (§4.10 item 1), the reductions in use, the censoring rule, the exclusions of §1.8 and A.
  - **`stall-witness`** is either a finite execution of the model that ends in a stall state (`model(b)`), with its kind, qualifiers and choice support, or a realized run with a wait-for fact (§4.13).
  - **`terminal-pending` is relative to the closed cone**: an actor outside the cone whose process end stays reachable could make a cone hang state terminal-pending in the whole process.
  - **Neither value is about the binary.** Deadness is preserved neither by over- nor by under-approximation (L-DEAD (b)), so neither value is a statement about the binary of B. Model stall witnesses are candidates until confirmed; only `deadlock-cycle` and `leaked-lock` witnesses that are neither idle nor terminal-pending can be confirmed by the wait-for oracle; witnesses on semaphores, queues, messages or predicates, `idle` and `terminal-pending` witnesses, and `choice-dependent` witnesses are never counted as hangs (§8.5). `no-stall-within(b)` is a statement about the model.
  - **Not modelled.** Undefined behaviour after a VIOLATING occurrence (e.g. C3's unchecked index into `AppData`) is recorded as that occurrence's consequence, not modelled as a transition: the model lets the process continue, so progress results after such an occurrence are relative to that continuation [I]. K-TEARDOWN instances get no progress result (`unknown(teardown_scope)`). Executions in which the use's actor instance terminates without an occurrence, or that end in a panic, are covered by neither the safety nor the progress axis; their only record is the observed consequence of a not-ready root occurrence that led to them and the harm that its caller-level Allowed gives (`unspecified_by_reference` under the defaults for K-SPAWN.osal and the K-SELF.es raise) (§6.4 item 34). σ_U∞ results that depend on a give-up that Φ has not declared time-based carry `tb-undeclared` (§1.8). Results that rest on L1's summary of a poll are relative to L1 (§6.2).
  - **Excluded routes.** No progress result is derived from a certificate, from prediction, from observation without a wait-for fact, or by transfer between σ, except L-DEAD (c), (d).

### 6.4 Not claimed

1. "Sound", "complete", "detects all races" or "no false positives", without the qualifiers above ([R06 §6.3 item 1]).
2. "Safe" or "no readiness violation exists" from the absence of an alarm; from a bounded exploration beyond its bound; from prediction or prefilter silence; or from a model whose obligations failed.
3. **"Timing-protected in σ_E"** or any σ_E class from the absence of bad runs. Only "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown" ([X1-M11]).
4. "Fixed-order" or "deterministic" from repeated failing runs; only from a σ- and M-stated certificate or exhaustive bounded exploration ([R06 S9]).
5. "Fixed-order" from a trace-local robust chain (VC_R) without a static certificate ([J1] §8).
6. Universal verdicts from the h-cut exploration view without h in b ([X1-C1]).
7. σ_E validity of an M_api-only verdict whose cone is M_impl-relevant ([X1-C2]).
8. "Trace-sound" for a predicted witness that failed A-COMM ([X1-M4]).
9. "Exact within b" for `dpor` while T-JACM is unverified; "one execution per Mazurkiewicz class" at all ([X1-M9]).
10. A σ_E frequency or class from a σ_U prediction or model witness; a σ_E class from held, gated or timeout-exercised runs (only `σ_E(held)`); any priority-based verdict carried between σ_R1 and σ_E ([R06 §6.3 item 14]).
11. "No transfer across σ" (too weak), or σ_U-to-σ_E frequency transfer (too strong).
12. "R2 is a race": its evidence and proof shape make it a fixed-order dependency error ([R06 S6]). Nor that R2's certificate comes from a generic template alone: it uses the generic specification K-ID **and** the framework rule R19 (§7.2 refinement accounting, [X1-M8]).
13. "C3 or C5 reproduced on v7.0.1": their buggy code is absent there ([R06 §6.3 item 9]).
14. "Existing tools fail on C1–R2": every external process-level tool is `NOT_RUN` in the survey; NOT_RUN is never a false negative ([R06 S5]); a `REPORTED` cell is not detection of the case unless its correspondence and evidence are checked (§8.3).
15. Anything about MLIR: the MLIR stage is excluded from this roadmap (D6), so no claim about an MLIR representation is made.
16. Generality from the five design cases; "new bugs" for derived, role-derived, seeded, mutant or configuration-changed tests ([R06 §6.3 items 7–8]).
17. Flight-representative timing or overhead from Linux `SCHED_OTHER` in permissive mode ([R06 §6.3 item 10]); RT verdicts for RTEMS/VxWorks before their profiles exist.
18. Novelty of HB/vector clocks, DPOR, PCT, lockset, delay injection, predictive analysis, SMT encodings, points-to analysis, **key-conflict lock reversal** (RVPredict/GPredict), **late-registration loss** (InitRacer) or **predicted use-before-init** (ExceptioNULL) or **framework typestate** (predictive typestate checking, Joshi & Sen; Lifestate) as mechanisms. Novelty is claimed only for the combination around the cFS semantics; the novelty of the G3 formalization is an open literature check ([R04 §8 item 5]; [X1-M12]).
19. Anything about F Prime or other frameworks beyond hypothesis H-F (§7.6).
20. Memory-model correctness of the ad hoc synchronization sites (assumed, A4); that the x86 TSO assumption justifies the source-language semantics, the compiler's choices or the whole execution (D10).
21. Any R2 count as a Kairos result before Kairos's own recorder reproduces the per-use set ([R06 §6.3 item 13]); "24" for any basis other than the ENV basis, whose generated module version table is one of its inputs (the count's dependence on that table is to be checked by Kairos's own recorder [I]).
22. "Lost packets" for rejected event requests: EVS rejects them before any packet is built ([R06 §6.3 item 15]).
23. OSAL F1 (lock leak), F7 (silent task loss), the timebase handshake loss or either App-record window as bugs before confirmation runs; derived confirmations are labelled derived.
24. "First process-race detector" ([R06 §6.3 item 11]).
25. A recall figure for real ordering bugs in general: the recall protocol measures detection of history-derived program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, labelled synthetic (§8.5).
26. Any progress, termination or deadlock-freedom claim from a certificate, and any reachability claim from a certificate's inference: reach is proved only by the run or confirmed model path recorded with the certificate (its reach item, FIXED_ORDER item 6, checked against T6, §4.5). A certified protected or fixed-order verdict says nothing about executions that do not reach the use; about permanent waits, deadlocks, lost providers or lost spawns, before, inside or after the use; or about whether an Allowed horizon closes (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
27. Containment of maximal executions. `Max(σ_U∞) ⊆ Max(σ_U)` is false (LP-TW). Real runs are finite executions, not maximal ones. σ_R1 maximal containment is not used (EXT-20261012 §4.2).
28. Transfer across σ of any of the following: reach; witnesses, hence `allowed-not-ready`, `schedule-dependent` and `ordinal-split`; stall witnesses (except deadlock cycles and leaked locks, L-DEAD (c), (d)); any progress result. Also not claimed: any σ_E progress statement obtained by transfer, any bounded (b ≠ ∞) statement transferred to σ_E, and any bounded σ_R1 statement transferred from a σ_U model whose cone the σ_R1 model does not contain.
29. "No stall", "deadlock-free" or "live" outside `exh` within b, and even there only about the closed cone model. Nothing about livelock, starvation or divergence: the progress notion is deadness only. Under σ_R1, whose deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation, priority starvation never makes the starved actor itself dead, but an actor that waits on a starved giver can be dead under unbounded σ_R1; bounded σ_R1 models avoid this only by truncation at h.
30. That an `ordinal-split` instance depends on the schedule. Schedule dependence is claimed only for `schedule-dependent`, whose two witnesses concern the same occurrence ordinal. Once nodes always satisfy this.
31. "Independent verification" of a certificate in any sense stronger than this: `kz proofcheck` re-validated its inference steps relative to the extracted facts, the skeleton, the closure lists, Φ and `libkz-core`, all of which it trusts, and the steps marked "via T5" also used the shared evaluator or rule engine (EXT-20261012 §4.3).
32. Any progress statement for K-TEARDOWN instances (`unknown(teardown_scope)`).
33. That a model stall witness of kind `other`, or one qualified `idle`, `terminal-pending` or `choice-dependent`, is a hang of the binary; that a refinement exists without the explorer.
34. Anything about executions in which the use's actor instance, or an actor on its spawn chain, terminates without an occurrence (a task lost to F7 after `OS_TaskPrepare` fails, `osapi-task.c:128–137`; the timebase thread's silent return, `osapi-timebase.c:386–390`), or that end in a panic. They are covered by neither the safety nor the progress axis. Their only record is the safety result of a not-ready root occurrence that led to them, with the observed consequence on its chain (`consequence`: task lost, panic, process abort, §3.3) and the harm that its caller-level Allowed gives (`unspecified_by_reference` under the defaults for K-SPAWN.osal and the K-SELF.es raise, §1.6); a σ_U panic reached through a spontaneous timeout has no not-ready root and leaves no record (EXT-20261012 §4.1).
35. That every execution of a `fixed-order` instance reaches a decision: a decision route says only that a bad witness exists in the cell, or that not-ready occurrences are bad at their decision. That `terminal-pending`, computed on the closed cone, holds for the whole process.
36. That σ_U∞ is free of give-ups that Φ has not declared time-based (HS's application monitor, `hs_monitors.c:107–160`; TIME's flywheel entry, `cfe_time_tone.c:1172–1186`): results that depend on them carry `tb-undeclared`. Anything about occurrences decided after a process-terminal step, which have no status. That a result resting on L1's summary of a poll holds with L1 switched off.
37. That specification-based or GPredict-style techniques cannot express Allowed outcomes, finite retries or bounded horizons. No re-implementation of such a technique is part of the evaluation since revision 1.2, and an ablation that removes an element from Kairos says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3, §8.4; EXT-20261012 §5.1).
38. What certificates or prediction add, from detection (alarm yes/no) alone. A plain state check given the same readiness precondition is expected to find every realized R2 occurrence [I]; the value of certificates and prediction is assessed only under classification and evidence (§8.5; EXT-20261012 §5.2).
39. Blindness for any item without an exposure and freeze record that shows no exposure before its governing freeze; blindness because expected verdicts come from fix texts; "blind evaluation" from rater masking alone (§7.2, §7.5 item 5; EXT-20261012 §5.3).
40. Intervals over occurrences, instances, mutants or runs as statements about independent defects or causes; any count of dynamic occurrences as a sample size; a count pooled across binaries, observation methods or pool keys (§8.2, §8.7; EXT-20261012 §5.4).
41. Class accuracy without a class label. R2 has no reference class statement (its fixed-order reading rests on code reading, `cases/R2.md` L45). C1, C2, C3 and C5 have reference statements that they are races (issue texts, the 6.4.2 VDD, CF's v7.0.1 code comment, C1's fix commit; §8.2), and some recall fix texts say "race condition" (§8.5 items 2–3); how such a statement maps to the per-(σ, M) classes is not fixed by any reference, and the PI decided on 2026-10-15 (P-CLASSGT) that the word is not converted into a tool class automatically, so a class label comes only from the stated model, the configuration and real evidence. Agreement with a pre-registered expectation, and agreement or disagreement with a reference statement, are reported as such, never as accuracy, and no accuracy denominator is made without a basis (§8.5).
42. "Reproduced" for C1, C2, C3 or C5 in the default configuration of v7.0.1: C1's lookup is latent (both semaphore names are empty, `cf_def_config.c:60, 78`), C2's apps are private (role-derived pair), and C3's and C5's buggy code is absent (item 13).
43. A result on one design case as evidence for another, or the five design cases as five independent samples: C3 and R2 share a resource family, C3 and C5 share one fix release, C5 and the App-record windows share a shape, and C1, C5 and R2 share the reserve-then-finalize shape ([LED §3.6], all hypotheses) at different layers (C1: OSAL object table, M_impl; C5 and R2: ES app record) (§8.2). In particular, an S3a result on R2's template in the TIME/TBL contexts at v7.0.1 is not a result on C3, a result on the App-record window A.3 is not a result on C5, and C1's `inactive(B)` proof in the default B and the K-PHASE census of the v7.0.1 bundle are generic-layer results on v7.0.1 counterparts, not results on C1 or C2 (§9.3, per-case paths and controls).
44. That a σ_E cell obtained by transfer says every in-scope occurrence of a real run is `READY`. It states `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` or `¬NR_i` over decided occurrences, and a `NOT_EVALUATED` early return is allowed by all three (§1.8 rule 2).
45. "Proved", "verified" or "certified", in a verdict, a report, a ledger rendering or a paper sentence, for a lemma whose proof status (§6.2) is not `proved`, for a lemma that is only listed as an obligation, or for a statement that rests on one; and any reading of `proved` as a statement about the binary (PS2, PS4).
46. That `reach = proved` is established by observation alone. It rests on L-CONT (c), and on L-MLEVEL, L-MAPI-CONE or L-REACH-CONE where its cell needs them, whose proof status the cell's ledger shows (§4.5.1).
47. "Events lost" or "packets lost" for R2's rejected event requests: EVS refuses the request before it builds any packet (item 22; `cfe_evs.c:185–189`), so the accurate statement is "event requests rejected". Also, any statement that a system call is or is not made, for R2 or elsewhere, from a debugger log alone: it needs code analysis (stated as such, [I]) or an observation of system calls under stated conditions. The OSAL probe of `baselines/availability.md` §4 observed one OSAL unit test, not cFS.
48. That the observer changes only time, or that observation has no effect. Neither is claimed. The observation effect is checked against an unobserved reference run, and for an internal event that has no such reference no effect conclusion is drawn (D1, §5.1, §8.6).
49. That a POWERON restart resets every persisted file, file cache or shared file system. It deletes the shm segments (§3.5); the initial state is recorded and not assumed clean (D17 (a)).
50. A reproduction of the private system of C2. The result is a role-based validation of the C2 mechanism. The assumption that the provider's late initialization does not fail applies to the reconstruction only and is no guarantee about cFS; the four cells of the 2 × 2 are not four independent real defects (P-C2, Q22).
51. A reproduction of the original 6.4.1 platform's run for C3 or C5 from the analysis-only parse with this container's 32-bit headers; a result that is sensitive to the headers before the sensitivity is resolved (D13 (a), (b)); an actual execution on a past code base from analysis-only work.
52. The RESERVED-window and lock-leak experiment as a part of C1's validation, or as one of the five cases' results (P-C1HOLD); a failure made by a hold as a natural one; a timeout observation alone as a permanent deadlock.
53. An acceptance, a TOLERATED status or a harm from a `rule` record alone or from the extracted candidate continuation; a precedence between conflicting reference texts that no reference sets; a researcher-authored requirement that turns K-SPAWN.osal or K-LOCKBAL into a readiness violation (a violation of a caller requirement) (QT-4, Q15, QT-5, QT-9).
54. A score or a ranking between existing process-level tools; a `NOT_RUN` cell counted as a miss; a `REPORTED` cell counted as detection of the case without checking; a re-implementation's result as an original tool's result; a statement about an unread tool's expressiveness or observation range from a search excerpt (§8.3, P-PAPERS).
55. A significance test, a general rate or an interval over the five design cases treated as independent samples (P-STATS); a class label derived automatically from the source's word "race" (P-CLASSGT); an independent review claimed before two independent raters exist (P-TRIAGE); a result on variant examples as a result beyond the variant set (D8/P-RECALL).
56. A real real-time execution result from the model-based σ_R1 (D4), or any result of the F1 `--wrap` gates or of PCT, which are deferred (D3).
57. A five-case validation completed by a record that a case will not be done, or by the result of other code (P-F1GATE, P-D18GATES (b)).

---

## 7. Openness

### 7.1 Discovery sources (the union)

Readiness specifications are written per **API and resource kind**, never per case, and the census instantiates them at every context-qualified site in cFE core and the 15 bundled apps. Candidates come from the union of these sources ([J1-13]):

| # | Source | Mechanism | Output | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Static census | every Φ template at every matching context-qualified site; key resolution by value analysis, header-MsgId analysis and table decoding | instances with a class or `unknown(reason)` | [A], [C] |
| 2 | Outcome mining | every not-ready outcome code observed at the API boundary (vocabulary from the extracted `\retval`/`@retval` lists) creates an instance even when no template names the API | `template: none` instances, triaged | [B §4.1] |
| 3 | Key-pair enumeration | for every resource key with lifecycle transitions and uses in different actors, the pairs Φ marks as conflicting | candidates | [B §4.1] |
| 4 | Retry/timeout-loop-derived specifications | every loop recognized by the poll/retry pattern around an operation implies a candidate readiness specification for it, whose extracted facts are the effective bound, the delay and the exit codes; its Allowed entry is `retry_until_ready(effective bound)` only where a `caller` record accepts the retry (an admission comment, an issue, a requirement). The pattern itself is a `code_pattern` record, side `rule`, which states no acceptance (§1.6: a rule alone never makes an occurrence accepted, QT-4), so without a `caller` record the entry is `unspecified_by_reference` and its occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the loop facts recorded | templates | [C §7.1] |
| 5 | Lifecycle symmetry | every kind with create and delete operations automatically gets K-REG, K-LIFE and K-GEN | templates | [C §7.1] |
| 6 | Context-lookup symmetry | every caller of a context-resolving helper (`GetAppRecordByContext`, `GetTaskRecordByContext`) gets a K-SELF instance with its own not-ready behaviour (§3.3) | templates | [X2-02] |
| 7 | API documentation | every `\par Assumptions` block (138 in cFE core API headers) is an annotation candidate; a human turns it into a Φ entry and the effort is counted. Examples: `cfe_evs.h:168–172` (K-ID); `cfe_es.h:400–403` (K-PHASE); `cfe_sb.h:449–464` (buffer lifetime) | templates | [R06 §5.3] |
| 8 | Code admissions | comments stating an ordering assumption or an acknowledged race: CF "start up race condition" (`cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`); CF TOCTOU (`cf_cfdp_s.c:447–463`); the OSAL lock invariant (`osapi-idmap.c:384–385`); the PSP exception-ring race (`cfe_psp_exception.c:72–86`); ES's "informational" wake (`cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:254–257`); the EVS guard (`cfe_evs_utils.c:613–623`); the TBL lock rationale (`cfe_tbl_api.c:145–149`); TIME's unregistration comment (`cfe_time_tone.c:1322–1325`); the PSP restart comment "if problems occur (e.g. deadlock) eventually this timeout will expire" (`cfe_psp_support.c:91–98`) | templates | [A §7.1], [C §7.1] |
| 9 | Belief deviance | for each API, the fraction of call sites that test its status; deviants are ranked (e.g. `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, `cfe_tbl_task.c:196` ignore `GetAppID`'s status). Engler et al. SOSP 2001 [V-me]. **A ranking only, never a verdict** | probe priorities | all |
| 10 | Always-one-order pairs | pairs observed in only one order across runs become flip candidates for prediction (CloudRaid ESEC/FSE 2018 [V-abs]) | candidates | [B §7.2] |
| 11 | Per-app profiling under variant bases | one-app-at-a-time runs collect potential publish → use communications independent of boot order (Snowboard SOSP 2021 [V-abs]); candidates only; also a cross-check of static publisher sets | candidates | [A §7.3] |
| 12 | Watchpoint discovery | during a publication window, a write watchpoint on the field records stores by other actors (DataCollider OSDI 2010 [V-abs]); read discovery only as a declared experiment (RW watchpoints cost ≈34 µs per read) | candidates | [B §7.2] |
| 13 | Configuration variants | new bases B′ (§7.4) | per-B′ results | all |
| 14 | Held-out protocol | §7.5 | held-out results | [R06 §5.6] |

Program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs (§8.5) are an **evaluation** instrument, not a discovery source: they measure what the union of sources misses.

### 7.2 Specification governance and anti-circularity

- **Two readiness oracles.** Φ-declared effects and bindings (the working model) and framework validity predicates (an independent oracle: support locations statically, outcome codes dynamically). Agreement is checked by O7 statically and O4 dynamically ([J1-12]; [J1 X9]).
- **Provenance per Φ entry.** Kind (`api_doc`, `requirement`, `code_admission`, `code_pattern`, `issue`, `researcher`), side (`supplier`, `caller`, `rule`, §1.6), `path:line` or requirement ID, sha256 of the cited text, author, authoring time, and which design cases the author had inspected for that API ([R06 §5.3 item 4]). The **source tier** (§1.6) is derived from these records at report time and is shown with every verdict.
- **App-profile layer** (fixes [X2-13]). App-private protocols (e.g. FM's parent–child queue) are never encoded in engines. If they are modelled at all, they go into `profiles/apps/<app>/*.kzp` with the same provenance and authoring record, and their authoring cost is reported separately from the framework profile. Without such an entry, instances that depend on them are `unknown(unmodelled)`.
- **Freezes by hash** ([J3-M11]; extended by EXT-20261012 §5.3):
  - **F1** (P-F1GATE, approved 2026-10-15): the **evaluation engine and rules**, frozen before the main evaluation: Φ, extractor, engines, calibration table, lints, **any re-implementation of a process-level technique with its authoring record** (§8.3), **the evaluation criteria** (the scoring rules and report fields of §8.5, the class-label rule (P-CLASSGT: the source's word "race" is not converted into a tool class automatically; `fixed-order` and `schedule-dependent` are judged from the stated model, the configuration and real evidence, and a class label is `unspecified_by_reference` while no such support exists), the unit rule of §8.2 and the statistical assumptions of §8.7), and the hashes of the per-case expectation sheets. **A case's expectation sheet is frozen by hash before the first tool output on that case** (§9.3), not at F1, so that waiting for F1 never loses a record that is due before a case's first output. A sheet gives, per development case, basis, (σ, M, b) cell and instance, the expected class, refinements, progress value and outcome label, and, where a reference makes a class statement, that statement with its citation. **F1 waits** until the choices and required details of D13, D22 and P-C2 are recorded and each of the five cases has its own reference configuration, its expectation sheet, the implemented functions it needs and its validation stages, or the reason and the limit on the research scope for a case that was not done (§9.3). A record that a case will not be done does not complete the five-case validation, and no case is completed by the result of other code;
  - **F2**: frozen before any held-out item is opened; **the mutation operators, the history query (keywords, matched field and case, and date cut), the expected-verdict rule, the equivalent-mutant exclusion rule** (§8.5) **and the exposure record of every held-out and recall item** (§7.5 item 5) are frozen before F2;
  - **post-freeze change log**: every rule, Φ entry, exception, encoding, scoring or expectation-sheet change after F1 is logged with its time, its reason, the item that prompted it and the verdicts it changes. Each item has a **governing freeze**: F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items. A change after an item's governing freeze marks it `post-freeze-adjusted`, and its changed verdicts are reported apart, with one exception: a Φ, rule or exception refinement between F1 and F2 that touches a development item is the planned `oracle refinement` of "Template-only first" below, labelled and reported apart as that line says. A change between F1 and F2 does not mark held-out or recall items, which F2 freezes with the change. A change to the evaluation criteria, an encoding or the expectation sheet after F1 is never an `oracle refinement`.
- **Template-only first.** The first evaluation run uses no case-specific entry. Later refinements are labelled `oracle refinement`, and the verdicts they change are reported separately ([R06 T2]).
- **Refinement accounting names framework rules** (fixes [X1-M8]). Every verdict lists the Φ entries its proof used, split into three groups: generic specifications, generic machinery, and **framework rules authored with a case known**. R2's certificate is reported as "generic specification K-ID + framework rule R19 (CORE-SERIAL)". R19 binds the loop, its stratum and its terminal escape. Its predicate is extracted from the code, and the checker re-derives it from the extractor's serialized exit trees, which are extracted facts (trust ledger part (ii)).
- **Ground truth versus blindness** (revised by EXT-20261012 §5.3). A fix or issue text is an external ground-truth source: it gives an item's instance, expected direction and whether the expectation concerns readiness or progress (§8.5 item 3). It does not make the item blind. Blindness is a property of an item relative to the people who author Φ, engines, any re-implementation of a process-level technique and scoring rules, and to the raters; it is claimed only where the exposure and freeze record (§7.5 item 5) shows that none of them saw the case, the fix or the expected verdict before the freeze that governs the item. The authors know R2, C1, C2, C3, C5 and both App-record windows, so every development-set run is non-blind; there, anti-circularity rests on header provenance, code-extracted predicates and the template-only-first rule. Items already named in this specification or in the case records are non-blind: the commits of §8.5 item 2 (`58abd38d` is also in the C5 case record), the mutant of the operator modelled on C1's buggy revision at C1's own site, the C1-derived table variant of §7.4, and the R2 history items named as controls in §8.2 and §9.3 (`b9bec567^`/`b9bec567`, `v7.0.0`, PR #2820). Rater masking (§8.5) is assessor masking, not dataset blinding.
- **Authoring effort** (person-hours, number of entries, lines of Φ, per layer) is measured and reported ([R06 R4]).
- **Lints** ([A §9.3]): constants in tool code only as `KZ_REF(value, "path:line")`; no case identifiers or case-specific symbols (e.g. `CFE_SB_Global`, `sem_name`, `C1`) in engine code outside `profiles/` and `tests/`.

### 7.3 Candidates beyond the five cases

Every row comes from a generic template applied by the census, not from case knowledge. Each stays a **candidate** until the loop produces evidence. A confirmation that needs a constructed schedule is labelled derived. All expected verdicts are [I]. Under D9 (decided 2026-10-15) the candidates are recorded as model inputs and as material for the extended evaluation; none is an independent goal of the first paper, and none stands in for a design case.

| Candidate | Template | Source facts | Expected first verdict [I] | Path to evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Core-task App-record window** (EVS, ES, SB, TBL, TIME, and their children created in TaskInit, before `cfe_es_start.c:818`) | K-SELF.es (all seven callers), K-SPAWN.app-record | §3.3; [J1 §1.1]; [LED §3.4] | open: O-ordered (ES lock) → prediction/explorer; `schedule-dependent(σ_U, M_api and M_impl)` and also under σ_U∞ (only a 50 ms yield separates them; no refinement). Progress under σ_U∞, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`): a skipped raise leaves the startup thread dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` and the created core tasks dead in their `CORE_READY` polls; every later instance with a hang state there carries timeout-masked-liveness and is chained to this instance, which carries `tml-root`. σ_R1[`P_sched^posix`]: expected protected within b by `exh` on one CPU [I] (the startup thread outranks the tasks; no certificate or transfer route exists there); σ_E: no bad witness expected in natural runs, so "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown" | lock-reversing prediction from default boots; derived F0 hold between `:810` returning and `:818` (D16) |
| **SB/TBL identity never published** (consequence of the window; status ignored at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, `cfe_tbl_task.c:196`) | K-ID with shape ABSENT; chain root = the window instance | §0.5 | the post-publication SB-AppId uses (199 / 205 / 205 per boot under gdb, [R2g §2]) and TBL identity uses: no protected certificate; schedule-dependent(σ_U), ABSENT, chained | as above; the K-ID oracle (`ILLEGAL_APP_ID` on every later SB event) |
| Script-app App-record window; `GetAppIDByName` success with `0xFFFFFFFF`; `RunLoop` false → zombie | K-SELF.es, K-REG.es | §3.3 | open: O-ordered → prediction; schedule-dependent(σ_U, σ_U∞); harms per chain | App. A.3 |
| OSAL lookup of a RESERVED record leaks the type lock (F1) | K-LOCKBAL (M_impl, by-name path) + K-REG.osal | `osapi-idmap.c:384–385` vs `:401–404` | implementation-defect candidate under M_impl, if reachable. Pairs where creation is certified earlier are protected as safety statements; their progress is open. The leaked lock shows up as confirmable `leaked-lock` stall witnesses, one wait-for root (the type lock), for every instance whose LP lies behind it: the creator's later uses (it is dead in `FinalizeNew`) and later operations on that object type that take the type lock (create, delete, by-name lookup, by-ID lookups in GLOBAL or EXCLUSIVE mode, and object iteration, `OS_ForEachObject*`, which takes every type's lock in GLOBAL mode, `osapi-idmap.c:1246–1256, 1360–1367`; for countsems not `Give`, `Take` or `TimedWait`, which use `OS_LOCK_MODE_NONE`, `osapi-countsem.c:147, 168, 189`), including the leaker's own re-entry (an instance of its own where the re-entry is another calling context) and `ES_BG_TASK` when it cleans up the leaker's exited app (`CFE_ES_CleanupTaskResources` → `OS_ForEachObject`, `cfe_es_apps.c:1565, 1759`), after which no app is cleaned up, restarted or reloaded. They are chained to the leaking lookup's not-ready occurrence [I] | explorer (`exh`, progress); the follow-up hold experiment with a wait-for oracle (App. A.4 step 5; P-C1HOLD: an OSAL-defect-candidate item, not counted among the five cases); litmus LP-LEAK |
