| HS system-monitor device start fails | (outcome mining) | `hs_sysmon.c:50–72`; `psp_module_list.cmake:9` | provider `env-absent` | App. A.6 |

**The candidates and the five design cases** [I] (EXT-20261012 §7; ADR-044). No candidate above is a design case, and no result on a candidate is evidence on a case. On the ENV basis, however, several candidates exercise, before any case basis exists, a mechanism that a case needs on its own basis, so the mechanism is tested there first; each case's own test stays on its path in §9.3, per-case paths and controls:

| Mechanism | Case that needs it on its own basis | ENV candidate that exercises it first (not case evidence) |
|---|---|---|
| lock-reversing prediction with A-COMM labels; schedule dependence from a same-ordinal pair; held confirmation (S5, S6) | C1 (D22); C3 and C5 (D13) | both App-record windows (App. A.2, A.3): prediction from default boots, holds under D16 |
| the explorer's bounded classes, `unreached(b)` and the kill criterion (S7) | C1, C2, C3, C5 | the App-record windows and `SBNSubPipe` (§4.10 item 9; the two C1-derived cones need C1's basis, D22) |
| an M_api/M_impl disagreement, K-LOCKBAL and `leaked-lock` stall witnesses | C1 at M_impl | the OSAL lock-leak row (F1) above |
| a PROTECTED certificate | no issue instance of the five; conditionally EVS's own-identity context in C3's 6.4.1 census (D13) and `58abd38d^` on the A.3 line (P-RGIT) | App. A.7's timebase lookups; RC10 and RC11 (§3.15) |
| `inactive(B)` with a proof | C1 in the default B (configuration closure while C1 is latent) | `K-ATOM.tbl-load`; the HS rows above with default tables |
| Allowed other than `none` | C1 (`retry_until_ready`); C5 (a) if Φ_6.4.x authors its propagation entry (Q16) | TO_LAB's `defer_by_design` (App. A.1 step 13); EVS's own-ID guard (`documented_drop`, §3.15 RC6) |
| barrier semantics, K-PHASE and the app-profile layer | C2 (P-C2) | the in-tree K-PHASE deviations of §3.6 (SBN, CS; development-set items found with C2 known, which the census must rediscover) |
| natural runs that may contain both outcomes at one ordinal | none of the five | `SBNSubPipe` (App. A.5) |

Mechanisms that no ENV candidate exercises are tested only on a case basis, and until then only by the litmus programs of App. A.8, which are evidence about the definitions, not about the cases: the plain reordering of an HB-unordered pair with extrapolation (C2, C1); σ_U∞ as a positive differentiator (`timeout-dependent`: C1, and C2 only if its provider's late init cannot fail, Q22); σ_R1 decided by a cited `P_sched` (C3 at 6.4.1); symbolic keys with ordinals per key (C1); merged-fix pre/post pairs (C1, C3, C5; C2's 2 × 2); version transfer (H-V, §7.6: C3, C5); and the wrong-identity adequacy gaps (C3, C5). Kinds that no design case and no candidate covers enter through the census and the held-out sources, and are reported in the openness indicators (§7.7).

### 7.4 Configuration variants and scenario families

The dependency graph is configuration-driven: six apps subscribe from tables, several take names or symbols from tables, and the schedule decides who wakes when ([R03 §7]). Each variant is a new basis with its own manifest and provenance status, never pooled with the default B. Nothing found only in a variant is a "new bug" of the default configuration (D18):

- tables that activate latent paths (CF `sem_name` with a provider, public-code-derived from nasa/bp [LED §1a]; C1's B′ is the public BP basis decided under D22 [D]; LC watchpoints/actionpoints; MD dwell symbols; HS AMT/EMT/XCT; CS tables);
- alternate schedule tables, including the wakeups absent from the default schedule ([R03 §6.2]);
- startup-script order; `MISSION_CORE_MODULES` order (a configuration-change experiment, never schedule evidence; the mechanism is documented, "Missions may add/remove/replace components in this list as needed. For the CFE core, the order of this list is the order in which they will be initialized", `cfe/cmake/mission_defaults.cmake` L25–L26, generated by `cmake/target/CMakeLists.txt` L55–L70; no reference gives an order with `sb` before `es` and `evs`; the only order with public precedent is the **task** order EVS, SB, ES, TIME, TBL before `b9bec567`, which corrects R2's ES half only; the precedent covers the task order only, because one v7.0.1 list drives both the EarlyInit phase and the TaskMain phase (`cfe_es_start.c:751`, `:775`) and ES's entry carries the CDS EarlyInit (`cfe_es_objtab.c`), so a transplant also sets an EarlyInit order (ES's CDS EarlyInit then follows EVS's and SB's) and the positions of `config`, `fs`, `osal`, `psp`, `msg`, `sbr` and `resourceid`, which are `unspecified_by_reference` and set by the PI as recorded deviations under D18; P-R2CTRL: this option, (c3), and option (d) are not part of the first validation and are added only when a question remains that the natural pair (c2) cannot answer);
- the cpu2 target present in the build tree;
- effective queue depth: `msg_max` (D19, decided 2026-10-15: the current reference value 10 is kept and recorded together with the queue losses it causes; if a loss obstructs a target path, a sensitivity configuration is designed separately, and the value is never changed silently), and **euid** (running as root removes the truncation, §1.3, so any root-run arm is a new B′);
- **reset type**: POWERON vs PROCESSOR with a preserved CDS, a volatile startup script on `/ram`, and restored critical tables (run sequences);
- inputs: restart/reload/delete (`B_restart`), table load/validate/activate, SB pipe info, the stop command (`B_stop`, K-TEARDOWN).

### 7.5 Held-out protocol

Adopted from [R06 §5.6]:
1. **Freeze F2** (§7.2) before any held-out item is opened.
2. **Held-out sources**, each counted separately and never added to "new real bugs"; each item carries the label of item 5:
   - (a) other cFS revisions (version transfer; the profile is re-bound by AST pattern); the 6.4.x revisions on which C3 and C5 are analysed are development bases, not held-out;
   - (b) other configurations (cpu2; table variants not derived from a design case); the C1-derived `sem_name` variant of §7.4 is a development basis, not held-out;
   - (c) issues mined with a query fixed before F2 (keywords and date cut), excluding C1, C2, C3, C5 and R2; an issue or fix already named in this specification or in the case records is labelled non-blind (item 4);
   - (d) **the recall protocol's program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs** (§8.5), labelled synthetic; a mutant of an operator modelled on a design case, at that case's site, and items from fixes named during design are non-blind;
   - (e) in-code admissions not used during design; an admission cited by a Φ provenance record was used.
3. Per-class metrics with denominators (§8.5); template-only vs refined on the five design cases.
4. **The five design cases and their fixes are excluded from the unobserved evaluation sets** (D8/P-RECALL, decided 2026-10-15): both queries exclude the fixes of the design cases that lie in the queried histories (CF #184's `833fdbb`, which a keyword query over the CF history selects; cFE #198's barrier commits; the 6.4.2 fixes of cFE #72 and #73 predate the git import, [LED] C5 row). Items that are only named in this specification or in the case records (for example `6932f1fe`, `58abd38d`) are kept and labelled non-blind (item 5); whether they are also excluded is not decided. The held-out and recall procedures are the **extended evaluation**: they are separate from the five-case validation, run after it, and record the query, the exposure and the freeze of every new source. Who runs the queries, and when relative to F2, is part of that procedure.
5. **Exposure and freeze record** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.3). For every development, held-out and recall item, an append-only record frozen with F2 gives:
   - the item, its kind and its source;
   - for each person who authors Φ, engines, any re-implementation of a process-level technique or scoring rules, and for each rater: whether and when they saw the case, its fix and its expected verdict, and how (case record, design document, search, review);
   - when the item was assigned to its evaluation set, and by whom;
   - the F1 and F2 hashes and times, and the hash of the pre-registered expectation sheet (§7.2);
   - its governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items) and the post-freeze changes that touched it (§7.2).

   An item is labelled `blind` only if the record shows no exposure of any of these people before its governing freeze. Otherwise it is `non-blind(case | fix | expected)`. `unknown-exposure` is treated as non-blind. An item touched by a change after its governing freeze, other than a labelled `oracle refinement` of a development item between F1 and F2, is `post-freeze-adjusted` and reported apart (§7.2). Results are reported per label, and no blind claim is made before the record exists.

### 7.6 Generalization hypotheses (stated, not claimed)

- **H-V (cFS versions).** Only Φ and the scheduling profile `P_sched` are version- and backend-specific; rules carry version guards. Test: with Φ_6.4.x, the 6.4.x backend's cited `P_sched` and unchanged engines, C3 at 6.4.1 should give, for each of its two chained instances (the K-ID use at TIME's `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` event and the use of EVS's own AppID in TIME's context), fixed-order under σ_R1 by `exh` only (S7) and schedule-dependent under σ_U, with Allowed `unspecified_by_reference` until Φ_6.4.x cites 6.4.1 references, and with the misattribution branch READY, which K-ID cannot express (an adequacy gap of the readiness specification, not an engine change), and C5 at 6.4.1 as schedule-dependent ([R06 §4 T5]; needs D13). If an engine change is needed, H-V is refuted in its strong form. Without a cited `P_sched` for that backend, no σ_R1 claim is made.
- **H-C (configurations).** Verdicts change only through B. Tested by §7.4.
- **H-F (other flight frameworks).** A profile for another framework (e.g. F Prime) would need actor classes, kinds, operations with bindings and LPs, rules, readiness specifications and configuration readers. F Prime's mechanisms were **not examined** in R01–R07, so nothing is claimed.

### 7.7 Openness indicators reported

Following [R06 §5.7]: readiness categories (RC1–RC15) covered vs categories found in the census, with every category that has no authored template reported as not authored (§9.3, expansion rule); the share of instances discovered automatically (sources 1–6, 10–12) vs authored; the share of verdicts that needed a case-specific refinement or a framework rule authored with a case known; held-out and mutant results kept apart from design-case results, and results on v7.0.1 counterparts kept apart from the results on the cases they resemble (§9.3); instances per variant B′, kept apart from the default B; recall per layer (§8.5).

---

## 8. Evaluation plan

### 8.1 Research questions

**Scope** [D] (D9 and D8/P-RECALL, decided 2026-10-15). The first paper's required scope is the five design cases and the controls they directly need. RQ2, RQ4, RQ6 (on the five cases and those controls), RQ7 and RQ8 are required. RQ1, RQ3, RQ5 and RQ9 belong to the **extended evaluation**, a separate procedure that runs after the five-case validation, records the query, the exposure and the freeze of each new source, and whose results neither replace a missing case nor count as results on the cases. The five design cases and their fixes are excluded from the unobserved evaluation sets. Persistent-state, timer and file families, and message-capacity loss, are recorded where a case needs them as model input; they are not independent goals of the first paper.

- **RQ1 Openness** (extended evaluation). How many readiness instances exist in cFE core and the 15 apps under the default B and its variants, per template kind and RC category? What share is decided by certificate, observation, prediction, exploration and confirmation, per (σ, M)? What share stays unknown, and why? What are the concrete/symbolic/⊤ key shares (esp. transmit sites)?
- **RQ2 Development cases.** With the template-only profile, what verdict records does Kairos produce, reported per case on its own phenomenon as detection, classification (classes and refinements per (σ, M, b), and the progress result) and evidence (§8.5), and which change with refinements? No case's result stands for another's (§8.2). R2 at v7.0.1; C1 on the public BP basis (D22); C2 role-reconstructed (P-C2: the result is a role-based validation of the C2 mechanism, not a reproduction of the private system); C3 (two chained instances; the misattribution recorded as a K-ID adequacy gap) and C5 (three instances) at 6.4.1, with Φ_6.4.x outcome maps, bindings and Allowed references (D13 and Q16, decided 2026-10-15); the two App-record windows (C5-shaped residuals at v7.0.1, derived). Each case is evaluated on its own basis against its control (§8.2 per-case subjects; §9.3, per-case paths and controls); a case that is not done at F1 is reported with its reason and the limit it puts on the research scope (P-F1GATE), never through its v7.0.1 counterpart or the result of other code, and a record that a case will not be done does not complete the five-case validation.
- **RQ3 Held-out generalization** (extended evaluation). On the frozen tool (F2), what does Kairos find in sources (a)–(e), with what sampled triaged precision (per instance, with the number of distinct causes among the Reported instances), realized share and unknown share, reported per blind label (§7.5 item 5)?
- **RQ4 Model fidelity.** O2 conformance on every trace and O8 on the sample; obligation failures per iteration; checker pass rate and certificate-mutation kill rate; T-OBS disablement, A-TYPE listing and `DECLARED_TRUSTED` counts; O9 refutations; anchor-audit coverage; closure resolution per rule; havoc and local-choice counts; summary-fallback counts ([J1-14]).
- **RQ5 Measured phenomena** (extended evaluation; [A §8.1]; [J2-12]). Does Kairos classify the order-dependent losses measured in the default bundle (queue-full drops 4–49 per boot; `NoSubscribersCounter` 15–26; overflows of `SBNSubPipe`, `TO_LAB_TLM_PIPE` and `DS_CMD_PIPE`, [BM §0, §3.3]) at both capacity levels (queue depth and MsgLim)? Does it predict which pipes can overflow? Oracles: put outcomes and the four SB counters; `PeakQueueDepth` only as SB-accounted depth. Label: LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm unspecified (caller level); the framework-level judgement (`fw_doc`, `fw_conf`) and the observed loss counts are reported beside it (§1.6 "Three judgements"). The loss counts are recorded with the configuration they were measured in (D19: `msg_max` stays 10), and a capacity-loss result never replaces the validation of a design case (D9).
- **RQ6 Value of each layer and of the loop**, on the five design cases and the controls they need (§8.2, §8.4), reported descriptively (P-STATS); the recall units belong to the extended evaluation.
- **RQ7 Cost and observation effect.** The six costs; the observation-effect comparison between observed and unobserved reference runs (D1, D11); uninstrumented-observable shifts; per-witness perturbation ratios (§8.6).
- **RQ8 Comparison among existing process-level race work** (revision 1.2; before it, a comparison with same-information re-implementations, EXT-20261012 §5.1–§5.2). For each of RacePro, SimRacer, SIMEXPLORER, RRF, DESCRY, SimEvo, ReDPro and SysPro and each of the five cases: the answers to the six analysis questions, each with its evidence, and one result state of `NOT_RUN`, `INAPPLICABLE`, `PATH_NOT_EXECUTED`, `OBSERVATION_GAP`, `ANALYSIS_GAP` or `REPORTED` (§8.3). Original-tool results, re-implementations and Kairos ablations are kept apart. Ablations answer RQ6, never RQ8.
- **RQ9 Recall** (extended evaluation; results hold for the variant set only, D8/P-RECALL). On pre-registered program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs, what share does each layer detect (class detection, §8.5 item 4), leave unknown (by reason), or miss; of the detected, what share is class-correct where a class label exists (P-CLASSGT); and how many certificates are contradicted (soundness failures)? Reported per mutant and per originating fix and cause, and per blind label (§8.5, §8.7).

### 8.2 Subjects

- **Development set** (labels per [LED], [R06 S10]): R2 (current code; observed under gdb in three runs, [R2g]; one cause, 3 violating static sites of the 39 SB sites that pass SB's AppId, 6 task × function contexts, 12 call strings, and 24 occurrences per boot, ES 20, EVS 4, on the ENV basis, [R2g §6]; the count's dependence on the generated module version table is not observed and is to be checked by Kairos's own recorder [I]); C1 on the public BP basis (D22; latent in the default B); C2 role-reconstructed (a constructed provider/consumer pair, a synthetic control; P-C2: a role-based validation of the C2 mechanism, not a reproduction of the private system); C3 and C5 at the 6.4.1 tarballs on the shared derived 6.4.x platform ([LED] X3; D13, decided 2026-10-15: the basis is placed before F1, and its first parse uses this container's 32-bit headers as an analysis-only, differing condition, D13 (b)(i)). For C3 that platform (32-bit x86 Linux, `-m32 -g -O0`, posix/pc-linux, from the 6.4.1 references) is a `deviation` from the Xilinx Microblaze platform its issue reports, whose OS, PSP, OSAL and toolchain are unavailable ([LED §1b], C3 row); C3's RQ2 and H-V results carry that deviation. The first parse with this container's 32-bit headers (D13 (b)(i)) is a further `deviation`: it is not the build host that the 6.4.1 documentation lists, and it is not accepted as a reproduction of the original platform's run. The Q16 correspondences (version-specific states, return values and source locations) are stated and tested; a failure of input or correspondence is not counted as a failure of the technique. C5's reference names no platform ([LED §3.2]), so C5 has no such deviation. **Dependence between cases**: C3 and R2 share a resource family ([LED §3.1]); C3's and C5's fixes are in the same 6.4.1 → 6.4.2 release diff (Trac #40–#42, [LED §3.3]), so their pre/post-fix pairs share one fix history; C5 and the two App-record windows share the K-SELF.es shape; C1, C5 and R2 share the reserve-then-finalize shape ([LED §3.6], all hypotheses) at different layers (C1: OSAL object table, M_impl; C5 and R2: ES app record); every recall operator is shaped on a design case (§8.5 item 1). None of these is counted as independent. **R2 is the only natural, reproduced case on the target revision.** It has no reference class statement: its fixed-order reading rests on code reading (`cases/R2.md` L45) and on three-of-three gdb runs, which do not prove it (§6.4 item 4). **C1, C2, C3 and C5 have reference statements that they are races**: CF #184's title and body, CF's v7.0.1 comment at `cf_cfdp.c:1273` and the fix commit `833fdbb` (C1); cFE #198's body (C2); cFE #73's and #72's titles and the 6.4.2 VDD's "startup race conditions … in cFE build 6.4.1" (C3, C5) ([LED]; the case records). How such a statement maps to the per-(σ, M) classes is not fixed by any reference; the PI decided on 2026-10-15 (P-CLASSGT) that the word is not converted into a tool class automatically. Classification on the development set is reported against the pre-registered expectation sheet of each case (§7.2), and each case's agreement or disagreement with its reference statement is reported as an outcome, not scored as accuracy (§8.5). C3 is a case where they can disagree: its references say "race", while H-V expects `fixed-order` under σ_R1 (§7.6). The schedule-dependent class has no natural ground truth on the target binary. That is why RQ9 exists ([X3-C1]).
- **Per-case subjects and controls** [D] (EXT-20261012 §7; ADR-044). Each design case is evaluated on its own phenomenon, on its own basis and against its control; the paths, stages and decisions are in §9.3, per-case paths and controls. A v7.0.1 counterpart of C1, C2, C3 or C5 tests generic layers only and is never a subject for that case. The design cases' pre/post-fix pairs are development-set controls: non-blind (§7.5 item 5), scored by the movement rule of §8.5 item 7, reported per case under RQ2, and never counted as held-out pairs, since EXT-20261012 §7 step 5 asks for pairs not used in design. A case that is not done at F1 is reported with its reason and the limit it puts on the research scope (P-F1GATE).

  | Case | Bug-bearing subject (U3) | Control (U2) and its expected movement [I] | Basis decision | v7.0.1 counterpart (not a subject for the case) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | R2 | the ENV basis (v7.0.1) | no merged fix, hence no pre/post-fix pair. Under P-R2CTRL (decided 2026-10-15): first the natural regression pair `b9bec567^`/`b9bec567` (c2); secondary the bundle tags `v7.0.0` → v7.0.1 (c1), which differ in the whole distribution and are not read as a change of the start order alone; optionally the change of PR #2820 applied as a no-change control (b; no movement expected). Constructed core orders, the lazy-resolution implementation and model-level mutants (c3, d, e, f) are not part of the first validation and are added only when a question remains that the natural pair cannot answer (§7.4). On a corrected half fixed-order is withdrawn and no protected verdict replaces it (App. A.1 step 15): configuration or revision sensitivity, never schedule evidence | none for its own instances (D1 for runs); P-R2CTRL for the control | none: R2 is on the target revision; its in-basis negative control E-post/E-TBL (§9.3) is part of R2's evidence |
  | C1 | the public BP basis (D22 [D]) at CF `281a941` (the fix base), with the public values of nasa/bp `b051eb0`; the reporter's `2a024d8` is not mixed with the public BP table ([LED §1b]) | CF `833fdbb`, the minimal pair with `281a941`; movement only in the (σ_U∞, M_api) cell (no refinement → `timeout-dependent`, `exh` only) and in the naturalness of the final failure (§8.5 item 7) | D22, decided (public BP basis; candidate pair `281a941` → `833fdbb`; with D18's C1 part); C1 D2–D4 (`cases/C1.md`) | the default B, where both lookups are `inactive(B)` (`cf_def_config.c:60, 78`): the path-inactive negative control |
  | C2 | a role-derived provider/consumer pair with the single-OPERATIONAL counter barrier (the 6.4.2 tarball, or v6.5.0a) or with the old provider ([LED §1b]) | a 2 × 2 of barrier granularity (single-stage counter barrier against the two-stage barrier of v6.6.0a) crossed with provider role (old against new). The four cells are shown as they are and are not counted as four independent real defects. The first control uses a provider whose late initialization completes normally; a provider that can fail is a separate variant (Q22, decided 2026-10-15), and the assumption that initialization does not fail applies to the reconstruction only | P-C2 and Q22 (decided); D13 for a 6.4.2 row | the v7.0.1 barrier's writer set and the K-PHASE census of the bundle |
  | C3 | the 6.4.1 tarball | the 6.4.2 tarball, whose diff carries Trac #40, #41 and #42 together, a fix history shared with C5 ([LED §3.3]); schedule-dependent → protected within b, by `exh` only | D13 (a)–(c), Q16 | the SB-stamped events of TIME's and TBL's TaskInit, inside E-post and counted with R2 |
  | C5 | the 6.4.1 tarball (6.4.0's `cfe_es_apps.c` is identical, [LED §1b]) | the 6.4.2 tarball (Trac #41, the lock-scope extension, in the same diff as C3's fixes); it moves only through `exh`, and to `unknown`, not protected, without S7; `58abd38d^`/`58abd38d` lies on the A.3 line, not #72; it is an auxiliary control (P-RGIT, approved 2026-10-15), is not a substitute for validating the original #72, and is not reported as blind | D13 (decided), Q16 | App. A.3, a residual candidate kept apart from #72 |

- **Census subject**: the v7.0.1 bundle under the default B, plus the variants of §7.4.
- **Recall subjects** (§8.5): program mutants of v7.0.1 and pre/post-fix pairs, labelled synthetic.
- **Held-out**: §7.5 (a)–(e), each counted separately.
- **Units of observation** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.4). Every count names one unit. A count in one unit is never used as the sample size of another, counts are never added across units, and runs are never pooled across program variants, observation methods or pool keys:

  | Unit | Definition |
  |---|---|
  | U1 defect cause | one fault, as stated by its issue or fix text or, for a candidate, by its code-reading account |
  | U2 fix history | one fix commit or release diff, with the issue texts it cites |
  | U3 program variant | one analysed artefact: a source revision or mutant, built into one binary, under one basis B or B′ |
  | U4 static instance | an instance `(κ, t, key, scope)` with `t` a context-qualified template (§1.6) |
  | U5 dynamic occurrence | one occurrence of an instance in one execution, with its ordinal |
  | U6 run | one boot of one U3 under one pool key and one observation method |

  Example, R2 [M]: U1 = 1; no U2 (the only PR was closed unmerged, `cases/R2.md` L49–52), so R2 has no pre/post-fix pair; U3 = the ENV binary; U4 = the instances at 3 violating static sites, reached through 6 task × function contexts and 12 call strings ([R2g §6]); U5 = 24 per boot; U6 = 3 (the gdb runs r2_g1–r2_g3, [R2g §6]). The other development cases have no U5 or U6 yet. Their U2–U4 are recorded per case, and C3 and C5 share one U2.

### 8.3 Comparison with existing process-level race work

**Scope and rules** [D] (revision 1.2; PI decisions of 2026-10-15: the comparison is among existing process-level race work, P-VALGRIND and P-TSAN are excluded, P-NIDHUGG is held, P-PAPERS is approved; ADR-046, ADR-047). The experimental comparison is among existing **process-level** race work and Kairos, on the five design cases. The former baseline table is withdrawn. Thread data-race tools (helgrind, DRD, ThreadSanitizer and the like) and re-implementations in the style of GPredict, happens-before analysis (FastTrack, WCP, SHB, SyncP, M2) or lockset are **not** experimental comparators; they are related work (§1.11). Nothing is installed or rebuilt for them: there is no helgrind/DRD run, no ThreadSanitizer rebuild and no valgrind reference point. Nidhugg is held for the first implementation: it is considered later only if a small-model cross-check of the explorer is needed and the input range it supports is confirmed ([R05 F11]: its README supports LLVM 8–19, and `llvm-18-dev` is an install decision), and it never replaces the comparison among process-level tools (P-NIDHUGG). Three kinds of result are kept apart in names, tables and conclusions:
- **(K1) Original tools**, run as released. `NOT_RUN` is never a false negative ([R06 S5]).
- **(K2) Re-implementations** of a process-level technique from its paper or code, made only when the original cannot be run. They are kept apart from original-tool results. Each records the events it was given beyond what the original collects, whether it extends the technique, who implemented it and which design cases they had inspected. Its result is a result of the re-implementation as built, and its faithfulness to the technique is [U] until the paper is read (P-PAPERS).
- **(K3) Kairos ablations** (§8.4) remove one element of Kairos. An ablation result is not a result of an existing tool, is never evidence that a prior technique cannot express a property, and no statement about what a technique can express is made without a primary source (P-PAPERS).

Reference observations of Kairos's own evaluation (the gdb records [R2g]; counter readings at stop) are labelled as such and are not comparisons. Counter readings show what counters show without order: at v7.0.1 EVS's not-registered branch increments `UnregisteredAppCounter` (`cfe_evs_utils.c:161`), but the `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` branch that R2 takes increments no counter (`cfe_evs.c:185–188`), so counters do not observe R2's rejected requests.

**Roles** [F: repository record, `baselines/availability.md` §1, 2026-10-08; `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.1]. The roles summarize the survey's scope. This specification does not fix them, and no tool's detailed capability is stated from a search excerpt. The survey's evidence grades are paper, code, secondary, excerpt and inferred.

| Tool | Role in the survey | Material on record in the survey | What to check first |
|---|---|---|---|
| RacePro (SOSP 2011) | detection | public code; paper not opened in the survey (the resolved-decisions record states that the author-public text was confirmed in the review before the decision); not runnable in the survey environment, because its recorder is a patch to an i386 Linux 2.6.35 kernel | the paper's §4–§6, the public code, the execution environment, how candidates are generated and validated |
| SimRacer | detection | no implementation found; paper not opened | observation and execution units; resource and order model |
| SIMEXPLORER | detection; exploration range | no implementation found; paper not opened | which system-level events and memory observations it covers |
| RRF | reproduction of a reported race | no implementation found; paper not opened | the locations, events and failure conditions it needs as input; execution control |
| DESCRY | reproduction from a failure log | no implementation found; the paper was read in full | the failure-log requirement; system-call events; dependence model |
| SimEvo | regression-related analysis | no implementation found; paper not opened | version pairs; how system events are used |
| ReDPro | role and detailed model not confirmed | no implementation found; deliberately not filled in the survey | not placed in a detection or reproduction row before its paper is read |
| SysPro | reproduction from bug reports | repository not readable; paper not opened | the events extracted from a report; the real control point and result |

In the survey no tool was run: every cell for the five cases is `NOT_RUN`, an availability record and no result about any case. The access routes that P-PAPERS now opens (author-public copies, institutional repositories, linked scholarly search) may change what is known; the survey's statements are not extended beyond the access it had.

**The six analysis questions** [D]. For every (tool, case) pair the comparison answers the same six questions and records the evidence each answer rests on:
1. Does the original tool collect the **observation events** the case needs?
2. Does it identify different names, IDs and fields as the same resource and the same generation?
3. Does it express the relation between creation, registration or completion of initialization and a dependent use?
4. Does it take as input, or infer, the required order or the allowed error handling?
5. Does it predict or explore orders that did not run, and can it confirm one in a real run?
6. Is its output a candidate, a successful reproduction or an error result, and does that correspond to the case's phenomenon?

Observability, expressibility and actual detection success are never merged in one cell.

**Result states** [D] (the names are proposed in `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.4 and adopted on 2026-10-15; they organize results and do not rank tools). Each (tool, case) cell has one:

| State | Meaning | How it is counted |
|---|---|---|
| `NOT_RUN` | not run, for a reason of environment, material or decision | not counted as a miss |
| `INAPPLICABLE` | the case is outside the technique's stated input or target range | reason and source location reported |
| `PATH_NOT_EXECUTED` | the tool ran but the target phenomenon did not execute | judgement of capability withheld |
| `OBSERVATION_GAP` | the target event executed but the needed information was not recorded | reported as a shortage of observation |
| `ANALYSIS_GAP` | the needed events were recorded but no dependence or candidate was formed | reported as a shortage of analysis for that input and criterion |
| `REPORTED` | a related candidate, reproduction or error was output | the exact correspondence to the case and the real evidence are checked |

`ANALYSIS_GAP` is not assigned in haste when the criterion for "the event was recorded" is itself insufficient. That a tool printed something is not detection of the case: a `REPORTED` cell is checked against the case's phenomenon.

**First comparison: RacePro** [plan, [I]; `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.3]. The first deliverable maps R2's AppId store, the passing of its value, the event request and the error return to RacePro's recorded events and candidate events: which rule of the original code applies, and which events have no counterpart. The same analysis follows for C1, C3 and C5; C2 uses only the conditions known from the role reconstruction (P-C2). The roadmap records, with a citation of RacePro's §4–§5 that is not re-checked here, that RacePro records the order of shared-memory accesses and forms its race candidates from the operations that system calls perform on shared kernel objects and from happens-before relations; its analysis [I] is that the information linking cFS-internal AppIds, name tables and service-initialization state to that candidate model may be missing. "RacePro cannot record shared memory at all" is not used as an explanation. One repository probe is relevant and is labelled as a probe, not a tool result: on OSAL's own unit test, creating a named counting semaphore and looking it up by name issued no system call (`baselines/availability.md` §4; one OSAL unit test, not cFS). That CF's lookup, the ES record writes or R2's rejected event requests issue none is code analysis [I], until the reference cFS is run under a system-call observer (decision A10 there; §6.4 item 47).

**From a confirmed shortage to a Kairos function** [plan, [I]; `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.5]. The table is a research hypothesis and a list of development candidates. It is linked to a comparison result only for a row whose shortage of the existing tool is confirmed, and a function the tool already has is not claimed as a difference.

| Kind of shortage | Candidate Kairos function | Cases to check |
|---|---|---|
| cFS-internal events are not in the candidate-generation input | observe internal API calls, state changes and value passing, and link them to the source | R2, C3, C5 |
| names, IDs and registration information are not tied to one resource | track name, object ID and generation, and the relation of a task to its AppId | C1, C5 |
| startup synchronization is mistaken for actual initialization completion | a rule that separates the start state from an app-specific dependent state | C2 |
| error return and recovery are not told apart | track the returned value, the retry and the final initialization result | C1 |
| only the observed execution is handled | prediction and exploration narrowed to the related tasks and resources, with confirmation on a real run | C1, C3, C5 |
| why a wrong order is fixed is not explained | source-based order evidence, its premises and its trust scope attached to the result | R2 |

**Output of the comparison.** Per (tool, case): the answers to the six questions with the evidence grade of each; the result state; whether the cell comes from an original run (K1), a re-implementation (K2) or an analysis; the events supplied or the technique extended, if any; the reason and the next check. Kairos's own report parts are in §8.5. **There is no paired test and no score between tools:** they differ in role, input and output, and the first case evaluation is descriptive (P-STATS, §8.7).

**Implementation discipline for a K2 re-implementation.** It is frozen at F1 (§7.2), has golden tests taken from the worked examples of the original paper where the paper is accessible (P-PAPERS), and records who implemented it and which design cases they had inspected. A re-implementation that runs over Kairos's traces depends on Kairos's probe plan, and the comparison says so.

**Withdrawn in revision 1.2** (listed so that no document cites them as current): the comparator table (FastTrack-style HB, WCP- and SHB-style relations, the GPredict-style predictor with the same information, the DefUse-style learned invariant, the DCatch-style framework HB, the RacePro-style syscall model over Kairos traces, uncontrolled repeated boots as a comparator, helgrind and DRD, ThreadSanitizer, Nidhugg, GenMC and CBMC rows), and the expressibility table of a readiness specification as a GPredict-style property. A plain state check over the raw R2 records may still be used as an internal correctness check of the recorder's classification; it is not a comparator and not a thread-level comparison group.

### 8.4 Ablations

One element is removed at a time, with the same engine and the same traces ([R06 S11]; [J1-14]; [J3-M8]). An ablation measures what an element adds to Kairos; it says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3; EXT-20261012 §5.1). **Every ablation is reported on the five design cases and the controls they need** (the recall mutants and pairs and the triage sample belong to the extended evaluation): per-unit changes against the full system in occurrence detection (§8.5), class, outcome label and evidence, class detection (§8.5 item 4) where the recall units are used, the decided share, the unknown share by reason, the false candidates removed by O1–O10, and the cost change (fixes [X3-M8]), **descriptively** (P-STATS: no test and no effect size is chosen for the first case evaluation; class-correctness only where a class label exists, P-CLASSGT).

1. **Static-only** (formerly "≈ A"): census + certificates (+ explorer if S7 passed); no traces. *Conditional on S7 for its explorer part.*
2. **Dynamic-only** (formerly "≈ B"): trace HB + prediction + confirmation; no census, no certificates.
3. **No feedback loop**: a single forward pass.
4. **No obligations** (O1–O10 off).
5. **σ_U∞ off**: no refinements.
6. **Robustness tags off**: no VC_R prefilter.
7. **M_api only** vs **M_impl**.
8. **Collapsed identities** (one identity per event).
9. **Atomic APIs** (no LPs; whole API at entry); **no capture points** (value conjuncts at the API's check).
10. **Conditional edges made unconditional** (soft barriers as must-edges).
11. **Implementation-level OSAL locks as synchronization**.
12. **No certificates first** (explore everything). *Conditional on S7.*
13. **No prediction**.
14. **Configuration closure off** (pointer analysis only); **message provenance off**.
15. **Looper restriction off** (thread program order between handlers).
16. **Readiness specifications off** (Kairos's own HB layer reports races without readiness specifications; an ablation of Kairos, not a comparison with a thread data-race tool).
17. **Writer sets from Φ only** vs **from S3a only** vs **S3a + S3b** (the value of two oracles and of points-to).
18. **L1, L2, L3 off**. *Conditional on S7 for L2 and L3.*
19. **`dpor` vs `exh`** on small cones. *Conditional on S7.*
20. **Syscall-only observation; memory-only HB**.
21. **Once and KEY-SEP off** (frontier only on single-spawn actors with no key separation): what the X1-M2/M3 machinery buys.
22. **A-COMM check off** (every prediction labelled `predicted`): how many witnesses change label, and how many of those are refuted on the binary.
23. **Allowed off** (P-ABL-ALLOWED, adopted 2026-10-15): the information about allowed handling is removed, that is, every Allowed entry is dropped and the caller-level Allowed of each instance is derived by §1.6 rule (1), `none` where `appreq` is specified and `unspecified_by_reference` otherwise. This single-element ablation reports the judgement, the share left undecided and the cost change. The definition is the architect's proposal [I]; the PI adopted the ablation by name.
24. **Outcome semantics off** (P-ABL-ALLOWED, adopted 2026-10-15): the meaning of returned values and results is removed, that is, the operations' outcome maps are not applied, so returned codes are not interpreted, entries over `codes` are `unspecified_by_reference`, and O4 has no code to compare; the state-based evaluation of the readiness precondition `Req` at its LPs is kept. It reports the same three things. The definition is the architect's proposal [I].

The former bundle that removed Allowed, outcome semantics, capture points and certificates together, named after GPredict, is **not kept** as a comparison (P-ABL-ALLOWED); ablations 9 (no capture points) and the certificate-related ablations remain single-element ablations of their own.

For the loop ablation, it also reports keys and indirect targets resolved, unknowns removed and model gaps detected per iteration.

### 8.5 Metrics

**Recall protocol** [D] (fixes [X3-C1]); part of the extended evaluation (D8/P-RECALL, D9), whose results hold for the variant set only; frozen before F2 (§7.2):
1. **Operators** ([R06 §5.6]; [R05 §6.2]): remove a wait; move a publication after a spawn; swap object-table entries; empty a retry bound (a single attempt, as at C1's buggy revision); drop a status check. Every operator is shaped on a design case [I], so every operator is recorded as authored with its design cases known, and its mutants at those cases' sites are non-blind (§7.5 item 5): swapping object-table entries on R2 (ES and EVS precede SB since `b9bec567`, [LED §1b]); emptying a retry bound on C1 (the inverse of its fix); moving a publication after a spawn on C5 and App. A.3; removing a wait on C3 (the inverse of its 6.4.2 order guarantee, Trac #40, [LED §1b, §3.3]) and, at v7.0.1, on App. A.3 (the trampoline's task-record poll); dropping a status check on the ignored statuses of R2's chain (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`) and of C5's `CFE_SB_CreatePipe` use. Recall is therefore reported per operator with that shape named, apart from the inverses of history fixes not read during design, and never as recall on order bugs in general (§6.4 item 25).
2. **History query** over the offline cFE (2047 commits, full history, [M] [X3, here]), OSAL and app histories: keywords and a date cut fixed before F2 (e.g. the 23 cFE commits whose full message matches `race|startup sync|order` case-insensitively at the pin are a starting set whose relevance is untriaged (matching subjects only gives 8 case-sensitive or 11 case-insensitive; over the CF history the same keywords select C1's own fix `833fdbb`; [M], read-only `git log`, EXT-20261012 §5.3), and the matched field and case are frozen with the query, [X3 §1]; examples `6932f1fe` "Fix #950, race condition in control request", `58abd38d` "Fix #978, store task parameters in task record"; both were named during design ([X3] L59), and `58abd38d` is also in the C5 case record, so items derived from them are non-blind, §7.5 item 5). For each relevant fix whose code is present at the pin, the **inverse** of the fix is re-created on v7.0.1 as a mutant. Whether the query excludes the design cases' fixes that lie in the queried histories is part of D8 (§7.5 item 4). Where a fix text makes a class statement (e.g. "race condition" in `6932f1fe` and `58abd38d`), it is recorded with the expected verdict (item 3).
3. **Expected verdict** of each mutant: taken from the fix's issue text and commit (POL reference kinds 1–2, L7–8): the readiness specification violated, the instance, the expected direction (e.g. "a use can precede the publication"), and whether the expectation concerns readiness or progress (a documented hang); before F2, the expected-verdict rule takes this last field from each mutant's fix text. Where the fix text makes a class statement in its own words (e.g. `6932f1fe` "race condition in control request"; `58abd38d` "Fixes a race condition …"), that statement is recorded with its citation. The fix text is a ground-truth source for these fields. It is not a source of blindness (§7.2). A recorded class statement does not become a class label automatically (P-CLASSGT, decided 2026-10-15): `fixed-order` and `schedule-dependent` are judged from the stated model, the configuration and real evidence, and for a fix text that makes no statement, or whose statement has no such support, the class label is `unspecified_by_reference`. No class rule or mapping is invented here.
4. **Scoring per mutant and per layer** (merged with the progress counting rule of EXT-20261012 §4.1). This is **class detection**, Kairos's recall score for RQ9; occurrence detection ("Report parts" below) is recorded beside it. A mutant counts as:
   - `detected`: a fixed-order or schedule-dependent class (or `ordinal-split`, where the fix text documents an ordinal-specific defect) with the expected instance and a compatible shape; or, for a mutant whose fix text documents a hang, a **counted** stall witness on the expected instance's progress axis or on the root of its progress chain. A stall witness is counted only if it is `realized`, or a model witness that is confirmable (kind `deadlock-cycle` or `leaked-lock`, neither idle nor terminal-pending) and not choice-dependent (§1.10). Counted witnesses are grouped by wait-for root set, and a root set counts once. A `fixed-order` class counts only because its `¬OK_i` is deciding (§1.10), so a `¬OK_i` without a decision route (`unknown(no_decision_route)`) is never `detected`;
   - `class-correct`, a sub-count of `detected` reported apart: the class per σ equals the mutant's class label. It is scored only for mutants that have a class label supported by a stated model, configuration and real evidence (item 3; P-CLASSGT); for the others class-correctness is `not-scored`, never counted as correct or incorrect;
   - `unknown(reason)`: this includes `progress unknown(not_checked)` and uncounted model stall witnesses for hang-documented mutants; their numbers are reported per qualifier;
   - `missed`: for a hang-documented mutant, protected with `no-stall-within(b)`; for the others, protected, inactive, or no instance.

   Results are reported per layer (static, trace, prediction, exploration, confirmation) and in total, **per mutant and per originating fix (U2) and cause (U1)**, and per blind label (§7.5 item 5). Exact binomial intervals over mutants are reported only with their unit and independence assumption (§8.7). Mutants from one fix, or from one operator at sites of one readiness specification, are not independent, so an interval over mutants is not a statement about fixes or causes. The share of model stall witnesses that are idle, terminal-pending or choice-dependent is reported as a model-precision measure; it is unknown [U] until S7.
5. **Soundness failures.** The following are reported as soundness failures, and confirmed on the mutant binary where possible:
   - a `protected` or `fixed-order` certificate on a mutant whose fix text documents the opposite **readiness** behaviour. This is the program-level test of the certificates; the certificate-mutation kill rate (§4.5) tests only the checker.
   - a `no-stall-within(b)` result, within the model's b, on a mutant whose fix text documents a hang. This is reported as a progress-model failure.

   A certificate on a mutant whose fix text documents only a hang is **not** a soundness failure, because certificates make no progress claim (§6.4 item 26).
6. **Equivalent mutants**: excluded only by a rule pre-registered before F2: the mutated site is `inactive(B)` with its proof, or the mutant's objects are byte-identical to the original's.
7. **Pre-fix/post-fix pairs** ([R05 §6.1 (iii)] L431) where fixes exist: the verdict must move in the direction the fix implies, on the axis the fix concerns: the safety class for a readiness fix, the progress result (a counted stall witness on the pre-fix side, none on the post-fix side within the same b) for a hang fix, and the refinement for a fix that trades an ordering-logic readiness violation for a timing margin (C1: schedule-dependent under σ_U∞ before, timeout-dependent after; `exh` only; the direction holds whether the pre-fix occurrence is NOT_READY_UNJUDGED or VIOLATING, since both are bad, §1.6).
8. **Time to first detection**: for σ_E observation, the number of natural runs (U6, within one pool key) until the first bad witness per mutant; for confirmation, the number of hold attempts; distributions, not means ([R05 §6.5]). Runs within a pool key are not independent samples of a population (same host, persisted state; §8.7), so these distributions are descriptive, and mutants from one fix are reported together.
9. **Build budget**: mutants are built incrementally in **one variant tree** (one full serial build ≈15 min, [R07] L423; one variant tree ≈0.8 GB, [R07] L420; 18 GB free, [R07 §1] and measured here); per mutant only the binaries, the compile DB and the hashes are archived. All mutants are labelled synthetic, never "new bugs".

**Triage** [D] (fixes [X3-M7]; extended evaluation):
- **Reported** = either a class `fixed-order` (whose `¬OK_i` is deciding), `schedule-dependent` or `ordinal-split` with outcome label `violation`, or a counted progress `stall-witness` (label `liveness`; counted once per wait-for root set as in item 4), reported on the root of its progress chain where one exists. Uncounted model stall witnesses are listed separately as progress candidates and are not triaged as reports.
- Scale [M] [X3, here]: 1,208 `CFE_EVS_SendEvent` and 133 `CFE_SB_TransmitMsg`/`Subscribe*` call sites in `cfe/modules` and `apps` (tests and stubs excluded, link membership not filtered), before context qualification; thousands of instances are likely [I]. Triage therefore uses a **stratified random sample per (class, RC category, deciding layer)**; the sample size is a **deviation** (finite rater time). Exact CIs are reported for the sampled instance population of each stratum: a design-based estimate that says nothing about how many causes there are. The number of distinct causes (U1) among the sampled Reported instances is therefore reported beside it.
- **Raters** (P-TRIAGE, decided 2026-10-15): where a quantitative judgement is made, **two independent raters** are used, masked to the producing layer, to the ablation and to each other. This is assessor masking, not dataset blinding (§7.2). At least one rater is not an author of the relevant rules; each rater's prior exposure to the design cases, their fixes and Φ is entered in the exposure record (§7.5 item 5), and disagreements are recorded. The agreement statistic is recorded as an assumption before triage. Until the people are secured, an independent review is not claimed as done and the corresponding quantitative precision is not claimed.
- **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER** verdicts are not rated: their agreement with the SB-counter and put-outcome oracle is reported instead. `not-ready-unjudged` verdicts are counted, not rated (their "correctness" is undefined by construction).
- **Rater effort** is reported with the other costs.

**Other metrics**:
- Instances per class, refinement, σ, M, provider, reach, progress value (with stall clauses, kinds, qualifiers and the counted/uncounted split, counted per wait-for root), evidence (both axes), RC category, source (§7.1) and source tier (§1.6), always with denominators ([R06 R3]). `NOT_RUN`, `NOT_MEASURED` and `NOT_FROZEN` are never zero or "pass" ([R06 R2]).
- Decided share per layer and per (σ, M); unknown share by reason; **anytime curves** (decided share against work units) for prediction, exploration and O8.
- **Report parts of a Kairos result** [D] (EXT-20261012 §5.2, kept in revision 1.2 as Kairos's own report format; `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §11.1). A result for a case, a control or an ablation has three separate parts:
  - **detection**, called **occurrence detection** where it must be told apart from the class detection of item 4: whether a not-ready occurrence of the expected instance is reported with either outcome label (a VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence, or, for a hang-documented item, a counted stall witness), recorded as detected, missed or false alarm, where a false alarm is such a report on a unit whose label expects no bad occurrence, such as the post-fix side of a pair. A certificate or a class without a reported not-ready occurrence is no occurrence detection; it shows under classification and evidence;
  - **classification**: class and refinements per (σ, M, b), the outcome label and the progress result, with the agreement with the pre-registered expectation sheet, the good and bad witnesses with their ordinals, and the reason a result stays undecided (for example an open horizon, no prediction, one side only). Where a reference makes a class statement, agreement or disagreement with it is reported as an outcome and is not scored (P-CLASSGT: the word "race" is not converted into a tool class automatically);
  - **evidence**: route (certificate, `exh` within b, prediction with its label, realized natural or held run); realization and naturalness; bound b; σ and M scope; reach; the trust-ledger part sizes, the steps re-validated via T5 and the proof status of each lemma (§6.2); `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries; the refutable items with their obligation status; whether `kz proofcheck` accepted the certificate.
- **Comparison with process-level work** (§8.3): per (tool, case), the answers to the six analysis questions with the evidence grade of each, and one result state. `NOT_RUN` and `INAPPLICABLE` cells are reported with their reason and are never counted as misses.
- **Modelling cost**: for Kairos's Φ and for each re-implementation, the stated authoring scope, the authoring hours, the number of entries or rules, and the modifications made after the freeze (§7.2).

  A tool without a field reports it as absent, never as zero. Field names are proposals for S9.
- Confirmation: realized (by naturalness), refuted and inconclusive shares; gate-unreached share; holds per confirmation; perturbation ratios.
- Static coverage ([R06 T3]): entry functions resolved; indirect call sites resolved per rule; key shares; message-provenance folding counts.
- Fidelity: as RQ4.
- Cost: work units, wall time and peak memory per stage; the six costs separately; profile size and authoring hours.

### 8.6 Overhead and perturbation protocol

**Observation-effect check** [D] (D1 approved with a corrected explanation, D11; revision 1.2). Running the cFS user and the reference binary unchanged is not the same as having no observation effect, and this specification does not claim that only time changes. The check compares the groups below on record loss, failures to match events (a request without its return, an event without its task), the time to boot and to OPERATIONAL, and externally visible functional results (console lines, counters read at stop, which pipes overflowed). For an internal event that has no counterpart in an unobserved reference run, no conclusion of "no effect" is drawn. Every run records the cFS process's actual user ID, command, priority and scheduling policy, environment and initial state, and whether it is an observation-only run or a controlled run (hold, stop, release). **Order of work (D11, decided 2026-10-15):** group A is re-measured on the kernel in use at S1 before the S1 observation-effect comparison; results measured on an earlier kernel are not added to the current reference runs. The kernel is recorded per run, since it has changed more than once (`-fc-v80` for the gdb reference runs, `-fc-v114` for [R07 §1] and the S1-R2 smoke run, `-fc-v139` on 2026-10-16; §1.7 Env); a kernel change between the re-measurement and the comparison repeats the re-measurement.

Groups ([J3-M6]; [A §5.8]; [B §5.6]):

| Group | Configuration | Separates |
|---|---|---|
| A | unmodified binary, no observer, **re-measured on the kernel in use at S1** (D11) | reference |
| B | uprobes registered with empty BPF programs | trap cost |
| C′ | minimal R2 plan (R2 sites and identity bootstrap only) | cost of a narrow plan |
| C | full plan | recording cost |
| D | C plus holds, **analysed per hold plan** against C runs with the same plan hash (fixes [X3-M2]) | control effect |
| W | the F1 `--wrap` build with gates disabled (only if the F1 arm is added; D3 is deferred) | code-layout effect of the variant |

**Measures, split by what can observe them** (fixes [X3-M2]):
- **Uninstrumented-observable** (compare A against B, C′ and C): from the console, in cFE time, **CORE_READY → OPERATIONAL** (a candidate measure; its spread is not known and is measured in the group-A re-measurement, D11) and the **span of the 15 "Loading file … APP:" lines** ([BM] L278: 2.2–3.3 ms warm); per-app init offsets; the OPERATIONAL line's wall time; counters read at stop ([BM §2.3]): `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` (4–49 in six identical runs, low power), `NoSubscribersCounter` (15–26), `MsgLimitErrorCounter`, `InternalErrorCounter`; which pipes overflowed.
- **Recorder-only** (compare C′, C and D): the per-use R2 set (task, SB function, EID), probe hits per site, drops, observer CPU time and placement.

Distributions are compared, not means. The unit is the run (U6) within one group, stratum and pool key, and runs are treated as exchangeable within interleaved groups only under the assumption recorded in §8.7. **Per-witness perturbation ratios** (§4.13) are reported with every realized witness, with no threshold. Every σ_E frequency is labelled `σ_E(plan P)`; equivalence to unobserved runs is claimed only for the uninstrumented-observable marginals that were measured.

**Controls**:
- **strata**: cold (first run after a VM reboot) and warm are separate strata, never pooled ([BM]: 2570 ms vs 1613–1633 ms; 849 ms vs 2.2–3.3 ms load span);
- groups are run **interleaved**, not in blocks; the interleaving order is recorded as an **assumption** with its reason (the host is shared and drifts, [R07 §1]); no order is presented as from-reference;
- **normalization boots** after every abnormal exit, excluded from pools; Init checked against the canonical S0 by content hash (§4.13);
- observer CPU time and placement recorded in every run.

### 8.7 Statistics and reproducibility

- **N** (the number of runs) is decided **per purpose at S1** (D14, decided 2026-10-15) and is a **deviation** (fixes [X3-M9]): reason, finite resources; effect, bounded results reported with exact intervals or as `unknown(budget)`; necessity, no reference defines it. Three purposes are kept apart: (1) a functional check (does the path run and is it recorded), (2) the observation-effect comparison (§8.6), (3) the evaluation of occurrence frequency. Each N rests on the pilot's results and the resource budget and is **frozen before the corresponding main experiment**; no N is chosen in this specification. Event-based stopping is preferred wherever it exists: exhaustion, fixed point, loop termination.
- **Statistical procedure** [D] (P-STATS, decided 2026-10-15). The first case evaluation uses **descriptive statistics and per-case evidence**: the cause, the fix, the static site, the dynamic occurrence and the run are counted apart (§8.2 units), full distributions and raw counts are reported, and no automatic significance test treats the five cases as independent samples (§8.2, dependence between cases). A separate quantitative experiment (for example the observation-effect comparison over runs, §8.6, or the recall protocol of the extended evaluation) fixes its unit of analysis and sample design first, and its procedure is frozen after that. The tests and intervals named in the table below are **candidates for such an experiment, none chosen**: Mann–Whitney U for times and counts over runs; Fisher's exact test for incidence over runs; standardized effect sizes; exact binomial confidence intervals for proportions. A choice would be recorded as an assumption (methodological literature is not a reference kind under [POL] L5–10). **Every test and interval states its unit (§8.2) and its independence assumption** (EXT-20261012 §5.4). Arcuri and Briand (ICSE 2011 [V-abs]) are cited as guidance; Klees et al.'s about 30 trials are known only from a secondary summary ([R05 §6.5], P) and are not used as a value.
- **Units of analysis and dependence** [D]. The handling of dependence is an assumption under P-STATS. No N, cluster size or threshold is chosen here.

  | Statistic | Unit | Independence assumption to record | Known dependence [I] | Options for a later quantitative experiment (P-STATS; none chosen) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | ablation deltas (§8.4) and, in the extended evaluation, per-unit comparisons | the labelled unit of one evaluation set (development, recall, triage sample, each held-out source); never pooled across sets | units' outcomes are independent given the tools | instances of one readiness specification share one certificate (R2's covers every R2 instance); instances and occurrences of one cause; mutants of one fix or of one operator at sites of one readiness specification | aggregate to U1/U2 clusters before testing; or report per unit with a cluster-level summary; or descriptive only |
  | exact binomial interval for recall (§8.5 item 4) | mutant (U3) | mutants are independent Bernoulli trials | inverses of one fix; one operator at several sites; mutants derived from a design case | report per mutant and per U2/U1; read the interval only for the frozen mutant population |
  | triage precision (§8.5) | sampled instance (U4) in a stratum | the stratified random sampling design | many Reported instances can share one cause | design-based estimate for the instance population; distinct causes reported beside it; no interval over causes unless the PI adopts one |
  | candidate run-level tests, Fisher's exact test and Mann–Whitney U (§8.6) | run (U6) within group, stratum and pool key | runs are exchangeable within interleaved groups | a shared, drifting host; persisted state; one binary per group | test only under the recorded assumption; otherwise "n of N (pool key)" |
  | time to first detection (§8.5 item 8) | runs per mutant within one pool key | as above | as above, and mutants clustered by fix | descriptive distributions |

  No count of U5 occurrences is ever a sample size: R2's 24 occurrences per boot are one cause at 3 static sites (§8.2).
- σ_E frequencies are pooled only over identical pool keys (B hash, plan hash, stratum), never across binaries or observation methods (e.g. R2's gdb runs [R2g] are not pooled with runs of Kairos's own recorder on the same binary).
- Every reported number can be regenerated from stored artifacts: the deterministic replay test of §4.14.

### 8.8 Threats to validity

- **Internal**: profile errors (certificates, checker, O3/O4/O7/O9); Clang/gcc differences (anchor audit); model fidelity (O1, O2, O6, O8); probe perturbation (§8.6, perturbation ratios); kernel drift and a shared host; persistent state across runs (normalization boots); `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries (listed; O9); re-implementations of process-level techniques, if any, that depend on Kairos's probe plan or are written by the authors with cases known (authoring record, §8.3); observation effects that cannot be compared with an unobserved reference run (D1, §8.6).
- **External**: one platform (Linux `SCHED_OTHER`, 4 vCPU, permissive mode, `msg_max` 10, euid ≠ 0); one OSAL backend (POSIX); one bundle version; RT behaviour only in the model (σ_R1[P_sched]; D4: a native real-time arm is a later, separate configuration, and a model result is never labelled a real real-time result); derived cases for C1, C2, C3 and C5 (C2 role-reconstructed); recall measured on synthetic mutants.
- **Construct**: readiness specifications are authored, so the oracle is only as good as its provenance (shown per verdict with the source tier); harm is judged only from caller-side references, and a supplier's documented result never makes an outcome allowed (§1.6); route and capacity losses are not readiness violations without a must-deliver `appreq` (LOSS-UNDER-ORDER), and their observed counts are reported regardless; mutants are inverses of fixes, which may not represent unfixed bugs; an ablation measures Kairos's elements, not what prior techniques can express, and a re-implementation measures itself as built; detection alone cannot show what certificates add, because a plain state check over the raw R2 records finds the observed rejected requests [I]; the tools compared are process-level work that differ in role, input and output, so no score between them is made (§8.3); references call C1, C2, C3 and C5 races, and R2 has no reference class statement, but no reference maps "race" to the per-(σ, M) classes, so no class label is derived from the word (P-CLASSGT, decided 2026-10-15), and a reference statement and a Kairos expectation can disagree (C3 under σ_R1), which is reported as an outcome.
- **Conclusion**: five design cases with one cause each and declared links (C3–R2 resource family; C3–C5 one fix release; C5 and the App-record windows one shape; C1, C5 and R2 the reserve-then-finalize shape at different layers, [LED §3.6]), so they are not five independent samples; only R2 has runs, and most classification differentiators on C1–C5 need the explorer (S7) and the case bases (D22, P-C2, D13); for C1, C2, C3 and C5 the thin MVC gives only generic-layer results on their v7.0.1 counterparts (C1's `inactive(B)` proof, the K-PHASE census, R2's template in C3's TIME/TBL contexts, the App-record window A.3), which are not evidence on those cases (§9.3, per-case paths and controls), so a result confined to R2 is reported as a single-case result; recall mutants and pairs cluster by originating fix and cause; triage intervals hold for instances, not causes; non-blind items are reported apart; possible held-out scarcity; N, sample sizes, caps and the handling of dependence are deviations or recorded assumptions and are reported.

---

## 9. Implementation roadmap

### 9.1 Directory layout (to be created later; nothing created now)

```
kairos/
  CMakeLists.txt                     # find_package(LLVM, Clang) from the pinned LLVM 23 build 1053047a; no MLIR (D6)
  include/kz/schema/kz_schema.h      # X-macro field lists: the only record-layout definition (C, _Static_assert)
  include/kz/core/*.hpp              # KEM types, Φ core expression AST, abstract-domain interface, component interfaces
  profiles/cfs-v7.0.1/*.kzp          # Φ: kinds, ops, effects+per-conjunct LPs, bindings, rules, readiness specifications, constants
  profiles/apps/<app>/*.kzp          # optional app-profile layer (provenance, separate authoring cost)
  profiles/freeze/                   # F1/F2 freeze manifests (hashes): any re-implementation of a process-level technique, evaluation criteria, per-case expectation sheets, recall protocol, exposure record
  lib/core/      container I/O, Φ parser, one evaluator (3 domains), rule engine, typestate, vector clocks, verdicts
  lib/llvm/      basis, extract (AST facts, derives, S3a writers, loop-exit trees), link (IR call graph, DWARF, MC,
                 anchor audit, app-unit probe), skel (two views, provenance, once, predicate extraction), modref (S3b)
  lib/static/    MustBefore (per M), PWB/once, certificates, census, plan (joint debug-register allocator)
  lib/proofcheck/ checker with independent inference (no analysis code shared with lib/static; links lib/core, the trusted T5; no LLVM) + certificate-mutation engine
  lib/trace/     decode, identity, HB (VC, VC_R, looper rule), per-conjunct LP intervals, routing, chains, mining
  lib/conform/   per-actor acceptance (O1/O2); global conformance O8 (on lib/predict's engine, from S5)
  lib/predict/   cone, A-COMM check, explicit search, prefilters, extrapolation, SMT-LIB2 emitter (optional)
  lib/explore/   model builder, exh and dpor engines, σ variants (σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched]), monitor, BPOR, L3 checks
  lib/confirm/   hold plans, ptrace + debug-register holder, attach-at-clone, wait-for oracle, F1 controller, PCT,
                 normalization-boot driver, perturbation ratio
  lib/baselines/ re-implementations of process-level techniques, if any are made (kept apart from original-tool results; frozen at F1; golden tests from the papers' examples)
  probe/bpf/     kz_probe.bpf.c (one generic program per phase; cookie-indexed descriptors; clang-18 -target bpf)
  probe/loader/  raw bpf()/perf_event_open loader (hand-written helper prototypes, <elf.h>), drainer;
                 perf-sample backend; optional tracefs backend
  gate/          kz_wrap.c (C99, cFS flags; F1 only, after D3)
  tools/kz/        core-only CLI: trace|conform|predict|explore|proofcheck|observe|hold|gate|loop|report|dump
  tools/kz-static/ LLVM CLI: basis|extract|link|modref|skel|static|plan
  tests/unit  tests/golden (canonical dumps)  tests/litmus (gcc-built C99 programs on the real OSAL, expected
              classes per σ and M; incl. App. A.8)  tests/mutation (certificates)  tests/property (evaluator
              agreement, HB, containment, loop monotonicity, dpor vs exh)  tests/recall (mutant manifests)
              tests/cfs (integration)
  docs/          formal model, Φ reference (grammar + semantics), provenance rules, ADRs (§11), diagrams (§12)
```

### 9.2 Engineering discipline (how messy code is avoided)

- **Interfaces first.** `kz_schema.h`, the Φ grammar **and its semantics** (core expressions, bindings, patterns), the verdict record and the component interfaces are reviewed before any implementation (S0).
- **One library per component plus a thin CLI subcommand**; components communicate only through KZ containers. Two binaries (§2.2).
- **No generated code** ([R06 K8]): X-macros instead of a schema compiler; no TableGen/ODS (MLIR is excluded, D6); a generic data-driven BPF program; handwritten gate wrappers; the explorer **interprets** the model.
- **No case-specific code; no bare constants** (§7.2 lints).
- **Tests at six levels**: unit tests; golden tests with canonical dumps (every Φ rule has one); a **litmus suite** on the real OSAL with expected classes per σ and M (incl. App. A.8); **property tests** (evaluator agreement across the three domains; HB transitivity; prefix containment (L-CONT (a), (b)) on small random models, with LP-TW as a negative test of maximal containment; loop monotonicity; `dpor` vs `exh`); **certificate-mutation tests**; and the **recall suite** of program mutants (§8.5).
- LLVM conventions for code linked with LLVM (no exceptions; `LLVM_ENABLE_EH=OFF`, [R07 §2.1]).
- **Provenance in every artifact**: B, Φ, tool and parent hashes; LLVM `1053047a`; gcc 13.3.0; kernel; backend; fidelity class.
- **Deterministic outputs**: sorted, hashed, work-unit budgets; golden tests and replay are stable.

### 9.3 Stages and exit gates

Each stage has its own exit gate and its own claims (claims table below); whether a cut is publishable depends on differentiation and evaluation, not on the stages completed (EXT-20261012 §9). The recorder stays narrow and model-driven (v0.1 §15; [J3-M5]; [J1-11]; [J2-15]). **The thin minimum viable core (MVC) is S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a** (fixes [X3-M5]); since P-LADDER was approved on 2026-10-15 it is a name for the claims table and the cut list, not a first completion condition. Effort estimates are **[I] planning guesses by the architect, not research conditions, not measured, and not used by any tool**; they exist so that the PI can cut scope.

**First target: the validation ladder** (ADR-044; PI decision P-LADDER, approved 2026-10-15: the ladder V0–V4 and the case paths are adopted; the R2 processing path is implemented in full first while the reference sources and configurations of the other cases are prepared; the earlier large minimal bundle as a whole is not required as the first completion condition). The first target is not the whole thin MVC but rung **V1** of the ladder below: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2 and S2-R2 that take from the stage gates only the items R2's chain needs. The full gates of S0–S3a are unchanged and form rung **V1b**, which is not a condition for completing V1 and is not required in full before V2 or V3 take the items they name. The ladder orders the dynamic end-to-end pipeline only by stage dependencies, inputs and decisions; R2's control (V2) and C1's rung (V3) run in parallel, and the order of EXT-20261012 §7 is the order in which results are reported. Before any V1 result is worded as a certificate, the lemma obligations of that wording are written and reviewed (V0-P). Case tracks run beside it, so that every design case is validated at its cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned public revisions and a frozen expectation sheet for its bug-bearing and fixed revisions) and enters at tool level after its basis decision and the stages its track row names (the ladder table and the per-case table below). A result on a v7.0.1 counterpart of C1, C2, C3 or C5 is never reported as evidence on that case. **The estimates are not summed here.** The external review's sum of the S0–S3a estimates (19–28 person-weeks) is correct arithmetic on [I] inputs. It is effort, not duration (S2 and S3a can overlap, §9.4), and it covers the thin MVC only. No estimate exists for V1 or for any other rung; that is `unspecified_by_reference` and is P-LADDER (b).

**Validation ladder and case tracks** [D] (ADR-044). The paper track (reading at pinned revisions, and an expectation sheet frozen by hash before the first tool output on each case, for R2, C1, C2, C3 and C5) needs no stage and no decision. Every tool output on a case or control is compared with its frozen sheet; a mismatch is investigated and reported, never tuned. The rungs are ordered only by the stage dependencies of the stage table, the inputs each rung names and the decisions it needs: V2 and V3 both follow V1, take from V1b only the items they name (§9.4), and do not wait for each other, and the order of EXT-20261012 §7 (step 3, then step 4) is the order in which their results are reported. **Expansion.** SMT (P-Z3: not a condition of the first implementation), DPOR (`dpor` after `exh` in S7) and Φ templates that no rung, track or step needs are added only after a rung (V1–V3), a case track (VP-D3, VP-D4, VP-D5) or an ENV-window step (S5 and S6 on the App-record windows, S7 on the development cones) shows a need. A kind is needed when a gate item of a rung or track, or a development cone of the S7 kill criterion (§4.10 item 9), names it. Kinds without an authored template are reported as not authored in the openness indicators (§7.7) and in RQ1's denominators, never omitted. MLIR is not an expansion: it is excluded from this roadmap (D6).

**V1 sub-gates** [D]. Each item is taken from the stage gate named; the rest of that gate is V1b's.

**E-post and E-TBL**, R2's in-basis negative control (used by V1, V1b and the per-case table). **E-post** is the set of K-ID instances in the default B whose value conjunct reads SB's identity cell `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` after SB's publication at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, in TBL, TIME, SB itself and the apps: the "post-publication SB-AppId uses" of §7.3 (row "SB/TBL identity never published"). **E-TBL** is the set of K-ID instances whose value conjunct reads TBL's identity cell, published at `cfe_tbl_task.c:196`: the "TBL identity uses" of the same row. Expected [I]: neither carries a fixed-order verdict, and both are open and chained to the App. A.2 root (§7.3). C3's v7.0.1 site, the SB-stamped events of TIME's and TBL's TaskInit, lies inside E-post and is counted with R2 (§8.2); E-TBL is another cell and holds no C3 instance. The negative-control list is kept per template and split by cell.

**Per-case paths and controls** [D] (ADR-044). For each design case: the cheapest reference-based level now (reading at pinned public revisions and a frozen, hashed expectation sheet; no implementation and no decision); the first tool step that needs no new decision, and what it tests; the first tool step on the case's own phenomenon, with its decision; and the control. No cell is a "safe" reading.

**Cut list, in the order scope would be dropped** (lowest claim value first) [I]: the S10 arms for D3 and D4 → S3b → S7 (explorer; without it no refinement and no model progress result is established, and only σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact remain) → O8 → S6 → S5. The recall protocol belongs to the extended evaluation (D8/P-RECALL) and is scheduled apart from the five-case validation.

The ladder's V1 and the paper track for all five cases are never cut, nor is the part of S0–S3a that a case's tool-level step needs. Every cut is reported with the case evidence it removes: cutting the 6.4.x basis arm of S10 removes C3's and C5's tool-level evidence and C2's 6.4.2 row, and is reported as a limit on the research scope (the basis is placed before F1, D13 (c), P-F1GATE); cutting the F1 gates or the native σ_R1 arm removes no case evidence; cutting S7 leaves C1's refinements and progress rows, C2's refinements, and the fixed sides of C3 and C5 unanswerable; cutting S5 or S6 removes C1's predicted and held arms. A cut that leaves tool-level evidence on R2 alone is reported as a single-case result (ADR-042).

### 9.4 What is built first

**Build order: the validation ladder** (ADR-044; approved as P-LADDER on 2026-10-15). The R2 processing path is built in full first, and the reference sources, configurations and expectation sheets of the other cases are prepared in parallel, without waiting for R2's completion. V0 first: the formal amendment (ADR-039–ADR-043) is adopted, and before any certificate or transfer wording is used the lemma obligations of that wording are written and reviewed (V0-P, §9.3 ladder table). Then V1: S0-R2 (formats, the K-ID subset of Φ, the evaluator's concrete and abstract domains), then S3a-R2 and S1-R2, which can overlap (S3a-R2 needs only S0-R2, plus S1-R2's run for reach), then S2-R2. V1b (the rest of the S0–S3a gates) overlaps V1. V2 and V3 then run in parallel and do not wait for each other: V2 needs from V1b only the App. A.2 instance of SB's `GetAppID`; V3 needs from V1b the app-unit closure and the app `.so` probe registration (CF is an app), plus C1's basis (D22, decided; to be built), S5–S7 for classes and S6 with P-C1HOLD for holds. Their results are reported in the order of EXT-20261012 §7 (step 3, then step 4); that is a reporting order, not a dependency. V4 lies inside S9. The case tracks run alongside the ladder: their reference-based level runs now, and their tool-level steps start after their basis decisions and the stages their track rows name (§9.3). At stage level the reasons are unchanged: S0 comes first because every later component depends on the shared formal core and the formats; S1 because ground truth must come from the new recorder before any analysis is trusted; S2 and S3a can overlap, so the stage estimates add up to effort, not to calendar time. The core that the stage plan never cuts (the R2 certificate, the census and the recorder) does not wait for prediction, confirmation or the explorer; recall for those layers belongs to the extended evaluation, which is scheduled apart from the five-case validation (D8/P-RECALL, §8.1). With tool-level evidence on R2 alone it is a single-case result (ADR-042), and whether it is publishable depends on differentiation and evaluation (EXT-20261012 §9), not on its completion. Evidence on C1's, C2's, C3's and C5's own phenomena waits for those stages and for each case's basis decision (§9.3, per-case paths and controls).
