## 10. Risks and open decisions for the PI

### 10.1 Decisions requested and their resolution (2026-10-15)

The full list, with options, references and what each decision blocks, is in **`PI_DECISIONS.md`**, which also gives each decision's resolved value, date (2026-10-15) and source (`PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md`, here "the resolved-decisions record"). A resolution is a **decision [D]** on the research plan, its scope and its wording. It is not a verified fact about a tool, a paper or a source, and it is not an execution record: nothing resolved here has been built or run. A decision that the record leaves open stays open: D18 except its C1 part, P-TRACEFS, and the details of D8/P-RECALL named in its row. IDs: v1's D-numbers are kept; renamed or new decisions use `P-` names, except D20–D22 and the flagged items QT-n and Qn of EXT-20261012.

The R2 processing path is built in full first while the reference sources and configurations of the other cases are prepared. The earlier large minimal bundle as a whole is not required as the first completion condition. No effort figure exists for any rung. | — |

The five design cases and their fixes are excluded from the unobserved evaluation sets; the query, the exposure and the freeze of each new source are recorded; results on variant examples are results on the variant set only. **Still open:** the mutation operators, the history query (keywords, matched field, date cut), the expected-verdict and equivalent-mutant rules, who runs the queries, and whether other items that are only named in this text are excluded. | the extended evaluation; F2 |
| P-TRIAGE | raters, masking, sample sizes, rater exposure | **Two independent raters for any quantitative judgement.** At least one is not an author of the relevant rules; prior exposure and disagreements are recorded; no independent review is claimed as done, and no corresponding quantitative precision is claimed, before the people are secured. | the extended evaluation |
| D9 | scope of the first paper | **Limited to the five cases and the controls they directly need.** Persistent-state, timer and file families are recorded as model input where a case needs them and are not independent goals; message-capacity loss does not replace the validation of a case. | — |
| D10 | memory-model stance | **Assumptions are marked and the guarantee is limited:** every result that uses the event-granularity visibility assumption says so; the x86 TSO assumption alone does not establish the correctness of the source language, the compiler or the whole execution; where it is unclear whether the assumption holds, the result is undecided. | — |
| D12 | citing the retired recorder's observations | **CORD results are not evidence in current documents.** The R2 evidence is the raw gdb records. The reference entry `[R2c]` and every citation of it were removed from this text in revision 1.2. | §0.11 |
| D16 | derived App-record probes | **Approved, labelled derived.** C5's original phenomenon is validated first; the present residual windows are a separate validation; neither is confused with C1's holds (P-C1HOLD). | — |
| D19 | `msg_max` variant | **The current reference value 10 is kept;** queue loss is recorded with the configuration. If a loss obstructs a target path, a sensitivity configuration is designed separately; the value is never changed silently. | — |
| D11 | re-baseline group A on `-fc-v114` (the kernel when the question was asked) | **Required, and before the S1 observation-effect comparison.** Results measured on the earlier kernel are not added to the current reference runs. Applied here as the kernel in use at S1, recorded per run: the VM kernel has changed again (`uname -r` reads `6.18.44-fc-v139` on 2026-10-16 [M]; §1.7 Env, §8.6). | — |
| D17 | stop method and normalization boots | **(a):** the CI ES POWERON restart command is the stop for normal terminations and the normalization boot after abnormal terminations; the initial state and the termination record are kept. POWERON does not reset every persisted file, file cache or shared file system. | — |
| D13 | 6.4.x derived platform for C3/C5 and H-V, with Q16 | **(a) accepted:** an analysis-only parse of a reconstructed compile DB counts as code-reading evidence, as an analysis-only differing condition and not as a reproduction of the original platform's run. **(b) starts with (i):** this container's 32-bit headers, recorded as a deviation (they are not the build host that the 6.4.1 documentation lists); if the relevant paths or type interpretations prove sensitive to headers or environment, it switches to **(ii)**, the literature host's sysroot (an install decision), and the affected results are withheld until the sensitivity is resolved. **(c) before F1.** Q16: the version-by-version correspondence of states, return values and source locations is stated and tested; a failure of input or correspondence is not counted as a failure of the technique; an actual execution on a past code base is reported as a product separate from analysis-only work. | the sysroot only if (i) proves sensitive |
| D18 | configuration and input variants (§7.4); C1's part is in D22 | **Open.** The resolved-decisions record does not address it; recommendation unchanged: latent-path tables and `B_restart` first. | §7.4 results (S9); P-R2CTRL options (c3), (d) if they are added |
| D14 | the number of runs N | **Decided per purpose at S1** (a functional check, the observation-effect comparison, the evaluation of occurrence frequency), from the pilot and the resource budget, and frozen before the corresponding main experiment. A deviation; no value is chosen here. | the values, at S1 |
| D15 | work-unit caps | **Work-unit budgets in deterministic units** (predicted nodes, search states, transitions); each cap is frozen after that stage's pilot; an excess is reported `unknown(budget)`. No value is chosen here. | the values, before S5 and the S7 review |
| D20 | option A or B for the first implementation | **A.** Certificates prove safety statements only; progress is reported separately from bounded exhaustive search or from permanent-wait evidence; a safety verdict is never read as the absence of deadlock. The completion conditions (§0.9) apply: a proof status per lemma, no "proved" for a listed obligation, the early-return `NOT_EVALUATED` consistent with the transfer to real runs, L-CONT (c) in the R2 ledger, checked steps told apart from trusted extracted facts. | — |
| D21 | the cone of the S7 kill criterion | **The closed cone** (the cone that contains the tasks and resources that affect the use). A result on a smaller cone is not used with the same guarantee. | — |
| D22 | C1's bases B′ and B′_buggy | **public BP basis; candidate pair CF `281a941` → `833fdbb`.** Public values of nasa/bp `b051eb0` [F] (`cases/C1.md` F9): the semaphore names `BP0_tsem`/`BP1_tsem`, the startup order CF then BP, the configured priorities CF 65 and BP 55. They are not values of the original reporter's environment. Unconfirmed [U]: the full version combination of cFS, cFE, OSAL and PSP and the content of the mutable references then; whether the reporter's provider is the public BP configuration; the real-time priority application, CPU count and affinity; the configuration and code differences of any move to other versions. Order of work: the version compatibility of the public integration first; if it cannot be built as it stands, the move to other versions is a derived basis with every difference recorded; the reporter's `2a024d8` is not mixed with the public BP table; the v7.0.1 default (empty names) is the path-inactive negative control only; D18's C1 part is folded in; option (iv), a configured name with no provider for arm A4, only if the PI wants that arm realized, with every condition `unspecified_by_reference` and set as a recorded deviation | V3 (needed before it, not by S9); C1 rows of App. A.4 and §7.3 |
| QT-2 | STATUS decision T2, re-asked | **Approved:** the provenance kind `requirement`; the SB rule for a send with no subscriber and R2's invalid-AppId error are kept apart (§1.6). | — |
| QT-3 | the three-judgement design of ADR-043 | **Approved:** the framework's documentation and API behaviour, the caller's requirement and the observed result are three layers; that a framework allows a returned result does not by itself satisfy the caller's requirement. | — |
| QT-4 | whether `rule`-only Allowed entries decide | **Strict alternative:** a rule alone never makes an occurrence accepted; entries without an original-source basis stay unjudged; code state changes and error returns are still reported (§1.6). | — |
| QT-5 | precedence among caller-level texts | **The conflict is stated and the final judgement withheld.** The document versions, targets and scopes are checked first; no priority is invented while the conflict remains. | — |
| QT-9 | K-SPAWN.osal and K-LOCKBAL | **Separated as an implementation-defect family.** No researcher requirement is added to make them readiness violations (violations of a caller requirement); only the part linked to an order violation is reported, on its own basis. | — |
| Q15 | the normal continuation of `returned_and_tested` | **Strict alternative:** the fact that the code has a follow-up path is told apart from the judgement that the path is an allowed recovery; without a basis the judgement is not made (§1.6). | — |
| P-NAME | the tool's name | **Kairos is kept as the working name.** A collision check is made just before publication; no expansion of the name and no academic meaning is invented. | publication |
| P-R2CTRL | R2's control basis B′ for V2 | **(c2) first, (c1) secondary, (b) optional,** worded as "the change applied as a no-change control" and not as "PR reverted". Directly built core orders (c3, d), the lazy-resolution implementation (e) and model-level mutants (f) are not part of the first validation; they are added when a question remains that the natural pair cannot answer. The criterion is whether the verdict is withdrawn where the order evidence differs; the withdrawal alone does not establish overall safety, and a configuration or version difference is no proof of schedule dependence of one binary. | — |
| P-C1HOLD | C1's holds | **Hold the provider immediately before its creation and release it on an event** (retry exhaustion or the final initialization result). The retry count and the delay are read from the chosen CF version. The wall-clock watchdog and the stop conditions are recorded before the main experiment; the retry count times the delay is not taken as a safe wall-clock limit. A hold-made failure is told apart from a natural run, and a timeout alone never decides a permanent deadlock. The RESERVED-window experiment moves to a follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate item that is not counted among the five cases. | — |
| P-C2 and Q22 | C2's role-derived pair; whether the provider's late init may fail | **Role reconstruction.** It starts with code reading and static analysis and goes on to the reconstruction experiment; the provider's late initialization and the consumer's dependent use are told apart; the request mode, initialization content, priority and timeout are reconstruction choices, recorded as such and not cited as the reporter's values; the 2 × 2 is prepared. **Q22:** the first control uses a provider whose late initialization completes normally; a failure-capable variant is separate; the assumption that initialization does not fail applies to the reconstruction only. The result is named a **role-based validation of the C2 mechanism**; the four cells are not four independent real defects. | — |
| P-RGIT | `58abd38d^`/`58abd38d` on the A.3 line | **Approved as an auxiliary control,** not a substitute for validating the original C5 (#72), whose past-version lock-gap validation goes first. It is run after the extra input-correspondence cost is confirmed, and it is not reported as blind. | — |
| P-F1GATE | what F1 waits for | **Approved.** The choices and required details of D13, D22 and P-C2 are recorded before F1. Each case has its own reference configuration, expectation sheet, needed functions and validation stages; for a case not done, the reason and the limit on the research scope are stated, and a record that a case will not be done does not complete the five-case validation. Expectation sheets are frozen before each case's first output; the evaluation engine and rules are frozen before the main evaluation. | — |
| P-D18GATES | gates that depend on C1's B′ and the case cones | **(a)** the small C1 example is allowed as a development test and does not replace the validation of the real C1. **(b)** a preliminary performance judgement is separated from the final judgement: the explorer's cost may be measured when only some cases are ready; the final evaluation and adoption judgement includes the relevant scope of the prepared cases, and missing cases are recorded as limits. | — |
| P-TRACEFS | allow tracefs dynamic events | **Open.** The resolved-decisions record does not address it; recommendation unchanged: no, perf-sample backend instead. | nothing |
| P-STATS | the statistical procedure | **The first case evaluation uses descriptive statistics and per-case evidence.** Cause, fix, static site, occurrence and run are counted apart; no automatic significance test treats the five cases as independent samples; a separate quantitative experiment fixes its unit and sample design first and its procedure is frozen then (§8.7). | — |
| P-CLASSGT | how a reference's class statement maps to the per-(σ, M) classes | **The source's word "race" is not converted into a tool class automatically.** `fixed-order` and `schedule-dependent` are judged from the stated model, the configuration and real evidence; agreement with the source is reported separately; no accuracy denominator is made without a basis. | — |
| P-ABL-ALLOWED | single-element ablations of Allowed and outcome semantics | **Two single-element ablations are adopted** (Allowed information removed; returned-value and result semantics removed), reporting the judgement, the undecided share and the cost change. The bundle named after GPredict is not kept as a comparison. | — |

### 10.2 Risks

| Risk | Likelihood / impact [I] | Mitigation | Retired by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S3a writer extraction too coarse: many conjuncts DISABLED by A-TYPE listings or escapes, so few certificates | medium / high | Φ effects with keys and guards; transition and key statuses; allocation records; S3b only if measured necessary; disablement counts reported | S3a O7 report |
| A `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entry (A-FRESH, A-OWNER, relational guards) is wrong | low / high | listed per certificate; O9 and O3/O4 refute; ablation 17 | S4 |
| Skeleton infidelity (havoc and star closure too coarse; inlining fallback) | medium / high | O1, O2 on every trace; O8 from S5; fallback counts; `unknown` on doubt | S4–S5 |
| Predicate extraction fails on a needed loop | medium / medium | recognized forms (a)–(c); else `unmodelled-sync`; R19's loop is form (b) | S3a |
| Message provenance too coarse (⊤ transmit keys) | medium / medium | header-MsgId analysis; table-packet decoder; key shares reported; RC5 partial until measured | S3a |
| Observation perturbation, and an observation effect that cannot be compared with an unobserved reference run | medium / medium | entry-only tokens; R-e capture; calibration first; per-witness ratios; `σ_E(plan P)` labels; the observation-effect comparison of §8.6 (D1); no conclusion of "no effect" for internal events without a reference | S1 |
| eBPF features not probed (cookies, ring buffer, task storage, stack capture) | medium / medium | perf-sample backend; P-LIBBPF | S1 |
| Persistent-state contamination across runs | high (by design of confirmations) / high | normalization boots; canonical Init hash; strata | S1, S6 |
| Predictor and explorer state explosion | high / medium | cones, L1–L3, key-level dependence, windows; work-unit caps with anytime curves; kill criterion | S5, S7 |
| `dpor` remains unverified (T-JACM status P) | medium / low | `exh` mode; label; P-PAPERS | S7 |
| Holds perturb other threads (binsem 2 s timedlock, timer callbacks, signals, attach at clone) | low / medium | gate-placement rules; joint allocator; watchdogs; `timeout-exercised` label; costs measured | S6 |
| Recall subjects scarce or unrepresentative (extended evaluation) | medium / medium | history query plus operators; pre/post-fix pairs; labelled synthetic; equivalent-mutant rule; recall reported per operator with the design case it is shaped on (§8.5 item 1); non-blind items labelled (§7.5 item 5) | S9 |
| Triage effort at census scale (extended evaluation) | high / medium | stratified sampling; oracle agreement for LOSS-UNDER-ORDER; rater effort reported | S9 |
| Profile authoring effort and errors | medium / medium | per-API templates; header extraction; loop-, symmetry- and context-derived templates; effort measured | S3a, S9 |
| Integration breadth (two binaries, ~18 subcommands, three interpreters) | medium / high | the validation ladder (first target V1, an R2 slice with sub-gates; the full thin-MVC gates as V1b); staged gates; cut list; one formal core | V1, S3a |
| Route/capacity verdicts read as "bugs" | medium / reputational | LOSS-UNDER-ORDER; harm unspecified without a must-deliver `appreq`; the framework-level judgement and the observed loss reported beside it (§1.6) | report review |
| 6.4.1 bases lack a compile DB | high / medium (H-V, RQ2 for C3/C5) | reconstruct from the 6.4.x make system; an analysis-only parse is accepted as an analysis-only, differing condition (D13 (a)); the first parse uses this container's 32-bit headers (D13 (b)(i)) and every input is listed with its reference status; the basis is placed before F1 (D13 (c)) | VP-D3 (before F1) |
| Kernel drift and host sharing | medium / medium | re-baseline; interleaved groups; strata; work-unit budgets for analyses | S1 |
| Validation concentrated on one case: the first rungs exercise one cause and one route (certificate and trace), and an R2 success is read as general validation | high / high | case tracks with their decisions taken on 2026-10-15; the per-case paths and controls written into §9.3; C1's rung (V3) not waiting for R2's control (V2); the expansion rule triggered by any rung, case track or ENV-window step; counterpart results never reported as case evidence; every case reported on its own phenomenon; F1 tied to the case decisions (P-F1GATE); a single-case result reported as such (ADR-042) | P-F1GATE; V4 |
| R2's control is a natural revision pair and not a fix; no referenced corrected-order basis exists (EXT-20261012 §7 step 3) | high / medium | P-R2CTRL decided: (c2) first, (c1) secondary, (b) optional, the other options only when a question remains; the expected record pre-registered (App. A.1 step 15); results labelled configuration or revision sensitivity; a withdrawn verdict is not read as overall safety | V2 |
| The existing process-level tools cannot be run (every survey cell is `NOT_RUN`), so the comparison of §8.3 is mostly an analysis from papers and code | high / medium | the six questions with the evidence grade of each answer; `NOT_RUN` never counted as a miss; the access routes of P-PAPERS first; re-implementations kept apart from original results | V4a |
| The 6.4.x parse with this container's headers is sensitive to the headers (D13 (b)(i)) | medium / medium | check the generation settings, include list, integer and pointer sizes and the paths that macros select; switch to the literature host's sysroot (an install decision); withhold the affected results | VP-D3 |
| C2's role reconstruction is read as a reproduction of the private system, or the cells of the 2 × 2 as four defects | medium / high | the result name "role-based validation of the C2 mechanism"; reconstruction choices recorded as such; the first control with a normally completing provider (Q22) | VP-D4 |
| A hold-made failure of C1 is read as a natural one, or a timeout as a permanent deadlock | medium / high | event-based release; labels; the wall-clock watchdog and the stop conditions recorded before the main experiment (P-C1HOLD) | V3 |

| C1's public BP basis may not build as it stands (nasa/bp's CI installs TinyCBOR into `/`, merges a mutable PSP branch and needs bplib; the CF pair and the bundle versions may be incompatible with it) | medium / high | the version compatibility of the public integration is checked first; a move to other versions is a derived basis with every difference recorded; every unreferenced value is a recorded deviation or `unspecified_by_reference`; installs are package decisions | D22 (decided; the check is part of V3) |
| The S5 gate and the S7 kill criterion depend on C1's B′ being built (a C1-derived trace; two of five cones) | high if the basis lags / medium | P-D18GATES | P-D18GATES |

---

## 11. Architecture decision records

Format: **Context**; **Decision**; **Alternatives**; **Consequences**. Status: *accepted for SPEC FINAL*. ADRs that depend on a PI decision say so; ADRs changed by the reviews name the issue.

**ADR-001 — C/C++ core and typed artifacts; no Python/JSON pipeline.** Context: the user discarded the Python/JSON prototype (CORD, [R06 K7]). Decision: all components in C++17 (C for BPF and gate objects); all artifacts in the typed KZ container; JSON appears only as CMake's input `compile_commands.json`. Alternatives: Python orchestration over C++ kernels; a schema compiler. Consequences: one toolchain (LLVM 23 + gcc 13.3 + clang-18 for BPF); review via `kz dump`; no generated code.

**ADR-002 — One formal core interpreted three ways.** Context: static, trace and model layers must agree ([J1], [J2], [J3]). Decision: one KEM type set, one Φ, **one evaluator with three abstract domains** (except that `kz proofcheck` decides `Pred_code ⇒ C` with its own symbolic procedure; ADR-041) (revised by [X3-M11]), and a concretization relation `t ⊒ e`. Alternatives: separate models per layer. Consequences: O1–O10 are possible; `libkz-core` stays LLVM-free; an agreement property test is mandatory.

**ADR-003 — Φ is an interpreted DSL with provenance and a specified semantics.** Context: framework knowledge must be versioned, cited and reusable (H-V); the three interpreters need one meaning per predicate ([X3-M11]). Decision: a hand-parsed language with three sublanguages fixed at S0: a typed core expression language over resource state (bounded quantifiers over declared kinds only), bindings from C fields to resources (checked by O7), and an AST-pattern language with a match semantics. Alternatives: C++ rule classes; MLIR ODS; quoted predicate strings (v1). Consequences: engines are framework-neutral; a Φ edit restarts the loop.

**ADR-004 — KZ container with X-macro schema, typed references and per-section versions.** Context: BPF, gate objects and C++ must share layouts; v1's layout had implicit padding and bare references ([X3-m6]). Decision: X-macro field lists expanded into struct, dump printer and offset asserts; explicit reserved fields; `sizeof` equals the sum of fields; typed references `(artifact sha256, section, index)`; Merkle parent hashes. Alternatives: schema compiler; JSON lines. Consequences: layout changes touch one list; hashes never depend on uninitialized bytes.

**ADR-005 — Static ordering is a MustBefore greatest fixpoint on the certificate view.** Context: v1 cut message loops at h while claiming b = ∞ ([X1-C1]); base cases and W_o recomputation were unstated ([X1-m2]). Decision: T-PO, T-SPAWN, T-OBS, T-TERM on `Sk^cert` (every unbounded loop star-closed); ∅ at every root; W_o recomputed per iteration with four checked exclusions (OWN-NOPATH, PWB, KEY-SEP); no lock rule. Alternatives: may-HB over templates (C); h-cut skeletons (v1). Consequences: sound but incomplete; loops cost precision (frontier nodes after loops only).

**ADR-006 — Certificates by PWB with once(τ), a checker that re-validates inference relative to a stated trusted base, and program-level falsification.** Context: dominance fails on early returns ([J2-1]); PWB needs one instance per actor ([X1-M2]); v1's R2 certificate missed a writer ([X2-01]). Decision: FRONTIER and ONCE-PWB rules with proved once facts; MUST_KEY must-writers for PROTECTED; SCOPE exclusion; `kz proofcheck` re-validates the inference steps relative to a stated trusted base (`facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, closure lists, Φ, `libkz-core`, run artifacts), recorded per certificate in a trust ledger (revised by ADR-041); certificates prove safety statements only (ADR-039); certificate mutations test the checker; program mutants test the analysis. Alternatives: `u ∈ MB(x)` (A); trace-local VC_R certificates (B). Consequences: R2 certifiable with listed trusted entries; once analysis and checker are extra code.

**ADR-007 — Writer sets from S3a AST extraction plus Φ effects, with key statuses and allocation records.** Context: v1 specified a from-scratch Andersen analysis over opaque-pointer IR inside the MVC ([X3-M4]); key semantics were may-only ([X1-M3]). Decision: field-based writer extraction at the AST level under A-TYPE with untyped-write and escape listings; out-pointer summaries; transition-aware filter; MUST_KEY / MAY_KEY / NO_KEY; allocation records with A-FRESH and A-OWNER as listed trusted facts refuted by O9; S3b points-to only if measured necessary. Alternatives: Andersen first (v1); declared effects only (C). Consequences: the MVC is smaller; ablation 17 measures what S3b would add.

**ADR-008 — Semantics family with per-M containment and well-defined refinements.** Context: v1's containment ignored M ([X1-C2]); σ_U∞ was ill-defined for exhausted retries and liveness ([X1-M5]); σ_R1 ignored OSAL's RT policy ([X2-07]). Decision: σ_U with exact retry bounds; σ_U∞ as σ_U without time-based transitions (revised by ADR-040); safety on finite executions and progress on dead actors (ADR-039, ADR-040); σ_R1[P_sched] with cited profiles and blocking delays (A-ZT); prefix containment of finite real runs in `Pref(σ_U, M_impl)` (ADR-040); M_api as API-contract claims; refinements per σ with their own bounds. Alternatives: σ_U + σ_E only (A); v1's family. Consequences: two analyses per OSAL-touching cone; no σ_R1 verdicts for backends without a cited profile.

**ADR-009 — A linearization point per conjunct, with capture points.** Context: v1 evaluated value-carried conjuncts at the wrong LP ([X1-M1]). Decision: every conjunct declares its LP; value conjuncts use the capture point found by `derives`; T-DERIVE is the generic translation from a stamp parameter to `cell(loc)`; proofcheck re-checks `derives`. Alternatives: one LP per use (v1). Consequences: traces bracket capture points; O4 maps outcome codes per conjunct.

**ADR-010 — Two OSAL model levels with faithful M_impl waits.** Context: POSIX internals create windows, timed waits and a lock leak ([R01 §7 item 6]; [X2-03]). Decision: M_api atomic at LPs; M_impl projects OSAL shared + POSIX source onto Φ_L-1, with by-ID vs by-name token conversion and attempts woken by broadcast or timed wake. Labels portability-hazard, implementation-defect-candidate, portability-hazard-evidence. Alternatives: API level only; always M_impl. Consequences: litmus tests validate Φ_L-1.

**ADR-011 — A verdict record with orthogonal axes and no "safe".** Context: [R06] R1–R7, S1; v1 mixed causes with outcomes and realization with naturalness ([X1-m3], [X1-M11]). Decision: per-(σ, M) cells, each with a safety class, a reach value and a progress result (ADR-039); provider axis; scope; realization and naturalness axes; σ_E classes only from natural witnesses on both sides. Alternatives: a single severity ladder; v1's single evidence ranking. Consequences: reports are multi-axis; tests check that no path produces "safe" or "timing-protected".

**ADR-012 — Context-qualified templates with a summary fallback.** Context: SB event sites are shared by all callers; full inlining is exponential ([X1-m7]). Decision: templates `(actor instance, call string, site)`; per-(actor, function) summaries when the skeleton work-unit cap is reached, with the precision loss stated. Alternatives: site-only templates. Consequences: per-context certificates; fallback counts measured.

**ADR-013 — F0 observation with perf-PMU uprobes and an own raw loader; perf-sample bring-up.** *Revised by ADR-049 (revision 1.2): F0 keeps the cFS user and the reference binary, but is not read as changing only time; the observation effect is checked against an unobserved reference run (D1). The existing environment is used first, and a maintained public BPF library is considered only if a needed function is shown missing (P-LIBBPF).* Context: highest fidelity on the unmodified binary; no libbpf ([R07 §4.7]); tracefs changes global state ([X3-m2]). Decision: uprobes at entry+4 registered by inode before launch; one generic BPF program per phase; perf-sample backend for bring-up; tracefs optional with host-state rules. Alternatives: `--wrap` (F1); source probes (F2); gdb; libbpf. Consequences: S1 must probe cookies, ring buffers, task storage and stack capture.

**ADR-014 — Causality only from tokens and protocol keys.** Context: timestamps give no HB ([R04 §2.1]; [J2-4]). Decision: per-thread `tseq`; per-object `objseq`; descriptor/FIFO; outcome-implied reads-from; poll exit status. Alternatives: clock reconstruction as HB. Consequences: no take-return probes on hot sites.

**ADR-015 — Return values captured only where needed, by a mechanism chosen by measurement.** Context: post-call `mov` probes are likely in the 31.7 µs class ([J3 §2.3]); a cheaper emulated-branch option exists ([X3-m3]). Decision: capture only at readiness use and edge-deciding sites; R-a…R-e measured per class in S1; deferred reads where statically valid. Alternatives: caller-side probes everywhere. Consequences: S1 produces the calibration table before a full plan.

**ADR-016 — Prediction by explicit-state, lock-reversing search with an A-COMM check.** Context: the central cFS class needs lock reversal; no solver is installed; sparse traces weaken MCM/RVPredict guarantees ([X1-M4]). Decision: Pred(π) with the key as conflict object and the lock as atomicity; "an acquire needs the lock free"; A-COMM check per witness, else `predicted-modelled`; prefilters with re-validation; work-unit caps with anytime curves; SMT-LIB only after P-Z3. Alternatives: SMT-only; sync-preserving prediction only. Consequences: no install; the predictor's soundness is stated relative to A-COMM.

**ADR-017 — Explorer with a verified exhaustive mode and a conditionally verified DPOR mode; gated.** Context: a test is not a proof; the disabling theorem is status P ([X1-M9]); v1's L3 condition was insufficient ([X1-M10]). Decision: `exh` (stateful exhaustive search) and `dpor` (source-DPOR + sleep sets, labelled unverified until T-JACM is checked); L3 as a mover reduction with statically checked conditions; differential test as validation; no optimality claim; stage S7 with a kill criterion. Alternatives: optimal DPOR claimed exact; no explorer. Consequences: the explorer may end as an ablation. Without it, no refinement is established, because `timeout-dependent` and `timeout-rescued` need universal σ_U∞ statements that neither certificates (σ_U only) nor prediction (witnesses only) provide; prediction can only rule a refinement out with a σ_U∞ witness. No model progress result exists either: every progress value stays `unknown(not_checked)`, except σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact (ADR-039, ADR-040).

**ADR-018 — MLIR behind gate G-MLIR.** *Superseded by ADR-051 (revision 1.2): the MLIR stage and the gate are excluded from the roadmap (D6).* Formerly unchanged from v1: plain C++ skeletons and core; G-MLIR at S8 with criteria (verdict equality, provenance completeness, code size, runtime); no ODS before the gate passes.

**ADR-019 — Version-robust site binding.** Context: line-number probes break across revisions ([J2-9]); same-named local copies exist ([X3-m3]). Decision: AST patterns with a match semantics, resolved through each basis's gcc DWARF (compile unit for local copies) and MC; version guards; anchor audit. Consequences: unresolved patterns listed.

**ADR-020 — Confirmation by F0 holds with explicit safety rules and normalization.** *Revised by ADR-049 and ADR-050 (revision 1.2): observation and control are kept apart and every run that uses a hold, stop or release is labelled a controlled run (D1); a failure made by a hold is told apart from a natural run, and a timeout alone never decides a permanent deadlock; for C1 the provider is held immediately before its creation and released on an event, the retry count and delay are read from the chosen CF version, and the wall-clock watchdog and stop conditions are recorded before the main experiment; the RESERVED-window experiment is a follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate item outside the five cases (P-C1HOLD, §4.13).* Context: constructed schedules must be feasible, safe and honestly labelled ([J2-10]; [J3-M10]); abnormal exits contaminate later runs ([X3-M3]); debug registers are shared ([X3-m1]). Decision: ptrace with debug-register breakpoints; empty program-lock set; placement rules; event release; reference watchdogs incl. `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; wait-for oracle; joint debug-register allocator; attach at clone measured; a normalization boot after every abnormal exit; naturalness labels and perturbation ratios. Alternatives: `--wrap` gates by default; fixed sleeps (rejected by [POL] L22). Consequences: at most 4 debug registers per thread for watchpoints plus gates.

**ADR-021 — Consistency obligations O1–O10 and the certificate check.** Context: authored rules need a "how do you know the model is right" answer ([J1 §6]). Decision: per-actor acceptance; outcome agreement with power reported on HB-decided uses; writer-set agreement incl. A-TYPE; allocation/ownership refutation (O9); global conformance from S5; contradictions (O3/O4/O9 against certified statements) suspend rules and end the loop; O6 (b) and O10 contest a bound without a suspension (ADR-039, ADR-040). Consequences: obligation failures are a fidelity metric.

**ADR-022 — A feedback loop with reproducibility.** Context: v1's fixed-point test included counts and never converged ([X1-m8]); budgets were wall-clock ([X3-M10]). Decision: per-iteration records; a fixed-point test over the comparable state only; an explicit lattice; suspension ends the loop; work-unit budgets; replay byte for byte; staged availability of components. Consequences: reproducible numbers.

**ADR-023 — Clang/LLVM for analysis only; the gcc binary is what runs.** Unchanged: Clang facts and IR for analysis; DWARF/MC anchoring to the gcc binary; two diagnostic-only flag adaptations recorded in B; IR used for the call graph only (pinned pipeline if ever used for writes).

**ADR-024 — Openness through a union of discovery sources, with governance.** Context: five cases cover 3 of 15 readiness categories ([R06 §5.1]). Decision: the 14 sources of §7.1; freezes F1 (incl. any re-implementation of a process-level technique (revision 1.2; before it, all comparators) and, since ADR-042, the evaluation criteria and the hashes of the pre-registered expectation sheets) and F2 (incl. the recall protocol and the exposure record); template-only first; refinement accounting that names framework rules; an app-profile layer. Consequences: authoring effort is real and is measured.

**ADR-025 — Comparators reported by detection, classification and evidence, frozen code and an external reference point.** *Superseded by ADR-046 and ADR-047 (revision 1.2): the comparison is among existing process-level race work, and there are no thread-level or same-information comparators and no external reference point.* Context: v1's comparators were all self-reimplemented with no metric, and the strongest same-family comparators were missing ([X3-M6]; [X1-M12]). Decision (revised by ADR-042): detection, classification and evidence per comparator and labelled unit, with a paired test on detection only; comparators frozen at F1 with golden tests from their papers; a GPredict-style lock-reversing predictor and a plain state monitor given the same property information, and a DefUse-style learner, added; a stripped variant, if kept, is a bundle ablation (P-ABL-ALLOWED); helgrind/DRD run through `RUN_CFS_WRAP`; the Nidhugg blocker stated as an install decision. Consequences: comparisons answer "what each model sees on the same labelled units", with Kairos-plan dependence stated.

**ADR-026 — A staged build with a thin MVC, estimates and a cut list.** *Amended by ADR-050 (revision 1.2): the thin MVC is a name for the claims table and the cut list and no longer a first completion condition (P-LADDER); S8 is removed (ADR-051).* Context: v1's MVC included the whole L1 layer and Andersen; stages had dependency inversions ([X3-M5]). Decision: thin MVC = S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a, reached through the validation ladder of ADR-044, whose first target V1 takes sub-gates from these stages; S4 without O8, predict and hold; O8 on S5's engine; D14 at S1; [I] estimates and a cut list. Consequences: the explorer, S3b and F1 can be cut without losing the core that the stage plan never cuts; with tool-level evidence on R2 alone that core is a single-case result, and cutting S5–S7 or S10 removes the other cases' own evidence (§9.3, ADR-044). Cutting the explorer removes the only route to model progress results and to refinements, so protected verdicts are then reported as safety statements with progress `unknown(not_checked)`; σ_E stall witnesses realized with a wait-for fact remain possible.

**ADR-027 — Two skeleton views.** Context: [X1-C1]. Decision: `Sk^cert` (star-closed, for every universal claim and `inactive`) and `Sk^h` (h-cut, for exploration only, h in b); proofcheck rejects `Sk^h`/`TRUNC` steps; litmus App. A.8. Consequences: universal verdicts are horizon-free; exploration results are bounded.

**ADR-028 — Teardown as a scope, modelled as on pc-linux.** Context: v1 modelled restart as non-terminal with `OS_DeleteAllObjects` concurrent with tasks, defeating the calibration certificates ([X1-M6]; [X2-04]). Decision: `es.shutdown` kind; requester terminal after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY`; teardown only on the PSP main thread after `CFE_ES_Main` returns; readiness specifications scoped "before the shutdown request"; K-TEARDOWN family; SCOPE exclusion in certificates. Consequences: default-B certificates are stated outside teardown; every experiment's stop is a teardown and is reported separately.

**ADR-029 — Message provenance and the looper rule.** Context: [X1-M7]. Decision: `Prov(p) = (Routes_B(p) ∩ Tx_B) ∪ Input_B(p)` as a least fixpoint over handler reachability, folding dispatch in both views; no between-handler HB edge; FIFO only as a prediction constraint. Consequences: command-only paths are `inactive` without input; shapes follow CAFA/DCatch.

**ADR-030 — Code-extracted await predicates.** Context: v1's T-OBS rested on authored predicate strings ([X1-M8]). Decision: poll loops recognized by pattern; predicates extracted by symbolic evaluation of the exit branch (three recognized forms); re-derived by proofcheck; L1 preconditions checked; Φ binds only names, strata and escapes. Consequences: R2's proof no longer depends on an authored predicate; unextractable loops are `unmodelled-sync`.

**ADR-031 — Generic K-SELF specification and branch-complete chains.** Context: [X2-02]. Decision: `es.self(exec) = LIVE` instantiated at all seven context-resolving callers with per-caller outcomes and harms; consequence chains record every branch, labelled [I] until run. Consequences: new harms (`app_zombie`, `service_task_absent`, `process_abort`, `panic`).

**ADR-032 — Two-phase SB transmit and two-level capacity.** Context: [X2-06], [X2-05], [X2-09]. Decision: route_lookup LP under the lock plus one put LP per destination; K-CAP and K-CAP.msglim; four counters as oracles, `PeakQueueDepth` labelled SB-accounted; header-carried MsgIds with encode/decode summaries and a table-packet decoder; K-MODE in SB's actor via R7 on SB's command pipe. Consequences: RC5 stays partial until key shares are measured.

**ADR-033 — Basis closure covers reset type, script selection, euid, CWD and CDS images; canonical Init.** Context: [X2-08], [X3-M3]. Decision: these items in B with sources; the script-selection rule evaluated in `basis`; `cap_B` conditioned on euid; A11 split by reset type; content-only Init hash; strata; normalization boots. Consequences: root-run arms are new bases.

**ADR-034 — Deterministic work-unit budgets and POL-compliant labels.** *Applied by ADR-049 (revision 1.2): N is decided per purpose at S1 and frozen before the corresponding main experiment (D14); each work-unit cap is frozen after its stage's pilot and an excess is `unknown(budget)` (D15); the statistics of the first case evaluation are descriptive (P-STATS, ADR-051).* Context: [X3-M9], [X3-M10]. Decision: every analysis budget is a work-unit count with a deterministic search order; wall time and memory are costs only; PI-chosen N, caps and sample sizes are deviations; event-based stopping preferred; anytime curves; statistics as an assumption. Consequences: byte-exact replay; honest bounds.

**ADR-035 — Recall measured on program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs.** *Revised by ADR-051 (revision 1.2): recall belongs to the extended evaluation, separate from and after the five-case validation; its results hold for the variant set only, and the five design cases and their fixes are excluded from the unobserved sets (D8/P-RECALL). The open details (operators, history query, expected-verdict and equivalent-mutant rules, who runs the queries) stay open.* Context: v1 measured nothing about misses; one natural case ([X3-C1]). Decision: §8.5 protocol frozen before F2; `detected` and `class-correct` scored apart, per mutant and per originating fix; blindness only from the exposure record (ADR-042); soundness failures counted; equivalent-mutant rule pre-registered; one incremental variant tree. Consequences: RQ9; mutants labelled synthetic.

**ADR-036 — Attach cookies and inode registration.** Context: IP-keyed descriptors break under ASLR; the `.so` fallback perturbed the phase under study ([X3-M1]; [X3-m4]). Decision: site id as attach cookie (per-site perf links or `uprobe_multi`), registration by inode before launch, verifier-conscious generic program, hand-written helper prototypes and `<elf.h>`. Consequences: no per-module map filling; the hold fallback is excluded from pools if ever used.

**ADR-037 — Corrected prior-work positioning.** *The comparator-related parts are superseded by ADR-046 (revision 1.2); the credits to prior work stay.* Context: [X1-M12]. Decision: credit InitRacer (late registration), RVPredict/GPredict (key-conflict lock reversal), ExceptioNULL (predicted use-before-init) and DefUse; record G3 novelty as an open literature check; claim only the combination (revised by ADR-042: GPredict is described as [R04] records it; predictive typestate checking (Joshi & Sen) and Lifestate are credited as prior art for framework typestate; no comparison or ablation is presented as evidence that a prior technique cannot express a property). Consequences: §1.11 and §6.4 item 18.

**ADR-038 — Triage by stratified sampling with masked raters and oracle agreement for losses.** *Revised by ADR-051 (revision 1.2): triage belongs to the extended evaluation, and P-TRIAGE requires two independent raters for any quantitative judgement.* Context: [X3-M7]. Decision: "reported" defined; stratified random sample per (class, RC category, deciding layer); raters masked to the producing layer, the ablation and each other (assessor masking, with each rater's exposure recorded; ADR-042); LOSS-UNDER-ORDER checked against the SB oracle; rater effort as a cost. Consequences: precision with exact CIs instead of full triage.

**ADR-039 — Safety statements, classes from a safety record, and a separate progress result.**
- **Context.** `protected` required `S_i = ∅`, but no certificate, lemma or checker step establishes it: L-MB and L-PR are conditioned on occurrences (EXT-20261012 §4.1). Rev. 1 of the amendment also showed that "one class per cell" fails when both universal statements hold, and that a schedule-dependent class from occurrences at different ordinals claims a schedule dependence that no single occurrence has.
- **Decision.**
  - Occurrences begin at their call; captures are attached to the call they feed; a call that returns before one of its conjunct LPs, with no conjunct found false, gives `NOT_EVALUATED`, which no statement counts; scope is decided at the decision event and is part of the instance; ordinals count per key over canonical actor-instance names, mapped to run identities through the closure. `returned_and_tested` accepts only at a normal continuation: the cited one (revised by ADR-048: the former default, the nearest post-dominator of the test, is now only an extracted fact `cont_candidate` and never an ACCEPT); a REJECT at the same node wins; an error exit or an error return is a REJECT; a documented propagation needs a reference. No status or horizon verdict is decided after or by a process-terminal step.
  - Each cell keeps, per bound, a safety record: the statements `¬Bad_i`, `¬OK_i` (with its decision route) and `¬NR_i` with their routes, and witnesses with their occurrence ordinals. Classes are a function of the record: `protected`, `fixed-order` (`¬OK_i` deciding: a bad witness in the cell, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the decision), `no-final-status` (both statements), `schedule-dependent` (a same-ordinal pair), `ordinal-split` (witnesses at different ordinals only); `¬OK_i` without a decision route is `unknown(no_decision_route)`. Existence is the reach axis. An `exh` witness against a certificate goes to obligation O10, which contests only the explored bound.
  - Permanent waits form a separate per-cell progress result (`stall-witness` with the set of clauses pre-use, in-use, horizon; `no-stall-within(b)`; `unknown(reason)`), evaluated on the product with an instance monitor, with kinds, qualifiers, wait-for roots (terminal strongly connected components of the wait-for graph) and progress chains (decidable conditions on the stall state). Only `exh` decides it; a realized hang with a wait-for fact, and deadlock cycles and leaked locks transferred between σ, can witness a stall. Timeout-masked-liveness is per instance, with the flag `tml-root` on every TML-relevant instance into which no chain leads.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Option B: keep `S_i = ∅` in protected and add a progress obligation to certificates. Rejected for the first implementation, for four reasons. It needs a new lock-order analysis, must-occurrence with progress, spawn completion, and checker steps of its own. It would withhold protected wherever M_impl models F7-type task loss. `S_i` is not preserved across σ. Star-closure exits in `Sk^cert` make it unprovable for actors that wait on loopers. Option A is adopted: the PI decided D20 = A on 2026-10-15 (ADR-049).
  - Reporting a stall as schedule-dependent (X1-M5). Withdrawn.
  - Making the classes exclusive with `unknown(horizon)` for the both-statements case (an earlier draft of EXT-20261012). Rejected: the class then depends on the order of routes.
- **Consequences.** Certified verdicts are rendered as safety statements with reach and progress. Model stall witnesses count as detections only when confirmable and choice-free. A progress certificate can be added later as a new progress value without changing any class.

**ADR-040 — Finite-execution semantics, prefix containment and deadness.**
- **Context.** Maximal-execution containment fails for σ_U∞. The quiescence rule is ill-defined when several retries are pending, almost never holds with perpetual actors, and makes σ_U∞ depend on L1 (EXT-20261012 §4.2).
- **Decision.**
  - Pref and Max are separated, and safety is defined on Pref; results are kept per bound.
  - L-CONT is prefix containment. For σ_U∞ it is an equality with the σ_U prefixes without time-based transitions, because σ_U∞ removes every time-based transition (non-periodic timeouts, delay-paced last-attempt failures at their deciding transition at level M, and the give-ups that Φ declares, among them the ES kill timer's forced kill `expire-waiting`); periodic waits, timer ticks and the cleanup of exited apps stay; give-ups that Φ has not declared yet (HS's application monitor, TIME's flywheel) are labelled `tb-undeclared`. A poll that L1 summarizes is one transition, so its actor can be dead under σ_U∞, and deadness preservation is part of L1's obligation. σ_R1 is a separate system with a projection.
  - Stall states are defined by dead actors on product states, per occurrence, with the excluded states as sinks and no fairness assumption; σ_R1 deadness uses σ_U's enabling relation. Actors left with only unpaired time-based transitions are stranded and, like censored actors, make the tests in their closure undecided.
  - `dpor` uses an extended dependence (shutdown request against the instance's LPs; decisions against a monitored actor's transitions; the teardown against a monitored actor's transitions).
  - Refinements are derived from per-σ results; without the explorer none is established.
  - One process-terminal list is used everywhere; states after it are excluded, and stalls before a process end that stays reachable without the stop input are `terminal-pending`. `TRUNC` regions and stranded actors are censored transitively.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Keep maximal containment with stuttering. This needs a new execution notion and a re-proof of every transfer.
  - Keep the patient-exhaustion rule and make quiescence cone-relative.
  - Exclude idle loopers and stalls behind pending panics from stall evaluation. Rejected: qualifiers keep them visible without counting them.
- **Consequences.** Transfer moves safety statements only, each separately. A.2, A.3, A.4 step 3, A.5, A.6, A.7, L-PATIENT, L-TIMEDWAIT and L-H2 are restated, and progress results are relative to the closed-cone model.

**ADR-041 — Trust ledger and the checker's stated base.**
- **Context.** `kz proofcheck` trusts `facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, the closure, Φ and the shared `libkz-core`, and some of its steps call `libkz-core`'s evaluator. The specification nevertheless said that it re-derived "everything except extraction" and shared no code (EXT-20261012 §4.3).
- **Decision.**
  - §4.5 names the trusted base and the re-validated steps, and marks the steps that call `libkz-core` beyond reading inputs ("via T5").
  - Every certificate and verdict cell carries a four-part ledger (`KZ_TRUST_LEDGER`, `KZ_TRUST`); the ledgers of derived cells list the transfer lemmas and run evidence they add.
  - A-EXTRACT and A-CORE become listed assumptions.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - Rebuild `Sk^cert` inside the checker. This needs LLVM inside the checker, which conflicts with its independence from producer code; it is left as an open question.
  - A second, checker-private evaluator for Φ conjuncts. Not adopted in the first implementation; the evaluator agreement property test and A-CORE cover it.
  - A report-only view.
- **Consequences.** Claims of independent verification are scoped, and the S3a gate requires the R2 ledger.

**ADR-042 — Comparison kinds, three outcomes, an exposure record and units (EXT-20261012 §5.1–§5.4).** *Superseded in part by ADR-046 and ADR-047 (revision 1.2): the same-information comparators, the paired test on detection and the per-comparator three outcomes are withdrawn; detection, classification and evidence stay as Kairos's own report parts (§8.5), and the exposure record, the units and the single-element ablations stay.*
- **Context.** The stripped MCM/GPredict-style row was called a same-family baseline but explained as an ablation. GPredict was characterized by an inference about its formalism. Alarm yes/no could not show what certificates add, since a plain monitor given the same readiness precondition detects observed R2. Fix texts were taken as a source of blindness. Occurrences, instances, mutants and runs were tested as if independent.
- **Decision.**
  - Original tools, re-implementations with the same or their own information, and one-element ablations are kept apart. No ablation is read as a statement about a prior technique.
  - GPredict is described as built; its formalism's expressiveness stays [U] (P-PAPERS).
  - Detection, classification (with the outcome label and the progress result; for a comparator without class output, the class the harness derives from its own witnesses by the same-ordinal rule) and evidence are reported separately, with each tool's good and bad witnesses and the reason a result stays undecided. Detection (occurrence detection) counts an alarm and a `not-ready-unjudged` report alike, for every tool. Only occurrence detection gets a paired test, for comparators and ablations alike; class detection is Kairos's recall score (§8.5 item 4). Classification is compared with a pre-registered expectation sheet frozen at F1; reference class statements ("race" for C1, C2, C3, C5) are scored only under a mapping the PI adopts (P-CLASSGT), and until then agreement or disagreement with them is reported as an outcome.
  - Blindness is claimed only from an exposure and freeze record, relative to each item's governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items). Rater masking stays, as assessor masking.
  - Units U1–U6 are named, and every statistic states its unit and independence assumption (P-STATS).
  - Single-element ablations of Allowed and of outcome semantics are candidates (P-ABL-ALLOWED).
- **Consequences.** RQ8 compares models given the same information. Claims of added value rest on classification and evidence, and only on what the comparators structurally lack: σ_U∞ and σ_R1 cells and M qualification, refinements, the progress axis, certificates and `exh` results within b. Every design case is reported on its own phenomenon, and a result on R2 alone, or on R2's template in another context, is a single-case result.

**ADR-043 — Three judgements: framework level, caller level, observed** [D] (EXT-20261012 §6.4). *Amended by ADR-048 (revision 1.2): QT-2 and QT-3 approved; the strict alternatives of QT-4 and Q15; QT-5 held; QT-9 separated.* Context: Allowed mixed supplier-documented results with caller handling, a supplier requirement was proposed as a caller acceptance (STATUS T2), and unspecified Allowed erased observed effects. Decision: `side ∈ {supplier, caller, rule}` on every provenance record; entries with a `caller` record are horizon monitors, `rule`-only entries decide nothing (revised by ADR-048; formerly monitors under the default and reported as rule-backed), supplier-only entries decide nothing; new field `appreq` with a precedence rule (a conflict with a `caller` entry leaves the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED; revised by ADR-048: the conflict is stated and the judgement withheld, QT-5); framework-level `fw_doc` per instance and `fw_conf` per cell in the reserved bytes; `consequence` always filled; outcome-label names kept. Alternatives: a full split of Allowed into two fields (more churn, same information); keeping the mix (the documented error return of R2 and the documented drop of SB then decide nothing consistently); treating the generic rules as caller-side (then a documented return code plus a branch discharges the caller's obligation with no caller text). Consequences (revision 1.1; revised by ADR-048, under which the labels of EVS's `GetAppID` occurrence, TIME's `CreateChildTask` calls and C1's pre-fix occurrence become NOT_READY_UNJUDGED): under the defaults then adopted no status of the five cases' rows was expected to change [U]: the expectation rests on the walk-through of §0.7 item 5, which is not kept as a separate record, and on the worked examples of App. A (A.1 steps 8 and 13a, A.2 step 3, A.4 step 3), and nothing was run; supplier-postcondition specifications without an `appreq` become `not-ready-unjudged`; one litmus expectation is restated; source tiers (§1.6, ADR-052) are reported apart, and rule-backed acceptances no longer exist (ADR-048); the census reports Allowed entries by side.

**ADR-044 — A validation ladder with per-case tracks as the first target (EXT-20261012 §7, §8).** *Approved as P-LADDER and amended by ADR-050 (revision 1.2).*
- **Context.** The thin MVC (S0 + S1 + S2 + S3a) was the first target. Its exit gates mix R2's chain with calibration items and items for other cases, so an R2-only slice cannot pass them as written. The external review found the plan large (its sum of the [I] estimates for S0–S3a is 19–28 person-weeks) and recommended the order: formal fixes, R2 end to end, a corrected-order control, C1's three outcomes, then held-out cases and pre/post-fix pairs. The PI found that checking concentrated on R2. A case-by-mechanism matrix showed that, without the basis decisions for C1 (D22, since decided, 2026-10-15), C3 and C5 (D13) and C2 (P-C2) and the bases they lead to, Kairos can produce tool-level evidence on R2's phenomenon only. On the ENV basis C1 is latent, C2's own instance is absent, C3's site is part of R2's in-basis neighbours, and A.3 is a residual candidate apart from C5's #72.
- **Decision.**
  - The first target is rung V1: R2's K-ID chain end to end on the ENV basis, with sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2 and S2-R2. The full stage gates stay, as rung V1b.
  - V0 (the formal amendment, ADR-039–ADR-043) comes first. Its proof item V0-P writes and reviews the lemma obligations that V1's certificate and transfer wording uses before that wording is used; the rest are written before F1. The review's step-1 condition (definitions, proofs and result display agree) is met in full only then.
  - V2 and V3 follow V1 and V1b and run in parallel; neither waits for the other. Their results are reported in the review's order (step 3, then step 4), which is a reporting order, not a dependency.
  - V2 evaluates a corrected-order control basis chosen under P-R2CTRL. Its pre-registered record is "fixed-order not issued; open, chained to the App. A.2 root; not protected", and it is labelled configuration or revision sensitivity.
  - V3 activates C1 on the public BP basis decided under D22 [D]: the candidate pair CF `281a941` → `833fdbb`, with the public values of nasa/bp `b051eb0` (semaphore names `BP0_tsem`/`BP1_tsem`, startup order CF then BP, configured priorities CF 65 and BP 55), which are not values of the original reporter's environment. The version compatibility of the public integration is checked first; a move to other versions is a derived basis with every difference recorded; the reporter's CF `2a024d8` is not mixed with the public BP table; the v7.0.1 default (empty names) is the path-inactive negative control only. The outcome partition has five arms (App. A.4 step 6). Arm A4 has no referenced basis and is shown by litmus unless the PI sets one as a recorded deviation.
  - V4a compares with existing process-level race work after F1 (revision 1.2, ADR-046 and ADR-047; formerly with same-information comparators). V4b evaluates held-out sources and pre/post-fix pairs after F2, and reports added rules, manual cost and `unknown` reasons.
  - Case tracks run in parallel. All five cases are read and pre-registered now. C3 and C5 go on the 6.4.x pair (D13), C2 is role-derived (P-C2), and the A.3 line uses `58abd38d` (P-RGIT). Their decisions were taken on 2026-10-15 (ADR-050), and F1 waits for the recorded choices and details (P-F1GATE). The per-case paths and controls are normative (§9.3).
  - No case is reported through a v7.0.1 counterpart.
  - SMT, DPOR and Φ templates that no rung, track or step needs are added only after a rung, a case track or an ENV-window step shows a need (MLIR is excluded, D6); the S3a census covers the templates the ladder needs, and other kinds are reported as not authored.
  - No effort figure is given for any rung.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - The thin MVC as the first target. Rejected: its gates contain non-R2 items, and the review's scale concern stands.
  - A strict R2 → C1 ladder as the only validation. Rejected: C2, C3 and C5 would meet the design only after F1, and the S5 gate and the S7 kill criterion depend on C1's B′.
  - C1 first. Rejected: C1 needs a derived basis and S5–S7 for any class, while V1 needs only D1.
  - V2 strictly before V3, as a dependency. Rejected: V3 needs nothing from V2, V2's default is "no referenced control", and the edge would make C1 wait for R2 twice.
  - Summing the estimates into a plan figure. Rejected: they are [I] planning guesses.
- **Consequences.**
  - R2's concentration remains at the evidence level until the case bases (C1: D22, decided, not yet built; C2: P-C2; C3 and C5: D13) are built and give tool output, and this is reported, not hidden.
  - A cut that leaves R2 alone yields a single-case result. The core that the plan never cuts is not, by its completion, a publishable result (EXT-20261012 §9).
  - The S5 gate and the S7 review depend on C1's basis being built (D22 decided; P-D18GATES).
  - RQ1's coverage depends on which templates are authored, and unauthored kinds are reported as such.

**ADR-045 — Exact σ_E wording, L-CONT (c) in the ledger, and a proof status for every lemma and guarantee (PI decision D20's completion conditions; open items 7 and 8 of `REVIEW_RESPONSE_20261012.md` §5).**
- **Context.** Transfer rule 2 stated for σ_E that no in-scope occurrence of any real run is "other than `READY`". That is stronger than L-PR, which proves that every decided in-scope occurrence is `READY` or `NOT_EVALUATED`, and a real run may contain an early return (LP-EARLY). The trust ledger listed L-CONT only for cells other than the certificate's own, although the certificate's reach item (FIXED_ORDER item 6; App. A.1 step 9) proves the reach of (σ_U, M_impl) from a run through L-CONT (c). Every lemma was a sketch or an obligation, and a verdict did not tell the reader which, so a lemma listed as an obligation could be read as a proved one.
- **Decision.**
  - Rule 2 and every σ_E statement say what L-FO and L-PR prove, over decided in-scope occurrences, allowing `NOT_EVALUATED`; litmus LP-EARLY gains the PROTECTED variant (c).
  - L-CONT (c) and L-STATUS are in part (iii) of the ledger of every cell whose reach or witness rests on a run, the certificate's own cell included; the run-side premises of L-CONT (c), L-MAPI-CONE and L-REACH-CONE are refutable by O1, O2, O4 and O5 (part (iv)).
  - Three proof-status values (`proved`, `depends-on-stated-assumption`, `unproved`) are defined once (§6.2), assigned to every lemma and guarantee in a table, carried by `KZ_TRUST.proof`, `KZ_CERT.proof_status` and `KZ_VERDICT_CELL.proof_status`, combined by the weakest-link rule and printed with every certificate-based statement. A lemma that is only listed as an obligation is `unproved`, and a status is raised only by a written proof.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  - A two-valued flag (proved or not): it hides which unproved items rest on stated assumptions and which have gaps.
  - Treating every sketch as proved relative to A: the sketches of L-MB, L-PWB and L1 have gaps that no assumption of §6.1 covers.
  - A report-only note: it is not carried by the record, so a tool could print "certified" without it.
  - Tying a status to a passed test or obligation: an observation refutes, it does not prove.
- **Consequences.** Under the statuses of this revision every R2 certificate cell prints "lemma status: unproved", because L-MB, L-ONCE, L-PWB, L-FO and L1 are `unproved`; the first R2 result is therefore worded as accepted by the checker relative to its trusted base and those lemmas, until V0-P raises them. The Korean mirrors and `plan/proof_status_R2.md` follow the table of §6.2.

**ADR-046 — The experimental comparison is among existing process-level race work; thread-level tools and re-implementations of thread-level techniques are related work (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §1 item 4, §4.4 (P-VALGRIND, P-TSAN, P-NIDHUGG, P-PAPERS) and §7 item 8.
- **Context.** ADR-025 and ADR-042 compared Kairos with same-information re-implementations of thread-level and prediction-based techniques (FastTrack-, WCP- and SHB-style relations, a GPredict-style predictor, a plain state monitor, DefUse, DCatch) and ran helgrind and DRD as an external reference point. The decisions of 2026-10-15 limit the experimental comparison to Process-Level Race work.
- **Decision.**
  - The experimental comparison is among RacePro, SimRacer, SIMEXPLORER, RRF, DESCRY, SimEvo, ReDPro and SysPro and Kairos on the five cases (§8.3).
  - Thread data-race tools and GPredict-, happens-before- and lockset-style re-implementations are not experimental comparators. GPredict, FastTrack and the like remain related work (§1.11). There is no helgrind/DRD run (P-VALGRIND excluded), no ThreadSanitizer rebuild (P-TSAN excluded), and Nidhugg is held (P-NIDHUGG).
  - A re-implementation of a process-level technique is allowed only when the original cannot be run, and it is kept apart from original-tool results; Kairos ablations are a third kind. No tool's capability is stated from a search excerpt (P-PAPERS: the reading of papers is approved and starts with accessible routes).
- **Alternatives considered.** Keeping the reference-point rows or the GPredict-style comparator: rejected by the PI. Keeping a plain state check as a comparator: not kept; it may serve as an internal correctness check of the recorder.
- **Consequences.** §8.3, §8.4, §8.5 and the V4a rung are rewritten; the comparator, reference-point and expressibility tables are withdrawn; the S9 estimate is flagged as not re-estimated.

**ADR-047 — Six analysis questions and six result states replace the baseline table (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §1 item 4, §7 item 8; the questions and states are those of `plan/ROADMAP_20261015.md` §10.2 and §10.4.
- **Context.** The earlier table gave comparators one metric and mixed `NOT_RUN` rows with re-implemented ones. The existing tools differ in role, input and output, and none was run in the survey.
- **Decision.** For every (tool, case) pair the same six questions are answered with the evidence each answer rests on, and the cell takes one state: `NOT_RUN`, `INAPPLICABLE`, `PATH_NOT_EXECUTED`, `OBSERVATION_GAP`, `ANALYSIS_GAP` or `REPORTED`. Observability, expressibility and actual detection success are never merged in one cell; `ANALYSIS_GAP` is not assigned in haste. The first comparison is RacePro on R2. A shortage is linked to a Kairos function only where the existing tool's shortage is confirmed.
- **Alternatives considered.** A single detection score per tool: rejected, because it would count `NOT_RUN` as a miss and mix kinds of result.
- **Consequences.** No paired test and no score between tools. Kairos's own result keeps its three report parts (§8.5).

**ADR-048 — Strict alternatives for caller-level acceptance and continuation; conflicts held; implementation-defect family (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (QT-2, QT-3), §4.1 (QT-4, QT-5, QT-9, Q15).
- **Context.** ADR-043 let a `rule` record decide the statuses of `rule`-only Allowed entries, and ADR-039 let `returned_and_tested` accept at the nearest post-dominator of the test. Both were defaults pending the PI.
- **Decision.**
  - A rule alone never makes an occurrence accepted (QT-4): only an entry with a `caller` record is a horizon monitor, and an entry without an original-source basis stays `unspecified_by_reference`, so its occurrences are NOT_READY_UNJUDGED. Code state changes, returned codes, error returns and exits are still reported as the occurrence's consequence.
  - The code fact that a follow-up path exists is told apart from the judgement that it is an allowed recovery (Q15): a `returned_and_tested` entry accepts only at a cited continuation, and the nearest post-dominator is extracted as the fact `cont_candidate`.
  - A conflict between an `appreq` and a `caller` entry, or between two `caller` entries, is stated and the judgement withheld, without an invented precedence (QT-5).
  - K-SPAWN.osal and K-LOCKBAL are an implementation-defect family; no researcher-authored `appreq` is added (QT-9).
  - QT-2 and QT-3 are approved: the provenance kind `requirement`; the SB rule for a send with no subscriber is kept apart from R2's invalid-AppId error; the three layers stay apart.
- **Alternatives considered.** The rule-backed default: rejected. A researcher requirement for K-SPAWN.osal and K-LOCKBAL: rejected.
- **Consequences.** Some labels disappear (rule-backed TOLERATED; VIOLATING resting on a rule's REJECT). EVS's `GetAppID` occurrence, TIME's `CreateChildTask` calls and C1's pre-fix occurrence become NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with their code facts recorded; C1's retry entry decides only through a `caller` record, CF's admission comment [I]; the classes are unchanged where a NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence is bad. App. A.2, A.3, A.4 and A.8 (LP-ALLOW) are restated.

**ADR-049 — Observation effect, run conditions and numbers (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (D20, D1, D17, D14), §2.1, §2.2, §4.1 (D11, D10, D12, D19), §4.2 (D15, D21).
- **Context.** F0 was described as "timing perturbed only"; the stop method, the numbers of runs and the work-unit caps were candidates; D20 was proposed.
- **Decision.**
  - D20 = A (ADR-039 stands as written for option A, with the completion conditions of §0.9).
  - D1 approved with the corrected explanation: the cFS user and the reference binary are kept; "only time changes" is not claimed; the observation effect is checked against an unobserved reference run (§8.6); observer and control functions are kept apart and controlled runs are labelled. D11: group A is re-measured on the kernel in use at S1, recorded per run, before the S1 observation-effect comparison.
  - D17 (a): the CI ES POWERON restart command ends normal runs and normalizes after abnormal ones; POWERON does not reset every persisted file, file cache or shared file system, so the initial state and the termination record are kept.
  - D14: N is decided per purpose at S1 and frozen before the main experiment. D15: work-unit budgets frozen after each stage's pilot; excess is `unknown(budget)`. D21: the closed cone.
  - D10: the visibility assumption is marked and limited. D12: CORD is not evidence in current documents. D19: `msg_max` 10 is kept and losses are recorded.
- **Alternatives considered.** Keeping "timing perturbed only": rejected as an unverified claim. Fixing N now: rejected, no reference defines it.
- **Consequences.** §1.7 Env, §5.1, §5.9, §5.10, §8.6, §8.7; ADR-013 and ADR-020 revised, ADR-034 applied; the Korean mirrors follow.

**ADR-050 — Case bases, controls and gates (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (P-LADDER), §3 (P-R2CTRL, D22, P-C1HOLD, D13, Q16, P-C2, Q22, P-RGIT, P-F1GATE), §4.1 (D16), §4.2 (P-D18GATES).
- **Context.** ADR-044 proposed the ladder and requested the case decisions.
- **Decision.**
  - P-LADDER approved; the thin MVC as a whole is not a first completion condition.
  - R2's control: (c2) first, (c1) secondary, (b) optional, worded as "the change applied as a no-change control" and never as "the PR reverted"; the other options only when a question remains. C1: the public BP basis (D22), the provider held immediately before its creation and released on an event, the retry count and delay read from the chosen CF version, the watchdog and stop conditions recorded before the main experiment, the RESERVED-window experiment moved to a follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate item outside the five cases (P-C1HOLD).
  - C3 and C5: D13 (a) accepted, (b)(i) first with the sensitivity-triggered switch to (ii), (c) before F1; Q16 correspondences tested. C2: role reconstruction, the first control with a normally completing provider, the failure-capable variant separate, the result named a role-based validation of the C2 mechanism (P-C2, Q22). P-RGIT: an auxiliary control.
  - P-F1GATE: the choices and details are recorded before F1; each case has its own configuration, expectation sheet, functions and stages, or a stated reason and limit; expectation sheets are frozen before each case's first output. P-D18GATES: a small C1 example is a development test; a preliminary performance judgement is separate from the final judgement. D16: derived probes approved.
- **Alternatives considered.** Treating a "will not do" record as completing a case: rejected. Completing a case by the result of other code: rejected.
- **Consequences.** §4.13, §7.2, §8.2, §9.3, A.4 step 5 and the per-case table are restated; ADR-020 is revised; ADR-044's consequences stand with these decisions taken.

**ADR-051 — Scope, statistics and tools (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §4.1 (D9, D8/P-RECALL), §4.2 (D3, D4, D6), §4.3 (P-STATS, P-CLASSGT, P-TRIAGE, P-ABL-ALLOWED), §4.4 (P-LIBBPF, P-Z3), §2 (P-NAME).
- **Context.** The first paper's scope included the census, held-out sources, recall, triage and several new resource families, with a statistical procedure and 22 ablations.
- **Decision.** The required scope is the five cases and the controls they directly need (D9); held-out, recall and triage are an extended evaluation, separate from the five-case validation, with the design cases and their fixes excluded from unobserved sets (D8/P-RECALL, P-TRIAGE). The first case evaluation is descriptive (P-STATS); the word "race" is not converted into a tool class automatically (P-CLASSGT); two single-element ablations are adopted and the GPredict-named bundle is not kept (P-ABL-ALLOWED). The MLIR stage is removed (D6); the `--wrap` gates and PCT come later (D3); a native real-priority arm comes later and separately (D4); the existing environment is used first (P-LIBBPF); an SMT solver is not a condition of the first implementation (P-Z3). The name Kairos is kept as a working name (P-NAME).
- **Alternatives considered.** Keeping the MLIR gate as an optional stage: rejected by D6. Automatic significance tests over the five cases: rejected, they are not independent samples.
- **Consequences.** ADR-018 is superseded and ADR-035 and ADR-038 revised; §2.6, §8.1, §8.4, §8.7, §9.3, §9.4 and §12 are restated; ablations number 24.

**ADR-052 — Terminology T1 = (a): readiness specification, readiness precondition, readiness violation, source tier (revision 1.2).** Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (T1) and §5; research note `background/08_terminology.md` (§5.1–§5.5 recommendation and mapping).
- **Context.** The object that Kairos authors and judges was called a "readiness contract". The research note found that the word fits a documented obligation on the caller (the Design-by-Contract sense) and an admitted assumption about the environment (the assume-guarantee sense), but not entries generated by a rule or entries without a reference, and that it brings expectations of blame and composition that Kairos does not meet; it recommended "readiness specification" and left the decision to the PI (08 §5.2, §7 Q1).
- **Decision** [D]. (a): the judged object is the readiness specification (준비 상태 명세); `Req` is the readiness precondition; a violation is a readiness violation; the provenance tiers are source tiers, derived at report time; the class refinement `tolerated` is `allowed-not-ready`. "API contract" and the tier name `contract-backed` stay for documented API semantics only. The researcher-made judgement is not called a "contract violation". The names of identifiers are chosen once and listed in Appendix C.
- **Alternatives considered.** Keeping "readiness contract" with the two-sense definition of 08 §5.7 (e2): not chosen by the PI. The noun "readiness property" for the set of good executions of an instance (08 §5.2, Alternative 2): not introduced in this specification.
- **Consequences.** Wording and identifier names only: no definition, lemma, guarantee, bound or number changed [I], except that the source tier adds a reporting rule (§1.6) and stores no field. The Korean mirrors, the diagrams and the paper text follow in their own steps; the Korean form is 준비 상태 명세 in full, never a bare 명세 (08 §5.1).

---

## 12. Diagrams to draw

Each diagram lists the nodes and edges it must show. Edge labels follow a colon. Dashed edges are PI-gated or feedback paths.

### D1 — System context
- **Nodes**: PI/Researcher; `kz` and `kz-static` (root session); pinned cFS tree (read-only); `build-native_std` (compile DB, `link.txt`, generated files, `.tbl`, gcc `core-cpu1` and app `.so` with DWARF); LLVM 23 build `1053047a`; system clang-18 (BPF backend); Linux kernel, recorded per run (`6.18.44-fc-v114` in [R07 §1], `-fc-v139` on 2026-10-16; §1.7 Env) (perf uprobe PMU with attach cookies, `bpf()`, perf breakpoint PMU, ptrace, tracepoints); run harness `env/run_cfs.sh` with the root-side hook (D1) and `RUN_CFS_WRAP` (gdb); cFS process `core-cpu1` (README mode, user `ubuntu`, 27 threads); persistent state (`/dev/shm/osal:RAM`, `EEPROM.DAT`, SysV segments, key files); research records; KZ artifact store; dashed later or conditional nodes: Z3 (P-Z3, not a condition of the first implementation), a public BPF library (P-LIBBPF, only if a needed function is shown missing), `llvm-18-dev` + Nidhugg (P-NIDHUGG, held), F1 `--wrap` variant (D3, later); since revision 1.2 there is no MLIR node (D6), no TSan variant (P-TSAN) and no valgrind reference point (P-VALGRIND).
- **Edges**: PI → kz: Φ edits, decisions, freezes F1/F2; cFS tree → kz-static: sources; build tree → kz-static: compile DB, link, tables, binaries + DWARF; LLVM 23 → kz-static: libraries; clang-18 → kz: BPF object; harness → cFS process: launch as `ubuntu`, stop method (D17), normalization boots; kz → kernel: perf_event_open (uprobes by inode, breakpoints), `bpf()`, ptrace; kernel → cFS process: traps, holds; kernel → kz: ring buffers / perf samples; cFS ↔ persistent state; kz → persistent state: S0 snapshot (read-only); records → kz: reference values, policy; kz → store → PI: reports; dashed: the gated nodes → kz.

### D2 — Components and data flow
- **Nodes (components)**: `kz-static` {basis, extract, link, modref (dashed, S3b), skel, static, plan}; `kz` {proofcheck, observe, trace, conform, predict, explore (dashed border, S7), hold, gate (dashed, D3), loop, report, dump}; `libkz-core` (shared).
- **Nodes (artifacts)**: `basis.kz`, `profile.kzp/.kz`, `facts.kz` (incl. derives, writers, loop-exit trees), `anchors.kz`, `skel.kz` (cert view, exploration view, once, provenance), `census.kz`, `plan.kz`, `run-*.kzt`, `hb-*.kz`, `witness-*.kz`, `holdplan-*.kz`, `verdicts.kz`, `loop-*.kz`.
