- **Edges**: basis → `basis.kz` → extract, link, skel; extract → `facts.kz`; link → `anchors.kz`; facts + anchors + profile → skel → `skel.kz`; skel + facts + profile → static → `census.kz` (annotate O7); census → proofcheck: certificates; facts + skel + anchors + profile + runs → proofcheck: trusted inputs (labelled "trusted"); proofcheck → census: accept/reject (CC) and trust ledgers; census + anchors → plan → `plan.kz`; plan → observe → `run-*.kzt`; run → trace → `hb-*.kz` (O3–O5, O9); run + skel → conform (O1, O2; O8 from S5); hb + facts → predict (A-COMM) → witness; skel + census → explore → witness (O6); witness → holdplan → hold/gate → `run-*.kzt`; analyses → `verdicts.kz` → report; loop orchestrates; dashed feedback F1 (keys, targets, opened script) → basis/skel; F2 (model gaps) → skel; F3 confirmation runs → trace; F4 obligation failures → profile (human review); `libkz-core` linked by everything except BPF.

### D3 — Analysis workflow (per readiness instance and (σ, M) cell)
- **Decision nodes**: "template matched (certificate view)"; "key resolved?"; "reachable under B + provenance?"; "writer sets complete? (A-TYPE, O7)"; "provider: none / env-absent / present"; "FIXED_ORDER certificate? (once, frontier/once-PWB, derives)"; "PROTECTED certificate? (MUST_KEY, SCOPE)"; "kz proofcheck (re-validates inference steps; trusts facts, skel, Φ)"; "progress candidate (certified instances)"; "open: probe obligations"; "observation"; "routing" (conforming / wrong-way / O-ordered / unordered); "prediction (A-COMM label)"; "explorer (exh | dpor; σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched]; exploration view, h in b; Q_reach, Q_bad, Q_ok, Q_stall)"; "confirmation (naturalness, perturbation ratio)"; "combine per cell (§4.12)"; "transfer to σ_E (M_impl or M agreement)"; "obligation failure".
- **Terminal nodes**:
  - classes (per cell and bound, derived from the safety record): `inactive`, `fixed-order` (with a decision route), `protected` (safety statement; `allowed-not-ready` from `exh` only), `no-final-status`, `schedule-dependent`/`timeout-dependent`, `ordinal-split`, `unknown(reason)` (including `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown, and `contested`);
  - per-cell reach: `proved`, `assumed`, `model(b)`, `unreached(b)`, `contested`;
  - per-cell progress: `stall-witness` with its set of clauses (pre-use, in-use, horizon), kind, qualifiers and wait-for root, `no-stall-within(b)`, `unknown(not_checked | bound | budget | unmodelled | teardown_scope | …)`, with the per-instance refinement `timeout-masked-liveness`, the flag `tml-root` and progress chains;
  - axes: provider, scope; `σ_E(held)`.
- **Edges** as in v1, plus:
  - provider none → class by badness (not a separate terminal);
  - certificate → one safety statement with progress `unknown(not_checked)` and a progress candidate, which goes to the explorer if one is available;
  - explorer → safety statements and witnesses (Q_bad, Q_ok, per ordinal), reach (Q_reach) and progress (Q_stall), as separate outputs; a stall never leads into a class;
  - transfer → a safety statement in the receiving cell, combined with that cell's own statements;
  - progress result → its root via a progress chain;
  - M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary → portability-hazard-evidence;
  - σ_E cell → `unknown(model_level)` when transfer rule 2 fails;
  - transfer to σ_E carries the safety statement only (b = ∞);
  - suspension → loop end.

### D4 — Research workflow
- **Nodes**: Φ authoring (provenance, authoring records, refinement accounting); validation ladder (first target, ADR-044): V0 formal fixes (with V0-P, the written lemma obligations) → V1 R2's K-ID chain on the ENV basis (sub-gates S0-R2, S3a-R2, S1-R2, S2-R2); V1b (the rest of the thin MVC gates); V2 R2's control basis B′ and V3 C1 on its B′, in parallel; V4a comparison with existing process-level race work; V4b held-out and pre/post-fix pairs; case tracks: paper reading and frozen expectation sheets for all five cases, C3 and C5 on the 6.4.x pair, C2 role-derived, the A.3 line; stages S0 → S1 → S2 → S3a (thin MVC) → S4 → S5 (+O8) → S6 → S7 (kill criterion); (S8 removed, D6); S3b (dashed, conditional); freeze F1 (the evaluation engine and rules, any re-implementation of a process-level technique, the evaluation criteria and the hashes of the per-case expectation sheets, each sheet frozen before its case's first output); template-only development runs; labelled refinements; freeze F2 (incl. recall protocol and the exposure record); held-out sources (a)–(e); recall mutants and pre/post-fix pairs; sampled triage (masked raters; rater exposure recorded); ablations (24, descriptive); comparison among existing process-level race work (six analysis questions and result states; original tools and re-implementations kept apart) and Kairos's report parts (detection / classification / evidence); exposure and freeze record; perturbation groups A, B, C′, C, D (per plan), W; normalization boots; descriptive statistics (P-STATS; N per purpose frozen, caps as deviations); claims table; PI decisions; deviation records.
- **Edges**: the stage sequence; S3a → F1 → template-only → refinements → F2 → held-out and recall; S1 → perturbation groups; stages → ablations, comparison, triage (extended evaluation) → descriptive statistics → claims table; exposure and freeze record → F1, F2; V0 → V1; V0-P → V1's certificate wording; V1 → V2; V1 → V3 (no edge between V2 and V3: the order of EXT-20261012 §7, step 3 then step 4, is a reporting order); S0, S1, S2, S3a → V1 (sub-gates) and V1b (full gates); V1b → V2 (the App. A.2 instance); V1b → V3 (the app-unit closure and the app `.so` probe registration); S5, S6, S7 → V3 (classes, holds); F1 → V4a → F2 → V4b; paper track → V1 and every case track (sheets frozen before tool output); case tracks → F1 (template-only on their own basis, or a recorded reason); the 6.4.x track → F1 (D13 (c): before F1); decisions → the rungs, tracks and stages they gate (P-LADDER → V1; D20 → V0; D1, D17 → S1; D14 → S1, S9; P-R2CTRL → V2; D22, P-C1HOLD → V3; D13 → the 6.4.x track; P-C2 → the C2 track; P-RGIT → the A.3 track; P-D18GATES, D15 → S5/S7; D16 → S6; P-F1GATE → F1; P-RECALL, P-TRIAGE, P-STATS, P-CLASSGT → S9; D3, D4 → S10); every deviation → deviation record.

### D5 — cFS layer mapping
- **Left (cFS)**: PSP (main thread → `OS_Application_Run`, `soft_timebase`/`cFS-Master`, reset decision and boot record, persistence areas, `CFE_PSP_Panic` (terminal), `CFE_PSP_Restart` (terminal for the requester; ShutdownReq; teardown on the main thread), exception ring); OSAL (idmap with by-ID/by-name tokens and timed waits, tasks, mutex (PI), binsem, countsem, queues (`msg_max`, euid), timebase/timers, modules/symbols with scope, files); cFE ES (startup thread, `CreateObjects` (EarlyInit; core loop: reserve `:803`, start `:810`, publish `:818`, clear `:823`, sync `:835`, panic), script selection and `StartApplications`, `AppCreate` (reserve `:821`, publish `:869`, free `:893`), trampoline, App/Task/Lib records, `SystemState`, `WaitForSystemState`, `MainTaskSyncDelay`, background task, `CleanUpApp` (core-table cleanup order)); EVS (register with memset, `SendEventWithAppID` two conjuncts, own-ID guard); SB (pipes, routes, route_lookup and puts, MsgLim, receive, subscription reporting in SB's task, header MsgIds, recursion guard); TBL; TIME (1 Hz chain, seqlock, synch slots by index); FS background; CDS; apps with tables and the selected script.
- **Right (Φ)**: kinds (incl. `es.self`, `cell(loc)`, `sb.msglim`, `es.shutdown`, `loader.sym`); operations with per-conjunct LPs; rules R1–R19, SR, P-*, RF; readiness specifications (K-REG, K-ID, K-SELF, K-SPAWN, K-PHASE, K-ROUTE, K-CAP, K-CAP.msglim, K-MODE, K-TBL, K-PERSIST, K-HANDOFF, K-LIFE, K-GEN, K-LOCKBAL, K-LOCK.cancel, K-ATOM, K-TEARDOWN); actor classes; model levels; P_sched profiles.
- **Edges** (examples): `:835` + panic → R19 (S, obs-fatal, predicate from code); `:803/:818/:823` → `es.app` RESERVED/LIVE/clear + allocation record ρ; `cfe_sb_task.c:138` → `cell(CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` + K-ID capture point; `OS_QueuePut` full → K-CAP; MsgLim → K-CAP.msglim; `osapi-idmap.c:384–385` vs `:401–404` → K-LOCKBAL (M_impl, by-name); `cfe_psp_support.c:87` → `es.shutdown` SHUTDOWN_REQ.

### D6 — Verdict state machine (per cell)
- **States** (class region, per cell and bound): `candidate`, `open` (not a class: rendered `unknown(<open-route reason>)`, §1.10, §4.6); `inactive`; `protected[cert]`, `protected[model b]`, `allowed-not-ready[model b]`; `fixed-order[cert]`, `fixed-order[model b]`; `no-final-status`; `ordinal-split`; `schedule-dependent` with refinement `timeout-dependent`; `contested` (rendered `unknown(model_fault)`); `unknown(not_reached | model_gap | bound | budget | key_unresolved | loss | unmodelled | writer_incomplete | horizon | horizon_static | no_decision_route | one_sided | unordered | O-ordered-only | conditional-observation | conformance | model_fault | model_level)`.
- **Parallel regions**:
  - evidence realization: `none` → `predicted-modelled` | `predicted-extrapolated` | `model(b)` | `predicted` → `realized`;
  - naturalness: `natural` | `held-F0` | `gated-F1` | `timeout-exercised`; `derived`;
  - provider;
  - **reach**: `assumed` → `proved` (b = ∞); `assumed` → `model(b)` | `unreached(b)` → `contested` (bounded b);
  - scope;
  - **progress**: `unknown(not_checked)` → `unknown(bound | budget | unmodelled | model_gap)` | `stall-witness[model b]` → `stall-witness[realized]` or `contested` | `stall-witness[realized]` (σ_E) | `no-stall-within(b)` → `contested`; `unknown(teardown_scope)` for K-TEARDOWN; flags `timeout-masked-liveness` and `tml-root`, set of clauses, kind, qualifiers and wait-for root; progress chain;
  - trust ledger (every cell; part (i) empty for verdicts without a certificate).
- **Transitions** (class region):
  - candidate → inactive / fixed-order[cert] / protected[cert] (static, per M; progress stays `unknown(not_checked)`);
  - candidate → open;
  - open → schedule-dependent (bad + good witness at the same ordinal);
  - open → ordinal-split (bad + good witnesses at different ordinals); ordinal-split → schedule-dependent (a same-ordinal pair found);
  - open → fixed-order[model b] / protected[model b] / allowed-not-ready[model b] / no-final-status (`exh`; (no, no) gives no-final-status);
  - a certified `¬OK_i` whose REJECT events lie after the decision, in its own cell or transferred → unknown(no_decision_route) with the statement shown; a later bad witness in the cell or `¬Bad_i` moves it to fixed-order or no-final-status;
  - protected or fixed-order → no-final-status (the other statement established, by `exh` or transfer);
  - schedule-dependent → refinement (derived from per-σ cells; `exh` only);
  - open → unknown(budget);
  - any → unknown(model_fault) (O3/O4/O9 against a certified statement; loop ends);
  - model-only statement + contradicting realized witness within its bound → contested (O6; no loop end);
  - certified or certificate-transferred statement + contradicting `exh` witness within its bound → the explored bound becomes contested (O10; directed check; the certificate's b = ∞ record keeps its state with a persistent contested mark; no loop end unless the witness is realized);
  - M_api cert + contradicting observation → portability-hazard-evidence (no state change at M_api);
  - unknown(model_gap | model_fault) → candidate (new Φ hash; new loop);
  - evidence refuted → model-fault record.
- **Transitions** (progress region):
  - unknown(not_checked) → stall-witness / no-stall-within(b) (`exh`, `Q_stall`);
  - unknown(not_checked) → stall-witness[realized] (σ_E: a wait-for fact that shows permanence, without a model witness);
  - stall-witness[model b] → stall-witness[realized] (wait-for oracle; confirmable kinds only);
  - stall-witness[model b] → contested (O6: the prefix to the stall state is realized and a witnessing actor is then observed to proceed);
  - no-stall-within(b) → contested (realized stall within b; O6);
  - no transition from a certificate into the progress region;
  - there is no protected[cert] → allowed-not-ready transition.
- **Invariants**:
  - no state is named "safe", "timing-protected", "live" or "deadlock-free";
  - no transition goes from "no alarm" to a universal class or to `no-stall-within(b)`;
  - σ_E universal (safety) states arise only via transfer rule 2 with b = ∞;
  - a stall witness never moves the class region;
  - every non-suspending transition moves up within its bound.

### D7 — Observation pipeline
- **Nodes**: `plan.kz` (sites, cookies, decoders, cost classes, debug-register allocation); loader (raw `bpf()`/`perf_event_open`, `<elf.h>`) | perf-sample backend | optional tracefs instance; uprobe registrations by (inode, offset) on `core-cpu1` and app `.so` (before launch); per-task write watchpoints; BPF programs (entry, point, return, watch); site-descriptor array indexed by cookie; per-thread `tseq`; per-object `objseq`; per-CPU ring buffers / perf buffers; drop counters / `PERF_RECORD_LOST`; drainer (CPU time and placement recorded); `run-*.kzt` (SITES, RAW_EVENTS, STRINGS, STACKS, LOSS, RUNMETA, PERTURBATION); `kz trace`; `kz hold` (ptrace, DR0–DR3, attach at clone); harness root hook; normalization-boot driver; cFS threads.
- **Edges**: plan → loader: register by inode, load descriptors; thread hits probe → BPF: trap (0.57 µs at entry+4; 31.7 µs class otherwise); BPF → cookie → descriptor; BPF → `tseq`/`objseq`; BPF → buffer (failure → drop counter); buffer → drainer → `run-*.kzt`; drainer → `kz hold`: live stream (release on event); `kz hold` → thread: stop/continue; harness hook → loader: start before launch; abnormal exit → normalization boot (unobserved, excluded); `run-*.kzt` → `kz trace`.

### D8 — The R2 certificate (ES and EVS halves)
- **Nodes**: capture points `c` (loads of `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` at argument evaluation in the SB event sites inlined in `CFE_ES_TaskInit` / `CFE_EVS_TaskInit`) and the slot checks; `m_ES` (return of the `CFE_ES_TaskInit` call, `cfe_es_task.c:114`), `m_EVS` (`cfe_evs_task.c:213`); raises `cfe_es_task.c:134`, `cfe_evs_task.c:229`; falsifiers `f_ES`, `f_EVS` (`cfe_es_start.c:803` per iteration) with allocation records ρ_ES, ρ_EVS; failure clears `:823` per iteration; observations `o_ES`, `o_EVS` (`:835`) with the extracted predicate; panic branches; SB spawn (`:810`, SB iteration); SB entry; writer `x` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`); INIT (zero-initialized `CFE_SB_Global`).
- **Edges**: `c` ↛ `m` (no path: FRONTIER); `m` →T-PO raise →T-OBS `o`; `:823` ✕ (OWN-NOPATH); `raise_ES` ✕ from `W_o(o_EVS)` (ONCE-PWB); `o` →T-PO (panic cut by T-TERM) SB spawn →T-SPAWN SB entry →T-PO `x`; INIT → "AppId = 0" (INIT_FALSE); `derives(c, CFE_SB_Global.AppId)`; a box listing every writer entry with transition and key status, and a trust-ledger box with the four parts of App. A.1 step 9a (re-validated steps, extracted facts, trusted entries, refutable items).

### D9 — Teardown scoping
- **Nodes**: requester actor (`CFE_ES_ResetCFE` → `CFE_PSP_Restart`: shm delete if POWERON → boot record → `ShutdownReq` store → `SIGUSR1` → delay `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` → `abort`, terminal); PSP main thread (`CFE_ES_Main` → return → `OS_Application_Run` sigwait loop → observe `ShutdownReq` → delay 100 → `OS_DeleteAllObjects`); in-scope uses; K-TEARDOWN uses; next run's B.Init.
- **Edges**: `ShutdownReq` store →T-OBS `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (SR ∈ MB); `OS_DeleteAllObjects` ⇢ disabler of every `osal.obj` (SCOPE-excluded from in-scope certificates); occurrences decided after SR → K-TEARDOWN; POWERON → segments deleted (not every persisted file, D17 (a)) and the initial state recorded; other exits → PROCESSOR reset → normalization boot.

### D10 — Two skeleton views
- **Nodes**: an actor CFG with init prefix, message loop (handlers H1, H2), exit; `Sk^cert` (loop head, back edge, iterate/exit choice, dispatch folded by `Prov`); `Sk^h` for h = 1, 2 (unrolled copies, `TRUNC` marker); consumers: MustBefore/certificates/inactive (from `Sk^cert` only), explorer/O8 (from `Sk^h`, h in b), proofcheck view check.
- **Edges**: CFG → `Sk^cert`; CFG → `Sk^h`; `Sk^cert` → static; `Sk^h` → explore; certificate step naming a `Sk^h` node → proofcheck: reject; litmus App. A.8 annotated "writer only in handler iteration 2".

---

## 13. Glossary

| Term | Meaning in this specification |
|---|---|
| **Actor / actor instance** | a thread of execution (OSAL task, raw thread or environment root); an instance is (static actor τ, generation, n), n counting the executions of τ's spawn sites by its spawner, n = 1 for once actors (§1.2) |
| **Allocation record ρ(n)** | the abstract record allocated by reservation node n (§4.2) |
| **Allowed** | the not-ready outcomes a readiness specification accepts, from a finite vocabulary, each with a reference horizon; at the caller level, only entries with a `caller` record decide; entries with only `rule` or `supplier` records decide nothing (QT-4 strict alternative, §1.6) |
| **Anchor / anchor audit** | the mapping of a source site or AST pattern to an instruction of the gcc binary; the audit lists sites without a unique anchor |
| **API contract** | documented API semantics (an obligation or a result that the supplier's documentation states); the OSAL API contract of the model level M_api (§1.4) and the source tier `contract-backed` (§1.6). It is not the name of the judged object (that is the readiness specification, decision T1) |
| **appreq** | the reference stating what a use must achieve for the calling application or the mission; default `unspecified_by_reference`; never a supplier-side text or a rule; a conflict with a `caller` entry is stated and the judgement is withheld, leaving the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED without an invented precedence (QT-5, §1.6) |
| **A-COMM** | the per-witness check that unobserved shared reads in the cone are observed, constant or covered by kept outcomes (§4.9) |
| **Assumed visibility** | an edge that holds only under A4; flagged on every verdict that uses it |
| **A-TYPE, A-FRESH, A-OWNER** | typed-access, allocation-freshness and ownership assumptions (§6.1) |
| **B (basis)** / **B′** | everything fixed except the schedule (§1.7) / a configuration or input variant: a new basis, never a "new bug" |
| **Bad / good witness / stall state** | a finite execution with a VIOLATING (or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED) in-scope occurrence / a finite execution with a READY or TOLERATED in-scope occurrence, each recorded with its occurrence ordinal (§1.6) / a reachable product state in which an actor dead before, inside or holding the horizon of an occurrence of the use exists (§1.8) |
| **Capture point** | the load from which a value-carried argument derives; the LP of a value conjunct (§1.4) |
| **Census** | the instantiation of every readiness specification at every context-qualified site, each with a class or `unknown(reason)` per (σ, M) |
| **Certificate** | a checkable proof object (rule instances, writer entries with statuses, once facts, derives facts, extracted predicates, frontier, reach) for one **safety statement**: `¬OK_i` (fixed-order when its REJECT is at the occurrence's decision), or `¬NR_i`, no reached occurrence decided not ready (protected). `kz proofcheck` re-validates its inference steps relative to a trusted base, marking the steps that call `libkz-core`, and its trust ledger records them |
| **Certificate view `Sk^cert` / exploration view `Sk^h`** | skeleton with every unbounded loop star-closed / skeleton unrolled to horizon h with truncation marks (§4.3) |
| **Cell** | the part of a verdict record for one (σ, M) |
| **Chain** | a readiness-violation link `i₁ ⇝ i₂` between occurrences, recorded with every branch (§1.6); a **progress chain** `i₁ ⇝_p i₂` attributes a stall of i₂ to a not-ready outcome of i₁ (§1.8) |
| **Comparison kinds (K1, K2, K3)** | an original tool run as released (K1; `NOT_RUN` is never a false negative) / a re-implementation of a process-level technique, made only when the original cannot be run and kept apart from original results (K2) / a Kairos ablation that removes one element (K3), which says nothing about what a prior technique can express (§8.3, §8.4) |
| **Six analysis questions / result states** | the questions answered for every (tool, case) pair of existing process-level race work, and the state of each cell: `NOT_RUN`, `INAPPLICABLE`, `PATH_NOT_EXECUTED`, `OBSERVATION_GAP`, `ANALYSIS_GAP`, `REPORTED` (§8.3) |
| **Cone** | the closed set of actors and objects relevant to a candidate (§4.10) |
| **C_σ** | schedule constraints of σ_R1; never HB; never combined with an observed prefix |
| **Dead actor** | an actor instance that is not terminated and that no reachable state enables (§1.8); independent of fairness; under σ_R1 measured with σ_U's enabling relation |
| **DECLARED_TRUSTED** | a writer, allocation or ownership entry that rests on a Φ guard or assumption the analysis could not verify; listed in every certificate that uses it |
| **derives(arg, loc, site)** | the fact that an argument's value is a copy chain from one shared location read at site |
| **Detection / classification / evidence** | the three report parts of a Kairos result: whether a not-ready occurrence of the expected instance is reported (occurrence detection; a VIOLATING and a NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence count alike) / the class, refinements, outcome label and progress result / route, realization, bound, scope, reach, trust ledger and proof status (§8.5). **Class detection** is Kairos's recall score of §8.5 item 4 (RQ9, extended evaluation) |
| **E-post / E-TBL** | R2's in-basis negative control on the ENV basis: the K-ID instances that read SB's identity cell after its publication at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` / TBL's identity cell; no fixed-order verdict is expected; C3's v7.0.1 site lies in E-post (§9.3) |
| **Evidence: realization / naturalness** | how a class is supported (certificate … realized) / how natural a realized run was (natural … timeout-exercised) |
| **exh / dpor** | the explorer's exhaustive stateful engine / source-DPOR engine (unverified while T-JACM is P) |
| **Exposure and freeze record; blind** | the append-only record of who saw which case, fix and expected verdict, and when, with the freezes and the post-freeze changes / an item label given only where that record shows no exposure before the item's governing freeze (F1 for development items, F2 for held-out and recall items) (§7.2, §7.5 item 5) |
| **F0–F3** | fidelity classes of [R07 §7] |
| **Framework-level judgement (`fw_doc`, `fw_conf`)** | what the supplier's references say about a not-ready result, and whether the result occurred as documented; never combined with the outcome label (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **Finite execution Pref / maximal execution Max** | the finite sequences of enabled transitions (safety is defined on them) / the finite executions with no enabled transition plus the fair infinite ones (used only for non-claims) (§1.8) |
| **Falsifier** | an event in the observer's actor that dominates an observation and definitely makes the observed conjunct false |
| **Fixed-order** | class: `¬OK_i` established and deciding (a bad witness in the cell, or a certificate whose REJECT is at the occurrence's decision), `¬Bad_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is READY or TOLERATED, and a bad witness exists in the cell, or not-ready occurrences are decided bad at their decision; that every execution reaches a decision is not claimed; without a decision route the class is `unknown(no_decision_route)` |
| **Frontier node m** | a node in the use's once actor with no path to the conjunct's LP and `m ∈ MB(x)` for every writer x |
| **Generation** | an incarnation of a resource or actor |
| **HB_π, VC, VC_R** | happens-before on a trace (looper rule applied); vector clocks over all definite edges and over unconditional/obs-fatal edges |
| **Horizon h** | message-loop iterations in the exploration view; not chosen; results reported per completed h |
| **Idle wait / hang state** | the first blocking wait of an iteration of a star-closed message or periodic loop / a stall state that is neither idle nor terminal-pending (§1.8) |
| **Inactive** | class: no execution reaches the use under B (certificate view, with message provenance) |
| **KEM** | Kairos Event Model |
| **Key / key status** | the identity of a resource within its kind / MUST_KEY, MAY_KEY or NO_KEY of a writer w.r.t. an instance key |
| **K-SELF.es** | the generic readiness specification "the caller's own App and task records are LIVE", at seven call sites |
| **K-TEARDOWN** | the instances `(κ, t, key, teardown)`, holding the occurrences whose decision event follows a SHUTDOWN_REQ LP: the complement of the default scope, classified by the same statuses and reported apart; progress `unknown(teardown_scope)` |
| **KZ container** | the typed binary artifact format (§2.3) |
| **L1/L2/L3** | lemma obligations: await summarization with extracted predicates; removal of a critical section whose lock is not a cone object and is acquired by no other cone actor, and whose body touches nothing in the cone and contains no blocking operation; mover-reduced critical sections |
| **LOSS-UNDER-ORDER** | caller-level outcome label for route/capacity specifications whose caller-level Allowed is unspecified by reference and that have no must-deliver `appreq` (where a must-deliver `appreq` conflicts with a `caller` entry, the label is `not-ready-unjudged`, §1.6 `appreq` step (3)); the framework-level judgement and the observed loss are reported beside it (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **LP** | linearization point, declared per conjunct |
| **M_api / M_impl** | OSAL model levels: API-contract model atomic at LPs / OSAL POSIX internals analysed |
| **M_impl-relevant** | an operation modelled differently at the two levels (idmap, task, binsem, queue paths) |
| **MB (MustBefore)** | static must-precedence, the greatest fixpoint of §4.4 |
| **Message provenance Prov(p)** | the MsgIds (and codes) that can reach pipe p under B (§4.3) |
| **Normalization boot** | an unobserved boot ending with the POWERON restart, run after an abnormal exit (§4.13) |
| **No-final-status** | class: both `¬Bad_i` and `¬OK_i` established; every reached occurrence is PENDING (or `NOT_EVALUATED`), or none is reached (§1.10) |
| **cont_candidate** | the nearest post-dominator of a test node, per loop iteration, that is not the function's return and at which neither the status nor the not-ready branch's writes are live; an extracted code fact reported with the occurrence, never an ACCEPT (§1.6, Q15 strict alternative) |
| **Normal continuation / error exit** | for `returned_and_tested`: the node named, with a citation, in the Allowed entry (ACCEPT; a REJECT at the same node wins); an entry that cites none is `unspecified_by_reference`, and the nearest post-dominator of the test (`cont_candidate`) is only an extracted fact (Q15 strict alternative) / a call of `CFE_ES_ExitApp`, `CFE_ES_ExitChildTask`, `OS_TaskExit`, `CFE_ES_ResetCFE` or `CFE_PSP_Restart`, or a terminal op (REJECT); an error return is also a REJECT unless a reference documents propagation (§1.6) |
| **O1–O10, CC** | consistency obligations and the certificate check (§2.5); O10 is model–certificate agreement: an `exh` witness against a certified or transferred statement makes the bound contested and starts a directed check |
| **Occurrence / ordinal / ordinal-split** | an occurrence begins at its call, with its value conjuncts' captures attached, and is decided by the call's return at the latest (`NOT_EVALUATED`, counted by no statement, if the call returned before one of its conjunct LPs with no conjunct found false) / (actor instance named across executions, k) for the k-th occurrence of the template with the instance's key begun by that actor / class: bad and good witnesses exist only at different ordinals; no schedule dependence is claimed (§1.6, §1.10) |
| **once(τ), once(n)** | the actor has at most one instance per execution / the node occurs at most once per execution (§1.2) |
| **On-behalf frame** | an identity frame for code run in one actor on behalf of another |
| **PENDING / horizon monitor** | a not-ready occurrence whose Allowed horizon has not ended; never counted as TOLERATED / the ACCEPT–REJECT–OPEN reading of an Allowed entry on its monitored actor (§1.6) |
| **Periodic wait** | a timed wait inside a star-closed loop whose timeout and success outcomes both continue the loop; its timeout is not a time-based transition (§1.8) |
| **Perturbation ratio** | probe and hold time added between two deciding events, over the code-derived separation protecting the other order (§4.13) |
| **Φ (profile)** | the declarative cFS knowledge base, with core expressions, bindings and patterns |
| **P_sched** | a cited target scheduling profile for σ_R1 |
| **Portability hazard (-evidence)** | protected under M_impl but violable under M_api / an M_api-only certificate contradicted by the binary |
| **Pred(π)** | the predictive model of a trace |
| **Pre-registered expectation sheet** | per development case, basis, (σ, M, b) cell and instance: the expected class, refinements, progress value and outcome label, with any reference class statement; frozen by hash at F1 (§7.2) |
| **Process-terminal step** | a terminal step that ends the process (on pc-linux: `abort`, `CFE_PSP_Panic`, the restart requester's abort, `main`'s return after the teardown); later states are excluded from stall evaluation, no status or horizon verdict is decided by or after it, and a stall before a still-reachable one is terminal-pending (§1.6, §1.8) |
| **Progress result** | per cell and bound: stall-witness (pre-use, in-use or horizon; kind, qualifiers, harm, progress chain), no-stall-within(b) (`exh` only), or unknown(reason), by default not_checked; never "live" (§1.8, §1.10) |
| **Protected / allowed-not-ready** | class: `¬Bad_i` established, `¬OK_i` not: no in-scope occurrence that is reached is VIOLATING or NOT_READY_UNJUDGED (with a certificate, `¬NR_i`: none is decided not ready); says nothing about executions that do not reach the use / protected with a TOLERATED occurrence (`exh` only) |
| **Provider axis** | present, none, env-absent, unknown |
| **PWB** | precedes-whenever-both-occur (FRONTIER or ONCE-PWB) |
| **Reach** | per cell and bound: proved (a run or confirmed model path of that σ at that M, with the per-cell conditions of §1.10), assumed, model(b), or unreached(b) (`exh`, or `dpor` labelled unverified); a bound refuted by evidence is `contested` |
| **Readiness category (RC1–RC15)** | one of the 15 categories of readiness specifications of [R03 §4] (called "readiness-contract kinds" there), with unchanged identifiers (§3.15) |
| **Readiness instance** | an instance `i = (κ, t, key, scope)` of a readiness specification (§1.6); the unit of the census and of a verdict record |
| **Readiness precondition (`Req`)** | the conjunction of required lifecycle or value predicates of a readiness specification, each with its own LP (§1.4, §1.6); a requirement on the key-selected resource, not a promise by any party |
| **Readiness specification (κ)** | the judged object `κ = ⟨id, U, sel, Req, Oracle, Allowed, appreq, harm, prov, authored, guard, scope⟩` (§1.6): authored, or generated by a fixed rule from the program text, before evaluation, frozen by hash, and never trained on runs. Formerly "readiness contract" (decision T1 = (a), Appendix C) |
| **Readiness violation** | an in-scope occurrence that is `VIOLATING`: the readiness precondition does not hold and the caller-level Allowed does not accept the outcome; a subclass of order violation where a provider of the state exists. An occurrence with caller-level Allowed `unspecified_by_reference` is not called a violation (§1.6) |
| **Recall protocol** | detection measured on history-derived program mutants and pre/post-fix pairs (§8.5); part of the extended evaluation, separate from the five-case validation, with results for the variant set only (D8/P-RECALL) |
| **Robustness tag** | per trace edge: unconditional, obs-fatal, obs-soft, schedule-chosen, assumed |
| **Schedule-dependent / refinements** | class: a bad and a good witness at the same occurrence ordinal / timeout-dependent (`exh` only; needs σ_U∞ reach); flag timeout-rescued; per-instance progress refinement timeout-masked-liveness, with the flag `tml-root` on every TML-relevant instance into which no progress chain leads (a chain root, or an unchained TML instance) |
| **Scope** | part of the instance `(κ, t, key, scope)`: an occurrence belongs to the default-scope instance iff no SHUTDOWN_REQ LP precedes its decision event, decided when the occurrence is decided; otherwise it belongs to the K-TEARDOWN instance |
| **Shape** | of a bad finite witness: WRONG-WAY, UNORDERED, ABSENT (no extension can contain the enabling transition), NOT-OBSERVED, STALE (§1.10) |
| **Side (`supplier` / `caller` / `rule`)** | of a provenance record, for a given readiness specification: whether it speaks for the supplier's behaviour, is a text saying what the using code must achieve or accepts, or is no text but a fixed rule of Kairos applied to the using code (a `rule` record never decides a status) (§1.6) |
| **Source tier** | what stands behind an instance: `contract-backed`, `requirement-derived`, `admitted`, `issue-derived`, `code-derived`, `researcher`, `unreferenced`; derived at report time from `prov` and the caller-level Allowed, weakest part wins; counts and verdicts are reported per tier (§1.6) |
| **σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1[P_sched], σ_E** | untimed; σ_U without time-based transitions (non-periodic timeouts; the failing last attempt of a delay-paced retry, which then waits if it is one transition at M and otherwise leaves its actor stranded; the Φ-declared give-ups, among them the forced kill of an app that has not exited; the expiry for an exited app stays); fixed-priority one core under a profile, a separate system projected into σ_U; the real environment (finite runs) |
| **σ_E(held), σ_E(plan P)** | realized but not natural / a σ_E statement under observation plan P |
| **Stranded actor** | under σ_U∞, an actor whose only σ_U-enabled transitions are unpaired time-based ones (the failing-result branch of a last attempt that is several transitions at M); its deadness tests and those of its closure are undecided (§1.8) |
| **Support locations** | the memory a conjunct's oracle reads, its bindings, or its `derives` location |
| **T-PO, T-SPAWN, T-OBS, T-TERM** | the MustBefore rules (§4.4) |
| **Template-only run** | an evaluation run with the frozen profile and no case-specific refinement |
| **Time-based transition** | a non-periodic timed wait's timeout, the failing outcome of a delay-paced retry's last attempt, or a give-up that Φ declares time-based (every countdown or clock comparison whose expiry abandons a wait or acts on another actor must be declared; undeclared ones are labelled `tb-undeclared`); σ_U∞ removes them (§1.8) |
| **Transition status** | COMPUTED, DECLARED_VERIFIED, DECLARED_TRUSTED, DISABLED |
| **Trust ledger** | the four-part record of a certificate or verdict cell: re-validated steps (own or via T5), extracted facts, trusted entries, refutable items (§4.5.1) |
| **Units U1–U6** | defect cause, fix history, program variant, static instance, dynamic occurrence, run; every count names one, and no count of one unit is the sample size of another (§8.2) |
| **unspecified_by_reference** | no reference fixes the value, and no value is chosen; chosen values are deviations |
| **v7.0.1 counterpart** | a site or window at v7.0.1 that shares a design case's shape while the case itself is latent, absent or older (C1 in the default B, C2's barrier, C3's TIME/TBL contexts, App. A.3); it tests generic layers only and is never reported as evidence on the case (§9.3) |
| **Validation ladder (V0–V4, V0-P) / case track** | the first target (approved as P-LADDER on 2026-10-15) and the order of the dynamic end-to-end work: the formal fixes (V0, with the written lemma obligations V0-P), R2's chain (V1), the rest of the thin-MVC gates (V1b), R2's control (V2) and C1 (V3) in parallel, comparison (V4a) and held-out evaluation (V4b) / a per-case path whose reference-based level runs now and whose tool-level steps start after its basis decision and the stages its track row names (§9.3, ADR-044) |
| **Wait-for root** | of a stall: a terminal strongly connected component of its wait-for graph (a lock cycle, or a cycle of predicate and object waits), or a sink with the wait it leaves unsatisfied; for a leaked lock, the object and its holder; counted detections are grouped by the root set (§1.8) |
| **Work unit** | a deterministic count (states, transitions, search nodes, fixpoint updates) used for every analysis budget |
| **Writer set W / W^must / disabler set D** | stores that can make a conjunct true / definitely make it true / can make it false, for a key |

---

## Appendix A. Worked examples (expected behaviour; nothing here was run)

### A.1 R2: fixed-order from a generic specification (K-ID) plus framework rule R19, fully written out

1. **Census.** `K-ID.send-with-appid` (provenance `cfe_evs.h:168–172`) is instantiated at every context-qualified `CFE_EVS_SendEventWithAppID` site. The SB sites pass `CFE_SB_Global.AppId` as `stamp` (39 SB sites, [R2g §6]). Contexts inlined from `CFE_ES_TaskInit` and `CFE_EVS_TaskInit` are distinct templates (ADR-012). No SB-specific readiness specification exists.
2. **Conjuncts and T-DERIVE** (fixes [X1-M1]). `Req = valid(arg.AppID) ∧ state(evs.reg(arg.AppID)) = REG`. At each SB event site, `derives(arg AppID, (CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId), c)` holds, where `c` is the load at argument evaluation (e.g. `cfe_sb_api.c:226–228`, re-read). The value conjunct `V` is evaluated at `c` over `cell(CFE_SB_Global.AppId)` (L-DERIVE). The registry conjunct plays no role here: EVS tests index validity first (`cfe_evs.c:185–189`), and `V` already fails.
3. **INIT and the writer set of V.** INIT: `CFE_SB_Global` is a zero-initialized global (`cfe_sb_task.c:40`, re-read), so `AppId = 0 = CFE_ES_APPID_UNDEFINED`: `INIT_FALSE(V)`. S3a writers of `(CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId)`:
   - `x`: the success-branch out-pointer store of `CFE_ES_GetAppID` instantiated at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` — W, COMPUTED, MUST_KEY (same location);
   - the failure-branch store of `UNDEFINED` (`cfe_es_api.c:758–760`) — neutral;
   - the EarlyInit `memset(&CFE_SB_Global, 0, …)` (`cfe_sb_init.c:50`, re-read) — writes 0, neutral;
   - A-TYPE listings must show no untyped write and no escaping address (`&CFE_SB_Global.AppId` is taken only at `:138`, into `GetAppID`'s summarized formal); otherwise the writer set is DISABLED and the certificate is withheld. Any further store found is listed.
4. **Observation `o_ES`** (ES iteration of the unrolled core loop; fixes [X2-01], [X1-M8]). `o_ES` is the success exit of `MainTaskSyncDelay` at `cfe_es_start.c:835`. `Pred_code` is extracted (form (b), §4.3): `∀ r ∈ AppTable: ¬(IsUsed(r) ∧ r.AppState < RUNNING)`. `C_ES` is its instance at `ρ_ES`, the record allocated by the falsifier `f_ES` = `SetUsed(RESERVED)` at `:803`. Right after `f_ES`, `ρ_ES` is used and its `AppState` is 0 (UNDEFINED): before `f_ES` no task exists, and the only `AppState` writer that can precede it is the zeroing memset at `:82`, so no freshness assumption is needed here.

   | Writer | Site | Actor | Treatment | Status |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | raise | `cfe_es_api.c:573` via `cfe_es_task.c:134` | ES main | kept; `m_ES ∈ MB` (T-PO) | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` STOPPED | `cfe_es_api.c:399` via `cfe_es_task.c:200` | ES main | kept; it comes after `raise_ES` and `m_ES` (T-PO), so it does not affect the result | COMPUTED [I] |
   | `RunLoop` raise | `cfe_es_api.c:471` | none of the concurrent actors | absent (call graph) | COMPUTED |
   | scan WAITING | `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` | `ES_BG_TASK` | neutral (same-record guard) | COMPUTED |
   | `CleanUpApp` record free | `cfe_es_apps.c:1407–1640` | `ES_BG_TASK` | excluded (external app with `AppState > RUNNING`; relational guard) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** |
   | failure clear `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:823` (ES iteration) | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH (only the panic follows) | COMPUTED |
   | EarlyInit `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:82` | startup thread | ONCE-PWB(w, `f_ES`) | COMPUTED |
   | later startup-thread writers (`:823` of later iterations, `cfe_es_apps.c:893`, script reservations) | — | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH (no path back to `o_ES`; the core loop is unrolled) | COMPUTED |
   | writers in EVS, SB, TBL, TIME, script apps and their children | — | spawned after `o_ES` | FRONTIER: `PWB(o_ES, w)` | COMPUTED |
   | `cFS-Master`, console | — | raw threads | no writers (S3a over their call graphs) | COMPUTED |

   Result: `m_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)` and `raise_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)`.
5. **Observation `o_EVS`** (EVS iteration; fixes [X1-M3](b)). Falsifier `f_EVS` = `:803` in the EVS iteration, record `ρ_EVS`. Right after `f_EVS`, `ρ_EVS` is used and `AppState(ρ_EVS)` = 0: this needs **A-FRESH** (`ρ_EVS` ≠ `ρ_ES` and never used before) and **A-OWNER** (ES main's and `ES_BG_TASK`'s `AppState` writers target `ρ_ES`, hence `NO_KEY` for `ρ_EVS`).

   | Writer | Site | Actor | Treatment | Status |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | raise | `cfe_es_api.c:573` via `cfe_evs_task.c:229` | EVS main | kept; `m_EVS ∈ MB` | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` stores | `cfe_evs_task.c:220, :257` | EVS main | kept; after `m_EVS` (no-ops inside the window, §3.3) | COMPUTED |
   | raise | `cfe_es_task.c:134` | ES main | **ONCE-PWB**: `once(raise_ES)` and `raise_ES ∈ MB(o_ES) ⊆ MB(f_EVS)` ⇒ `PWB(raise_ES, f_EVS)` | COMPUTED |
   | `ExitApp` STOPPED | `cfe_es_task.c:200` → `cfe_es_api.c:399` | ES main | excluded: guard `Type != CORE` on the owner record, which is `ρ_ES` (A-OWNER) with `Type = CORE` (MUST_KEY store at `:795`, ES iteration); also KEY-SEP vs `ρ_EVS` under A-FRESH. Not dominated by `m_EVS` and not PWB-ordered (an early `ExitApp` after a timed-out CORE_READY wait is reachable in σ_U) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** (A-OWNER) |
   | scan WAITING | `cfe_es_apps.c:1144` | `ES_BG_TASK` | neutral | COMPUTED |
   | `CleanUpApp` record free | `cfe_es_apps.c:1407–1640` | `ES_BG_TASK` | excluded (relational guard) | **DECLARED_TRUSTED** |
   | failure clears `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:823` (ES and EVS iterations) | startup thread | OWN-NOPATH | COMPUTED |
   | EarlyInit `memset` | `cfe_es_start.c:82` | startup thread | ONCE-PWB | COMPUTED |
   | writers in SB, TBL, TIME, script apps | — | spawned after `o_EVS` | FRONTIER | COMPUTED |

   Result: `m_EVS ∈ MB(o_EVS)`.
6. **MustBefore chain.** `m_ES ∈ MB(raise_ES)` (T-PO) → T-OBS → `m_ES ∈ MB(o_ES)` → T-PO along the startup thread (panic branches have no successors, T-TERM; the failure branch through `:823` leads only to the panic) → `spawn_SB` (SB iteration) → T-SPAWN (single spawn site; `once(SB main)`) → SB entry → T-PO → `x`. Hence `m_ES ∈ MB(x)`. The EVS half is the same with `m_EVS` through `o_EVS`; the EVS iteration precedes the SB iteration in the generated core order ([R02 §1.3]).
7. **Frontier and once.** `once(ES main)` and `once(EVS main)`: each has one spawn node in the unrolled core loop, on the once startup thread, outside every star-closed region. `m_ES ∉ Anc(c)` for every capture point `c` inside `CFE_ES_TaskInit` (called once, before the message loop; in `Sk^cert` the loop's back edge returns to the loop head after `m_ES`). **The early returns do not matter**: `CFE_ES_TaskInit` returns at L340, L371, L381 or L393 after failures, and `CFE_ES_TaskMain` still raises at L134 (re-read); the proof never needs the use to dominate the raise ([J2-1]).
8. **Badness, in three judgements.** Caller level: `appreq` = `cfe_evs.h:169–172` (caller side), `Allowed = none` (the header names the system log, a different API, as the alternative); harm `event_request_rejected`. Framework level: `CFE_EVS_APP_ILLEGAL_APP_ID`, documented as `\retval` (`cfe_evs.h:199`), error severity (`cfe_error.h:115–119, 267`), with no counter and no event (`cfe_evs.c:186–189`): `fw_doc` = {`documented-result`}; `fw_conf` = `as-documented` in the cells backed by the runs (every violating use is followed by that result, [R2g §7]), `unknown` at M_api. Observed: every SB caller discards `ILLEGAL_APP_ID`; consequence "status ignored" and "event rejected": EVS refuses the request at `cfe_evs.c:185–189` before it builds any packet, so there is no packet of that event to lose, and the wording is "event requests rejected", never "events lost" or "packets lost" (§6.4 items 22, 47).
9. **Verdict.**
   - **σ_U.** `fixed-order(σ_U, M_api)` and `fixed-order(σ_U, M_impl)`: the safety statement `¬OK_i` at b = ∞, deciding because `Allowed = none` decides at the occurrence (§1.10). By L-FO, every reached in-scope occurrence has `V` false at its capture point, so it is decided at its call and is `VIOLATING`. The ES-context `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses come from several templates: the version-event loop template has 13 ordinals per boot with the reference version table (one per module version event from `CFE_Config_IterateAll`'s loop, `cfe_config_get.c:180–187`; `cfe_es_task.c:235–241, 265, 466`; [R2g §6]), and the INIT_INF, INITSTATS, Mission-version and BUILD_INF events (EIDs 1, 2, 91, 92; `cfe_es_task.c:442, 449, 259, 287`) are four templates with one ordinal each. Whether the extractor resolves the loop's indirect callback call (`cfe_config_get.c:184`) into the same call strings is [U]. The frontier puts every occurrence of every template before the store at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`, so there is no ordinal-split. The certificate contains task creation, which is an M_impl-relevant operation, so the M_impl certificate is computed on the M_impl skeleton, where T-SPAWN still holds.
   - **σ_U∞ and σ_R1.** These cells hold the same statement by transfer rule 1(a) (same M, b = ∞), with the flag `SAFETY_TRANSFERRED`; it stays deciding, because the badness item's REJECTs lie at the occurrence's decision (`Allowed none`), which decides in every σ (§1.10).
   - **Reach, per cell.**
     - (σ_U, M_impl): `proved` once S1 shows a run with an occurrence (L-CONT (c)).
     - (σ_U, M_api): the cone contains task creation, so L-MLEVEL does not apply; `proved` only if the prefix of every closed-cone actor up to the occurrence passes the M_api-conformance check (L-MAPI-CONE; §4.7 item 6: no `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE`, no lost task, no leaked type lock); otherwise `assumed`.
     - σ_U∞ (both M): in addition, the transitions of the closed cone's actors on the prefix contain no time-based transition (L-REACH-CONE): no soft-barrier or poll timeout, no exhausted delay-paced retry, and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake, which the ES, EVS and SB children's self-lookups in `OS_TaskPrepare` can take (§3.3) and which only the prefix probes show; otherwise `assumed`.
     - σ_R1: `assumed`.
   - **σ_E.** The cell comes from M_impl by transfer rule 2: no decided in-scope occurrence of any real run is `READY` or `TOLERATED` (`¬OK_i`, deciding); a `NOT_EVALUATED` occurrence, whose call returned before the conjunct's LP, is allowed. Reach is `proved` once S1 re-observes a decided and evaluated occurrence (through L-CONT (c), step 9a).
   - **Progress.** Every cell has progress `unknown(not_checked)`, because certificates make no progress claim. If the explorer runs, at M_api σ_U∞ stall states of these instances are expected, with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`), only behind a skipped core raise, chained to the App. A.2 root (`tml-root`); at M_impl also behind a core task lost to F7 (`OS_TaskEntryPoint` calls `OS_TaskExit` after a failed `OS_TaskPrepare`, `osapi-task.c:128–137`), chained to that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence. They are not R2 findings, and the statement is relative to default B [I].
   - **Evidence.** Realization is `certificate`, plus `realized/natural` observations `n of N` with their pool key.
   - **Shape.** WRONG-WAY where SB publishes later, in the witness or its recorded run. ABSENT where SB's own window is hit: SB's `GetAppID` at `cfe_sb_task.c:138` is a once node whose failure branch has then already been taken, so no extension of the witness contains the success store (§1.10). Both are bad.
   - **Trust ledger**: see step 9a. Part (iii) contains the `DECLARED_TRUSTED` entries:
     - ES half: the `CleanUpApp` relational guard;
     - EVS half: A-FRESH, A-OWNER and the `CleanUpApp` guard.

     The rendered label `trusted:<n>` (not a sub-label, §1.10) counts these entries: the part-(iii) entries of kind `DECLARED_TRUSTED`. All of them can be refuted by O9 and O3/O4, and none is proved by them.
9a. **Trust ledger of the R2 certificates** (EXT-20261012 §4.3). There is one certificate ledger per half and per M, for the cells (σ_U, M_api) and (σ_U, M_impl) at b = ∞. The ledgers of the other cells are derived from them (§4.5.1). The contents below are expected [I]; nothing was run.
   - **(i) Re-validated by `kz proofcheck`** ("own" unless marked "via T5").
     - *view*: every step lies on `Sk^cert`.
     - *ONCE*: the following once facts:
       - once(PSP main), by definition;
       - once(ES main), once(EVS main) and once(SB main): each has a single spawn node in the unrolled core loop, on the once startup thread, outside every star-closed region, so the exclusivity condition of once(τ) holds trivially; the three spawn nodes are distinct and run one after another;
       - once(`raise_ES`) and once(`cfe_es_start.c:82`).
     - *T-PO*, by the checker's dominator routine:
       - `m_ES ∈ MB(raise_ES)` (ES half) and `m_EVS ∈ MB(raise_EVS)`, the raise at `cfe_evs_task.c:229` (EVS half);
       - the kept `ExitApp` stores after their half's `m`: ES main's `ExitApp` STOPPED via `cfe_es_task.c:200`, which follows `:134` (step 4's `ExitApp` row); EVS main's `ExitApp` stores at `cfe_evs_task.c:220, :257`, which follow `m_EVS` (A.1 step 5);
       - the startup-thread chain from `o_ES` (resp. `o_EVS`) to `spawn_SB`, with the panic branches cut;
       - SB entry → `x`;
       - for every FRONTIER exclusion, the startup-thread path from `o_ES` (resp. `o_EVS`) to the spawn site of the writer's actor or of its spawner.
     - *T-TERM*: the declarations of `CFE_PSP_Panic` and `abort` for pc-linux, with their citations, on which the panic cuts of T-PO rest. Their truth is a T4 entry.
     - *T-SPAWN*: SB main's spawn-site list equals the closure's list (one site, `:810` in the SB iteration); and, for every actor whose writers the FRONTIER exclusions of A.1 steps 4–5 remove (EVS, SB, TBL, TIME, the script apps and their children), its spawn-site list equals the closure's list, each site lying after `o_ES` (resp. `o_EVS`) on the once startup thread or in an actor spawned after it.
     - *T-OBS* at `o_ES` and `o_EVS`:
       - the falsifier condition at `:803` (via T5: evaluated on the abstract post-state);
       - `Pred_code` (form (b)) re-derived from the serialized `:835` exit trees with the checker's own evaluator (own);
       - R19's binding of the `:835` loop, its stratum and its terminal escape, resolved through T5's rule engine (via T5) [I];
       - the L1 preconditions, checked on the extracted mod set and lock region;
       - `Pred_code ⇒ C_ES` (resp. `C_EVS`), decided by the checker's own procedure (C's syntax tree via T5's parser);
       - `W_o` recomputed from the writer sections, with its exclusions: OWN-NOPATH for `:823` and for the later startup-thread writers; ONCE-PWB for `:82` and, in the EVS half, for `raise_ES`; FRONTIER for writers in later-spawned actors; KEY-SEP as consistency of the `NO_KEY` statuses in the EVS half.
     - *FRONTIER*: no `Sk^cert` path `m_ES →* c` (resp. `m_EVS →* c`), for every capture point `c`.
     - *DERIVES*: the copy chain from the stamp argument to `(CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId)` at `c` (e.g. `cfe_sb_api.c:226–228`).
     - *INIT_FALSE(V)* (via T5): evaluated on the INIT image.
     - *badness*: `Allowed = none`, so every not-ready occurrence is `VIOLATING` at decision.
     - *statuses*: every transition and key status is consistent with the writer sections, and the `DECLARED_TRUSTED` list is complete.
     - *reach* (per cell, step 9): after S1, the run exists, contains an occurrence, and, for the (σ_U, M_api) cell, passes the M_api-conformance check on every closed-cone actor. The absence of time-based transitions on the cone actors is checked in the derived σ_U∞ cells' ledgers, not here (§4.5.1).
   - **(ii) Extracted facts**, from `facts.kz`, `skel.kz`, `anchors.kz`, the closure and, for reach, the run and prefix-probe records, recorded by hash:
     - the 39 SB `SendEventWithAppID` sites and their contexts in `CFE_ES_TaskInit` / `CFE_EVS_TaskInit` ([R2g §6]);
     - the copy-chain AST facts;
     - the INIT image: `CFE_SB_Global` is zero-initialized (`cfe_sb_task.c:40`);
     - the S3a writer sections of `(CFE_SB_Global_t, AppId)` and of `AppState`/`IsUsed`, with their transition classifications and statuses:
       - `x`: W, COMPUTED, MUST_KEY, at `cfe_sb_task.c:138`;
       - neutral: the failure-branch store `cfe_es_api.c:758–760`, the EarlyInit memset `cfe_sb_init.c:50`, and scan WAITING `cfe_es_apps.c:1144`;
     - the A-TYPE listings: no untyped write, and `&CFE_SB_Global.AppId` is taken only at `:138`;
     - the record tag `Type = CORE` (`cfe_es_start.c:795`) and the `PendingAppId` flow (`:814`);
     - the serialized `:835` exit trees, with the loop's mod set and lock region;
     - the generated core-table order (B, `cfe_static_module_list.c`, [R02 §1.3]);
     - the rows based on the call graph: the `RunLoop` raise is absent from the concurrent actors; `cFS-Master` and the console have no writers;
     - the `Sk^cert` graph (unrolled core loop, star-closed message loops, inlined TaskInit contexts), on which every no-path, dominance and once result is computed;
     - the closure's spawn lists for SB main and for every actor named in a FRONTIER exclusion.
   - **(iii) Trusted entries.**
     - *Φ*:
       - `K-ID.send-with-appid`: the readiness precondition; the capture-point and slot-check LPs; the outcome map `cfe_evs.c:185–189`; `Allowed none`; harm `event_request_rejected`;
       - T-DERIVE;
       - the effects of `CFE_ES_GetAppID`;
       - R19: binding of the `:835` loop, stratum S, `obs_fatal`, terminal escape. The predicate itself is re-derived, not trusted;
       - the T-TERM declarations of `CFE_PSP_Panic` and `abort` for pc-linux;
       - the `AppState`/`IsUsed` bindings;
       - Φ_L-1, for the M_impl certificate.
     - *`DECLARED_TRUSTED`*: the `CleanUpApp` relational guard (both halves); A-FRESH and A-OWNER (EVS half), including the `ExitApp` STOPPED exclusion.
     - *Assumptions*: A1, A3, A4, A9, A-SKEL, A-WS, A-TYPE, A-EXTRACT, A-CORE; A2 and A6 (reach, T6); and, for the M_impl certificate, A7-impl, A7-api and A10; A7-api also for the (σ_U, M_api) cell's run-based reach.
     - *Lemmas*, each with its proof status (§6.2); the weakest of them is the status that each certificate cell prints:
       - for the certificate's own cells (σ_U, M_api) and (σ_U, M_impl): L-MB (`unproved`), L-ONCE (`unproved`), L-PWB (`unproved`), L-DERIVE (`depends-on-stated-assumption`), L-FO (`unproved`), L1 (`unproved`) and L-STATUS (`unproved`). L-SCOPE (`unproved`) only if a SCOPE exclusion is used in `W_j`; none is expected [I];
       - **for the reach item of those cells:** L-CONT (c) (`depends-on-stated-assumption`) for (σ_U, M_impl), whose reach S1 proves from a run, and for (σ_U, M_api), whose reach a run proves only through L-MAPI-CONE (`unproved`), which itself starts from the run's LP-granular projection and so also lists L-CONT (c). L-MLEVEL is not used, because the cone is M_impl-relevant;
       - for the derived cells (σ_U∞, σ_R1, σ_E): L-MON (`unproved`), L-CONT (a) (`proved`), (b) and (b′) (`unproved`) as the cell's σ requires, L-CONT (c) for the σ_E cell, which comes from M_impl, L-TRANS (`unproved`), and L-REACH-CONE (`unproved`) for the σ_U∞ cells whose reach a run proves.

       Under these statuses every R2 certificate cell prints "lemma status: unproved".
     - *Tool base*: `libkz-core` (Φ parser, evaluator for the steps marked via T5, container I/O) and the checker's tool hash.
   - **(iv) Refutable, not proved.** Every status is `not run` until S1/S2.
     - A-FRESH and A-OWNER: by O9.
     - The `CleanUpApp` guard: by O3/O4, but only on runs in which `CleanUpApp` runs inside the window. Default boots are not expected to contain such runs [I].
     - A-SKEL: by O1/O2 on every trace, and by O8 from S5.
     - The closure: by O5.
     - Once and MB facts: by O3.
     - Φ effects, LPs, `derives` and `Pred_code`: by O4(a)/(b). Any SB-AppId use that succeeds before `x` refutes the certificate.
     - Writer completeness: by O7 (at model build).
     - L-CONT (c), L-MAPI-CONE and, in the derived σ_U∞ cells, L-REACH-CONE: through their run-side premises, by O1 and O2 (an actor sequence that the certificate-view automaton does not accept), O4 (an outcome that disagrees with the computed state) and O5 (an actor or key outside the closure), on the runs R.
     - *Exercised-path limit* [I]: default boots exercise the success paths only. The following cannot therefore be refuted by default-boot obligations: the `:823` → panic branch; the early returns at `cfe_es_task.c:340/371/381/393`; the timed-out-wait `ExitApp` path (step 5).
     - Reach is the one item that an observation establishes, and only through L-CONT (c) and the other lemmas named above for its cell.
   - *Tests outside the ledger* (not obligations, so not part-(iv) entries, §4.5.1): A-CORE is exercised by the evaluator agreement property test and the golden tests, and writer completeness also by the program mutants of §8.5; their results are reported with the checker and recall metrics (§4.5, §8.5), not as a refutation status.
10. **Refinement accounting** ([X1-M8]): "generic specification K-ID + framework rule R19". R19 binds `MainTaskSyncDelay`'s loop at `:835`, its stratum and its terminal escape; the predicate is extracted from the code and re-derived by `kz proofcheck` from the extractor's serialized `:835` exit trees (trust ledger part (ii)).
11. **S3a exit mutations** (each must be rejected): `m_ES` replaced by a node inside `CFE_ES_TaskInit` before `c` (in `Anc(c)`); the `once(ES main)` fact dropped; the ONCE-PWB fact for `raise_ES` dropped from the EVS certificate; `:823` re-inserted into `W_o(o_ES)` without its OWN-NOPATH proof; the extracted predicate replaced by one that forgets that RESERVED counts as used; the `derives` link dropped; a certificate step moved into a `TRUNC` region.
12. **Expected per-use set** (a check of basis sensitivity and the S1 gate, not new evidence): ES 20 = 17 `SEND_NO_SUBS` + 2 `SUBSCRIPTION_RCVD` + 1 `PIPE_ADDED`; EVS 4 = 1 `SEND_NO_SUBS` + 2 `SUBSCRIPTION_RCVD` + 1 `PIPE_ADDED` (the measured split, `results/R2_gdb_v701.md:47–49`; the earlier "2 + 1 + 1", read in ES's order, contradicted it); the 17 ES `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses come from five recorded call strings, 13 from the version-event callback loop (`CFE_Config_IterateAll` → `CFE_ES_ModSrcVerCallback` → `CFE_ES_GenerateSingleVersionEvent`, `cfe_es_task.c:241, 218`) and one each from four single-ordinal strings: `CFE_ES_INIT_INF_EID` (1, `cfe_es_task.c:442`), `CFE_ES_INITSTATS_INF_EID` (2, `:449`), `CFE_ES_VERSION_INF_EID` (91) for the mission version event (`:259` → `:218`) and `CFE_ES_BUILD_INF_EID` (92, `:287`) ([F] re-read in the pinned tree; the five strings and their counts 13 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 are in the gdb stacks `env/logs/r2_g1_trace.jsonl` to `r2_g3_trace.jsonl`, summarized in `results/R2_gdb_v701.md:101`); total 24 per boot on the ENV basis ([R2g §2]). For a basis without the generated module version table the expected count is not stated here: the 13 version events are the part expected to depend on it [I], and the recorder determines the rest (S1 gate).
13. **Chain.** The 17 `SEND_NO_SUBS` uses (13 from the version-event loop template and one from each of four single-ordinal templates, step 9) are linked to K-ROUTE instances for ES's event packets (MsgId 0x808), transmitted before any subscription ([R2g §2]). Their consumer is TO_LAB's telemetry pipe, which subscribes to `CFE_EVS_LONG_EVENT_MSG_MID` from its table (`to_lab_sub.c:50`, re-read; that this MID is 0x808 is [I]), and whose documented deferral (TO_LAB subscribes after `CFE_ES_WaitForStartupSync`, `to_lab_app.c:64–68`) is `defer_by_design` (§3.15 RC5): the monitored actor is TO_LAB's main task, which does not exist yet at ES's transmits, so each occurrence is PENDING at its transmit and TOLERATED when TO_LAB executes its subscription, or VIOLATING at the same ordinal when TO_LAB terminates or can no longer subscribe first, e.g. when its own App-record window makes `TO_LAB_init`'s `CFE_EVS_Register` fail (`to_lab_app.c:134`, App. A.3); the K-ROUTE instance is therefore schedule-dependent, chained to that window's occurrence [I]. A panic decides none of these occurrences (§1.6). A K-ROUTE instance whose consumer has no documented deferral has `Allowed = unspecified_by_reference` and the label LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm unspecified. The K-ROUTE template for ES's version-event packets is reached once per module version event from `CFE_Config_IterateAll`'s loop (`cfe_config_get.c:180–187`; `cfe_es_task.c:235–241, 265, 466`), so it has several ordinals per boot and is classified per ordinal. For a K-ROUTE instance whose consumer has **no** documented deferral, early-without-route and later-with-route occurrences give `ordinal-split`, not schedule-dependent, unless a same-ordinal pair is shown; for TO_LAB's pipe, whose deferral is documented, the early occurrences are PENDING and then TOLERATED, or VIOLATING at the same ordinal, as above.
13a. **The chain in three judgements.** Framework level for the 17 K-ROUTE occurrences of step 13 (ES's event packets; MsgId 0x808 [I], [R2g §2]): `fw_doc` = {`documented-not-error`} (cSB4305.5's rationale); `fw_conf` = `deviates` in the cells and runs of this window, because the `SEND_NO_SUBS` events that cSB4305.5's shall-text requires are rejected by EVS with `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` (17 in ES's context [M, R2g §2]; one more `SEND_NO_SUBS` use occurs in EVS's context, 18 in all, and its MsgId attribution is open [U]). The window's `NoSubscribersCounter` increments are cleared later when SB initializes its HK packet (`cfe_sb_task.c:164–166`; `CFE_MSG_Init` clears the message, `cfe_msg_init.c:45`) [I]; that is recorded as an observed consequence, not as a deviation, since the shall-text requires only the increment. Caller level: as in step 13 (TO_LAB's `defer_by_design`, or LOSS-UNDER-ORDER where no consumer reference speaks). Observed: "message lost" for ES's event packets (the packets were built and sent; SB's documented no-subscriber result) and "event rejected" for the `SEND_NO_SUBS` events of cSB4305.5 (EVS refuses those requests before it builds any packet, so they are not lost packets); in this window the loss of ES's packets leaves no trace that survives it, because the events that would record it are rejected and the counter increments are cleared [I].
14. **Not claimed**:
   - that R2 is a race;
   - any count as a Kairos result before S1;
   - determinism from repeated runs;
   - that the certificate uses no framework rule;
   - that the uses are reached in every run (reach is a separate axis);
   - any progress statement;
   - that the checker verified the extracted facts or the skeleton.

15. **Expected record on a corrected-order control** (V2; pre-registered before any run; [I]). On a built basis in which SB's publication precedes a half's uses (P-R2CTRL options (c1)–(d); the first validation uses (c2), (c1) and the no-change control (b), and (c3), (d), (e), (f) only if they are added later): FIXED_ORDER item 4 fails for that half, because the success store of SB's `CFE_ES_GetAppID` call (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`) now precedes the uses, so **fixed-order is not issued**. PROTECTED (b) fails as well, because `CFE_ES_GetAppID`'s failure branch stores `UNDEFINED` and SB ignores the return value (which clause fails is settled at S3a), so the verdict is **not protected**. That call is the App. A.2 K-SELF.es instance, which is open, so the half is **open, chained to the App. A.2 root**, with class `unknown` until S5/S7 and schedule-dependent(σ_U) with shape ABSENT expected after them. Natural runs give "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown". On a half whose order is unchanged, fixed-order is expected again; on a no-change control (option (b)), fixed-order is kept. Line numbers are v7.0.1's (failure store `cfe_es_api.c:758–760`) and are rebound per basis by the anchor audit (the store is at `cfe_es_api.c:741` at `v7.0.0`). For option (e), the lazy-resolution change, the expectation is written and frozen only after the code exists, because the halves it moves and whether the App. A.2 chain exists depend on the code written. For option (f), the model-level mutant, only the static part applies (no run, O4 or reach is exercised). Every such result is configuration or revision sensitivity, never schedule evidence and never evidence that the default configuration races. That fixed-order is withdrawn where the order evidence differs does not by itself establish overall safety.

### A.2 The core-task App-record window and the unpublished SB/TBL identity (INFERENCE until run; [J1 §1.1], [LED §3.4])

1. **Instances.** K-SELF.es at every context-resolving call made by a core task (and by children it creates in TaskInit) before `cfe_es_start.c:818`: EVS `GetAppID` (`cfe_evs_task.c:273`); ES's `CFE_EVS_Register` in `CFE_ES_TaskInit`; SB `GetAppID` (`cfe_sb_task.c:138`); TBL (`cfe_tbl_task.c:196`); TIME's, incl. its `CreateChildTask` calls (`cfe_time_task.c:160, 173`); `ES_BG_TASK`'s creation (`cfe_es_backgroundtask.c:214`); every `WaitForSystemState` raise.
2. **Static.** `W` = `CFE_ES_AppRecordSetUsed(ρ, PendingAppId)` at `:818` (startup thread). The child's MB contains the spawn and, through T-OBS at `GetTaskFunction`, the task-record fill (`cfe_es_apps.c:694–703`), but not `:818`. No certificate either way → open, routed **O-ordered** (both LPs under the ES lock).
3. **Prediction.** From a default boot, reverse the startup thread's critical section at `:816–818` and the child's `GetAppID` critical section; the child's poll keeps its success outcome because the task record was filled earlier. A witness is expected → `schedule-dependent(σ_U, M_api and M_impl)`. Its label is per instance (§4.9 item 7a): where the occurrence is decided at the use (the raises; SB's and TBL's `GetAppID`, whose status is ignored; and, under the strict alternative of Q15, EVS's `GetAppID` and TIME's `CreateChildTask` calls, whose `returned_and_tested` entries cite no continuation and so make the occurrence NOT_READY_UNJUDGED at the use), A-COMM decides between `predicted` and `predicted-modelled`; where an entry's REJECT follows the decision (a `returned_and_tested` entry that cites its continuation, or a `retry_until_ready` entry), a predicted reversal is predicted not-ready evidence (`PENDING`), and it becomes a witness only by horizon completion, labelled `predicted-extrapolated`, where the completed region passed O2; the other instances follow the same test. Under σ_U∞ no timeout is involved → still schedule-dependent (no refinement). The EVS caller's `GetAppID` (`cfe_evs_task.c:273`) is tested, and its not-ready branch returns `Status` from `CFE_EVS_TaskInit` (`:274–278`), so every post-dominator of the test is the function's return, which returns the status: the extracted `cont_candidate` is none (§1.6), and `CFE_EVS_TaskMain` then calls `CFE_ES_ExitApp(CORE_APP_INIT_ERROR)` (`:215–220`). Those are code facts, recorded as the occurrence's consequence. No reference cites a continuation for EVS's handling, so the occurrence is NOT_READY_UNJUDGED (QT-4, Q15 strict alternatives), neither TOLERATED nor VIOLATING. Progress under σ_U∞ [I], with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay` (`cfe_es_start.c:860–918`): when a raise is skipped, the startup thread is dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` and the core tasks already created are dead in their `CORE_READY` polls (`cfe_es_task.c:134`); under σ_U the startup thread panics; that panic is the observed consequence of this instance's not-ready occurrence (consequence `panic`; the skipped raise's harm is `unspecified_by_reference`, §3.3), not a progress result, and it decides no horizon (§1.6). The instances behind it (the later core tasks, the script apps, the R2 and A.7 uses, TO_LAB's deferred K-ROUTE occurrences) have hang states there; each carries timeout-masked-liveness on its own cell and is attached by a progress chain to this instance, which carries `tml-root` and is what the report lists. This instance's own σ_U∞ progress can be `no-stall-within(b)`: its occurrence is decided at the call and has no horizon. At M_impl the same stall states can also arise from a core task lost to F7 (`osapi-task.c:128–137`), with that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence as the chain root. Under σ_R1[`P_sched^posix`] the startup thread outranks the tasks, so [I] the window cannot open on one CPU.
4. **Consequences** (every branch, §3.3): EVS (a1) `service_task_absent`, (a2) `panic` after 30 s, (b) `process_abort` after `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` = 10 s (the abort precedes the 30 s panic); ES/SB/TBL/TIME raise skipped → `panic`; SB/TBL identity UNDEFINED for the run → every later SB/TBL `SendEventWithAppID` use bad with shape ABSENT, chained to this root (the root is reported, not each dependent use; the gdb runs saw 199 / 205 / 205 SB-caller uses per boot after the publication, [R2g §2]); TIME's children → `task_never_runs` if `CreateChildTask` fails.
5. **Confirmation (D16, derived).** Hold the startup thread after `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` returns (`cfe_es_start.c:810–814`) and before `CFE_ES_LockSharedData` at `:816`; its program-lock set is empty there. Release when the child's `GetAppID` returns.
   - Watchdog: the smallest reference timeout the hold can make expire — the already-created core tasks wait in `WaitForSystemState(CORE_READY, CFE_PLATFORM_CORE_MAX_STARTUP_MSEC)` = 30000 ms (`default_cfe_core_private_internal_cfg.h:68`), and where the predicted continuation includes a restart request, `CFE_PSP_RESTART_DELAY` = 10000 ms (`pc-linux/inc/cfe_psp_config.h:57`) → 10000 ms for EVS's window.
   - Oracle: the `GetAppID` outcome; for SB, `ILLEGAL_APP_ID` on every later SB event; for EVS, which branch of the chain occurred (abort, panic or missing EVS task).
   - The run may end abnormally, so a **normalization boot** follows (§4.13). Labels: `realized`, `held-F0`, `derived`, with the perturbation ratio over the 50 ms poll separation.
6. **σ_E**: no natural bad witness is expected; the record reads "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs (pool key); σ_E class unknown". No claim of timing protection.

### A.3 The script-app App-record window (correcting [B §7.2])

1. **Instances.** K-SELF.es at each script app's context-resolving calls (`CFE_EVS_Register` → caller context → `GetAppID`; `RunLoop`; `WaitForSystemState`; `CreateChildTask`, e.g. FM at `fm_child.c:86`); K-REG.es at peers' `GetAppIDByName`, whose oracle classifies the sentinel success as not-ready ([J1 §1 check 4]).
2. **Trace routing.** The publication (`cfe_es_apps.c:861–869`) and the use's LP are both under the ES lock: the **O-ordered** route to prediction, not "unordered → SHB" ([J1 §1 check 3]; [J3 §2.2]).
3. **Expected**: for K-SELF.es, `schedule-dependent(σ_U, σ_U∞)` at ordinal 1 (`RunLoop` runs on every iteration and so has several ordinals, but only ordinal 1 can fall in the window); for K-REG.es at a peer that calls `GetAppIDByName` periodically (HS's application monitor, `hs_monitors.c:69`, where its table is active; CS's application checksum, `cs_compute.c:307`, where enabled [I]), the ordinal that falls into the target's window varies by execution, so `schedule-dependent` needs a same-ordinal pair at some k and is otherwise `ordinal-split`; evidence per instance (§4.9 item 7a): `predicted` or `predicted-modelled` where the occurrence is decided at the use (`RunLoop`, the raise, and every entry whose caller-level Allowed is `none` or `unspecified_by_reference`), and `predicted-extrapolated`, by horizon completion, only where an entry with a `caller` record has a REJECT that follows the decision (none is expected in this window: the `returned_and_tested` entries at `CreateChildTask` calls cite no continuation and decide at the use); harms per branch: registration failure → app error exit; `RunLoop` false with the window open → `app_zombie` plus, under σ_U, two soft-barrier timeouts; with the window closed → `app_error_exit`. Under σ_U∞ the soft barriers do not time out: on the zombie branch the startup thread's barrier poll (with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, `cfe_es_start.c:860–918`) and every app in `WaitForStartupSync` are dead (TO_LAB's deferred K-ROUTE occurrences then have a horizon stall, harm `hang_or_deadlock`), which gives hang states for the instances behind them; each carries timeout-masked-liveness and is attached by a progress chain to this instance, which carries `tml-root` [I]. σ_E: "no σ_E bad witness in n of N runs; σ_E class unknown".
4. **Confirmation** ([B §7.2], corrected placement). Hold the startup thread in `CFE_ES_AppCreate` after `CFE_ES_StartAppTask` returns (`:848–854`) and before the lock at `:861`; its lockset is empty there. Release when the child's first context-resolving call returns.
   - Watchdog: the smallest timeout among the waits of already-started actors that the held startup thread can make expire (their `WaitForSystemState`/`WaitForStartupSync` arguments, constant-evaluated; the script barrier `CFE_PLATFORM_ES_STARTUP_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT_MSEC` once the script phase has begun).
   - Oracle: `CFE_ES_ERR_RESOURCEID_NOT_VALID` (`cfe_es_api.c:757–761`); `RunLoop` returning false; the sentinel success for peers. Labelled derived; normalization boot if the run ends abnormally.

### A.4 C1 in a derived configuration, and the OSAL lock-leak path under M_impl

1. **Default B.** CF's lookup sits behind a non-empty `sem_name` test (`cf_cfdp.c:1270`); both default entries are empty (`cf_def_config.c:60, 78`) → `inactive(B)`; the proof is the folded path condition in `Sk^cert`.
2. **B′** (public-code-derived, [LED §1a]; decided under D22 [D]: the public BP basis, candidate pair CF `281a941` → `833fdbb`): a non-empty name and a provider app that creates the counting semaphore in its init. From the references, as public values of nasa/bp `b051eb0` and not as values of the original reporter's environment: the names `BP0_tsem`/`BP1_tsem` (`.github/buildconfig/{tx,rx}/tables/cf_def_config.c` L40, L54; `fsw/custom/bp_semcfg.c` L43–L49), the startup order CF then BP and the configured priorities CF 65 and BP 55 (`.github/buildconfig/cfe_es_startup.scr` L6–L7), and the provider protocol, create in init (`OS_CountSemCreate` from `BP_FlowInit`, initial count 10). The CF pair is `281a941` (the parent of the fix) and `833fdbb` (the fix); the public BP table's layout matches `281a941`, not the reporter's `2a024d8`, so the reporter's revision is not mixed with the public table. Unconfirmed [U]: the full version combination of cFS, cFE, OSAL and PSP and the content of the mutable references then (nasa/bp's CI merges a mutable PSP branch, installs TinyCBOR into `/` and needs bplib); whether the reporter's provider is the public BP configuration; the real-time priority application, the CPU count and the affinity; the CF revision that the BP CI fetched (`833fdbb` by inference); the retry constants, which are read from the selected sources and fixed for no other revision (25 and 100 ms at `833fdbb`, `cf_platform_cfg.h` L329–L349; `281a941` has a single lookup, `cf_cfdp.c` L993–L1001); and the configuration and code differences of any move to other versions. **Order of work:** the version compatibility of the public integration is checked first. If it cannot be built as it stands, the move to other versions (for example CF `15a871e` at v7.0.1 with its 25 × 100 ms retries, `cf_internal_cfg.h` L172–L173, L184–L185, and CF's ENV startup entry at priority 70) is a derived basis, and every difference from the public values is recorded as a `deviation`. Any provider delay is event-based only; the hang bound of step 5's follow-up experiment stays `unspecified_by_reference`. Neither app waits on a phase → no MB relation → open. Observation shows either success (outcome-implied reads-from from the provider's publication, an O-edge through the countsem table lock) or `NAME_NOT_FOUND` followed by retries.
3. **M_api.**
   - **The entry** (QT-4 strict alternative): CF's `retry_until_ready` entry decides statuses only through a `caller` record, CF's admission of the start-up race (`cf_cfdp.c:1272–1278`, kind `code_admission`, side `caller`; §7.1 source 8; RC1, §3.15) [I]. The recognized loop alone (`code_pattern`, side `rule`) would leave the entry `unspecified_by_reference` and every not-ready attempt NOT_READY_UNJUDGED. The classes below are those with the `caller` record.
   - **σ_U**: the retry loop is unrolled exactly to the effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES`; exhaustion is reachable only after the last attempt. The explorer finds an execution in which every attempt precedes the creation (delays are yields) → `CF_INIT_SEM_ERR_EID` (`cf_cfdp.c` ~L1295) and engine initialization fails (consequence per [A §A.2]) → `schedule-dependent(σ_U)`. Prediction may extend an observed retry to the effective bound; such a witness is `predicted-extrapolated`.
   - **σ_U∞**:
     - **The loop under σ_U∞ at M_api.** The loop is delay-paced. The failing outcome of its last attempt is a time-based transition, so σ_U∞ removes it. At M_api the attempt is one transition (the lookup), so under σ_U∞ the last attempt is enabled exactly when the lookup would succeed.
     - **While the provider can still run.** No finite execution of σ_U∞ is bad: a not-ready attempt's monitor (`retry_until_ready`) can only ACCEPT, when a later attempt is READY, or stay OPEN, because its REJECT event, the exhaustion, is time-based. So `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds, which `exh` decides within b. Since `class(σ_U) = schedule-dependent` and σ_U∞ reaches the use, the refinement is **timeout-dependent** (§1.8, L-REF) [I]. Without the explorer no refinement is established.
     - **At the pre-fix revision** (CF `281a941`, the fix base of the chosen pair, a single lookup: `cf_cfdp.c` L993–L1001, `cases/C1.md`; the structure below is read at the reporter's `2a024d8`, which is not mixed with the public BP table, and is re-read at `281a941` by the anchor audit [U]), the handling is a status test with no cited continuation. The test (`cf_cfdp.c:1016` @2a024d8) lies in the channel loop, which is unrolled exactly; its not-ready branch raises `CF_EID_ERR_INIT_SEM` and jumps to `err_out: return ret;` (`:1018–1021, 1052–1053` @2a024d8), which the success branch also reaches, and `ret` is returned. The extracted `cont_candidate` is therefore none (§1.6), the error is returned, and CF then exits with `APP_ERROR` ([LED §1a]); those are code facts, recorded as the occurrence's consequence. No reference cited here says that this handling is accepted or names a continuation for it [I], so under the strict alternatives (Q15 and QT-4, decided 2026-10-15) the entry is `unspecified_by_reference` and the occurrence is NOT_READY_UNJUDGED without any time-based transition. A `caller` record found later for the pre-fix handling would change the label, not the class: a NOT_READY_UNJUDGED occurrence is bad (§1.6).
