It stays schedule-dependent under σ_U∞: the fix of C1 ([LED §1b]) turned an ordering-logic readiness violation into a dependence on a timing margin.
     - **If the provider never runs**, no cFS basis is used (none has a reference); litmus L-PATIENT variant 2 (App. A.8) shows the result: the certificate's REJECT, the exhaustion, lies after the decision, so at b = ∞ the class is `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown in every σ; within b, `exh` gives fixed-order under σ_U (the exhausting execution is a bad witness) and `no-final-status` under σ_U∞; under σ_U∞ the retrying actor is dead at the call node of its last attempt (`stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})`, a hang), and σ_U escapes only by exhaustion, hence **timeout-masked-liveness** [I].
     - **At M_impl** the lookup is several transitions (§1.4), so the time-based transition is the branch at `cf_cfdp.c:1285` taken with `NAME_NOT_FOUND` in the last iteration; after a failing last lookup CF has released the type lock and is **stranded** (§1.8): on that path the deadness tests of CF and of the actors it could enable (the startup thread's script barrier) are undecided, `unknown(unmodelled)`. That does not make the cell's progress unknown, because a stall state found elsewhere in the cell is a witness (§4.10 item 7; next bullet). A provider spawn lost to F7 is treated in step 4.
     - **Behind the core phase** (both M) [I]. CF is a script app, spawned after the core phase, so, as for the uses of App. A.1 and A.7, a σ_U∞ pre-use stall state of its lookup is reachable behind a core raise skipped inside the App-record window (App. A.2 step 3; with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, `cfe_es_start.c:860–918`; with L1 switched off the result is `unknown(unmodelled)`) and, at M_impl, also behind a core task lost to F7 (`osapi-task.c:128–137`): the startup thread is dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` with CF's spawn ahead. The (σ_U∞, M) cell's progress is therefore `stall-witness({pre-use})`, harm `task_never_runs`, chained to the App. A.2 root (or to that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence). Under σ_U the startup thread escapes by the barrier timeout (a panic), so CF's σ_U cell carries timeout-masked-liveness with the flag `CHAINED` (§1.8), at M_api and at M_impl.
     - **A single early lookup** is `allowed-not-ready` (`retry_until_ready`, from `exh`).
4. **M_impl.** The loop is unrolled, not summarized. `OS_CountSemGetIdByName` uses a **by-name** token, so in the RESERVED window with the name visible it does not wait: it returns `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` at once (`osapi-idmap.c:401–404`), which ends CF's loop (`cf_cfdp.c:1285–1288`, [R01 §1.8]); by K-LOCKBAL the countsem table lock stays held (vs the documented invariant `:384–385`); the provider then blocks in `FinalizeNew`. CF's not-ready outcome `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` is outside its `retry_until_ready` codes, so its monitor gives REJECT at decision: `VIOLATING` (safety). No time-based transition is needed, so `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` holds at M_impl, and there is no timeout-dependent refinement there. CF has returned from the lookup that took the countsem type lock and then either exits through its init-failure path (its `CFE_ES_ExitApp` stores STOPPED and loops in `OS_TaskDelay`, `cfe_es_api.c:399, 410–413`) or, with a re-bind command in B.Input, re-enters an API that takes the same lock (through `CF_CmdEnableEngine`, another calling context and therefore a separate instance); either way the lock is leaked (§1.8). CF's own instance has no stall state from the leak: CF holds the lock and does not wait on it. The deadness test finds, as dependents chained to CF's VIOLATING attempt with one wait-for root (the countsem type lock): the provider dead in `FinalizeNew`; every later operation that takes the countsem type lock (create, delete, by-name lookup, GetInfo, and object iteration, `OS_ForEachObject*`, which takes every type's lock in GLOBAL mode, `osapi-idmap.c:1246–1256, 1360–1367`; not `OS_CountSemGive`, `Take` or `TimedWait`, which use `OS_LOCK_MODE_NONE`, `osapi-countsem.c:147, 168, 189`) blocked before its LP, e.g. SCH_LAB's `OS_CountSemCreate` (`sch_lab_app.c:167`) with its lookup at `:176` ahead; and `ES_BG_TASK`, dead in `CFE_ES_CleanupTaskResources` when it cleans up CF after the STOPPED store (`expire-stopped`; `CFE_ES_CleanUpApp`, `cfe_es_apps.c:1249`, calls it outside the ES lock at `:1508, 1565`, and it calls `OS_ForEachObject` at `:1759`), after which no app is cleaned up, restarted or reloaded. All are confirmable `leaked-lock` stall witnesses, harm `hang_or_deadlock` (litmus LP-LEAK, LP-CASE-C1). On the `NAME_NOT_FOUND` path (e.g. a provider spawn lost to F7), CF's last attempt is not atomic at M_impl, so under σ_U∞ CF is stranded after it: the deadness tests on that path and in CF's closure are `unknown(unmodelled)`, and that path establishes no timeout-masked-liveness. The cell's progress is nevertheless `stall-witness({pre-use})` behind a skipped core raise (and behind a core task lost to F7), chained to the App. A.2 root, with timeout-masked-liveness (`CHAINED`) on CF's σ_U cell at M_impl (step 3, "Behind the core phase"). The result is an **implementation-defect candidate** (F1). The M_api result for this instance is not transferred to σ_E (the cone is M_impl-relevant and the levels disagree): `portability-hazard` is not the label here, since the readiness violation exists only under M_impl.
5. **Confirmation plans** (P-C1HOLD, decided 2026-10-15). The normal path records the creation and lookup events directly. For the failure path of (3): hold the provider **immediately before its create call** (lockset empty) and release it on an **event**, which is CF's retry exhaustion or its final initialization result; the provider is created after observation starts, so the holder auto-attaches at clone on the startup thread (§4.13), and that stop is recorded. The retry count and the delay are read from the chosen CF version and are never fixed to 25 or 100 ms for every version. Their product is not taken as a safe wall-clock waiting limit: the observation effect and the real end time are checked in a pilot, and the wall-clock watchdog and the stop conditions are recorded before the main experiment. A failure made by the hold is labelled apart from a natural run, and a timeout observation alone never decides a permanent deadlock. For (4) there is **no part of C1's validation**: the RESERVED-window experiment (hold the provider at `OS_CountSemCreate_Impl` entry, inside the RESERVED window, name visible, type lock **not** held, [R01 §1.5 step f], until CF's lookup returns, then release; oracle: a wait-for fact, the provider blocked on the countsem table lock by futex/wchan while CF has already returned) is a **follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate item**. It is not a start condition of C1's validation and is not added to the number of the five cases' results. It would confirm CF's VIOLATING occurrence and the leaked lock (K-LOCKBAL), which are safety results; it is a realized progress `stall-witness` only where it also shows permanence, i.e. CF has exited or is itself blocked acquiring the same lock (§1.10 σ_E cells, §4.13). The harness kill of the deadlocked run is followed by a normalization boot. Labelled derived.
6. **Outcome partition** (per key; ordinal (CF, k) = attempt k; V3). A1 immediate success: (CF, 1) READY. A2 success after retry: (CF, 1 … k−1) TOLERATED, (CF, k) READY, k ≤ R, where R is the effective `CF_STARTUP_SEM_MAX_RETRIES` of the chosen revision, read from its selected sources and fixed for no other (25 at CF `833fdbb`, `cf_platform_cfg.h` L329–L349, and at CF `15a871e`, `cf_internal_cfg.h` L172–L173; D22); at a single-attempt revision (CF `281a941`, the pre-fix side of the chosen pair, or the reporter's `2a024d8`) A2 does not exist. A3 final failure with a late provider: (CF, 1 … R) VIOLATING, all rejected by the exhaustion (`CF_INIT_SEM_ERR_EID`, `cf_cfdp.c:1295` at the revision where the event is so named; the line and the name are rebound per basis by the anchor audit); at CF `281a941`, (CF, 1) VIOLATING by the error return (step 3). A4 final failure with no provider for the name: **no referenced basis exists** (the referenced provider creates both configured names, and a cFS basis without the provider has no reference); A4 is shown by litmus L-PATIENT variant 2 and LP-CASE-C1 (App. A.8) with the provider never running, or recorded as "not realized: no referenced configuration"; only on a provider-less basis that the PI sets as a recorded deviation is it realized, told apart statically by provider `none` for that key. A5, at M_impl only (a follow-up OSAL-defect-candidate item, P-C1HOLD: not an arm of C1's validation and not counted among the five cases), the RESERVED hit: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` ends the loop at once (`cf_cfdp.c:1285–1288`), VIOLATING at the decision, with the lock-leak path of step 4. READY and TOLERATED at one ordinal are both good; schedule dependence needs a good and a bad witness at the same ordinal in one build, configuration and semantics. What each stage decides: S3a, the census (open), and A4 only on a provider-less basis that realizes it; S1 + S2, the natural statuses per ordinal (`n of N` per arm), no class; S5, the reversal evidence of the S5 gate; S6, A3 under a hold (`held-F0`, `timeout-exercised`, `σ_E(held)`) and A5 only in the follow-up item (P-C1HOLD); S7, the classes and refinements of step 3 and the stalls of step 4.

### A.5 Startup-burst capacity: `SBNSubPipe` (K-MODE + K-CAP + K-CAP.msglim, LOSS-UNDER-ORDER)

1. **Instances** (re-stated per [X2-05], [X2-06]).
   - **K-MODE**: SBN transmits `ENABLE_SUB_REPORTING` and `SEND_PREV_SUBS` to SB's command pipe (`sbn_subs.c:43–60`); SB's main task flips `sb.mode.subreport` (LP `cfe_sb_task.c:1226`, reached from `:296`) and runs the replay `CFE_SB_SendPrevSubsCmd` (`:1169`) as a transmit loop in **SB's actor**. Subscribers that subscribe after the flip read the flag after the SB unlock without a lock (`cfe_sb_task.c:659`, `assumed`) and transmit a `ONESUB` report from **their own task** (`cfe_sb_api.c:1192–1195`). The chain runs through R7 on SB's command pipe, with K-CAP and K-CAP.msglim instances for that pipe.
   - **K-CAP** at each put into `SBNSubPipe` (effective capacity 10, [BM §3.1]); the puts come from SB's replay loop and from subscriber tasks (TO_LAB's 37 global subscriptions from its table, [R03 §2.3, §2.15]).
   - **K-CAP.msglim** for SBN's subscriptions to the report MsgIds, with the limit constant-evaluated at SBN's subscribe sites.
2. **Tier 1.** The maximum-weight-closure backlog bound over the certificate-view MB order of puts and gets exceeds 10 (TO_LAB alone can add up to 37 reports, and the replay loop is star-closed), so there is no protected certificate → open. The attribution of the burst between SB's replay and the subscribers' reports was an inference in [R03 §5.2] ([J2 §7]); the census's instance identification checks it.
3. **σ_E.** Overflow was observed in 5 of 6 M1 runs, and the sixth (A3) showed a different interleaving with `TO_LAB_TLM_PIPE` overflowing instead ([BM §3.3]). These are prior observations by the M1 harness ([BM]; D12 concerns the retired recorder, not this record); Kairos's recorder re-observes them in S1/S2.
4. **Verdict.** The puts into `SBNSubPipe` from one template have occurrence ordinals (e.g. TO_LAB's reports, from one looped subscribe template). `schedule-dependent(σ_E)` needs a same-ordinal pair: some put ordinal k delivered in one natural run and overflowing in another, e.g. a run like A3 against one of the five overflowing runs [I]. One overflowing run, in which early puts are delivered and later ones overflow, gives only `ordinal-split(σ_E)`. Either is transferred up to σ_U as witnesses; label LOSS-UNDER-ORDER, harm `unspecified_by_reference` (caller level); framework level: cSB4305.3, `documented-result`; observed: the per-run `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` values ([M] 33, 37, 4, 49, 45, 41 in six M1 runs, [BM §0]). Oracles: per-put outcomes, `PipeOverflowErrorCounter` and `MsgLimitErrorCounter`; `PeakQueueDepth` only as SB-accounted depth. Part of RQ5.

### A.6 HS system monitor: provider `env-absent` (correcting [A], [R03 §3 row 20])

`HS_SysMonInit` finds the `linux_sysmon` device: the module is in the PSP's fixed list (`psp_module_list.cmake:9`), so `FindByName` succeeds; a failure there would be handled (`hs_sysmon.c:50–54`). The `SET_RUNNING` command then fails, and HS returns `CFE_STATUS_EXTERNAL_RESOURCE_FAIL` (`:65–72`), because `/proc/schedstat` is absent on this kernel ([R01 §2.6]; [R07 §1]).

Φ declares the device's RUNNING transition guarded by `env(/proc/schedstat present)`, and B.Env records it absent. Outcome mining finds the not-ready code, and O4 agrees → provider `env-absent`. With no writer enabled in B, every reaching execution has the not-ready outcome: class `fixed-order` with outcome label `not-ready-unjudged` (Allowed is `unspecified_by_reference`), reach proved by the observed failure in (σ_U, M_impl); at M_api only by the M_api-conformance check of the prefix of every closed-cone actor (L-MAPI-CONE, §6.2), because the closed cone contains HS's spawn chain with task creation (an M_impl-relevant operation), so L-MLEVEL does not apply, as for R2 and LP-DL; under σ_U∞ only if the cone actors' prefix shows no barrier timeout and, at M_impl, no OSAL-internal timed wake (§4.7 item 6), else `assumed`. This is not an order problem. It is reported separately, with the measured consequences: HS init stops at `HS_SysMonInit`; its startup sync is skipped; CPU utilisation is reported as `0xFFFFFFFF` ([BM §0]).

### A.7 Calibration: timebase lookups are protected outside teardown (safety statements)

`cFS-Master` is created and started by the PSP's module init on the main thread before `CFE_ES_Main` (§3.5). `x*` (its publication LP, name constant `"cFS-Master"`) is a `W^must` entry with `MUST_KEY`, COMPUTED, and is in MB of every cFE task entry by T-PO on the PSP main thread and T-SPAWN.

Disablers of `osal.obj<timebase>("cFS-Master")` (fixes [X1-M6], [X2-04]):
- `OS_DeleteAllObjects` (`cfe_psp_start.c:507`): `SHUTDOWN_REQ ∈ MB` by T-OBS on the `ShutdownReq` loop → **SCOPE-excluded**;
- any `OS_TimeBaseDelete` site found by the census: none is expected in the default B [I]; any found must be PWB-ordered, or the certificate is withheld.

TIME's and SCH_LAB's lookups (`cfe_time_task.c:311`, `sch_lab_app.c:176`) are therefore `protected(σ_U, M_api)` and, since the record is already LIVE when any lookup runs (`x*` is the final publication), `protected(σ_U, M_impl)` [I], as safety statements (`¬NR_i`: every decided in-scope lookup occurrence is READY or NOT_EVALUATED, so every reached one is READY). The σ_U∞ and σ_R1 cells follow by transfer rule 1(a), and the σ_E cell follows from M_impl by transfer rule 2. Progress is not claimed: it is `unknown(not_checked)` in every certified cell. With the explorer at M_api, a σ_U∞ stall state for these lookups is reachable only behind a skipped core raise, which leaves the startup thread dead at `cfe_es_start.c:835` with TIME's and SCH_LAB's spawns ahead [I] (with L1 applied to `CFE_ES_MainTaskSyncDelay`, `cfe_es_start.c:860–918`, §6.2; with L1 switched off the result is `unknown(unmodelled)`); it is chained to the App. A.2 root, which carries `tml-root`. At M_impl it is also reachable behind a core task lost to F7 (its `OS_TaskPrepare` fails and it calls `OS_TaskExit`, `osapi-task.c:128–137`), chained to that task's K-SPAWN.osal occurrence. These statements are relative to default B: in C1's B′ at M_impl, SCH_LAB's lookup also lies behind a leaked countsem type lock (its `OS_CountSemCreate` at `sch_lab_app.c:167`), a `leaked-lock` stall chained to CF's VIOLATING attempt (A.4 step 4). A lost timebase handshake does not affect these lookups: they run in TaskInit and AppInit before `OS_TimerAdd` and wait for no callback (`cfe_time_task.c:311`, `sch_lab_app.c:176`, re-read); it affects only the 1 Hz pipeline and SCH_LAB's loop (§3.13). All are stated **outside teardown**. SCH_LAB's lookup may run after `CFE_ES_Main` returns past a soft timeout (`cfe_es_start.c:225–228`); it is still in scope and still protected, because teardown needs a shutdown request. Command-triggered resets need no special treatment: their teardown disablers are SCOPE-excluded too, and with an empty B.Input the reset handlers are inactive by message provenance anyway. The timebase thread's own handshake (K-SPAWN.osal, §3.3) is a separate instance. This is the check that the tool does not invent races where the code orders.

### A.8 Litmus programs added by the reviews (gcc-built C99 on the real OSAL; expected classes per σ and M)

| Litmus | Program shape | Expected result | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **L-H2** (fixes [X1-C1]) | a looper whose handler for message M1 sets a flag; its handler for M2 creates resource R only if the flag is set; another actor sends M1 then M2 (in-B transmits); a consumer looks R up | provider `present`, not `none`; not `inactive`; no certificate in either direction from `Sk^cert`; explorer: with h = 1 the writer is not reached, `Q_bad` gives a bad witness, and `¬OK_i` is withheld by safety-side censoring (the looper's region beyond the cut contains the writer), so the class within b = 1 is `unknown(bound)` with the bad witness recorded; with h = 2 schedule-dependent at the consumer's single ordinal | the certificate view star-closes the loop; `kz proofcheck` rejects a fabricated certificate that names a node beyond h |
| **L-ONCE** (X1-M2) | a spawn site inside a loop creates two instances of one entry; a frontier argument would put a use in instance 1 before a writer in instance 2 | once(τ) fails; frontier and once-PWB forbidden; schedule-dependent | the once analysis and its proofcheck re-validation |
| **L-OWN** (X2-01) | an observer with a falsifier and a writer on a failure branch that ends in `abort()` | the certificate passes with OWN-NOPATH; a variant in which the failure branch rejoins the observation must lose the certificate | OWN-NOPATH and its reachability re-check |
| **L-SCOPE** (X1-M6) | a disabler that runs only after a shutdown-request store; a use before and a use after the store | the first use protected outside teardown; the second a K-TEARDOWN instance | SCOPE exclusion |
| **L-PROV** (X1-M7) | a command-only handler that writes a resource; B.Input empty vs one command | inactive vs active | message provenance and its fixpoint |
| **L-CAPTURE** (X1-M1) | a value loaded from a shared cell, then the publisher writes, then the API checks the copy | the outcome follows the capture point; O4 agrees; a predictor that targeted the check LP would accept a false good witness | per-conjunct LPs and `derives` |
| **L-PATIENT** (X1-M5; restated by EXT-20261012 §4.2) | a delay-paced retry with a provider; variant with a provider that never runs (a unit-test condition) | **Variant 1** at M_api: σ_U is schedule-dependent at ordinal 1 (attempt 1 READY in one execution, VIOLATING by the exhaustion's REJECT in another); σ_U∞ is protected with `allowed-not-ready` (`exh`) and reached, because the last attempt is one transition and waits until it succeeds → timeout-dependent (`exh` only); progress is `no-stall-within(b)` in both σ. At M_impl, if the attempt is an OSAL by-name lookup: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` decides an attempt VIOLATING without a time-based transition, so `Bad_i(σ_U∞)` and no timeout-dependent; the leaked type lock gives `leaked-lock` stall states to the provider and later lock-taking operations; on the `NAME_NOT_FOUND` path the actor can be stranded (`unknown(unmodelled)` in its closure). **Variant 2**: at b = ∞ the certificate's `¬OK_i` (later attempts excluded from ACCEPT by its INIT_FALSE and empty writer set) has no decision route, because its REJECT, the exhaustion, lies after the decision: `unknown(no_decision_route)` with `¬OK_i` shown in σ_U and σ_U∞; within b, σ_U is fixed-order (`exh`: the exhausting execution) and σ_U∞ `no-final-status` (every reached attempt stays `PENDING`); progress at M_api under σ_U∞ is `stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})`, a hang (the retrying actor is dead at the call node of its last attempt) → σ_U cell flags timeout-masked-liveness and `tml-root`; at M_impl, if the attempt is an OSAL lookup, the actor is stranded → `unknown(unmodelled)`. **Variant 3** (a retry paced by `OS_BinSemTimedWait` on a semaphore nobody gives, provider absent): the exhaustion is not time-based, but each timed wait is; σ_U `unknown(no_decision_route)` at b = ∞ and fixed-order within b; σ_U∞ `no-final-status` within b and `unknown(no_decision_route)` at b = ∞ (no σ_U∞ execution is bad, although the REJECT event itself is not time-based); progress σ_U∞ `stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})` → TML and `tml-root` on σ_U | σ_U∞ without time-based transitions at the deciding transition per M; horizon monitors; decision routes only from REJECTs at the decision; per-bound safety records; deadness; stall clause sets; stranding; refinements |
| **L-MIMPL** (X1-C2) | a by-name lookup racing a creation (RES_CN window) | M_api protected only if certified; M_impl: `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` reachable; no σ_E transfer from M_api; an observed `INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` yields `portability-hazard-evidence`, not a model fault | transfer rule 2; O4(b) at M_api |
| **L-L3** (X1-M10) | a critical section containing a cancel-and-join of another thread (as in `CFE_ES_DeleteChildTask`) | the L3 check fails; the section stays fine-grained; the cancellation-with-lock-held execution is reachable | mover conditions |
| **L-TIMEDWAIT** (X2-03) | a by-ID lookup of a RESERVED record with unrelated unlocks of the same type; a variant whose creator is held or lost before `FinalizeNew` | σ_U: exhaustion by timed or broadcast wakes. σ_U∞: exhaustion by broadcast wakes only. While the creator can still finalize, its finalizing unlock broadcasts and wakes the waiter to a LIVE record (the wait loop re-checks the record after every wake), so there is no stall. In the variant, from a state in which no further unlock of the type is reachable, the waiter is dead under σ_U∞: an in-use stall, because the waiter is inside its call and the deciding check is still ahead [I]; σ_U escapes by the timed wake (`timeout-masked-liveness`) | the two wake causes; time-based transitions; deadness relative to the remaining unlocks |
| **LP-DL** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | (a) `loc` VALID at INIT, with no writer or disabler. A once actor T0 creates mutexes `m1` and `m2` and spawns τ_A and τ_B. τ_A: take `m1`, take `m2`, use `loc`, give `m2`, give `m1`. τ_B: take `m2`, take `m1`, give `m1`, give `m2`. `OS_MutSemTake` has no timeout. (b) τ_A runs its body twice (constant-trip loop, unrolled) and gives a semaphore `go` after each iteration; τ_B first takes `go` | **Certificate only**: `protected(σ_U, M)` (b = ∞, `¬NR_i`), reach `proved` or `assumed` per cell, progress `unknown(not_checked)`. **`exh`**: protected within b; progress `stall-witness({pre-use})`, kind `deadlock-cycle`, wait-for root the cycle, confirmable, harm `hang_or_deadlock`, in (a) and, at ordinal (τ_A, 2), in (b). The same in σ_U∞ (recomputed) and at M_impl. σ_E: the safety statement by rule 2; progress `unknown(not_checked)` unless a run hangs with a wait-for cycle. Never "protected" without its progress value, and never `no-stall-within(b)` | L-PR's scope; progress candidates with all four queries; the closed cone includes the locks on τ_A's path, so τ_B is in the cone; **L2 keeps τ_B's inner `m1` section** (its lock is a cone object acquired by τ_A), which a body-only L2 condition would remove; L3 keeps τ_A's `m1` section fine-grained; per-occurrence pre-use stalls (b); product states; the rendering rule |

(c) W takes `s` without a timeout and then uses `loc2`; only a litmus teardown actor X, which runs after a litmus SHUTDOWN_REQ store made on B.Input's stop event, gives `s` | **(a)** σ_U: protected, reach `model(b)` through the timeout and `proved` by a run at M_impl, progress `no-stall-within(b)`, flags `timeout-rescued` and timeout-masked-liveness (with `tml-root`, since no chain leads into W's instance). σ_U∞: protected at b = ∞ (rule 1(a)), `no-final-status` within b, reach `unreached(b)`, progress `stall-witness({pre-use})`, a hang; timeout-dependent not applicable (σ_U is protected, not schedule-dependent; the use is also unreached under σ_U∞). σ_E: safety statement by rule 2; reach proved. **(b)** σ_U: `stall-witness({pre-use})` qualified `terminal-pending`, harm `terminal_pending(abort)` (not a hang), in both bases; post-abort states are excluded sinks. σ_U∞: in `B_skip` a hang (`hang_or_deadlock`), so σ_U has timeout-masked-liveness; in `B_give` `no-stall-within(b)`. **(c)** every model σ (σ_U, σ_U∞, σ_R1): W is dead for the default-scope instance (excluded states are sinks), a hang of kind `other`, not terminal-pending (the stop input is not counted); σ_E: `unknown(not_checked)`, since a σ_E stall witness needs a wait-for fact for a `deadlock-cycle` or a `leaked-lock` (§1.10); the use after X's give is a K-TEARDOWN instance | prefix containment holds, while maximal containment fails; transfer rules 1–3 give one consistent record per cell and bound; refinements as derived predicates; the unified process-terminal list, the exclusion, the sink rule and the terminal-pending qualifier |
| **LP-LEAK** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | (a) M_impl: P creates counting semaphore "S"; La looks "S" up by name once, gives `b` and exits; Lb takes `b` without a timeout, then looks "S" up. (b) A two-conjunct litmus readiness specification at a call `use_iu(x)` by U: `x` is a capture of `loc3`; the body takes `m3` untimed, reads the registry `r`, gives `m3`; H takes `m3` and waits forever on a semaphore nobody gives | **(a)** La's lookup: `OS_ERR_INCORRECT_OBJ_STATE` in P's RESERVED window → REJECT at decision (outside `codes`), VIOLATING; schedule-dependent at ordinal (La, 1). Lb's lookup: `stall-witness({in-use})` (Lb is inside its call), kind `leaked-lock` (the non-recursive type lock's holder La returned and exited), wait-for root the type lock, confirmable, harm `hang_or_deadlock`, progress chain from La's lookup. M_api: no leak, no stall; levels disagree → implementation-defect candidate. **(b)** `stall-witness({in-use})`, kind `other` (H has a path to its release), not confirmable | per-occurrence occurrences that begin at the call; the in-use clause; leaked-lock by holder state; progress chains; the M_api/M_impl split |
| **LP-SCOPE2** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | a two-conjunct use `use2(x)` by U, `Allowed none`: `x` is a capture of `loc` (a non-scope disabler D can run before it), and the body reads `reg` unconditionally; Q makes a litmus SHUTDOWN_REQ store on B.Input's stop event; a SCOPE disabler d of `reg` follows the store | store before U's call → out of scope (K-TEARDOWN); capture false → decided at the call, VIOLATING, in scope if the store follows the call, unchanged by a later d; capture true and store and d before `reg`'s read → out of scope; class within b schedule-dependent at (U, 1) | scope at decision; L-SCOPE; L-STATUS (b); `dpor` against `exh` with the extended dependence |

Retry variant (b): an early-returning attempt is no ACCEPT; fixed-order within b, `unknown(no_decision_route)` at b = ∞. Variant (c): occurrences with `p != NULL` are `READY`, those with `p == NULL` are `NOT_EVALUATED`; `¬NR_i` holds in σ_U (L-PR) and, by transfer rule 2, over every real run at M_impl, including a run in which `lk` returns `ERR_ARG`: that run is no O4(b) failure and no counterexample to the σ_E cell (the wording of rule 2 before 2026-10-15, "other than `READY`", would have been contradicted by it); reach is `proved` only by a run that contains a call with `p != NULL`, and `assumed` if every observed call has `p == NULL` | `NOT_EVALUATED`; L-FO and L-PR with a call that returns before its LP; O4(b); O10; the wording of transfer rule 2 |
| **LP-ALLOW** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | a litmus resource `R` set by P, an API `get(R)` returning `OK` or `NOT_READY`; one variant per Allowed kind: (a) `documented_drop` performed by U's own module and documented by a comment there (side `caller`, the shape of EVS's own-ID guard); (a2) the same drop performed by a second API `send(R)` and documented only in its header (side `supplier`); (a3), (a4) (a) and (a2) with a must-deliver `appreq`; (b1r) (b1) with no comment (side `rule`); (b) `returned_and_tested` with callers that (b1) rejoin, (b2) return an error, (b3) ignore the status, (b4) use a wrapper with documented propagation, (b5) call `OS_TaskExit`, and real code shapes: (b6) goto-cleanup in an unrolled loop (C1 at 2a024d8), (b7) a test in a star-closed loop with or without a not-ready write, (b8) an API that tests internally before a documented propagation (`CFE_EVS_Register`), (b9) test, log, rejoin and retest (`TO_LAB_init`); (c) `fallback(alt)`; (d) `defer_by_design` with the deferral point in actor D, (d1) spawned after the use, (d2) existing but blocked, (d3) a spawner that may `abort()` before spawning D, (d4) D failing its own init in a window, (d5) (d1) with a must-deliver `appreq`; (e) `exit(EXIT_DOC, src)`; (f) no caller-level entry, with and without `appreq` | (a) TOLERATED (other not-ready outcome: VIOLATING); (a2) NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with `fw_doc` as the header says (a supplier-only entry decides nothing); (a3) NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named (VIOLATING or TOLERATED once a precedence is set); (a4) VIOLATING; (b) is run twice, with the continuation cited by a `caller` record (b-cited; a litmus reference, a unit-test condition and no research condition) and with none cited (b-uncited, Q15 strict alternative): in b-uncited every (b) variant is NOT_READY_UNJUDGED, with the extracted facts (`cont_candidate`, error exit, non-success return, next operation) in its consequence, and the results below are for b-cited; (b1) TOLERATED at the cited join; (b1r) NOT_READY_UNJUDGED (a `rule` record alone never accepts, QT-4 strict alternative; VIOLATING with an `appreq` that requires `R`'s state); (b2), (b3), (b5) VIOLATING; (b4) TOLERATED if the wrapper's caller tests and rejoins, VIOLATING if it ignores the status; (b6) VIOLATING (the status is returned before any cited continuation; `cont_candidate` is none, recorded as a fact); (b7) VIOLATING with the not-ready write, TOLERATED at the iteration end without it; (b8) VIOLATING if the caller ignores the result, TOLERATED if it tests and rejoins with the status dead, VIOLATING in both without the documentation entry; (b9) VIOLATING; (c) TOLERATED, VIOLATING if another call comes first; (d1) PENDING then TOLERATED when D subscribes; with D's spawner blocked forever, a horizon stall (monitored actor not yet spawned), harm `task_never_runs`; with D exiting first, VIOLATING; (d2) horizon stall, `hang_or_deadlock`; (d3) PENDING and no witness where T0 aborts, so protected with `allowed-not-ready` within b; (d4) schedule-dependent, chained to D's window occurrence; (d5) NOT_READY_UNJUDGED with the conflict named; (e) TOLERATED with the documented code, VIOLATING otherwise; (f) VIOLATING with `appreq`, NOT_READY_UNJUDGED without; `consequence` filled in every variant; `allowed-not-ready` only from `exh` | every Allowed kind; the extraction of the candidate continuation per loop iteration (a fact, not a judgement), liveness of the status, REJECT precedence, error exits and documented propagation; no verdict by or after a process-terminal step; the not-yet-spawned monitored actor and its place in the closed cone; `dpor` against `exh` for (d) |
| **LP-RESTART** (EXT-20261012 §4.2) | a scanner S (constant-trip loop of scans paced by `OS_TaskDelay`) keeps for app A a `stopped` flag and a litmus countdown declared time-based (timer 0 expires at once); on a B.Input request S sets a positive timer; at expiry S deletes queue Qa and then A's child C, which uses Qa (resources before tasks, as `CFE_ES_CleanUpApp` runs the module cleanups, `cfe_es_apps.c:1525–1545`, before deleting the tasks, `:1561–1574`; §3.8), and re-spawns A; A either (v) sets `stopped` and loops in `OS_TaskDelay` or (n) never checks the request; instance: K-LIFE.osal at C's `OS_QueuePut(Qa)` | σ_U: schedule-dependent in both variants. σ_U∞: (v) the expiry after `stopped` (`expire-stopped`) is not time-based, so the deletion and generation 1 still happen → `Bad_i(σ_U∞)`, no refinement; (n) the forced kill (`expire-waiting`) is time-based → `¬Bad_i(σ_U∞)` within b, reached → timeout-dependent; S is never dead. With C deleted before Qa, σ_U has no bad witness (negative check) | the narrowed kill-timer transition with outcome labels; K-LIFE and generations under σ_U∞ |
| **LP-CASE-C1** (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2) | provider P creates countsem "S"; L retries a by-name lookup of "S" (`if (ret != OS_ERR_NAME_NOT_FOUND) break; OS_TaskDelay(d);`, litmus bound), then on failure (r) re-binds with a second lookup at another call site or (x) stores `stopped` and loops in `OS_TaskDelay`; bystander B creates countsem "T" and then looks it up; in (x) a cleaner Cl waits for `stopped` and calls `OS_ForEachObject` | (σ_U, M_impl): schedule-dependent at (L, 1) for key "S"; L's own instance has no stall state; `exh` dependents, one wait-for root (the countsem type lock): P dead in `FinalizeNew`, B dead in its create with its lookup ahead, in (x) Cl dead in object iteration, in (r) L dead at its re-bind, an in-use stall of the separate re-bind instance; all `leaked-lock`, confirmable, counted once, chained to L's VIOLATING attempt. (σ_U∞, M_impl), P creating only after L's last lookup: L stranded → `unknown(unmodelled)` for tests in L's closure; B's and P's tests are decided (they do not wait on L). (M_api) with P never running: L dead, `stall-witness({pre-use, horizon})`, timeout-masked-liveness on σ_U | per-M time-based transitions; stranding and its closure; leaked-lock with a re-entering leaker and an iterating cleaner; instances per calling context; grouping by wait-for root set; `OS_CountSem*` and iteration lock modes |
| **LP-CASE-C2** (EXT-20261012 §4.1–4.2) | barrier actor Bt polls P's `declared` or `stopped` flag with a soft litmus timeout (the analogue of `AppState < AppStateId` with STOPPED above RUNNING), then sets `phase = OP`; R waits for `phase = OP` and calls `svc(P)` (`Req = late = DONE`, `Allowed unspecified_by_reference`); variants: (buggy) P declares before its late init; (fixed) P declares after it; (fixed, fail) P's late init can fail, after which P sets `stopped` and exits; (fixed, cycle) P's late init waits for a message R sends only after `svc` returns | (buggy) schedule-dependent in σ_U and σ_U∞, no refinement. (fixed) σ_U schedule-dependent (the bad execution needs Bt's timeout); σ_U∞ `¬Bad_i`, reached → timeout-dependent. (fixed, fail) a TB-free bad execution through `stopped` → `Bad_i(σ_U∞)`, no refinement. (fixed, cycle) σ_U fixed-order within b; σ_U∞ `unreached(b)`, `no-final-status` within b; R dead before its call (pre-use stall, kind `other`, wait-for root the cycle {P, R, Bt}, a terminal strongly connected component; no chain root, not counted); timeout-masked-liveness and `tml-root` on R's instance | no-root progress results; the wait-for root of a predicate-wait cycle; `tml-root` on an unchained instance; the reach condition of timeout-dependent; a failed provider passing the barrier; a soft timeout turning a hang into a fixed-order readiness violation |
| **LP-CASE-C3** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | registry `reg[]`; cell `X` (initially 0, the slot of actor E) and cell `Y` (initially out of range); E stores `Y := 0` then sets `reg[0]`; S stores `X := 1` then sets `reg[1]`; T calls `send(X)` (range and `reg[X]` checks); on unregistered it calls `notreg()`, which reads `Y` (guarded, no undefined behaviour); instances (1) at `send(X)` (`valid(X) ∧ reg[X]`), (2) at `notreg()`'s read of `Y` (`valid(Y)`), both `Allowed none` | (1) not ready iff T's call precedes the registration of the identity it carries; READY with `X = 0` after E registered, the misattribution that K-ID cannot express (recorded as an adequacy gap); (2) only on (1)'s not-ready branch, chain (1) ⇝ (2), bad iff E has not stored `Y`; both schedule-dependent at their single ordinals, also under σ_U∞ | two identity cells; value and registry conjuncts; readiness-violation chains; the K-ID adequacy gap of C3 |
| **LP-CASE-C5** (EXT-20261012 §4.1) | registrar G sets `cnt := 1`, creates task A, then under mutex `m` writes `tab[A] = USED` (A can run before the write), and spawns W after setting `cnt`; A reads `tab[self]` in `reg_evs()` (which, like `CFE_EVS_Register`, tests the status internally and returns it under a documented propagation; A ignores it), in `mkpipe()` (status ignored) and in `runloop()` (failure returns false, `Allowed unspecified_by_reference`; success decrements `cnt` once); W polls `cnt == 0` with a soft litmus timeout, then calls `ops()` (`Req = cnt = 0`, `Allowed unspecified_by_reference`); variant (early-W): W is spawned before `cnt` is set | A's three uses: schedule-dependent at ordinal 1 (`runloop` has several ordinals; only the first can fall in the window). W's instance: σ_U schedule-dependent at (W, 1) (W times out when `runloop` failed); σ_U∞ W dead at its poll on that branch, a hang chained to A's `runloop` occurrence → timeout-masked-liveness and timeout-dependent on W's instance, `tml-root` on A's `runloop` instance. (early-W): W can pass before `cnt` is set, a TB-free bad execution → `Bad_i(σ_U∞)`, no timeout-dependent; TML still holds | an API that tests internally before propagating, then an ignored status; ignored status; NOT_READY_UNJUDGED; ordinals with only ordinal 1 in the window; a progress chain with a not-ready root; a counter race that must be excluded before timeout-dependent is claimed |

**Completion conditions of EXT-20261012.**
- **§4.1.** LP-DL has a deadlock before the use while readiness at the use is proved. No route yields an unconditional protected: every cell carries its reach value and its progress value (`unknown(not_checked)` or a stall witness) and, where it rests on a certificate, its lemma status (§6.2), and G2 and §6.4 item 26 state that the certificate makes no progress claim. The closed-cone rule (§4.10 item 1 (i)), which puts τ_B in the cone, and the L2 lock condition, which keeps τ_B's inner `m1` section, make sure that `exh` never prints `no-stall-within(b)` for it; variant (b) checks the per-occurrence pre-use clause.
- **§4.2.** LP-TW progresses with timeouts and stops without them. Its σ_U∞ finite executions are σ_U finite executions, while its σ_U∞-maximal execution is not σ_U-maximal. The transfer rules carry only safety statements down and recompute reach and progress per σ, and the classes are computed per bound from the combined record, so the σ_U and σ_U∞ cells do not contradict each other in any order of evaluation.
- **Beyond the two conditions.** LP-SCOPE2, LP-EARLY, LP-ALLOW (with the real code shapes (b6)–(b9), (d3), (d4)), LP-RESTART, L-PATIENT variant 3 and LP-CASE-C1/C2/C3/C5 make each Allowed kind and each design case's mechanism and code shape checkable on the v7.0.1 OSAL; they are evidence about the definitions, not about the cases.

## Appendix B. Literature relied on (status as verified in [R04], [R05], [R06])

Since revision 1.2 the thread-level tools and techniques below are related work or the source of a mechanism; none is an experimental comparator (§8.3, ADR-046). The experimental comparison is among existing process-level race work, whose papers are read under P-PAPERS; their statuses are in `baselines/availability.md`.

- Lamport 1978; Fidge 1988; Mattern 1989; Netzer & Miller 1992; Lu et al. 2008 — V-abs.
- FastTrack 2009; ThreadSanitizer 2009/2011; Eraser 1997; O'Callahan & Choi 2003 — V-abs (Djit+ 2003: P).
- CP 2012; WCP 2017; SHB 2018; M2 2020; SyncP 2021; Vindicator 2018; maximal causal models 2012; RVPredict 2014; GPredict 2015; **ExceptioNULL 2012** — V-abs.
- DefUse 2010; AVIO 2006; Velodrome 2008; ConMem 2010; CTrigger 2009; Xiong et al. 2010; Tian et al. 2008 — V-abs.
- EventRacer 2013; DroidRacer 2014; CAFA 2014; **InitRacer 2017** — V-abs. DCatch 2017 — P.
- CloudRaid 2018; Snowboard 2021; FCatch 2018; DataCollider 2010 — V-abs. KCSAN design document — V-doc.
- RacePro 2011; DESCRY 2017; RaceFuzzer 2008; Portend 2012 — V-abs.
- DPOR 2005 (guarantee wording UNVERIFIED); source/optimal DPOR POPL 2014 (JACM extension to disabling: P); BPOR 2013; fair stateless model checking 2008 (strongly fair scheduler); PCT 2010 (mechanism P); SCTBench 2014 — V-abs.
- Alglave, Kroening & Tautschnig 2013; Wang et al. 2009 — V-abs.
- SIERRA 2018; SDV 2006; Holzmann & Joshi 2004 — V-abs. FlowDroid 2014 — V-me.
- Andersen 1994; SVF 2016; MLTA 2019 — V-abs.
- Soundiness 2015; Arcuri & Briand 2011; Klees et al. 2018 (trial count: P) — V-abs. Methodological literature is used for assumptions, never as a `from-reference` value ([POL] L5–10).
- Strom & Yemini 1986 (typestate); Engler et al. 2001 — V-me.
- Boehm 2012 (seqlocks), cited only as verified in [R02 §10].
- Tool documentation (V-doc in [R05]): Nidhugg README (LLVM 8–19; deterministic pthread threads); GenMC README (LLVM 19–22).
- Standard algorithmic fact used without a verified citation: maximum-weight closure via minimum cut (K-CAP bound), as in [A §4.6].
- Joshi & Sen, ASE 2008 (predictive typestate checking of multithreaded programs); Meier, Mover & Chang, Lifestate, ECOOP 2019 (framework-lifecycle typestate and protocol specifications checked over traces): bibliographic records V per `background/08_terminology.md` L754, L758; content known only from abstracts and the external review; cited in §1.11 as prior art only, and no difference from them is final until they are read (P-PAPERS). GPredict 2015's property formalism (§II.B) is known here only from the external review and a search snippet [U] (P-PAPERS); the roadmap of 2026-10-15 and the resolved-decisions record state that its author-public text was read in the review before the decision (§1.11).
- **Open literature checks** (no claim rests on them until done): the left/right-mover reduction theorem behind L3; the JACM 2017 statement of source-DPOR with disabling (T-JACM); the novelty of timeout-conditional barrier edges (G3, [R04 §8 item 5]); the HB rule tables of DroidRacer, CAFA, EventRacer and DCatch ([R04 §8 item 3]).

No other literature is relied on.

## Appendix C. Terminology mapping (decision T1 = (a); old → new)

Source: `PI_DECISIONS_RESOLVED_20261015.md` §2 (T1) and §5; `background/08_terminology.md` §5.1–§5.6; ADR-052. The names were chosen once for this specification. The mapping is the agreed reference for this document, the Korean mirrors, the diagrams and the implementation, which keep the old words until their own steps (§0.11). The old names are listed so that older text, records and code can be read; they are not synonyms in new text. File extensions (`.kzp`, `.kz`, `.kzt`), the identifiers `K-*` and `RC1`–`RC15`, κ and every field name are unchanged.

**C.1 Terms in prose**

| Old | New | Where | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| readiness contract(s); "a contract" meaning κ | readiness specification(s) (준비 상태 명세); first use in a paper: provenance-tracked readiness specification | §0.1, §1.6 and everywhere | κ unchanged. The bare word "specification" is not used for κ, because it is this document's name; it is used only inside fixed phrases such as "generic specification K-ID" |
| readiness-contract instance; contract instance | readiness instance | §0.1, §1.6 "Instances", D3 | the instance is still `i = (κ, t, key, scope)` |
| `Req` as "the required state / requirement" | readiness precondition | §0.2, §0.6, §0.7, §1.4, §1.6, §2.3, §3.6, §3.15, §6.3, §6.4, A.1, glossary | the symbol `Req` and the keyword `requires` are unchanged |
| contract violation; violation (of κ) | readiness violation, a subclass of order violation | §0.7, §1.6, §1.7, §1.8, §1.10, §1.11, §6.3 G3, §6.4, §7.3, §8.5, §8.8, §10.1, A.4, A.8 | `VIOLATING`, the outcome label `violation` and the enum value `VIOLATION` are identifiers and unchanged. "Order violation" (the class of the literature) is unchanged |

| class detail / refinement `tolerated` | `allowed-not-ready` | §0.1–§0.3, §0.7, §1.6, §1.8, §1.10, §3.10, §4.5, §4.10, §4.12, §6.2, §6.4, §7.3, §12 D3 and D6, glossary, A.4, A.8 | prose: "an allowed not-ready outcome", never "Kairos tolerates". No record, field, section type, tool name, file name or directory name of §2.3 and §9.1 contained "contract" apart from the items above.

**C.3 Unchanged on purpose**

- "API contract", "OSAL API contract", "API-contract model" and "API-contract claims" (M_api), and the tier name `contract-backed`: documented API semantics.
- κ, `Req`, `Oracle`, `Allowed`, `appreq`, `harm`, `prov`, `authored`, `guard`, `scope`; `K-*`, `RC1`–`RC15`; the occurrence statuses `READY`, `TOLERATED`, `VIOLATING`, `PENDING`, `NOT_READY_UNJUDGED`, `NOT_EVALUATED`; the outcome labels `violation`, `LOSS-UNDER-ORDER`, `not-ready-unjudged`, `liveness`; the harm values; "message provenance".
- Quotations of external sources and of earlier documents and the titles of cited papers: for example the quoted claim "none of 52 works calls the requirement a 'contract'" (§0.8) and the phrase "readiness-contract kinds" of [R03 §4] (§3.15 heading).
- In the Korean mirrors the form is 준비 상태 명세 in full, never a bare 명세; the same mapping applies there (`background/08_terminology.md` §5.5 B).
